## 2026-06-23

- Reviewed `paper.tex` for proof bookkeeping. The main `7/9` optimization is algebraically consistent once the certified route-potential accounting is in place.
- The proof needed explicit amplification across `O_c(eta^{-2})` restarted phases. A per-phase constant success probability would otherwise multiply along a route; using failure probability `O(1/D)` only costs an extra `O(log D)` factor.
- The spherical theorem is stated for fixed `(a,b)`, while the reduction uses `(a,b)` varying with `eta`. The constants are uniform because these correlations stay in a compact neighborhood depending only on fixed `c`.
- The reduction theorem should expose polynomial factors in `eta^{-1}` as well as `log n`; for the chosen `eta=L^{-2/9}` these are still lower-order polylogarithmic factors.
- Local TeX install is missing `cleveref.sty`, so `paper.tex` now has a minimal fallback for local compilation.
- Refactored the Euclidean part from an external "certified ball recursion" into a certified score-and-ball tree. The important state is now explicit: score edges, recentering edges, certified radii, query activation by expanded balls, depth, and route-potential invariants.
- The remaining mathematical bottleneck is no longer hidden as a black-box theorem, but the route-potential invariant is still the key thing a fully explicit cap-selection implementation must maintain.

## 2026-06-24

- Reoriented `paper.tex` away from the restart-loss baseline and toward the polylogarithmic target in `notes2.md`.
- Replaced the old spherical primitive with continuation-mass pruning. The expected-work and far-candidate bounds are proved exactly; near-pair survival is now an explicit one-sided spherical survival assumption.
- Replaced route-depth restart accounting with posterior first-hit and radius Bellman cancellation. The exact threshold is `tau=lambda^2`, which turns the recursive child charge into `alpha F(parent)`.
- Added the cohort-or-leaf assumption as the central remaining coupling theorem between ordinary paths and detector cohorts.
- Split the spherical survival gap into a proved good-leaf first moment and a remaining one-sided LCA second-moment assumption. The first moment uses the tilted measure, the endpoint unit square, and a Gaussian-bridge union bound for the continuation barrier.
- Refined the spherical witness set to "tame good" leaves with an analytical upper barrier on the common score coordinate. This upper barrier is not enforced by the algorithm, but it is needed to make the LCA second-moment target plausible by preventing prolific high shared prefixes.
- Promoted the tame-good LCA second moment from an assumption to a lemma. The key cancellation is in the common coordinate: after multiplying the split-depth probability by the ordered pair count `B^{2k-j}`, the main exponential term cancels because the common-coordinate drift has `mu^2=2 log B`; the remaining endpoint-difference density is summable over split depths.
- Added an exponent-improvement ladder to the paper: continuation-mass spherical filtering alone moves the restart-style target from `(log n)^{7/9}` overhead to roughly `(log n)^{2/3}`, one geometric improvement gives `(log n)^{1/2}`, two geometric improvements give `(log n)^{1/3}`, and the Bellman cohort proof removes the independent phase term entirely.
- The draft now has one named probabilistic bottleneck: cohort-or-leaf. This is the right place to focus next, because the exact continuation-work, spherical survival, posterior first-hit, and Bellman cancellation pieces are all written as self-contained lemmas.
- Tightened the polylog endpoint assumption from a local child-level cohort-or-leaf event to a route-level event. A local inverse-polylogarithmic success probability at each geometric level would multiply over a polylogarithmic-depth lineage and would not justify the claimed polylogarithmic theorem.
- Read `notes3.md` and added a restartable-slice route to the `2/3` rung. The recursive object is now the leaf-conditioned slice `C[U,w]`, not the original full child; this removes the atypical-prefix/full-child budget obstruction.
- The `2/3` accounting is now explicit: one restartable stage has polylogarithmic overhead, certified slice radii shrink by `1-Theta(eta^2)`, bounded-diameter localization gives `O(eta^{-2} log log n)` restarts, and optimizing `eta log n + eta^{-2}(log log n)^2` gives `exp(O_c((log n)^{2/3}(log log n)^{2/3}))`.
- Replaced the informal RANSAC cap-finder audit with a tuple cap-finder lemma. If a `2 eta` cap has mass `tau`, a tuple of `Theta(eta^{-2})` points from that cap has an empirical mean whose direction captures an `Omega(eta)` fraction of the cap at margin `eta`; repeating `tau^{-O(eta^{-2})}` proposals finds such a certified slice with high probability.
- Promoted the restartable-slice `2/3` milestone from a conditional reduction to an explicit proposition. The one-stage overhead now follows from continuation-mass hard leaves, residual-scan control, tuple cap finding, and the leaf-conditioned slice budget; remaining cleanup is mostly high-probability constants and localization details.
- Added budgeted continuation blocks. The spherical decoder can be run with depth based on any `N >= m`, giving query budget `N^rho` and far candidates `m N^{-(1-rho)}`. Choosing `N=m(R/r)^{2(1-rho)/rho}` makes `N^rho` exactly the Euclidean radius potential `F(m,R)`, so radius weighting is now part of the same score block rather than a separate wrapper.
- Folded in explanatory TikZ diagrams for the continuation barrier, LCA second moment, cap recentering, shell angular gap, Bellman cancellation, cumulative score-and-ball tree, and the `2/3` balance. The diagrams are not new assumptions, but they make the proof dependencies easier to audit.
- Added prefix-conditioned slice accounting. For a prefix-free score frontier, slices inside a score bucket have query charge `sum nu^s F(child) <= J^{1-rho} F(parent)` by Hölder and storage charge at most `m F(parent)`. This removes the full-child atypical-prefix obstruction on the accounting side and reduces the preferred polylog endpoint to prefix-slice lineage survival.
- Read `notes4.md` and added a randomized one-tree route to the `1/3` rung. The useful shift is to treat almost-total cap splits as randomized geometric edges inside the same cumulative score tree: always follow the residual, follow the cap with probability `z^s Psi(child)/Psi(parent)`, and use `Psi(R)=1+(R/r)^4`. This gives deterministic query-potential contraction when `delta^rho << eta^2`, while the near-lineage thinning probabilities telescope to an inverse-polylog factor.
- The new `1/3` proposition is conditional on two explicit adaptive score-process statements: an information-time LCA/tube lemma for random cap-discovery frontiers, and a mixed first-hit operator inequality charging terminal cap-free scans plus thinned geometric children in one global score clock. With `eta=(log n)^(-2/3)` and `delta=eta^{3/rho}`, the residual scan factor remains polylogarithmic and the only nonpolylog overhead is `exp(O_c((log n)^{1/3}))`.
- Added a continuation-mass route from the randomized one-tree construction to the polylog rung. The new adaptive continuation lemma extends the one-sided spherical first/second moment to predictable triangular score schedules, so near-lineage score survival is inverse-polylog even with recentering. Choosing `eta=Theta(loglog n / log n)` makes geometric slack, residual charging, thinning, and `eta^{-1}` factors polylogarithmic. The remaining randomized endpoint is now a single mixed continuation operator for far-work accounting over cap-discovery frontiers.
- Proved the mixed continuation operator for the randomized cap-thinning route. Terminal cap-free accepting leaves are charged to their inverse-polylog fraction of low-correlation points; first cap-split frontiers are charged by thinned cap contraction plus the prefix-free Hölder bound `sum nu^s mu^rho <= 1`. The main theorem is now stated through this self-contained randomized route rather than the older cohort-or-leaf assumption, which remains only as a possible generalization to arbitrary certified child hierarchies.
- Added an explicit projected bounded-diameter localization lemma. A polylog-dimensional Gaussian projection plus randomly shifted grids reduces the problem to projected cells of radius `r polylog n`; near pairs share a cell with constant probability, far distances are preserved for the fixed query with high probability, and the exponent only changes by `O_c(1/log n)`.
- Tightened the localization proof so bad projected copies do not hide an expected-work cost. The projection dimension now makes the all-data-points preservation failure probability `n^{-Omega(1)}` for a fixed query, and each bad localized query is capped by brute-force scanning its projected cell, giving negligible expected contribution.
- Audited the notes4 one-tree/polylog route and found that the mixed continuation proof was using a normalized-path Hölder step that is not justified for the branching continuation decoder: a point or query can occupy many prefix nodes. Downgraded the main self-contained theorem to the proved restartable-slice `2/3` bound, kept the randomized one-tree route as the conditional `1/3`/polylog target, and made the missing branching first-split power estimate explicit as the mixed continuation operator.
- Tightened the older posterior/cohort route by explicitly separating normalized detector laws from raw ordinary retained-prefix multiplicity. The posterior first-hit and prefix-slice Hölder inequalities are now stated only for one-token detector mass, where prefix-free sums are genuinely at most one. This makes the work-side Bellman cancellation exact again and leaves the real polylog obstacle as the cohort-or-leaf coupling between ordinary continuation paths and detector activations.
- Added a vote-budgeted activation lemma. Once detector tokens are materialized, opened recursive children are paid deterministically by the votes they consume: `sum opened F(child) <= F(parent)` up to the chosen logarithmic oversampling. This removes activated-child work accounting from the hard part; what remains is the probabilistic near-lineage coupling showing that the true child gets enough votes, or an ordinary residual path finds the near point.
- Made the detector law concrete: detector tokens are sampled by a one-token continuation-mass walk that chooses one retained child proportional to its exact remaining continuation mass, or dies. Prefix-free detector mass is therefore normalized by construction, not an extra assumption. The remaining coupling question is now specifically whether these continuation-sampled detector tokens hit the true near-lineage activations with inverse-polylogarithmic probability.
- Re-read notes4 for the `1/3` rung and removed the obsolete adaptive-score assumption from that section. The tube calculation is now only intuition; the formal `1/3` proposition uses the proved adaptive-continuation survival lemma and assumes only a cube-root-scale mixed frontier/work inequality. This narrows the remaining one-tree obstacle to branching first-hit accounting.
- Tested a tempting detector-shadowing shortcut and found the flaw: analytic continuation mass is harmonic only in expectation over the next Gaussian children, not over the realized finite sibling set. Added the exact identity that detector probability for a retained leaf is the ideal `H(leaf)/H(root)` telescoping term divided by a product of realized sibling-inflation factors. This turns the detector part of cohort-or-leaf into a concrete martingale/second-moment target: prove polynomial inflation along tame-good near leaves.
- Split detector inflation into on-spine continuation ratios and off-spine sibling noise. Conditional on a fixed retained spine, the off-spine product has expectation bounded by `prod_i(R_i+(B-1)/B)`, where `R_i=H(v_{i+1})/H(v_i)`. Thus detector shadowing at a retained activation prefix follows from the scalar condition `sum_i R_i=O(log K)` with token cost proportional to `H(root)/H(prefix)`. This is a narrower target than bounding all realized sibling inflation directly, and avoids incorrectly requiring detectors to shadow full accepting leaves.
- Checked the scalar spine-ratio target against the deterministic tilted linear spine. There `H(next)/H(current)` is a fixed positive constant for most levels, so the sum is `Theta(K)`, not `O(log K)`. The single-spine detector-shadowing condition is therefore only an early-activation sufficient condition. Long near routes need either aggregate detector mass over many good prefixes or an ordinary cap-free scan route.
- Improved the randomized one-tree parameters from the conservative `delta=eta^{3/rho}` in `notes4.md` to the maximal contraction scale `delta=Theta_c(eta^{2/rho_c})`. This keeps `delta^rho=O(eta^2)` for thinned cap-split contraction and still has `H delta=o(1)` because `rho_c<1`, while reducing the residual-scan and cap-finder polylog factors. Also separated the randomized cap-thinning gap from the detector-vote cohort gap in the conclusion: the former needs a branching first-split work estimate, not near-lineage detector coupling.
- Narrowed the notes4 mixed-frontier assumptions. Added a cap-free terminal scan accounting lemma: if every scanned cap-free accepting bucket has an `alpha` fraction of points with correlation at most `2 eta`, then scans are paid by low-correlation accepting collisions with cost `alpha^{-1} exp(O_c(eta log n)) F_4`. After this, the cube-root and polylog assumptions only concern thinned geometric first-split/frontier potentials.
- Read Andoni--Beaglehole 2022 after downloading it. The useful proof pattern is not the Hamming coordinate hash itself, but the per-node min-max/Bellman certificate: a local inequality against every adversarial query-neighbor pair telescopes by induction through the whole tree. Added a self-contained Bellman frontier certificate lemma for our score tree: if a predictable prefix potential supermartingale dominates local first-split child charges, then the mixed continuation operator follows by optional stopping. This replaces one global-looking route to the remaining gap by a local potential target.
- Added a budget-gated cap-splitting variant: with any predictable budget supermartingale, the query only treats an almost-total cap split as live when its thinned recursive charge fits the local budget. This makes the recursive work side unconditional for live splits. Also checked the obvious query-only continuation budget and found it fails in early balanced prefixes by a factor `B^{(1-rho) ell}`, so the eventual certificate must be data-aware or must deliberately defer early splits.
- Instantiated the Bellman framework with a canonical mixed continuation budget `B_mix(u)=Psi D(u)^rho Q(u)^(1-rho)/H0`, where `D` is aggregate data continuation mass and `Q` is query continuation mass. Hölder plus concavity proves this is a prefix supermartingale with root value `F_4(m,R)`. In homogeneous balanced prefixes it has the same `B^{-ell}` scale as the query-only budget, so it is a valid baseline work certificate but still too conservative to make typical early cap splits live.
- Added a budget-thinned first-opportunity rule: instead of requiring a cap split to fit the local budget deterministically, activate it with probability proportional to `B(u)/Gamma(u)`. Optional stopping then proves the activated geometric work bound for any prefix-supermartingale budget. This turns the balanced-prefix obstruction from a work blow-up into a near-lineage survival/value-delivery question.
- The Andoni--Beaglehole proxy-success idea suggests carrying the dual value surrogate `sigma=1/F` alongside the work surrogate `F`. The fixed detector threshold `T_C=F(C)` is a full-activation special case; a cleaner cohort target is partial value-budgeted search, where `b_C` votes buy `b_C/F(C)` conditional success value. This may avoid needing to materialize a full child budget at every lineage activation, but it requires a splittable recursive continuation procedure and a worst-case value-delivery lemma, not an average over the unknown near point.
- Read `notes5.md`. The new route replaces cap discovery by posterior-superlevel children of a finite score channel. The key exact identity is a negative-moment compiler: Poisson posterior thresholds make the expected child mass negative moment equal to the channel affinity `sum_j K_p(j)^beta K_q(j)^(1-beta)`. Top-`xi` collapse plus `xi=m^{-rho/(1+rho)}` gives the macro arithmetic `xi^{-(1+rho)}=m^rho`. This yields a clean conditional polylog theorem if one can compile the continuous Gaussian likelihood-ball channel into `B ~= xi^{-1}` balanced, query-decodable outcomes preserving the restricted negative moment. This does not improve the unconditional `2/3` exponent yet, but it gives a different and arguably sharper remaining theorem than the cap-frontier operator.
- Added an executable finite-channel diagnostic for Conjecture 9.1. Given channel matrices `K_data,K_query`, it computes the lower-quantile quantity `g(q)+H_eta(p,q)` exactly, with `tau_j` as the top-`eta` posterior threshold. It also includes balanced-softmax offsets, Gaussian/whitened/cross-polytope panels, effective support, and Hessian condition-number measurements. This is now the main empirical test for whether finite query-decodable quantization is plausible; it does not change the proved `2/3` theorem.
- Added a sweep harness for the finite-channel diagnostic and a low-guard planted-pair mode. The critical random-sphere experiment is misleading for Conjecture 9.1 because `g(q)` is about `1/2`; the isolated mode with near correlation `0.95` has `g(q) ~= 1/n`, so `H_eta` is the signal. In a small sweep (`d=16`, `c=2`, `B=16 eta^{-1}`, two seeds), the whitened Gaussian panel had much larger lower quantiles than the other panels, but still drifted down: `score_q05 ~= 0.109, 0.077, 0.059` for `n=300,1000,3000`. This supports prioritizing whitening/curvature, but it is not yet evidence for a constant lower bound.
- Found that the apparent finite-channel drift was largely a temperature artifact. After adding an inverse-temperature scale to the balanced softmax, the low-guard whitened-Gaussian diagnostic with `B=16 eta^{-1}`, `d=16`, two seeds, and near correlation `0.95` gives best `score_q05 ~= 0.565, 0.537, 0.481, 0.487` for `n=1000,3000,10000,30000`, with median effective support about `1.1--1.5`. This points to the concentrated-`K_z`/posterior-contraction regime rather than the well-conditioned-curvature regime. It is still experimental evidence, not a proof: with only `B=eta^{-1}` the same diagnostic collapses by `n=30000`.
- Added a deterministic posterior-rank margin certificate: if a non-negligible fraction of the tilted affinity `K_p^beta K_q^(1-beta)` lies on outcomes where `R_j(p)` beats the top-`eta` threshold by `exp(Delta)`, then `H_eta` is large by a factor `1-exp(-beta Delta)`. For softmax the tilted law is exactly `K_z` with `z=beta p+(1-beta)q`, so the next proof target is precise: show that the high-temperature, balanced, whitened panel puts most `K_z` mass on posterior-rank-margin outcomes while keeping the unrestricted affinity inverse-polylogarithmic. In the small high-temperature smoke run (`n=300`, `B=10`, scale `8`), the lower 5% quantiles were `affinity ~= 0.79`, tilted margin mass `~= 0.85`, and certified bound `~= 0.46`, so the observed `H_eta` is explained by the deterministic certificate, not by an unstructured numerical artifact.
- Added the smooth product filter theorem directly to `paper.tex` as a channel-free terminal engine. It proves constant single-table recall and `m^rho polylog(m)` query/update/far-candidate work for spherical terminal search. This does not solve localization, but it removes the final spherical amplification penalty from the non-channel cap-thinning architecture: after a localization layer creates the critical spherical gap, terminal search is no longer the source of inverse-polylog overhead.
- Read `notes6.md` and reframed the posterior route around structured decoding rather than finite explicit quantization. The new scale-window compiler shows the exact arithmetic: if a mass scale `xi` can be decoded output-sensitively, `xi^{-rho}` independent groups turn restricted negative-moment value into constant hit probability, and this fits the `m^rho` budget for every nonempty scale `xi >= 1/m`. Thus the old `xi=m^{-1/(2c^2)}` barrier is only the cost of enumerating all outcomes.
- Added a near-terminal structured-channel endpoint to `paper.tex`. Conditional on an implicit posterior decoder that finds buckets of size `polylog(m)` with inverse-polylog restricted negative moment and scale-normalized query/storage moments, the ANN query becomes one posterior layer plus direct terminal scans, giving `n^rho polylog(n)` query and `n^{1+rho} polylog(n)` space. This is a sharper conditional target than the finite balanced softmax channel, but it is still not an unconditional improvement beyond the proved `2/3` exponent.
- Replaced the near-terminal structured-channel target by a stronger geometric reduction and a weaker decoding target. The continuous Gaussian likelihood-ball argument is now in `paper.tex` as a self-contained theorem: unless a uniform guard of size `m^rho polylog(m)` succeeds, there is constant negative-moment value on children that contain the near point and no invalid point. Such a child only needs a stored representative, not a recursive search or terminal scan.
- Added dyadic structured amplification. If a structured decoder preserves inverse-polylog pure value across mass scales `1/m <= xi <= m^{-rho} polylog(m)`, repeating scale `xi` about `xi^{-rho}` times gives constant hit probability, and the dyadic sum is still `m^rho polylog(m)` query and `m^{1+rho} polylog(m)` stored descriptions. The remaining posterior gap is now specifically output-sensitive preservation of the pure likelihood-ball value, rather than finite quantization or near-terminal bucket construction.
- Proved the ideal continuous likelihood-ball scale profile in the paper. For each dyadic mass scale, the ideal active `rho`-moment and stored `(1+rho)`-moment are already `O(xi^rho)`, and the pure guard-failure value from the continuous theorem is exactly the sum of the dyadic negative moments. This narrows the posterior gap further: only query-decodable materialization of stored representatives is missing; the continuous geometry, dyadic work arithmetic, and moment bounds are all self-contained.
- Read `notes6.md` as theory guidance rather than evidence. Added a rank-prefix identity for the ideal likelihood-ball process: after the Gaussian score `Y` is fixed, the threshold continuum is exactly a distribution over score prefixes with weights `r_i^alpha-r_{i+1}^alpha`. This reframes the remaining posterior target as output-sensitive random-score prefix reporting, not finite channel quantization. It does not improve the unconditional theorem beyond the proved `2/3` exponent.
- Found an `alpha=0` maximum-score collapse of the likelihood argument. If `Y ~ N(tq,tI)` with `t=Theta_c(log m/r^2)`, then with high probability every invalid point has score `<Y,x>-t||x||^2/2` at least `Theta_c(log m)` below the true near point. Thus an additive `Theta(log m)` maximum-score oracle would solve the node in one layer, with no dyadic scales or representative materialization. This is a sharper non-channel target, but not a proof of polylog overhead: the missing primitive is smoothed additive MIPS/exact-nearest-neighbor at the optimal exponent.
- Audited whether the smoothed score-max target can be implemented by ordinary ANN. The margin lemma now allows any `t >= C log m/r^2` and gives score gap `Theta_c(t r^2)`. Taking `t` large can make the perturbed center `Y/t` lie within `a r` of the query, but then a black-box implementation needs ANN at approximation `(c-a)/(1+a)=c-O_c(a)`. Keeping the exponent loss polylog forces `a=O(loglog m/log m)`, so this is circular rather than an implementation of the score-max primitive.
- Rechecked the budget-thinned cap route. The optional-stopping budget buys expected work, but not near-lineage value: proving polylog through this route still needs a cumulative opportunity lower bound along tame-good near paths, strong enough that the extra activation failures over `O(eta^{-2} loglog n)` radius drops cost only polylogarithmic probability. Also made the score-max target exact: additive score loss `epsilon t r^2` is equivalent to additive squared-distance error `2 epsilon r^2` for the perturbed center `Y/t`.
- Softened the smoothed endpoint further. On the same margin event, the Gaussian Gibbs distribution with weights `exp(<Y,x>-t||x||^2/2)` assigns total mass at most `m^{-2}` to invalid points after increasing the constant in `t >= C log m/r^2`. Thus an approximate Gibbs sampler, not an additive maximizer, is enough for the one-layer smoothed route. This may be closer to what a product-filter or kernel-sampling implementation can supply.
- Weakened the smoothed sampler target again. The proof only needs one-sided domination `Q_Y(x) <= polylog(m) G_Y(x)`, not total-variation approximation to the Gibbs law; valid points may be distorted arbitrarily. Also recorded the exact Gaussian-kernel and Gumbel-max forms of the same distribution, making the open primitive a smoothed kernel-sampling/rand-max problem rather than a channel problem.
- Added a small compiler for that target: any output-sensitive reporter that finds a list with inverse-polylog Gibbs mass and samples from the Gibbs law restricted to the list automatically gives the required pointwise domination. This reframes the smoothed route as high-mass reporting, not partition-function estimation.
- Read `notes7.md`. Its large-range spherical calculation is already essentially covered by the self-contained smooth product terminal filter in the paper, so it does not close the Euclidean gap. I changed the smoothed endpoint to make the mass-capture reporter the primary assumption: with inverse-polylog probability, report a list with inverse-polylog Gaussian-kernel mass, then sample inside the list. This is a sharper and more implementation-facing target than an abstract dominated sampler.
- Tightened the smoothed endpoint again by adding the resident-guard alternative to the reporter assumption. A global mass-capture requirement for every query is too strong when Gibbs weights are nearly flat, because no sublinear explicit list can carry inverse-polylog mass; in that regime a uniform guard is the right mechanism. The remaining smoothed target is now guard-or-capture for promised near pairs.
- Read `notes6.md` from a theory angle. The useful correction is that the finite-channel barrier is an explicit-enumeration artifact: evaluating `B ~= xi^{-1}` outcomes and repeating `xi^{-rho}` times forces the coarse `xi >= m^{-rho/(1+rho)}` scale. Structured decoding can use finer scales, but raw negative-moment value is not a hit probability; the paper now states the barrier and keeps scale-aware dyadic amplification as the self-contained compiler.
- Refined the budget-thinned cap route. The canonical mixed budget plus optional stopping already proves expected activated geometric work; the new activation-factor lemma says the remaining near-lineage loss is exactly a product of local `beta_q(u)=min(1, Hbar(u)^rho Q(u)^(1-rho)/H0)` factors. This turns the cap-thinning gap into a value-delivery statement rather than a vague frontier-work statement.
- Incorporated the external Gumbel-MIPS critique into the smoothed route. A certified `O(loglog m)` additive optimizer for Gumbel-perturbed Gaussian scores gives one-sided Gibbs domination by a short Gumbel-max argument, hence the smoothed endpoint. Ordinary multiplicative ANN does not supply this primitive; in high dimension it may return a point with polynomially smaller Gaussian weight because common orthogonal distance dominates the multiplicative ratio.
- Added an oblivious-sparsification barrier for the structured posterior route. The continuous likelihood-ball moment bounds alone still allow `Omega(1/xi)` disjoint possible witnesses, so replacing the continuum by a small query-independent random panel needs a new geometry-specific sensitivity theorem; otherwise the right target remains query-dependent structured decoding.
- Read `FINDINGS.md` and `notes9.md`. The useful theoretical takeaway from k-means experiments is not Lloyd's objective itself but overlapping certified covers: multi-assignment is the mechanism that breaks routing misses, and the theory-native replacement objective is to minimize inflated-ball power stabbing `sum_{active} |P_i|^rho Psi(R_i)` plus storage `sum_i |P_i|^{1+rho} Psi(R_i)`. Added a certified cover compiler and a power-stabbing cover target to `paper.tex`.
- Tightened the certified-cover route into a conditional polylog theorem. A flat k-means-style cover is circular unless children have smaller certified radii, and a per-level polylog slack in active stabbing would multiply over `polylog(n)` radius levels. The right target is a decodable cover with true `1-Theta(eta^2)` contraction in inflated-ball power stabbing and nonincreasing storage power; under that assumption the recursion gives `n^rho polylog(n)`.
- Added the balanced-fanout obstruction for recursive covers. If a query stabs `K` comparable children, the active power grows by `K^{1-rho}`, so balanced k-means fanout cannot satisfy the contraction needed for a worst-case recursive proof. This aligns with the experiments: fixed top-a k-means is a strong practical shallow router, while a proof route needs low stabbing, skewed almost-total splits, or randomized thinning.
- Added a self-contained finite surrogate for improving on k-means in practice: with candidate certified filters fixed, choosing extra point-filter memberships to cover sampled near pairs is a monotone submodular coverage problem, so greedy uniform-cost replication gives a `1-1/e` approximation. This explains why a true theory-native replacement should optimize near-pair coverage or inflated-ball power stabbing, not centroid margins or squared reconstruction error.
- Refined the budget-thinned cap route. The previous single-lineage product of local activation factors is only a sufficient condition and can be too pessimistic in balanced prefixes. Added an activated-good-route moment compiler: conditionally on the score tree, the first moment sums products over required opportunities and the second moment charges each pair by the union of their opportunity sets. The remaining non-channel target is now an LCA-style first/second moment estimate for aggregate activated near routes, parallel to the spherical survival proof.
- Added a conditional polylog endpoint for the budget-thinned route. The optional-stopping work bound, cap-free scan accounting, deterministic storage contraction, and cap-thinning telescoping already prove the algorithm once the aggregate activated value-delivery moment assumption holds. This separates the old mixed-frontier work assumption from the sharper near-side moment theorem still missing.
- Ruled out a tempting flat-shell shortcut. Running the constant-recall smooth product filter directly on radial shells of a bounded-diameter ball gives spherical correlations `a=1-r^2/(2R^2)` and `b=1-c^2r^2/(2R^2)`, whose exponent tends to `1/c^2` as `R/r` grows. This is polynomially worse than `rho_c=1/(2c^2-1)`, so recentering to the critical `b≈0` normalization remains necessary.
- Added a safe interpretation of empirical k-means: it can be used as an optional certified cover accelerator when a capped decoder verifies the active power-stabbing sum, but the worst-case proof must fall back to the score-and-ball node unless a uniform cover theorem is proved. The theory-native replacement is a power-cover Lloyd objective: add memberships by marginal uncovered near-pair weight divided by marginal storage/stabbing power, and update centers by certified radius or quantile-radius rather than squared-error means.
- Strengthened the finite near-pair cover surrogate from unit-cost greedy to cost-sensitive density greedy. With fixed membership costs, allowing one extra membership preserves the `1-1/e` submodular approximation, and a strict-budget version gets the standard half loss. This justifies the practical power-cover assignment rule when marginal storage/stabbing costs are frozen or dyadically bucketed during an assignment phase.
- Turned the cost/value surrogate idea into a formal fractional value-budget compiler. If child `C` has work surrogate `F(C)` and one full recursive call succeeds with inverse-polylog probability, then `b_C` units of detector budget buy proportional success value `min(b_C,F(C))/F(C)` up to the same inverse-polylog factor, while expected work is at most the allocated budget. This replaces the older all-or-nothing vote materialization target with a cleaner fractional value-delivery target.
- Replaced the budget-thinned near-side target by a fractional value-delivery moment compiler. Required route events are now generic value opportunities with probabilities `min(1,b_u/W_u) sigma_u`, so the old activated-route count is only the binary special case. The remaining assumption is first/second moments for the delivered-value variable `Z_val`, while work remains charged by optional stopping plus the fractional budget compiler.
- Made the k-means alternative more operational: the paper now spells out a certified-cover Lloyd template that initializes from k-means or medoids, greedily adds point-filter memberships by uncovered near-pair value per marginal storage/stabbing power, updates centers by certified or quantile radius, and uses a capped query decoder so the cover remains a safe accelerator before a uniform power-cover theorem is proved.
- Added an LCA value-flow certificate for the budget-thinned route. For a laminar trie of tame near routes, the second moment of delivered value is bounded by `sum_u A(u) V(u)^2`, where `A(u)` is the shared-prefix success weight and `V(u)` is suffix delivered value below `u`. This makes the remaining aggregate-value assumption checkable in the same style as the spherical LCA second-moment proof.
- Added a local LCA-energy certificate. A Bellman multiplier `M` satisfying `V(u)^2 + sum_child gamma M(child) V(child)^2 <= M(u) V(u)^2` bounds the whole LCA energy by `M(root) V(root)^2`; as a corollary, square-nonexpansive route tries cost only their depth. Ordinary score branching is square-nonexpansive, so the remaining energy problem is isolated to truly thinned geometric/fractional-budget opportunities.
- Added an exact energy-spine toll decomposition. The LCA energy equals `V(root)^2 * E[sum_j prod_{i<j} chi(U_i)]`, where `chi(u)=sum_child gamma V(child)^2 / V(u)^2` and the spine is biased by child square energy. Thus the remaining near-side gap is equivalent to proving only `O(log log n)` positive accumulated thinning toll along this energy-biased spine.

## 2026-06-25

- Re-read the k-means findings as a theory signal. The useful abstraction is not Lloyd's squared-error objective but selective overlapping filters: flat certified ball covers can be nonselective on concentrated high-dimensional nodes because the inflated balls blanket most queries. Added a cap-slack certified cover compiler to `paper.tex`, using the Cauchy-Schwarz slack `<u,q> >= theta-r` for correctness while keeping the child-radius certificate separate. This points to a power-filter Lloyd alternative that optimizes cap-query stabbing and recursive radius power directly.
- Simplified the budget-thinned value-delivery target. Added a unit-capped value-flow certificate: if fractional route weights can be pruned so every prefix has remaining suffix value at most one, then the LCA second moment is automatically bounded by `(H+1)V(root)` and success is at least `V(root)/(H+1)`. The remaining non-channel polylog target can now be phrased as a max-flow lower bound for inverse-polylog root value after unit prefix caps, rather than a full LCA-energy/toll estimate.
- Made the capped-flow target computable by a saturated Bellman recursion `U(u)=min(1, sum gamma U(child))`. This identifies the exact best unit-capped fractional route value, so the remaining proof obligation is to lower-bound `U(root)` on the tame-route trie.
- Added the min-cut dual for the saturated value: `U(root)` equals the minimum over route cuts of the prefix-success mass `sum A(u)`. This turns the remaining non-channel proof target into a cutset lower bound: every antichain hitting all tame routes should have inverse-polylogarithmic total prefix weight.
- Replaced the budget-thinned polylog endpoint assumption's old first/second-moment requirement by the saturated value condition directly. The conditional theorem now needs `U(root) >= log^{-O(1)} n` for the tame-route trie, with the cutset dual as the equivalent formulation.
- Generalized the cap-slack/k-means cover discussion to a Lipschitz-score filter compiler. Ordinary k-means balls are the special score `phi_i(x)=-||x-o_i||`, while cap or learned filters use more selective scores with the same `phi_i(q) >= theta_i-L_i r` correctness slack. This gives a precise theory-native alternative to k-means: optimize score-filter stabbing and storage power rather than squared reconstruction error.
- Added a dominated route-law certificate for the saturated value target. `U(root) >= p` is equivalent to a probability law on good routes whose prefix mass is at most `A(u)/p` at every trie node. A toll corollary says it suffices to construct a tilted route law with only `O(log log n)` positive excess `log_+(lambda/gamma)` over the activation probabilities. This reframes the cutset proof as a domination estimate for the tame-good route law.
- Strengthened the route-law certificate to a typical-route version. If a route law has low prefix domination height on an `h` fraction of its mass, then `U(root) >= h exp(-B)` after pruning to that good event; in particular expected positive edge toll `O(log log n)` already gives inverse-polylog saturated value. This matches the kind of estimate the adaptive continuation proof is likely to produce.
- Added a prefix-energy route-law certificate. A tilted route law with `sum_u Lambda(u)^2/A(u) <= polylog(n)` already implies inverse-polylog saturated value. This is the closest formulation to the existing tame-good LCA second moment: prove a chi-square prefix energy bound against activation prefix mass instead of bounding every route or even expected toll directly.
- Turned the k-means discussion into a more concrete alternative: balanced cap-power filters. The proposed router centers/whitens locally, learns balanced cap or Lipschitz score filters, assigns overlap by uncovered near-pair value per marginal storage/stabbing power, and queries with the certified slack `<u,q> >= theta-r`. This keeps k-means as an initializer or fallback while optimizing the active-power quantity used by the proof.
- Added a local Bellman certificate for the route-law prefix energy. The normalized subtree energy obeys `E(u)=1+sum_w (lambda_{u,w}^2/gamma_{u,w}) E(w)`, so a multiplier `M` or an energy-spine toll bound on `chi(u)=sum_w lambda^2/gamma` implies the global prefix-energy certificate. Ordinary unthinned score branching is square-benign because `gamma=1` gives `chi<=1`; the remaining loss is now isolated to truly budget-thinned opportunities.
- Removed the need to guess the route law in the prefix-energy target. The optimal route law has an exact harmonic Bellman recursion `E(u)=1+1/sum_w gamma_{u,w}/E(w)`, equivalently `W(u)=S(u)/(1+S(u))` with `S=sum_w gamma W(w)`. Proving `W(root)>=polylog^{-1}` for the tame-route trie is now a concrete sufficient condition for the polylog endpoint.
- Compared the harmonic route value with the saturated/min-cut value. For route height `H`, the harmonic value satisfies `U(u)/(h(u)+1) <= W(u) <= U(u)`. Thus the computable harmonic recursion is equivalent to the saturated cut target up to the already-polylog route depth, and a saturated lower bound automatically yields a prefix-energy route law with energy at most `(H+1)/U(root)`.
- Updated the k-means/certified-cover discussion using the new empirical findings. Flat inflated-ball covers remain a safe capped accelerator but are not the main theoretical target because concentrated data can make their stabbing nonselective. Added a shell-normalized cap-cover lemma and reframed the candidate practical alternative as a recursive fixed-overlap angular/Lipschitz score-filter tree, with k-means only as an initializer or fallback.
- Added a cut-energy certificate for the saturated route value. A route law whose chi-square energy `sum_{u in C} Lambda(u)^2/A(u)` is at most `M` on every antichain route cut implies `U(root) >= 1/M` by Cauchy plus the min-cut dual. This removes an unnecessary all-prefix/depth loss when the proof can directly control stopping frontiers such as cap-discovery cuts.
- Added the matching frontier square-toll identity. For a fixed route cut, its cut energy equals the expectation of `prod chi(u)` under the square-biased route spine stopped at that cut, where `chi(u)=sum_w lambda(u,w)^2/gamma(u,w)`. Thus the remaining stopping-time proof can aim for `O(log log n)` positive square toll up to each frontier, with no all-prefix depth summation.
- Sharpened the k-means alternative into an explicit score-power training loss: empirical active power `sum_active |P_i|^rho Psi(R_i)` plus storage power and uncovered near-pair penalties. This makes the proposed balanced cap/Lipschitz score-filter tree a direct optimizer of the recursive proof quantity, while ordinary k-means remains only an initializer or fallback.
- Added a self-contained residual cap-seeding lemma, capturing the analyzable part of k-means++: sampling directions from any residual weight distribution discovers every low-diameter cap carrying an `alpha` fraction of that residual after `O(alpha^{-1} log(1/delta))` samples. This makes k-means++ useful as a certifiable proposal mechanism, while the proof burden remains the active-power/stabbing certificate.
- Added a weak residual-cover compiler. If every nonnegligible residual exposes an `alpha`-heavy certifiable cap accepted by the active-power verifier, then repeated k-means++-style residual sampling covers all but an `epsilon` residual after `O(alpha^{-2} log(1/epsilon) log(...))` verified proposals. This isolates the remaining cover theorem as a weak residual cap condition rather than any Lloyd convergence claim.
- Added an offline proposal-amplification lemma to reflect that build time is not the scarce resource. Arbitrarily many candidate caps/score filters can be generated, rejected, or optimized over during preprocessing; only the final certified cover contributes to index space and query time. This shifts the theory target toward existence and deterministic certification of small low-stabbing covers.
- Added a residual-scan compiler and a conditional polylog endpoint from weak offline score covers. The remaining local theorem can now allow expensive build-time residual sampling and exact optimization, leave a residual block of size `F_4 polylog` for direct scan, and still get the same `n^rho polylog n` query and `n^{1+rho} polylog n` space once the active-power/storage certificates hold.
- Added an exhaustive residual-cover compiler. With build time treated as cheap, the residual sampler can be replaced by enumerating every residual data direction at each round; any `alpha`-heavy certifiable cap promised by the weak residual condition is then found deterministically, leaving only the geometric active-power certificate as the remaining obstacle.
- Added a finite-pool offline optimizer lemma. A huge proposal pool from k-means++, medoids, data directions, pair directions, learned scores, or exhaustive enumeration can be searched by arbitrary preprocessing; only the retained cover that passes deterministic active-power/storage certificates counts toward query time and index memory. This turns k-means++ into a proposal mechanism rather than the theorem object.
- Added a finite-pool fractional dual. Failure of a finite score-cover pool now yields an explicit obstruction: point prices, query-load prices, and a storage price such that every candidate child has priced point mass at most its charged storage plus active-query footprint, while the total point price beats the residual and budget prices. This reframes the remaining cover theorem as ruling out a concrete dual certificate.
- Added the dual-price proposal separation corollary. For a finite universe of certified children, a fractional cover exists exactly when every surplus dual price system has a positive-gain child. This makes expensive preprocessing look like column generation: solve the current LP, use the dual prices to search for a cap/medoid/pair-direction/learned filter with positive reduced gain, and keep only the final certified cover.
- Added a fractional cover materialization lemma. A finite fractional score cover with bounded atoms can be rounded to an actual stored cover with only logarithmic inflation in active query power and storage, while the uncovered points become a comparable residual scan. This lets the dual-price/column-generation route feed the weak offline score-cover endpoint rather than stopping at a fractional certificate.
- Added a finite activation-pattern verifier. For any finite retained score-filter family, the worst all-query active power is exactly the maximum over its finite set of realizable activation patterns, so the all-query verifier becomes a finite list of linear constraints. This removes the separate "finite exact decoder cells" caveat from the fractional-materialization route; pattern enumeration can be arbitrarily expensive preprocessing.
- Added an atom-safe subchild separation lemma. Any subset of a certified child is still certified, and for fixed dual prices the best atom-safe subchild of a parent proposal is obtained by sorting resident points by point price and trying feasible cardinalities. This turns the bounded-atom requirement for fractional materialization into a preprocessing normal form rather than a separate geometric assumption.
- Clarified the offline cover model: preprocessing time is deliberately not charged, and may include exact LP/IP solving, exhaustive proposal enumeration, repeated randomized attempts, or learned proposal generation. The ANN theorem charges only retained certified children, residual scans, and query-time decoder operations.
- Closed a finite-pool modeling seam with a pattern-complete fractional cover lemma. The LP can impose constraints for every realizable activation pattern of the full proposal universe, or any finite superset, so a failed all-query fractional cover yields dual prices on activation patterns rather than on sampled queries. Materialization gives an actual retained cover with all-query active-power bounds when the pattern entropy is polylogarithmic; otherwise the LP remains a dual/separation diagnostic or must be replaced by an exact integral certificate.
- Added a linear score pattern-entropy lemma. Cap, shell-normalized cap, and affine score filters in projected dimension `k` realize only `N^{O(k)}` query activation patterns, and atom-safe subchildren do not increase this because they duplicate parent activation predicates. After projected localization with `k=polylog(m)` and a quasipolynomial-sized distinct score pool, the pattern-complete materialization loses only a polylogarithmic factor.
- Added a price-density subchild certificate and averaged proposal separation. For any certified parent proposal and dual prices, the best atom-safe stored subchild is its highest point-price prefix, and positive gain is exactly `A_k > Psi(R)(sigma k^{1+rho}+Lambda k^rho)`. Randomized proposal mechanisms now only need positive expected reduced gain; build-time enumeration can find the winning column and discard the rest.
- Added a guard-restricted cover compiler. Active-power constraints and finite-pool dual prices only need to range over low-guard queries; high-guard queries are handled by a resident sample, and a capped decoder can abort if that sample misses. This removes high-density query patterns from the remaining geometric separation theorem.
- Added offline Pareto compression. With preprocessing treated as cheap, the finite-pool verifier can search all accepted retained covers and choose the memory-minimal or query-minimal certified cover; the proposal pool, failed randomized attempts, and pattern enumeration are build-time only. This also clarifies that pattern-complete fractional rounding introduces a logarithmic index overhead only as a proof route from fractional covers, not when an integral low-stabbing cover is certified directly.
- Added a low-guard shadow compiler. The recursive cover only needs to cover points that are within distance `r` of some low-guard query; points whose entire near-query neighborhood is high-guard can be omitted from the recursive cover because resident sampling already handles every query for which they matter. The finite-pool dual can now put point prices only on this exposed shadow, further narrowing the remaining separation theorem.
- Added the shadow finite-pool dual. The fractional program can take the low-guard shadow as its ground set, intersect every proposal with that shadow before computing query and storage powers, and extend dual point prices by zero outside it. Price-density separation now sorts exposed high-price points only, so dense guard-solved points cannot dilute a useful proposal.
- Added the shadow price-density compiler. If every surplus dual system supported on the low-guard shadow has a certified parent proposal whose highest-price exposed prefix beats the storage and active-pattern price, then column generation plus pattern-complete rounding builds the weak offline score cover. This turns the remaining score-cover theorem into one concrete exposed-prefix separation statement.
- Promoted the build-time convention to the preliminaries. For offline certified cover compilers, preprocessing may be arbitrarily expensive and may discard huge proposal pools; the charged resources are only retained index space and query work.
- Added dual-weighted cap seeding. Sampling data directions from the current dual point prices is the column-generation analogue of k-means++: any price-heavy certified cap whose atom-safe prefix beats the active/storage charge is found with logarithmically many offline samples, or deterministically by exhaustive enumeration.
- Added the middle-density singleton obstruction. Even with free preprocessing and low-guard restriction, retaining all exposed singleton children can force active power `m^{1-rho}/polylog`, polynomially above `m^rho` when `rho<1/2`; the remaining proof needs selective activation or route-value delivery, not just exhaustive singleton materialization.
- Added budgeted pure-alternative sampling. The middle-density singleton barrier is an all-reporting barrier: if a query can sample from pure terminal alternatives, one verified sample succeeds regardless of how many valid alternatives exist; if it samples from a superset, only the inverse pure fraction is paid. This reframes the terminal part of the gap as active pure sampling rather than range reporting.
- Added shared-predicate decoder accounting. Offline atom-safe expansion may retain many subchildren under the same parent score predicate; the query evaluates each distinct predicate once, while child-record enumeration and metadata are charged by the existing active/storage power sums because every nonempty child has unit lower-bounded `F_4` weight. This removes a hidden per-subchild overhead from the finite-pool route.
- Added Las Vegas offline certification. Since build time is not charged and certificates are deterministic, an offline randomized cover generator only needs positive probability of finding an accepted cover; preprocessing can repeat until certification and store only the first accepted cover. This weakens proposal-generation requirements without changing query time or index memory.
- Added weighted terminal-sampler compilation. Terminal alternatives can be grouped and sampled with surrogate weights; success depends on total pure sampling mass, not on the number of pure alternatives listed. This turns middle-density terminal singletons into a sampling-mass target aligned with posterior rank-prefix reporting and saturated value delivery.
- Added dominated child pruning. If a retained child is contained in another retained child whose activation predicate is at least as broad on the promised query domain, the smaller child can be deleted without hurting routing and while decreasing active/storage power. Nested atom-safe prefix chains can therefore be compressed to maximal retained members after certification.
- Added spherical D^2 residual seeding. The k-means++ analogue now appears as a self-contained one-step hitting lemma: sampling uncovered shell points proportional to their squared distance from current directions finds any compact cluster carrying an alpha fraction of residual potential with probability 1-exp(-alpha T), after which deterministic cap certification decides whether to store it.
- Added Las Vegas seeded shadow separation. In the low-guard dual-price route, a seed distribution only needs positive probability of proposing a certified parent whose exposed high-price prefix has positive reduced gain for the current dual prices; unbounded preprocessing can repeat the seeded search and column generation until the finite certified cover is found, while query time and memory charge only the final retained cover.
- Added threshold-tightened score filters. Any nonempty Lipschitz-score child can raise its threshold to the minimum score of its retained points; routing is preserved by Lipschitz slack, activation only shrinks, and the threshold becomes one of finitely many data scores. This removes a continuous threshold parameter from finite seeded proposal pools.
- Added finite score-template closure. A seeded build routine only needs to propose score-ball templates `(center, radius, score, Lipschitz constant)`; preprocessing expands each template into finitely many data-score superlevel parents, and arbitrary retained children become atom-safe subchildren with inherited activation predicates. This pushes thresholds and memberships into the existing finite prefix machinery.
- Added radius-tightened score filters and finite center-score closure. For score filters, the certified radius can be tightened to the maximum retained distance without changing activation, so a seed only needs to propose `(center, score, Lipschitz constant)`; preprocessing enumerates data-score thresholds and data-distance radii, then uses the atom-safe prefix machinery for retained subchildren.
- Added offline center-score saturation. With build time treated as cheap, a finite center-score seed family can be closed under all data thresholds, data radii, and retained submemberships, then optimized by deterministic certification/Pareto compression. This makes k-means++, learned scores, and exhaustive data directions proposal generators only; the stored index pays for the final certified cover, not the saturated build-time universe.
- Added priced cover compilation and offline recursive Pareto pricing. The verifier can charge each active child by its actual certified descendant query/storage price instead of the closed-form `|P_C|^rho Psi(R_C)` surrogate, and build-time dynamic programming over a finite acyclic candidate universe can keep the best certified query-memory frontier. This unifies ball recursion, score-filter recursion, residual scans, and terminal samplers as columns in one priced cover process.
- Added a sampled priced-cover compiler. A query can sample retained active children from a certified distribution, pay only expected sampled child price, and succeed after polylog trials when every unresolved near pair has inverse-polylog route mass on children containing the near point. This separates the all-reporting active-power certificate from the sampling/value route needed for middle-density singletons and posterior prefixes.
- Added a finite sampled-cover dual. For a fixed query, the existence of sampling probabilities with low expected child price and prescribed route mass is equivalent to the absence of route-demand prices on unresolved near points where every active child has nonpositive sampled reduced gain. This gives the sampled analogue of the finite-pool dual and makes the remaining route-mass theorem a positive-gain child separation problem.
- Added a finite sampled-route verifier. The sampled LP only depends on a query's active-child pattern and unresolved near-point set, so preprocessing can certify sampled route mass over a finite signature family and store/implicitly decode one sampler per signature. This makes the all-query sampled route-mass condition finite, with signature metadata explicitly charged if it is not compactly decodable.
- Added a seed-saturate-certify compiler. K-means++, spherical D^2, dual-weighted, learned, or exhaustive seeds can generate center-score templates; preprocessing then closes them under data thresholds/radii, submemberships, recursive Pareto prices, and sampled-route certificates, and stores only the accepted Pareto-optimal retained cover. This turns extra build compute into better query-memory optimization without adding to the charged index.
- Added sampled-route cohort materialization. Any feasible sampled-route distribution for a finite signature can be replaced by a uniform cohort of `O(h0^-1 log |N|)` active child records, preserving route mass and expected price up to constants. For inverse-polylog route targets this makes the sampler integer and polylog-sized per signature, leaving signature decodability rather than fractional sampling as the remaining metadata issue.
- Added an activation-pattern sampled-route verifier. Instead of including the exact unresolved near set in the query signature, preprocessing may certify one LP per activation pattern against the union of all near points compatible with that pattern. This is a stronger feasibility condition, but when it holds the sampled decoder needs only the ordinary activation-pattern decoder plus a small cohort sampler.
- Added an activation-cohort sampled compiler and fixed the cohort materialization to handle subprobability samplers by explicit no-op records. When the activation-pattern LPs are feasible, preprocessing stores one small child/no-op cohort per activation pattern; query time samples uniformly from that cohort, so the retained object is integer and activation-decodable with no real-valued vectors.
- Refined the offline-build resource boundary. The activation-cohort route now computes the activation pattern once and reuses it for all sampled trials, giving query work `D(q)+B(L+1)` instead of `B(D(q)+L)`. Added a resource certificate spelling out that arbitrary LP/pattern/cohort search is free only at build time; retained pattern decoders and cohort tables still have to fit the local query-memory budget.
- Added a prefix-list decoding compiler inspired by code/lattice list decoding. The score tree can be viewed as an implicit Euclidean code: if the query can enumerate exactly prefixes whose certified upper score stays above threshold and the resulting list size is polylogarithmic, then deterministic list decoding plus metric verification gives routing. This gives a non-sampling alternative target: prove a list-size/route-cut bound and an output-sensitive prefix enumerator for the structured score tree.
- Strengthened the list-decoding route into a concrete stack/sphere-decoder normal form. A priority prefix decoder explores only prefixes whose admissible upper score can still reach threshold, and an exponential prefix-moment certificate `sum g(u) exp(beta U_q(u)) <= exp(beta Theta) F_4 polylog` implies the query list fits the local `F_4` budget. This offers a deterministic alternative to activation-pattern cohort tables: prove an admissible-score list-size theorem plus a near-route-above-threshold theorem.
- Connected list decoding back to continuation mass. If a terminal route's score is the minimum log continuation mass along the route, then the `beta=1` exponential list certificate is exactly the old continuation-mass proof: `sum H_q(u)` controls the stack size after thresholding at `k^{-A}`. Thus ordinary score blocks are already sphere-decoder/list-decoder instances; the remaining polylog target is to build similarly tight admissible prefix potentials through recentering and budget-thinned child generation.
- Added a budget-stable recentering decoder. Any prefix-supermartingale Bellman budget can be used directly as the priority-list potential: prefixes whose entire route has budget at least `tau` and whose local recursive charge fits the budget are enumerated with expected `tau^-1 K F` prefix work and recursive charge at most `(1-kappa eta^2)F`. With the canonical mixed budget this gives `F_4 polylog` deterministic handling of budget-stable recentering opportunities, leaving only budget-unstable opportunities for the sampled/value-delivery layer.
- Specialized the route-law toll target to the canonical mixed budget. Stable recentering edges have zero toll; a budget-thinned edge pays at most `log_+(Gamma/((1-kappa eta^2) B_mix(u)))`. Therefore a route law with expected cumulative canonical budget defect `O(log log n)` already gives inverse-polylog saturated route value. This turns the remaining near-side theorem into a concrete budget-defect estimate along a tilted tame route law.
- Added an extreme-pruning analogue for budget thinning. Instead of forcing the uniform canonical profile, the query may use a predictable multiplier `r_q(u)` as long as the profile has certified expected budget `E sum r_q(u) B_q(u) <= F_4`; the activated recursive work remains bounded, while the route-law toll becomes `log_+(Gamma / ((1-kappa eta^2) r_q(u) B_mix(u)))`. This imports the useful part of lattice sphere-decoder pruning without relying on a black-box lattice theorem.
- Simplified pruning to a direct activation-allocation normal form. The query may assign a predictable budget `Y_q(u)` to first opportunities with `E sum Y_q(u) <= F_4` and activate with probability `min(1,(1-kappa eta^2)Y_q(u)/Gamma(u))`; recursive work still contracts and the route-law toll is just `log_+(Gamma/((1-kappa eta^2)Y))`. The remaining theorem can now be stated as finding one decodable subprobability allocation over first opportunities that gives every near point a tame route law with `O(log log n)` expected allocation defect.
- Weakened sampled-route correctness from per-witness to existential near-set mass. For ANN, a query with multiple near points only needs to hit one valid witness; the new existential sampled-cover compiler and finite dual require mass on children intersecting the unresolved near set as a whole. Singleton near sets still recover the old guarantee, but dense or multi-witness low-guard states no longer force independent route mass for every possible near point.
- Collapsed exact existential sampled signatures to a two-child normal form. The existential LP has only one success constraint plus query-price and probability budgets, so an extreme feasible sampler uses at most two useful children; rounding the two weights gives an integer no-op cohort of size `O(h0^-1)` with no `log |N|` factor. The metadata bottleneck is therefore exact near-set signature decoding, not fractional sampler materialization.
- Added a flat structured spherical-code decoder target from the lattice/list-decoding viewpoint. A single code panel is enough for the terminal spherical engine if it has output-sensitive cap enumeration, `m^rho polylog` query and point load, `m^rho polylog` far co-load, and inverse-polylog near hit probability; this isolates what a Construction-A, group-orbit, polar/list-recovery, or lattice sphere-decoder construction would need to prove instead of analyzing the adaptive score tree.
- Added the water-filled optimal activation allocation. For a fixed successful route law, normalized edge charges `a_e`, and route occupation weights `ell_e`, the best decodable budget profile is `y_e=min(a_e,t ell_e)`; the remaining near-side theorem can now target one scalar water-filled defect plus allocation decodability instead of guessing a pruning multiplier or activation profile.
- Added robust water-filled allocation minimax. A single query allocation works for a finite family of witness route laws iff every mixture of their edge-occupation profiles has small water-filled defect; this turns the multi-witness near-set issue into one mixture-robust scalar defect bound rather than a separate allocation for each possible near point.
- Added the layer-cake water-filled tail certificate. The robust defect is the integral of the route-occupation mass of opportunities whose normalized charge-to-occupation ratio exceeds the water level by `exp(s)`, so the remaining near-side proof can target a summable tail bound over all finite mixtures instead of manipulating the allocation variables directly.
- Looked up the list-decoding/lattice angle and added the concrete product-code decoder target. The useful template is Becker-Ducas-Gama-Laarhoven style structured filtering: replace the random score tree or k-means router by an implicit block-product spherical/code panel, query it with a stack/list decoder using admissible suffix scores, and prove the existing prefix exponential-moment plus near-route hit bounds. Extreme pruning maps cleanly to the water-filled allocation profile.
- Added a self-contained additive product-panel decoder lemma. For block scores with threshold reporters and admissible suffix upper bounds, the priority decoder's live stack is bounded by the explicit product partition certificate `P_beta`, while terminal scan work is bounded by `T_beta`; the remaining product-code theorem is now only the geometric spectrum and near-hit analysis.
- Added the product-panel spectrum compiler. If a product panel has certified decoder work, storage exponential moment, far co-load exponential moment, and inverse-polylog near-pair hit probability, then polylog many independent panels give the terminal ANN engine with `m^rho polylog` query and `m^{1+rho} polylog` space. The storage and far conditions factor block-by-block, making the remaining code/lattice theorem much more concrete.
- Added block tensorization for product panels. After splitting data/query thresholds by block, the decoder partition, storage partition, far co-load partition, and coordinatewise near-hit probability all factor. The product-code route is now reduced to proving one-block exponential partition and hit estimates, then choosing enough independent blocks for the desired exponent.
- Added product-panel rate bookkeeping. The tensorized product-code theorem now has scalar one-block rates: query and storage sums must be at most `rho`, far-separation sum at least `1-rho`, and near-loss sum only `O(log log m)`. This aligns the code/lattice route with the existing spherical exponent calculation.
- Added the soft-list-recovery reporter interface after looking up more Euclidean list-decoding work. The transferable theorem object is not Lloyd-style centroid convergence or a black-box decoding radius; it is a certified threshold reporter for a structured code/lattice block, whose stack size is charged by the existing `P_beta`/`T_beta` certificates. Build time can search over rotations, cosets, Construction-A moduli, RS evaluation sets, frozen sets, scalings, and pruning profiles, but the retained panel must still satisfy the product-panel rate bounds.
- Added a positive-moment bridge for water-filled allocation. Instead of proving a full summable dyadic tail for every robust mixture, it is enough to prove a small excess moment `sum ell^alpha ((a/(t ell^alpha))^p - 1)_+`, with `M/p = O(log log n)`. This turns the near-side allocation gap into a low-temperature energy estimate over charge/occupation ratios, which is closer to the existing square-energy and branching-random-walk style targets.
- Added a water-level-free version of the moment bridge. If a robust mixture has total opportunity occupation at most `H0`, then `tau=1/H0` is automatically below the water level, so the defect is bounded by the raw scaled moment `sum ell^alpha ((H0 a/ell^alpha)^p - 1)_+ / p`. For polylogarithmic route depth and `p = Theta(1/log log n)`, the depth normalization is constant; the next geometric target no longer has to solve for the water level.
- Extracted the raw-log form of the water-level-free certificate. The exact remaining defect can be bounded by `sum ell^alpha log_+(H0 a/ell^alpha)`, so a very concrete sufficient target is bounded charge density `H0 a <= C ell^alpha` on occupied unstable opportunities and total unstable occupation `H0 = O(log log n)`. The positive-moment target remains useful when bounded density fails on a small tail.
- Looked up the code/lattice list-decoding route again. The useful transfer is sharper than "decode a lattice": BDGL's product-code filters already identify the smooth-decodable-subcode problem, but their random-product smoothness has `2^{O~(sqrt d)}` slack, which is only `m^{o(1)}` in logarithmic dimension. For polylog overhead the theorem target is a certified block code/lattice with one-block query/storage/far/near rates satisfying the product-panel bookkeeping with polylog slack, plus an output-sensitive threshold reporter. Expensive build time can search over Construction-A/D, Barnes-Wall, RS-derived, or repulsive spherical codebooks, but the retained index pays only for the certified decodable panel.
- Added the transition-aware water-filled certificate. The earlier allocation defect used `log_+(a_e/y_e)`, throwing away the route-law transition probability. The exact toll is bounded by `log_+(lambda_e a_e/y_e)`, so the right water-filled charge is `lambda_e a_e`. In the raw-log bound, `ell_e = Lambda(u) lambda_e` cancels the branch probability and changes the concrete density target from `H0 a_e <= C ell_e` to the weaker prefix condition `H0 a_e <= C Lambda(u)`. This is also the direct bridge to square-energy tolls: the ideal profile `y_e=lambda_e a_e` makes `gamma_e >= lambda_e`, hence `sum lambda^2/gamma <= 1`; water-filling is the budget-constrained version. A robust envelope corollary keeps the improvement when witness route laws share or have a decodable upper envelope for their local transitions.
- Added the antichain prefix-density flow bridge. A route law with prescribed lower bounds `Lambda(u) >= d(u)` exists iff every antichain has total demand at most one; the proof is a self-contained tree recursion. Applying this with `d(u)=H0 a_*(u)/C`, where `a_*(u)` is the largest outgoing normalized charge at prefix `u`, gives a new sufficient condition for transition-aware water-filling: every antichain of required prefixes has `sum a_*(u) <= C/H0`. Then some route law has prefix density `H0 a_e <= C Lambda(u)` and defect at most `H0 log C`. This replaces "guess a good route law" by a cut-style maximum local charge bound, much closer to the existing min-cut/stopping-frontier machinery.
- Looked up the code/lattice literature from the sequential-decoding side. The sharper algorithmic lesson is that a score tree should be decoded with a Fano/stack metric: raw query reliability minus a certified local rate or branch-price bias. In our notation that bias is supplied by continuation mass, Bellman budget, or transition-aware water-filled allocation. Thus the lattice alternative is not nearest raw center or Lloyd cells, but a Fano-biased output-sensitive list decoder whose prefix exponential moment is the cutoff-rate/list-size certificate.
- Added a self-contained Fano-biased prefix-list compiler. Nonnegative branch prices preserve admissibility and can only reduce the exponential list-size moment; lowering the threshold by the cumulative price lists the same reliable route. Applying this to `log_+(a/y)` or transition-aware `log_+(lambda a/y)` prices shows that an `O(log log n)` water-filled defect costs only a polylogarithmic factor in deterministic stack-decoding work. This converts allocation tolls from a sampling-only object into ordinary sequential-decoder bias.
- Added the robust Fano allocation compiler. For a finite witness family, if one common water-filled allocation has expected branch-price defect `B` against every witness and the raw reliable routes have margin `2B`, then one Fano-biased priority decoder lists a valid route for every witness with constant route-law mass. The unpriced prefix/terminal moments only pay `exp(2 beta B)`, hence polylog when `B=O(log log n)`. This combines the robust minimax allocation and deterministic list-decoding routes into one sufficient condition.
- Added an existential antichain-Fano certificate aligned with ANN rather than per-witness materialization. If the union of valid terminal routes has antichain maximum-charge sum `sum a_*(u) <= C/H0`, every successful route has raw reliability margin `2H0 log C`, and the unpriced list moments are in budget, then the antichain flow supplies a route law and transition-aware water-filled allocation, and one Fano-biased stack decoder lists a valid route with `exp(O(H0 log C))` overhead. For `H0 log C=O(log log n)` this is polylog, leaving a single geometric target: antichain charge plus raw-margin/list-moment bounds for the successful-route trie.
- Looked up list-decoding codes/lattices as an alternative to treating k-means as the theorem object. The useful new compiler is a hard-list-recovery lift for soft Euclidean scores: repeat each coordinate across score thresholds, run an ordinary list-recovery oracle on the repeated-coordinate code, and filter by the exact soft score. This makes sparse outer codes, Construction-A/D cosets, Barnes-Wall style recursive lattices, folded RS/expander list-recovery, and polar multiprobe panels fit the same certified threshold-reporter interface. Expensive build time may search for good panels, but retained query/memory are certified by output-sensitive list recovery plus the random-like spectrum inequalities, not by Lloyd convergence.
- Added a local potential certificate for the antichain gap. If a nonnegative prefix potential satisfies `a_*(u) <= Phi(u)` and `sum_child Phi(child) <= Phi(u)`, then every antichain in the successful-route trie has total maximum outgoing charge at most `Phi(root)`. The stronger drop inequality `a_*(u)+sum_child Phi(child) <= Phi(u)` is a telescoping sufficient condition. This turns the remaining global antichain bound into a local superharmonic/potential-drop target and composes directly with the existential antichain-Fano certificate.
- Strengthened the local-potential route by constructing the route law directly. A superharmonic potential with `Phi(root) <= C/H0` and `a_*(u) <= Phi(u)` can be normalized and pushed top-down through the trie, assigning each child at least its potential mass. The resulting leaf law satisfies `H0 a_*(u) <= C Lambda(u)` at every prefix, so the same decodable potential supplies the route law, the transition-aware water-filled allocation, and the Fano branch prices without a separate antichain-flow materialization step.
- Made the superharmonic certificate canonical. The least feasible potential is the backward DP `Phi*(u)=max(a_*(u), sum_child Phi*(child))`, and `Phi*(root)` is exactly the maximum antichain charge. This gives an offline verifier for proposed successful-route tries: compute `Phi*`, accept iff `Phi*(root) <= C/H0`, then retain only a decodable accepted trie/potential/Fano reporter. The certificate is complete among superharmonic potentials, so preprocessing search is not hiding an extra existential choice.
- Packaged the remaining local proof obligations into a finite accepted-route-trie compiler. A proposed trie is accepted by four explicit checks: canonical superharmonic root, raw reliability margin, unpriced prefix exponential moment, and terminal-price exponential moment. With `H0 log C = O(log log m)` these checks imply a Fano-biased decoder with only polylog overhead. The remaining geometric task is now exactly to construct accepted route tries for every unresolved local ANN state.
- Re-read the list-decoding/lattice angle through finite trellis and sequential lattice decoding. The useful abstraction is a compressed route DAG: code and lattice decoders should store a compact trellis/recursive state graph, while the proof unfolds it into the accepted route trie. Added a compressed route-DAG verifier: a state potential satisfying `a_*(v) <= Phi(v)` and `sum_edges Phi(child) <= Phi(v)` pulls back to a superharmonic trie potential, so Fano-biased decoding gets the same polylog certificate without materializing the route tree. This makes "the score tree is a lattice decoder" precise and avoids charging build-time proposal universes to the index.
- Strengthened the prefix-moment route certificate. The local maximum outgoing charge `a_*(u)` only needs to be dominated by the normalized prefix-moment mass in the descendant subtree below `u`, not by the single prefix weight at `u`. The suffix sum of those weights is a superharmonic majorant, so the antichain/root-potential check follows from the same Fano moment used for stack size. The remaining construction can now aim to prove a suffix charge-density inequality rather than separately proving stack-size and route-law certificates.
- Looked up the list-decoding/lattice route from the sampling side. Added a sampled structured-panel compiler: a code/lattice panel can avoid all-reporting if the query samples labels from a certified Gibbs or discrete-Gaussian law and the panel satisfies storage, far sampled co-load, and near sampled mass bounds. This is a theorem-shaped alternative to k-means multi-assignment and to additive Gumbel-MIPS over data points: redundancy contributes sampling mass rather than active list size, while the retained index is still certified by explicit spectrum inequalities.
- Specialized the sampled-panel compiler to Gibbs product panels. Query labels sampled proportional to `exp(beta S_Q)` give a self-contained certificate: storage is the usual data partition moment, far sampled co-load is the mixed/query partition-function ratio, and near success is Gibbs mass on labels that store the near point. For full products all three tensorize blockwise, giving a concrete code/lattice theorem target with no output-list term.
- Added Gibbs sampled rate bookkeeping. The sampled product-code/lattice route now has scalar one-block exponents: storage rate at most `rho`, sampled far-separation rate at least `1-rho`, and near Gibbs-mass loss only `O(log log m)`. Unlike the reporting product-panel route there is no query-list rate, so this isolates exactly where sampling may beat output-sensitive enumeration.
- Added a tilted moment lower bound for near Gibbs mass. For one block, the probability under the query Gibbs law that the near point passes the data storage threshold is at least `(M_gamma-1)_+^2/M_2gamma`, where `M_gamma` is the tilted storage-score partition ratio. This replaces the opaque near-mass condition by a first/second exponential moment target, aligning the sampled code/lattice route with the other positive-moment certificates in the paper.
- Added the critical tilted-Gaussian sampled spectrum calculation.
