At the Euclidean critical normalization `a=1-1/c^2, b=0`, an ideal Gaussian filter family with label intensity `m`, storage threshold `T^2=2(1-rho)log m`, and query tilt `beta=T/a` has storage rate `rho`, sampled far load `m^{-(1-rho)}`, and constant near sampled mass. This identifies the exact continuous spectrum a compact lattice/code sampler must discretize, and explains why sampling can avoid enumerating `m^rho` active filters.
- Added a naive Gaussian-label quadrature barrier. If `M=m^nu` iid Gaussian labels are used to discretize the tilted sampler, the tilted partition estimator has relative variance `m^{2 tau/a^2-nu}`. Storage and sampled far-load budgets require `nu<=tau+rho` and `tau>=1-rho`, which are incompatible with bounded-variance quadrature at the critical `a=1-1/c^2`. This rules out "just sample a huge random pool" as the proof route and sharpens the need for structured code/lattice quadrature.
- Added a weighted Gaussian quadrature panel verifier as the positive finite target after the iid barrier. A retained label set with arbitrary preprocessing-chosen weights gives the sampled terminal ANN bounds if it passes three exact checks: unweighted bucket storage, tilted sampled far load, and tilted near mass. The weights affect only query sampling probabilities; retained buckets and the sampler are what count.
- Added tilted weight linear feasibility. For a fixed retained label pool, clearing the tilted partition functions turns the far-load and near-mass sampling constraints into homogeneous linear inequalities in the label weights. Failure has a Farkas dual that prices bad queries and near witnesses; a structured code/lattice label oracle can be attacked by column generation against this reduced-obstruction certificate.
- Added the continuous Gaussian finite-witness lemma. On any finite query/witness test set, the unrestricted critical Gaussian label universe has strictly feasible tilted weights; an empirical sample plus Caratheodory gives finite weighted labels. This narrows the code/lattice target: the missing theorem is low-storage, query-decodable quadrature with polylog slack, not existence of tilted weights in the ideal spectrum.
- Added the small-signature Gaussian quadrature compiler. If the finite query/witness constraints collapse to at most `m^rho polylog(m)` exact signatures, the Caratheodory support has that many labels, so unweighted bucket storage is automatic and the query can sample by scanning the support within the `m^rho` budget. This isolates signature entropy and compact decoding as the remaining obstruction.
- Added the metric-net signature barrier. Preserving constant near mass in the critical tilted Gaussian spectrum by query-direction representatives needs angular mesh `Theta(1/sqrt(log m))`; in the usual `d=Theta(log m)` terminal dimension this gives `m^{Theta(log log m)}` cells, much larger than the small-signature budget. The useful signatures have to be reliability/rank/code/lattice states rather than ordinary spherical nets.
- Added bucket-quotient weighted quadrature. Labels with identical stored data buckets can be merged: storage pays once per bucket, while the query samples the aggregate tilted mass of all labels in that bucket. This makes the finite code/lattice target less tied to raw label count; a trellis or lattice shell may have high internal label entropy if it exposes few bucket classes and can sample their aggregate weights.
- Added a compressed aggregate sampler for quotient panels. If labels are paths in an acyclic trellis with multiplicative query reliabilities, a backward partition-function recursion samples terminal bucket classes with probability equal to aggregate tilted path mass. The sampled code/lattice target can now be stated as graph-size plus bucket-spectrum certificates rather than explicit label enumeration.
- Added the pure-prefix leader quotient for the structured posterior route. In the likelihood rank-prefix process, any pure prefix containing the near point certifies that the top-scoring point for the same Gaussian score is already a valid answer. Thus rank prefixes need not carry separate stored representatives for ANN; their intensities can be aggregated by score leader, turning the remaining posterior target into a leader-quotient sampler/reporter rather than explicit prefix-bucket materialization.
- Added dyadic leader-quotient amplification. Hidden likelihood prefixes can remain analysis labels with masses and pure-value accounting; the query-facing decoder reports only distinct leaders and pays only the active leader count. This removes the per-prefix stored-child description term from the posterior route, leaving the implementation target as a compact leader reporter whose own space is certified separately.
- Added Poisson leader superposition and the likelihood leader-intensity law. The hidden prefix process quotients exactly to independent point clocks with rates equal to aggregate rank-gap weighted intensity over score directions whose leader is that point. The remaining posterior theorem is therefore a structured reporter for these leader-intensity measures, not for rank prefixes themselves.
- Added a lifted Euclidean decoding normal form for the code/lattice route. A Fano-biased product score with fixed branch prices is exactly list decoding a lifted Euclidean code/lattice, where prices become extra squared-distance coordinates; Gibbs sampling from biased scores is the corresponding discrete Gaussian sampler. This makes the "score tree as lattice decoder" interpretation self-contained and shows why nearest raw centroids miss the certificate.
- Added Poissonization of normalized leader samplers. If a code/lattice/trellis can compute the total tilted leader intensity and sample from the normalized law, drawing a Poisson number of samples gives exactly the independent-clock quotient process. This upgrades the posterior target from an all-listing leader reporter to a compact partition-function sampler; the old structured decoder remains only a stronger sufficient implementation.
- Added the ideal likelihood leader-law certificate. The continuous likelihood process already has constant total quotient leader intensity at each dyadic scale and constant scale-weighted valid-leader intensity under guard failure. Thus all geometry and amplification arithmetic are discharged; the clean remaining posterior theorem is a compact sampler for the normalized point measures Lambda_{q,xi}, with hidden-label and all-listing decoders only sufficient implementation forms.
- Added the scale-amplified alpha-leader collapse. Multiplying each dyadic likelihood scale by the amplification factor xi^{-rho} cancels the rank power, so the amplified posterior is equivalent up to constants to one max-tilted leader law with density L_q^{1-alpha} max_x L_x^alpha. This law has constant total mass and constant valid mass for every near pair, with no guard condition; the remaining posterior target is now a compact sampler for this single max-tilted law.
- Looked at the list-decoding/lattice direction again and added the Construction-A theta quotient. A discrete-Gaussian law on a Construction-A lattice factors exactly into a soft theta-reliability law on the residue code plus independent one-dimensional integer lifts; threshold listing similarly reduces to a soft residue-code superset before sphere enumeration. This makes the lattice alternative more concrete: analyze residue-code list recovery and tilted partition functions, then certify storage/far/near sampled spectrum, rather than invoking a black-box CVP decoder.
- Rewrote the max-tilted alpha-leader target as a Gaussian Voronoi capture law. On the cell where x is the likelihood leader, the max tilt is L_x^alpha, so the intensity of x equals exp(-alpha(1-alpha)t||x-q||^2/2) times the probability that x is the exact nearest neighbor of a Gaussian perturbation centered at (1-alpha)q+alpha x. This removes the opaque max from the target and reframes the remaining sampler as compact smoothed Voronoi-cell quadrature.
- Added query-centered alpha-leader reweighting. With `alpha=Theta(1/H)`, `H>=max(D,log m)`, and `t=Theta(H/r^2)`, the max-tilted leader law equals the exact nearest resident cell of `Z~N(q,I/t)` size-biased by `exp(alpha t/2 (||Z-q||^2-||Z-NN(Z)||^2))`; the second-moment tail is polylog-truncatable. Thus alpha-leader sampling reduces to repeated nearest-cell decoding plus scalar rejection.
- Reframed the nearest-cell endpoint as a structured decoder normal form rather than a black-box exact-NN assumption. The list-decoding/lattice literature suggests the right retained object is a compact code/lattice/trellis or quotient-panel decoder with certified cell-spectrum bounds; k-means or Lloyd can propose cells at build time, but the proof object is the decoder certificate.
- Weakened the posterior endpoint from exact nearest-cell decoding to tilted witness-listing. For `Z~N(q,I/t)`, it suffices that a retained list or bucket reporter contains the true near point `p` with constant mass under the tilted weight `exp(alpha t/2 (||Z-q||^2-||Z-p||^2))`; verified scalar rejection then succeeds with only a polylog trial factor. This gives a k-means++/facility-bucket theorem target: prove tilted witness capture plus scan-load bounds, not Lloyd convergence or exact Voronoi decoding.
- Added a Poisson multi-assignment rate-coverage compiler. If point `p` is replicated to labels with budget `a_p` and assignment law `mu_p`, while the query probes labels `B`, the exact miss probability is `exp(-a_p mu_p(B))`; averaging this under the tilted witness law gives the witness-list loss. The remaining k-means++/facility theorem is now a scalar redundancy certificate: tilted miss, reported incidence load, and total replication budget.
- Converted rate coverage to point-label intensities `b_{x,a}` and added the finite convex verifier. Tilted miss is `E R exp(-sum_{a in B} b_{p,a})`, load/storage are linear, so proposed facility panels can be accepted by a convex feasibility check. Failure has a dual obstruction pricing near witnesses, query load, and storage; those prices are the right column-generation signal for new k-means++ seeds, code labels, or lattice buckets.
- Fixed the convex verifier proof to separate the upper hull of violation vectors from the origin. The storage coordinate is what makes the upper hull closed: any convergent certificate sequence has bounded total intensity and hence a convergent subsequence of `b`. The list-decoding/lattice literature search reinforces the same implementation target: compact soft/list-recovery reporters and Fano-biased stack potentials, with k-means++ useful as a build-time proposal mechanism rather than the theorem object.
- Added the one-label reduced-cost formula for rate coverage. Given a dual obstruction and current intensities, a new label has residual value `G_x(a)` for point `x` and dual price `c_a`; assigning intensity `s` changes the Lagrangian by `G(e^{-s}-1)+cs`, so the optimal update is `s=log(G/c)_+`. This gives a precise theory-guided alternative to k-means++: propose centers/labels by residual tilted witness coverage per query-load/storage price.
- Added finite column generation for rate coverage. If a finite candidate bucket family is feasible, then any infeasible restricted convex solve with an attained dual minimizer must expose an inactive point-label pair with positive reduced gain `G_x(a)>c_a`; adding such pairs exhaustively finds a feasible active certificate. Build time can therefore search aggressively over k-means++ seeds, code labels, or lattice buckets, while the proof obligation is only that the finite candidate family itself contains a feasible rate-coverage panel.
- Added the whole-bucket reduced-gain objective. For a proposed label, the optimal simultaneous intensities across all points separate, and the exact dual improvement is `sum_x Phi_c(G_x(a))` with `Phi_c(G)=G-c-c log(G/c)` above price. This is the bucket-level objective a facility, code, or lattice label generator should optimize at build time.
- Looked up the list-decoding/lattice angle again and added seeded reduced-gain search. A k-means++, code, or lattice proposal routine only needs to expose a positive whole-bucket reduced-gain label against the current finite convex dual obstruction; exact verification then accepts the label and column generation proceeds Las Vegas. This makes expensive build time useful without relying on Lloyd convergence or a black-box lattice decoder.
- Added implicit reduced-gain separation. The label universe no longer has to be enumerated up front: an exact code/lattice/trellis/facility separator either returns a positive reduced-gain quotient label or certifies that the current dual obstruction rules out every finite-support panel in that implicit universe. This is closer to the list-decoding view, where the retained object is a compact separator/reporter rather than a materialized list of all labels.
- Added the variational form of reduced-gain separation. The nonlinear whole-bucket gain is the supremum over tentative point intensities of a signed weighted coverage surplus over label reporter events. This gives a concrete separator target for code/lattice/facility generators: solve weighted coverage in the implicit label family, then verify the exact gain before adding the column.
- Added layer-cake reduced-gain separation. Positive nonlinear gain is equivalent to one positive uniform-intensity layer on a subset of points, and under an intensity cap the scalar layer only needs a logarithmic dyadic grid. This sharpens the implicit separator target from arbitrary continuous point profiles to subset-weighted coverage objectives.
- Added pointwise reduced-cost separation. Whole-bucket gain is positive exactly when some point-label pair has residual tilted coverage above its dual load/storage price. Thus the completeness oracle for implicit code/lattice/facility universes is a pointwise weighted coverage separator; whole-bucket gain is still useful for choosing stronger greedy columns.
- Added a soft list-decoding reduced-cost separator. If the pointwise reduced-cost score decomposes into coordinate reliabilities over a code, trellis, or lattice-residue universe, any certified threshold list decoder plus exact filtering is a complete implicit separator. This makes the score-tree-as-lattice-decoder analogy operational without importing a black-box decoding theorem.
- Added a unified soft-decoded rate-coverage compiler. A compact code/lattice/trellis decoder handles online reporter enumeration, while any exact reduced-cost optimizer may drive offline column generation during preprocessing; the convex verifier remains the object that certifies storage, reported incidence load, and tilted witness miss. Empty-label decoder output is charged explicitly as query overhead.
- Added the threshold-event reduced-cost normal form. When online reporter events are coordinatewise threshold predicates, the offline reduced-cost separator is exactly a signed weighted threshold-coverage problem over those tests, not necessarily an additive soft-decoding score. This identifies the real code/lattice build-time oracle while keeping query reporter costs separate.
- Added positive-coverage screening for reduced cost. Any signed reduced-cost witness must first pass the nonnegative positive-coverage threshold, so the offline generator only has to list labels with enough positively priced reporter tests; negative tests are handled by exact verification. This is a cleaner target for code/lattice/list-recovery proposal routines.
- Added dyadic unweighting for positive coverage after checking the list-recovery/list-decoding literature. Arbitrary nonnegative dual weights can be grouped into dyadic classes, so build-time candidate generation can use unweighted agreement/list-recovery calls and leave the original real-valued reduced-cost objective to exact verification. This makes the score-tree-as-decoder analogy closer to standard code and lattice list decoding.
- Added scalar redundancy water filling for fixed reporters and assignment laws. In the Poisson multi-assignment certificate, the optimal replication budget for point `p` is determined by the marginal tilted coverage equation `D_p(a_p)=gamma`; points with `D_p(0)<=gamma` get no extra copies. This is the clean theory version of optimizing redundancy with expensive build time, independent of whether the proposal labels come from k-means++, codes, or lattices.
- Added a finite-signature rate-coverage envelope. A continuous query domain can be certified without a metric net if queries are grouped by reporter signatures and preprocessing proves finite upper envelopes for the tilted miss coefficients and load coefficients in each signature. This turns the k-means/code/lattice panel verifier into a small-signature theorem target rather than a finite sampled-query heuristic.
- Added a finite decoder-transcript rate-coverage envelope after revisiting list-decoding/lattice-decoding papers. A retained code, trellis, polar, or lattice quotient decoder can certify tilted miss, reporter work, and scan load by finite transcript states rather than by enumerating all label subsets. This is the self-contained version of the "score tree is decoding a lattice" idea: the proof object is a transcript-level envelope plus exact filtering, not a black-box CVP/list decoder.
- Added a compressed decoder-DAG rate-coverage envelope. A reporter with exponentially many possible transcripts can now be certified on its compact acyclic state graph: tilted state-reach envelopes pay miss probability at terminal bucket states, ordinary reach envelopes pay internal decoder work and scan load. This is closer to what list-recovery, polar, trellis, and lattice-enumeration implementations actually retain.
- Added an output-sensitive decoder-DAG rate-coverage envelope. Enumerating decoders may report many terminal buckets; the miss certificate uses responsibility weights on reported outputs, so it can charge one useful terminal or a convex mixture without multiplying the tilted miss by the output-list size. Scan work is still charged to every reported bucket. This is the right interface for pruned sphere enumeration and list-recovery style reporters.
- Added a reliability-erasure sweep reporter inspired by GMD and Chase decoding. One hard symbol plus one confidence per coordinate gives an O(h)-transcript decoder that returns any codeword satisfying a simple confidence-weighted agreement inequality. This is the lowest-overhead list-decoding/lattice-decoding alternative to full repeated-coordinate soft list recovery, but it needs a spectrum theorem showing near routes have the required weighted agreement.
- Added a soft-score-to-GMD margin compiler. If the gap between the best symbol and every other symbol in a coordinate dominates a confidence value, then any codeword close enough to the coordinatewise top soft score satisfies the GMD weighted-agreement inequality. This changes the code/lattice theorem target from a raw GMD assumption to a top-gap score-margin spectrum statement.
- Packaged the GMD margin condition as a retained block certificate. A high-margin query signature can use ordinary errors-and-erasures decoding plus exact score filtering as a certified soft-threshold reporter with only O(h) erasure transcripts and output-sensitive scan charging. This is the most concrete low-overhead replacement for full soft list recovery so far.
- Added a Chase-GMD block certificate. Branching over a small set of ambiguous coordinates and promoting the guessed symbols to confidence 1 weakens the required GMD margin outside that set; the cost is the explicit branch product times O(h). If the ambiguity product is polylogarithmic, this is still compatible with the desired overhead and gives a concrete alternative to full soft list recovery for medium-margin signatures.
- Added top-list Chase completeness and an ordered-statistics information-set reporter after revisiting the code/lattice decoding angle. Top lists are enough when omitted symbols already fall below the soft threshold, and a systematic chart gives an OSD-style alternative: enumerate only high-score information words, extend through the code or quotient basis, and exact-filter. This makes "score tree as lattice decoder" more operational and uses expensive build time to search for reliable charts rather than relying on Lloyd convergence.
- Added the minimum-entropy information-set compiler. For a linear block, the OSD list-size exponent is exactly the score slack plus the minimum basis weight for coordinate weights `log sum_a exp(beta(omega_i(a)-m_i))`; the best chart is found by greedy matroid basis selection. For MDS/Reed-Solomon-style blocks this reduces to choosing the k smallest coordinate entropies, so the remaining spectrum target is a scalar entropy-margin bound rather than a full soft list-recovery theorem.
- Added a superset product-panel decoder and plugged in OSD blocks. This fixes the accounting distinction between true reported codewords and false information-set candidates: a block with entropy `Lambda_j` contributes an explicit `exp(Lambda_j)` multiplier to the prefix moment at the decoded block, rather than hiding candidate work in the output-sensitive scan term.
- Added superset/OSD product-panel rate bookkeeping. The OSD query factor is now explicit block-by-block: the product-panel `PP1` certificate needs `e^{-beta theta_{Q,j}} Z_j^Q` and `(1+A_j+exp(Lambda_j)) exp(beta(M_j-theta_{Q,j}))` to multiply to at most `m^rho polylog`; storage, far-load, and near-hit remain the existing product moment tests.
- Added a direct Euclidean list-decoder reporter after checking the code/lattice literature. If a block score is an embedded Euclidean score, a certified Euclidean-metric list decoder already returns the threshold superlevel set by one radius conversion; the repeated-coordinate hard-list-recovery lift is only needed for more general bounded soft scores. This is the lower-overhead route suggested by Euclidean Reed-Solomon, Construction-A, and lattice quotient decoders.
- Added a standalone budget-thinned one-tree work bound. Optional stopping for the canonical mixed budget now proves the full query-work recurrence for any eta, giving the 1/3 work scale at eta=(log n)^(-2/3) and polylog work at eta~(log log n)/log n. The old mixed-frontier assumption is no longer needed for work in the budget-thinned algorithm; the remaining gap is purely saturated near-route value delivery.
- Revisited list-decoding and lattice-decoding papers as an algorithm-design source. The useful interpretation is that the score tree is a lifted route lattice with Fano branch prices, not a data-point lattice to feed to a nearest-center oracle. Added text making the required decoder interface explicit: admissible prefix upper scores, compact transcript/state-DAG envelopes, and exact terminal filtering. Also clarified the k-means++ analogue: use build-time reduced-gain seeding against the convex dual obstruction, then accept only labels that pass the exact rate-coverage verifier.
- Added unit-success skeleton contraction for the harmonic route value. A score-only/deterministic region with exit frontier `F` satisfies `1/W(entry) <= 1/sum_{z in F} W(z) + depth`, so ordinary score branching and budget-stable deterministic recentering contribute only polylogarithmic harmonic resistance. The remaining near-value theorem can now be isolated on the contracted skeleton of genuinely thinned geometric or fractional-delivery opportunities.
- Looked up the code/lattice list-decoding route again with the current accepted-route and rate-coverage certificates in mind. The useful additional framing is negative: existing lattice list decoders, GMD/OSD, Construction-A theta quotients, and product-code filters do not by themselves prove the ANN endpoint. What they can provide is a compact reporter/separator whose state-DAG, prefix moment, storage spectrum, far co-load, and tilted near mass are then checked by the paper's certificates. This keeps expensive build-time search useful while avoiding any black-box nearest-lattice or Lloyd-style convergence assumption.
- Added harmonic resistance normal form and ported unit-region contraction. The optimal prefix-energy recursion is now written as a series/parallel resistance law: child conductances `gamma/R` add, and a one-child edge adds `R/gamma` plus one unit of local resistance. A deterministic score region with many first thinned exits can therefore be contracted to one aggregate conductance `sum gamma_e W(child)` plus its depth. This tightens the remaining value target to proving enough conductance across the skeleton of genuinely nonunit ports.
- Added a port-skeleton saturated comparison. After contracting unit-success regions, define a skeleton saturated value using only the nonunit port probabilities. The original harmonic value is at least the skeleton saturated value divided by the sum of contracted region depths along a skeleton route. Equivalently, it now suffices to prove that every cut of the port skeleton has inverse-polylogarithmic activation mass; ordinary score branching has been fully removed from the near-value cut target.
- Rechecked the list-decoding/lattice literature angle against the new port-skeleton target. The sharper algorithmic lesson is that a code or lattice panel helps only if it turns a nonunit probabilistic port into deterministic threshold enumeration, or into a sampler with an explicit state-DAG cut certificate. A black-box approximate closest-vector decoder is still too weak; a Fano-biased list decoder with admissible prefix upper scores makes the decoded block unit-success, so the skeleton contraction charges only prefix-moment work and polylog depth rather than detector-vote/cohort overhead.
- Added a canonical-defect splitting lemma for budget-thinned ports. The activation loss at an unstable port is bounded by a pair-score affinity defect `log_+(H0/(H_p^rho Q_q^s))`, a resident-surprise defect `rho log_+(H_p/Hbar)`, and the harmless `O(eta^2)` thinning slack. This turns the remaining skeleton cut-mass theorem into a sharper route-law target: prove only `O(log log n)` cumulative affinity-plus-surprise. Low-surprise prefixes are already paid for by the canonical budget; high-surprise prefixes are exactly the exposed posterior/facility buckets that rate-coverage or list decoding should cover.
- Added the posterior-superlevel cancellation lemma. If `H_p/Hbar >= exp(L)`, then the resident posterior superlevel `{x: H_x >= exp(L) Hbar}` has size at most `exp(-L)` times the bucket, so recursing on that decoded superlevel cancels the `rho L` resident-surprise term in the canonical defect. This makes the high-surprise handoff exact: a structured decoder for posterior superlevels reduces the value proof back to the pair-score affinity defect.
- Added the certified threshold-handoff corollary after another list-decoding/lattice literature pass. A code, lattice, trellis, polar, GMD/Chase, or OSD reporter only needs to output a certified cover of the posterior superlevel with polylog charge slack; the smaller resident count still cancels surprise, and the extra slack contributes only `O(log log m)`. This is the precise sense in which the score tree is a generic lattice decoder: the useful structured alternative decodes posterior threshold sets, not nearest raw centroids.
- Added the affinity-cut posterior handoff. After a posterior-superlevel child is decoded with slack `Xi`, its activation probability is at least `(1-kappa eta^2) H_p(u)^rho Q_q(u)^s/(Xi H0)` capped at one. Therefore the remaining near-value theorem can be phrased as a port-skeleton min-cut lower bound under these local affinity weights, rather than as a cumulative pathwise pair-affinity defect. This is a cleaner target for the adaptive second-moment or cut-energy proof.
- Tightened the affinity handoff to charge the activated cover object, not just an individual reported child, and added the affinity cut-energy certificate. A law on good port routes with `sum_{s in C} Lambda(s)^2/A_aff(s) <= polylog` on every skeleton cut gives inverse-polylog value; equivalently, bound the stopped square-spine toll `sum log_+ chi_aff`, where `chi_aff=sum lambda^2/omega`. This is now the exact second-moment target for the remaining posterior route.
- Revisited list-decoding codes/lattices as algorithm templates. The useful additional simplification is local: after a decoder reports a port list, the optimal route law is affinity-proportional, `lambda_e=omega_e/sum omega`, so the square toll is exactly `1/sum omega` and the square-biased spine is the same route law. This turns the remaining code/lattice theorem into proving that decoded port lists have only `O(log log n)` cumulative outgoing-affinity deficit across skeleton cuts, rather than proving a separate route-law inequality.
- Added dyadic outgoing-affinity boosting. The scale-window negative-moment compiler can be repeated with an extra `log(1/epsilon)` factor so the useful decoded child ports themselves have total outgoing affinity at least `1-epsilon`; choosing `epsilon=(H+1)^-2` makes the affinity-proportional square toll summable over a skeleton of depth `H` while adding only `O(log H)` local work. This removes a hidden constant-recall-per-port multiplication from the structured posterior route without introducing an aggregate success-port abstraction.
- Looked up the list-decoding/lattice-decoding angle again after the score-tree-as-lattice observation. The paper already has the right abstraction: Fano-biased threshold reporters, not raw closest-center decoders. The useful additions are May--Ozerov as the dual use of nearest-neighbor search inside ISD, sampling-decoding as a lattice Gibbs-panel precedent, and recent expander/folded-RS list-recovery work as plausible outer-code engines. None of these removes the remaining ANN spectrum theorem; they sharpen which decodable panels are worth testing and certifying.
- Added the leader-quotient version of outgoing-affinity boosting. After dyadic repetitions are boosted by `log(1/epsilon)`, Poisson superposition over the actual reported leaders gives merged leader-port weights `1-exp(-Lambda*)` whose total useful affinity is at least `1-epsilon`. This proves quotienting hidden rank prefixes by score leader preserves the affinity-proportional skeleton toll, so leader aggregation no longer hides a constant-recall-per-level loss.
- Composed boosted leader affinity with the port-skeleton value theorem. If each nonunit skeleton state is boosted to deficit `(H+1)^-2`, the affinity-proportional spine pays total square toll at most `2`, so the harmonic value is `Omega(1/L(root))`. This makes the posterior-route gap purely the construction of compact boosted leader reporters; the recursive value accounting is no longer a separate obstacle.
- Revisited list-decoding codes and lattices as separators rather than nearest-center oracles. The useful new bridge is that after positive-coverage screening and dyadic unweighting, ordinary list recovery on the repeated-coordinate projection of coordinate-membership tests is a complete reduced-cost separator; exact filtering restores the original signed weighted objective. This makes expander/FRS/tensor/Construction-A list-recovery machinery directly usable in build-time column generation without requiring a custom weighted decoder.
- Added finite Poisson realization for rate coverage. For a finite signature/transcript certificate with constant miss slack, `K=O(log number_of_constraints)` independent Poisson copies suffice so that one realized bucket structure passes every finite tilted-miss, load, and storage check simultaneously. Build time can sample until the finite certificate passes, so the retained rate-coverage panel no longer relies on an uninspected expected-over-preprocessing layer.
- Specialized finite Poisson realization back to the rate-coverage signature language. The new retained-bucket corollary converts finite envelope inequalities directly into one fixed bucket index whose tilted witness miss, reported incidence load, and storage all pass for every signature/query. This makes the facility/k-means++/list-recovery route a concrete retained-index certificate rather than a fractional-intensity certificate plus implicit rounding.
- Rechecked list-decoding code/lattice papers as alternative algorithm sources. The useful clarification is a decoder-side versus spectrum-side split: Barnes-Wall, Construction-A, Euclidean Reed-Solomon, OSD, expander, and folded-RS results can provide compact threshold reporters, samplers, or reduced-cost separators, but the ANN endpoint still requires the retained panel's storage, far co-load, and tilted near-mass spectrum at the critical exponent. Added this distinction to the routing discussion to avoid treating any lattice decoder as a black-box proof.
- Added a Las Vegas builder for the small-signature Gaussian quadrature case. If the finite query/witness signature set has size at most `m^rho polylog(m)`, uncharged preprocessing can repeatedly sample Gaussian label batches, solve the finite tilted-weight LP, and keep a basic feasible support of size at most the number of signatures. Thus the small-signature boundary case is constructive; the remaining obstruction is compact signature/decoder entropy, not finding the finite Gaussian weights.
- Added a finite-envelope Gaussian quadrature builder. Exact query representatives are not necessary: if a compact signature system supplies signed upper envelopes for the homogeneous tilted-weight LP coefficients, with strictly negative Gaussian mean, Las Vegas preprocessing finds a retained weighted panel of support at most the number of envelope constraints. This moves the all-query quadrature target from metric nets to certifiable coefficient envelopes.
- Added the random-position tensoring baseline from `notes10.md`. In a terminal spherical instance where invalid points have query correlation at most `O(1/log m)`, the smooth product filter with `k=O(c^2)` tensor blocks gives one-table constant recall, `m^rho log^{O(c^2)} m` query/update/far-candidate work, and `m^{1+rho} log^{O(c^2)} m` storage. The same holds w.h.p. for independent random invalid directions in dimension `Omega_c(log^3 m)`. Also recorded the worst-case extension suggested by the notes: bucket-net recursion should be charged by recursive cost divided by conditional near-hit probability, leaving a Renyi average list-size/DAG-sharing theorem for dense tangent-cap configurations.
- Looked up LP range-reporting/LP-query literature. The useful transfer is not the online fixed-dimensional query bound, which degenerates in logarithmic ANN dimension, but the violator-separation template: optimize on a small candidate basis, use range reporting to expose violated constraints, then verify exactly. Added a paper remark tying this to the reduced-cost separator: if a candidate label family has a low-dimensional halfspace or semialgebraic parameterization, expensive preprocessing can use a weighted violator reporter to propose positive reduced-cost labels, while the finite convex verifier remains the retained-index certificate.
- Promoted the notes10 bucket-compression idea from a remark to a compiler theorem. A finite approximation ladder closes to `m^rho polylog(m)` query and `m^{1+rho} polylog(m)` space if every compressed-filter step has (i) per-probe near collision at least `m^{-rho}/polylog`, (ii) success-normalized query moment `E |N_F|^{rho'} <= a m^rho polylog`, and (iii) shared-DAG storage moment `sum mult(A)(1+|A|^{1+rho'}) <= m^{1+rho} polylog`. This makes the remaining gap exactly the Renyi average list-size and canonical bucket-net sharing theorem, not the recursion accounting.
- Added a tuple-profile certificate for the compressed-bucket Renyi moments. The packing number of a bucket is bounded by ordered separated tuples from the data that all fall in the cap; for Gaussian caps, each tuple contributes at most `exp(-I(K,b))` where `I` is the nonnegative Chernoff energy determined by its Gram matrix and shifted thresholds. Fractional moments follow by interpolation: the query side with exponent `rho'<=1` needs only the tilted first tuple sum, and the space side with exponent `1+rho'<=2` needs first and second tuple sums. The remaining theorem is now an explicit packing-entropy minus cap-energy inequality over tuple profiles.
- Added the additive-IVF product-panel certificate. Orthogonal additive pivots make the tuple score `-||y-sum c_j||^2/2` block-additive, so buckets `(i0,i1,...)` are exactly product-panel labels and the existing `PP1`--`PP4` verifier gives the same `m^rho polylog(m)` terminal bound. For full additive quantization in one ambient space, the cross term `sum_{i<j}<c_i,c_j>` is now isolated as a query-independent interaction price: it must be small, structured, or included in a priced prefix reporter; Lloyd/additive-quantization distortion by itself is not the theorem object.
- Added the nonorthogonal priced-pair additive-IVF compiler. For two full-space codebooks, the exact pair score is `S_q(a,b)=chi(q,a)+zeta(q-a,b)`, so after reporting a first pivot `a`, the second codebook is decoded against the shifted query `q-a`. The new reporter bound charges first-prefix partition work, shifted second-reporter work, and actual pair outputs; the spectrum compiler then needs exactly four pair quantities: reporter work, stored pair incidences, far pair co-load, and near pair hit probability. This makes full AQ analyzable without pretending the cross term tensorizes.
- Added a finite retained priced-pair certificate for additive IVF. Build time may generate AQ, k-means++, random, list-decoding, or column-generation pair panels arbitrarily, but the retained index is accepted only after finite signature envelopes certify reporter work, stored incidences, far scan load up to the first verified neighbor, and near-witness coverage. This separates heuristic proposal from deterministic proof and makes compact query signatures the remaining obstruction for full additive quantization panels.
- Added a fractional LP and materialization bridge for priced-pair additive IVF. A finite pair-panel proposal pool is sufficient once it satisfies reporter-work, far-load, storage, and witness-coverage constraints fractionally; with inverse-polylog coverage, atom-bounded columns, and polylog signature entropy, randomized rounding produces a deterministic retained FRP certificate. The dual prices give the right build-time objective for k-means++, AQ, learned, or list-decoding proposals: find positive reduced-gain pair panels, not low Lloyd distortion.
- Added the priced-prefix additive-IVF reporter and spectrum compiler. For any number of full-space additive codebooks, the score identity is recursive: after selecting prefix sum `A`, the next codebook sees shifted query `q-A`. Admissible suffix scores certify an output-sensitive live-prefix decoder, and the terminal theorem needs the same four spectra as the pair case. This removes the artificial two-codebook restriction from the AQ route.
- Factored the finite retained additive-IVF proof into a scored-label certificate. Pair buckets, prefix AQ tuples, codewords, trellis terminals, and lattice residues now all reduce to the same four finite column quantities: reporter work, stored incidences, far scanned incidences, and near-witness coverage. The fractional materialization theorem also lifts to scored labels, so the finite verifier no longer depends on a pair-specific representation.
- Lifted the build-time LP dual and column-generation story to scored labels. The reduced-gain separator is now generic in the label representation: k-means++, AQ, code/lattice, trellis, or learned proposals only need to beat witness-value prices minus reporter-work, far-load, and storage prices. If a finite proposal universe is feasible and an exact separator is available during uncharged preprocessing, column generation finds a sparse fractional support before the existing materialization step.
- Added a rectangular interaction-cover compiler for nonorthogonal additive IVF. When the AQ interaction price `sum_{i<j}<c_i,c_j>` has a polylog-sized product-rectangle cover with certified lower endpoints, the query can use ordinary raw-score product decoders on each rectangle and exact-filter by the true AQ score. Storage and far-load are bounded by rectangle-overcounted exponential moments, so low-entropy interaction structure gives a product-style route before falling back to shifted-prefix or generic scored-label decoding.
- Audited the compressed-bucket compiler after the provability warning. The recursion is still a promising theorem route because its remaining gap is the concrete Renyi list-size inequality, but the space proof was overcharging coordinates inside every shared DAG child and then incorrectly collapsing the sum to `dm`. Fixed the accounting: original coordinates are stored once globally, while canonical bucket-net children store identifiers, metadata, and incidence structures only; the `m^{1+rho}` moment pays for the non-coordinate recursive index.
- Strengthened the compressed-bucket DAG accounting further. Recursive child storage should be charged once per distinct canonical bucket net, not once per filter occurrence; occurrence multiplicity is metadata and query-law mass. Added a first-moment query lemma showing that the query-side Renyi condition follows from the usual success-normalized expected bucket load, so the remaining compressed-bucket theorem is a storage theorem: prove a distinct-net entropy-energy bound, with occurrence first/second co-load as a sufficient certificate.
- Added an obstruction to the easy occurrence-co-load storage proof. A positive-correlation spherical code inside a tangent cap has polynomially large occurrence second moment, and for sufficiently large correlation even the occurrence `(1+r)` moment is polynomially above the target at critical single-cap scaling. This proves the compressed-bucket endpoint must exploit distinct canonical net quotienting or dense-cap sharing; a pure occurrence-moment theorem would be false.
- Added the sampled additive-IVF/AQ route. Exact suffix partition functions for nonorthogonal additive codebooks satisfy a residual-query recursion, so a top-down sampler draws tuples from the true Gibbs law `exp(beta <q,sum c_i> - beta ||sum c_i||^2/2)` without rectangular interaction covers or all-listing prefix search. The corresponding sampled AQ compiler needs only stored tuple incidences, far sampled co-load, and near Gibbs mass, making compact partition evaluation the real theorem target for AQ/lattice-style panels.
- Sharpened compact sampled-AQ decoding to a finite-state quotient certificate. A layered state graph is enough if its aggregate path weights equal the true Gibbs mass of all AQ tuples mapped to each retained bucket label; backward partition sampling then draws bucket labels exactly. The ANN compiler pays only stored quotient buckets, far sampled co-load, near sampled mass, and the state-graph evaluation time, so the remaining theorem can target compact trellis/lattice/residue-state entropy rather than explicit tuple enumeration.
- Added the stability test for sampled AQ quotienting. Merging tuple labels into retained buckets preserves storage exactly, improves near mass, and inflates far sampled co-load by only `K` when every loaded quotient class has its aggregate Gibbs mass within `K` of the loaded tuple mass. A simple sufficient condition is polylog quotient class size and `beta * score_spread = O(log log m)`, which is the provable form of "AQ/k-means buckets may be compressed if the merged alternatives are locally score-indistinguishable."
- Added responsibility-thinned sampled AQ quotienting. Instead of scanning the full merged union bucket, a quotient reporter may emit each point with probability comparable to its hidden Gibbs responsibility inside the sampled quotient class. This preserves near sampled mass and inflates far sampled load by only the thinning constant `K`; ordinary IVF union scanning is exactly the special case where `theta=1_{x in C_l}`, which recovers the earlier stability condition. This lowers the implementation target from score-stable quotient classes to output-sensitive responsibility reporters.
- Added the fractional dense-filter cover pivot. Dense balls, score caps, AQ/facility buckets, and spherical filters are now columns in one near-pair LP with update side, query activation, recursive query price, and storage price. The Farkas dual prices near-pair demand, query-local load, and storage; positive reduced gain is the right build-time objective. Poisson materialization rounds finite feasible covers, so the remaining theorem is the weighted dense-versus-spherical dichotomy rather than a hard branching or Lloyd/k-means++ analysis.
- Added the finite-signature filter-cover endpoint. A signature records active filters plus a superset of possible near witnesses, reducing continuous near-pair correctness to a finite LP. The conditional endpoint now proves the key accounting point: if the LP uses full descendant normalized query prices and storage prices, recursive dense overlap is charged locally and does not multiply over levels. The remaining gap is geometric feasibility/separation for low-entropy signatures, not recursion accounting.
- Added the weighted spherical reduced-gain test. Against any filter-cover dual prices, a spherical bucket law with the usual near-hit, far co-load, and update spectrum has positive reduced gain unless the dual puts large query-price mass on high-correlation bucket contents. This proves the spherical side of the fractional dense/spherical dichotomy and narrows the dense gap to converting high-correlation co-load into a radius-contracting column.
- Added the high-correlation dense-slice separator. Intersecting a spherical bucket with the high-correlation cap around a priced query gives a certified radius-contracting singleton-signature dense column. If the Renyi-priced slice moment is below the average captured demand, some dense slice has positive reduced gain. Therefore any remaining dual obstruction must have both large raw high-correlation co-load and large recursive dense-slice moment; the next theorem must rule out that simultaneous pair, likely via quotienting/shared dense slices.
- Added dense-slice prefix separation. For any high-correlation slice with closed-form child prices, sorting the singleton-query dual demand prices makes every profitable atom-safe sub-slice appear as a prefix. Thus an unresolved dense obstruction must satisfy a Renyi density envelope on every price superlevel inside every high-correlation slice, ruling out hidden high-price cores and sharpening the remaining weighted pseudo-randomness target.
- Added dense-slice quotient separation. A proposed merged dense filter with update side, activation predicate, and certified child price has positive reduced gain whenever its de-duplicated assigned primitive-slice demand exceeds query-price and storage-price charges. This turns the "merge overlapping dense regions" step into an explicit LP-dual separator with a duplicate-incidence factor, rather than a greedy clustering rule.
- Added the residual dense-cover obstruction certificate. If spherical buckets, whole dense slices, dense prefixes, and dense quotients all have nonpositive reduced gain, then the dual prices must satisfy the explicit system RO1--RO5: large high-correlation co-load, large dense-slice moment, every slice prefix below the Renyi density envelope, and every quotient below its de-duplicated query/storage price. The remaining geometric theorem is now the impossibility of this finite inequality system for a sufficiently rich quotient universe.
- Added the local residual-separator sufficiency corollary. If a finite quotient universe rules out every dual price system satisfying RO1--RO5, then the finite filter-cover LP is feasible by Farkas duality. This connects the new residual geometric theorem directly to the fractional filter-cover polylog endpoint, with no extra recursion or branching argument.
- Added the quotient-packing reduced-gain criterion. A distribution over dense quotients is enough to defeat a residual dual whenever its de-duplicated assigned primitive-slice demand exceeds its averaged query/storage price; conversely, if no quotient has positive gain, every such packing law has demand at most price. This makes the remaining geometric separator operational as a weighted quotient-packing problem.
- Added the conic quotient-packing normal form. The quotient separator can use arbitrary nonnegative build-time intensities rather than normalized distributions; after normalization, any positive signed objective exposes a positive-gain quotient. This gives preprocessing a linear search certificate while leaving retained storage/query charges to the final filter-cover LP.
- Added dyadic unweighting for quotient packing. Expanding a conic quotient packing gives de-duplicated demand-pair incidences; if the weighted demand beats price by a \(2J\) factor across \(J\) dyadic price classes, then one class already beats the same price. Candidate generation may therefore search one unweighted price layer at a time, followed by exact weighted verification of the original conic objective.
- Added the unweighted quotient coverage screen. Once a dyadic price layer is fixed, any quotient whose de-duplicated unweighted coverage times the layer price exceeds its dual query/storage price has positive reduced gain, and any positive conic layer witness contains such a quotient. This reduces build-time proposal generation to ordinary priced coverage on one layer, with exact reduced-gain verification before retention.
- Added the layer quotient separator target. The fractional dense-overlap route now has a concrete final geometric certificate: after spherical buckets, whole dense slices, and atom-safe prefixes fail, one dyadic demand-price layer must contain a quotient with `theta_j C_j(G) > P(G)`. This makes k-means++, AQ, cap nets, LP/range-reporting violator search, and list/lattice decoders proposal mechanisms for the same exact reduced-gain certificate rather than independent theorem assumptions.
- Added captured-demand dyadic layer extraction. The spherical near-hit lower bound implies that one dyadic demand-price layer carries at least a `1/O(J)` fraction of the primitive high-correlation demand, so the quotient separator can search a uniform unweighted layer without losing anything stored in the retained index.
- Added hinge-form query activation for quotient separators. For a proposed quotient core, the best query side is exactly the set of queries whose layer coverage beats their local query-load price; after restricting to those positive margins, the only remaining test is whether the summed margins pay the one-time storage price. This removes another artificial dense-filter design choice from the theorem target.
- Added dyadic query-margin layering. If a quotient core has hinge surplus with logarithmic slack, one uniform query-margin class already pays the storage price, so build-time quotient generation can search ordinary unweighted demand layers and unweighted query-margin layers before exact reduced-gain verification.
- Added atomized quotient and top-degree prefix reductions. Quotient certificates may now use selected point-slice incidences rather than whole primitive slices, and within a fixed certified container/query layer the best `k`-point update side is the degree-sorted prefix. This reduces the remaining dense separator to choosing a container, query-margin layer, and cardinality, followed by exact reduced-gain verification.
- Added the atomized prefix-density envelope. If no top-degree prefix inside a fixed container/query layer has positive atomized gain, every degree superlevel in that container must obey the same Renyi-style query/storage price envelope as the original dense-slice prefix obstruction. The remaining geometric separator can now target contradiction with these degree envelopes.
- Added the dyadic atomized-prefix screen. When atom-safe cardinalities are downward closed, any prefix with a factor-2 atomized gain slack yields a positive-gain power-of-two prefix. Thus proposal generation only has to search logarithmically many prefix cardinalities per container/query layer; retained columns are still accepted by the exact reduced-gain verifier.
- Added the atom de-duplication normal form. Any atomized quotient certificate with duplicate bound `b` can be replaced by a duplicate-free subcertificate that keeps one primitive slice witness per covered near pair and loses no normalized coverage. The atomized separator can therefore work with pair-support degrees and `b=1`; duplicate factors remain only for older whole-slice quotient certificates.
- Added pair-support saturation for atomized cores. After de-duplication, any atom family inside a fixed container/query layer is dominated by the saturated relation of all feasible near-pair incidences witnessed by some primitive slice. The remaining atomized separator no longer chooses an atom family: it chooses only the certified container, query layer, and prefix cardinality, then verifies exact reduced gain.
- Added the saturated pair-support envelope test. For a fixed certified container and query layer, absence of a positive saturated prefix is equivalent to a concrete Renyi-style envelope on the saturated support-degree sequence; with downward-closed atom safety, checking only dyadic cardinalities loses a factor of two. The remaining geometric separator is now to find a certified container whose saturated pair-support degrees violate this envelope.
- Added the saturated container-packing separator. The dense-container search is now itself conic/fractional: any nonnegative intensity certificate over container/query-layer/prefix records whose saturated pair-support benefit beats recursive query and storage price exposes a positive reduced-gain prefix. This makes k-means++, AQ, range-reporting, and list-recovery proposal mechanisms for one exact reduced-cost condition rather than separate algorithmic assumptions.
- Added the saturated query-hinge normal form. For a proposed update core inside a certified container, the best query side is exactly the positive support-minus-load margin set; if those margins pay storage, pair-support saturation converts the core into a positive reduced-gain prefix. The remaining separator can now be phrased as a priced bipartite support-density problem rather than a joint choice of update and query sides.
- Added the fractional query-weight degree normal form. The hinge objective equals a max over fractional query weights of a weighted top-k support degree minus query/storage price; if no atom-safe query-side prefix has positive reduced gain, every fractional query weighting in the convex hull of atom-safe query sets obeys the corresponding degree envelope. This turns the residual dense target into a weighted top-k bipartite degree inequality.
- Added the layer-cake query-threshold separator. Any violation of the fractional weighted top-k degree envelope integrates over query thresholds, so one ordinary threshold query layer already exposes a positive reduced-gain saturated prefix. Fractional query weights are now only a dual-analysis device; retained filters remain integral threshold layers and top-degree prefixes.
- Added the fractional threshold-container packing separator. A build-time dense separator may now produce conic certificates over containers with fractional query weights and prefix scales; layer-cake materializes any positive certificate as one ordinary threshold query layer plus a saturated top-degree update prefix. This keeps the retained index integral while making the geometric search fully fractional on the query side.
- Added the local-cover bridge and a posteriori pricing for threshold-container prefixes. A residual dual defeated by a fractional query-weighted container packing now directly implies finite filter-cover feasibility, and any exposed threshold prefix can be charged by its actual tightened/Pareto descendant price rather than the search surrogate. This folds ordinary ball recursion and terminal filters into the same dense-overlap LP without extra branching cases.
- Added the Pareto-priced dense-core layer-cake lemma. The remaining dense theorem can now search directly for a smaller certified child \(U\) whose fractional query-weighted support beats its actual query and storage price; one query-weight threshold then yields a positive reduced-gain filter. This is the most compact form of the fractional analogue of dense ball recursion.
- Added the Pareto dense-core residual envelope. Failure of every threshold activation of every certified core is now exactly the weighted support inequality \(PDCE1\) for all fractional query weights; violating that envelope is sufficient for local filter-cover feasibility. The remaining geometric theorem is therefore a concrete min-max statement: high spherical co-load must violate the core envelope, or else the residual should already be spherical-pseudorandom.
- Added the Pareto core hinge normal form and dyadic margin screen. For a proposed certified core, optimizing over fractional query weights is exactly the positive support-minus-load margin sum, and a logarithmic dyadic margin search exposes an integral query layer whenever the hinge surplus has slack. The residual dense separator now chooses the update core; the query side is canonical.
- Added the fractional Pareto-core packing separator. Build-time search may now produce any conic combination of certified cores and fractional query weights; if its total reduced-cost value is positive, layer-cake plus the hinge normal form exposes one ordinary positive-gain threshold activation. This makes the dense proposal objective exactly the core hinge \(FPC2\), while failure of every packing is the weighted pseudo-randomness condition for the residual spherical analysis.
- Added the exact weighted Pareto-core hinge separator. The theorem-level core oracle can optimize the original dual prices \(\alpha_{p,q}\) directly via \(WPC2\), so dyadic demand layers are only an unweighted proposal-generation screen rather than an inherent source of logarithmic overhead in the fractional dense-cover proof.
- Added the exact weighted core-law local-cover bridge. Any residual dual defeated by a weighted law over Pareto cores now implies finite filter-cover feasibility directly, so a separator with access to the original dual prices can bypass dyadic demand unweighting end to end.
- Added the slice-to-core domination certificate. The spherical high-correlation slice law only has to be fractionally dominated by certified Pareto cores with total query/storage price below the captured dual mass; singleton slices recover the old dense-slice test, while any useful quotient or facility bucket is now exactly a price-reducing compression of that primitive incidence profile.
- Added the dual obstruction to slice-to-core compression. If no fractional domination by the current core-record family beats the captured mass, a finite LP dual produces a new pair potential \(\beta\) whose incidence against every proposed core is bounded by that core's Pareto price; this is now the exact weighted pseudo-randomness certificate left for geometry or richer proposals to defeat.
- Added the compression-dual separator target. The remaining fractional dense/spherical theorem is now phrased as ruling out simultaneous feasibility of a residual filter-cover dual and a slice-core compression dual obstruction; if this separator holds for a rich core-record family, local filter-cover feasibility follows directly.
- Added the compression-dual hinge normal form. For a fixed Pareto core, all query-weighted record constraints reduce to one positive-margin inequality under the compression potential \(\beta\), yielding an explicit two-potential residual certificate \((\alpha,\beta)\) whose impossibility is now sufficient for local cover feasibility.
- Added balanced residual segment normalization. The compression potential can be scaled to have exactly the same primitive captured mass as \(\alpha\), and convexity keeps every interpolation between \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) inside the same Pareto-core hinge envelopes; the remaining obstruction is therefore a fixed-mass convex residual.
- Added the one-potential captured-mass separator. Using the balanced residual segment and the spherical near-hit lower bound, any failed local cover would yield a single nonnegative potential with large primitive high-correlation captured mass while every Pareto core obeys its hinge envelope; ruling out that single-potential object now suffices for local filter-cover feasibility.
- Added normalized one-potential obstruction. The remaining single-potential theorem is homogeneous, so any counterexample can be scaled to unit primitive captured mass with total query/storage price below \(1/\pi_1\); this removes an irrelevant scale parameter from the geometric target.
- Added primitive hit-kernel cover equivalence. The one-potential core separator is exactly the Farkas dual of a fractional cover of the primitive spherical hit kernel \(r_{p,q}\) by Pareto-core records at \(\pi_1\)-scaled query/storage budgets; this turns the current obstruction back into the fully fractional dense-overlap primal object.
- Added hit-kernel cover materialization. A feasible primitive hit-kernel cover scales by \(1/\pi_1\) into an ordinary near-pair filter cover and then Poisson-rounds into retained Boolean core records with the usual logarithmic query/storage inflation, so the current primal target is directly implementable inside the finite filter-cover compiler.
- Added the primitive slice-law baseline. If the raw high-correlation slices produced by the spherical bucket law have \(\pi_1\)-scaled query and storage moments, then assigning each slice intensity \(\Pi(z)\) is already a feasible primitive hit-kernel cover; quotienting/compression is now isolated to the case where those raw recursive moments are too large.
- Added excess slice-moment repair. The hit-kernel cover can keep the affordable part of the raw primitive slice law and replace only a fractional overbudget sublaw, provided Pareto cores dominate the deleted incidence and the net query/storage loads fit the \(\pi_1\)-scaled budgets; the remaining dense theorem is now an overbudget-sublaw repair problem.
- Added the finite repair dual. For a fixed deletion sublaw, the replacement problem has its own Farkas dual and positive repair-gain column-generation oracle; this makes dense merging a local reduced-gain search over replacement cores with saved primitive costs as the residual budgets.
- Added the joint deletion-repair dual. Optimizing over the deletion sublaw produces a hinge rule: delete primitive slices exactly when their saved recursive query/storage price exceeds their exposed near-pair value under the current dual prices. The remaining dense theorem is now to rule out the joint obstruction, not to guess a k-means-style dense family.
- Added the joint repair separator corollary. If every repair-dual price system satisfies the non-strict deletion hinge inequality, then the joint repair LP is feasible and the excess slice-moment repair lemma yields the primitive hit-kernel cover. This makes the finite dense-overlap proof target explicit.
- Added the capped primitive-moment normal form. The joint repair hinge is exactly the inequality \(\sum\Pi\min\{P_{z,q},A_{z,q}\}\le\pi_1(L\sum\tau+\sigma S)\), so raw overbudget primitive slices are irrelevant unless their exposed near-pair value pays their recursive price.
- Added the paid primitive-sublaw normal form. The capped moment is the recursive price of the canonical sublaw \(\omega_{z,q}=\Pi(z)\min(1,A_{z,q}/P_{z,q})\), and each retained unit is paid by exposed near-pair value. The remaining dense separator can target paid overbudget primitive mass rather than the full slice law.
- Added singleton saturation for paid laws. If primitive singleton slices are included as build-time records, no-positive-gain prices force \(A_{z,q}\le P_{z,q}\), so the paid sublaw has total price exactly \(\sum\gamma r\). The remaining compression target is therefore the value-proportional paid primitive law.
- Added the paid quotient reduced-gain criterion. Probability-weighted paid primitive incidences \(\sum\Pi(z)A_{z,q}\) are automatically de-duplicated because each near pair has total primitive law mass at most one, so a core capturing paid value above its query/storage price has positive repair reduced gain without the older duplicate factor.
- Added the paid compression local-cover bridge. If every surplus singleton-saturated paid primitive law admits a conic compression into assigned Pareto cores whose probability-weighted paid value beats query/storage price, the primitive hit-kernel cover is feasible. This makes the fractional dense-filter-cover proposal an explicit theorem target rather than a heuristic clustering rule.
- Added the canonical paid-core hinge normal form. For a proposed update core, the optimal paid primitive assignment is all visible whole slices and the optimal query side is the positive-margin set, so the paid separator only has to find a core violating \(\sum_q(h_U^\gamma(q)-T_U\tau_q)_+>\sigma S_U\).
- Added the atomized paid-core hinge normal form. Whole-slice containment is not necessary: assigning paid atoms inside a core is automatically de-duplicated by the primitive hit-kernel mass \(r_{p,q}\le1\), so a core with \(\sum_q(\bar h_U^\gamma(q)-T_U\tau_q)_+>\sigma S_U\) already forces positive reduced gain and hence a hit-kernel cover.
- Added the weighted atomized-prefix normal form. For any certified container and fractional query weighting, the optimal \(k\)-point paid core is the top-\(k\) paid-degree prefix; failure of every such prefix gives the explicit residual envelope \(D_y^\gamma(k)\le\psi(k^\rho\sum_q\tau_qy_q+\sigma k^{1+\rho})\).
- Added the paid atomized-prefix separator. The dense-side theorem is now the statement that every surplus paid dual violates one certified container-prefix envelope; otherwise the exact residual counterexample is a paid potential whose degree superlevels obey \(PPRE1\)--\(PPRE2\) in every certified container.
- Added the paid-prefix endpoint bridge. The conditional polylog theorem now explicitly factors through the spherical primitive hit law, paid hit-kernel cover, finite signature filter cover, and the final \(d\,n^{\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n\) endpoint; the remaining geometry is isolated to ruling out the paid-prefix residual envelope or proving it spherical-pseudorandom.
- Clarified the finite-certificate gate for the fully fractional dense-cover route. A cover over all continuous queries is only an analytic guide; the retained proof object must pass through finite signatures, exact near-set signatures, or equivalent finite transcripts before duality and Poisson rounding apply. Proposal mechanisms such as \(k\)-means++, AQ, range reporting, and list/lattice decoding remain build-time search tools for finite positive-gain columns, not assumptions in the stored data structure.
- Added the weak-type form of the paid-prefix residual envelope. If no paid prefix has positive reduced gain, every paid-degree superlevel in a certified container is either query-price small, storage-price massive, or below the balanced density \(C_\rho\psi A_y^\rho\sigma^{1-\rho}\); this makes the next geometric contradiction target explicit.
- Added the forbidden weak-type superlevel separator. A geometric/list-decoding proposal now has a precise acceptance test: return one certified paid-degree superlevel in the middle regime forbidden by the weak-type envelope, and the finite duality exposes a positive paid prefix and hence the primitive hit-kernel cover.
- Added cardinality-net robustness for the weak-type separator. A geometric or list-decoding routine no longer has to hit an exactly available prefix size: if the available sizes form a constant-net such as powers of two, any heavy superlevel with constant slack rounds down to an available paid prefix and still proves the hit-kernel cover.
- Added the layer-cake weak-type separator. The geometric target may now be an averaged certificate: any finite weighted collection of levels, or a truncated Lorentz/Rényi moment of the paid-degree sequence, whose surplus beats the recursive query/storage envelope forces one forbidden superlevel and hence the hit-kernel cover.
- Added the aggregate list-moment weak-type separator. The averaged paid-degree target can now be certified from only aggregate list mass and a storage \((1+\rho)\)-moment: if captured paid mass \(B\) beats \(\psi(\widehat A M_0^{1-\rho}M_1^\rho+\sigma M_{1+\rho})\), a forbidden level exists. This matches the notes10 average list-decoding shape more directly than a pointwise superlevel test.
- Added the aggregate list-moment residual envelope. A failed paid-prefix separator now has a single averaged obstruction: every finite level panel must obey \(B\le\psi(\widehat A M_0^{1-\rho}M_1^\rho+\sigma M_{1+\rho})\), with the capped-list version \(M_{1+\rho}\le H^\rho M_1\). Proving any uniform violation of this envelope implies the primitive hit-kernel cover.
- Added the success-normalized aggregate list-panel target. The remaining list-decoding/range-reporting theorem can now be stated as a balanced panel whose captured paid hit mass dominates its success-normalized query moment and storage moment. This is the formal bridge from notes10's "bucket cost divided by success probability" invariant into the fractional dense-filter LP: output one balanced aggregate panel, and the paid-prefix machinery converts it into a hit-kernel cover.
- Added the average list-size panel criterion. Because the aggregate panel is homogeneous, a separator can work with a probability law over levels: prove \(E[\delta h_\delta]>\psi(\widehat A(Eh_\delta)^\rho+\sigma E h_\delta^{1+\rho})\), or the capped version, and the finite paid-prefix separator follows. This is the clean expected-list-size form for a tensorized spherical, lattice, or range-reporting proof.
- Added the random list proposal criterion. A build-time separator no longer has to output threshold superlevels or exact prefixes: any random list inside a certified container is accepted if its expected paid value beats \(b\psi(AE K^\rho+\sigma E K^{1+\rho})\), where \(K\) is the list size and \(b\) is the available-cardinality net loss. The verifier rounds the proposal to an available top-degree prefix, so k-means++, AQ, range reporting, and lattice/list decoders can be analyzed through the same value-per-moment inequality.
- Added seeded and marginal-incidence versions of the random list criterion. Seeded proposal laws reduce to proving an average value-per-moment inequality over seeds, after which one deterministic seed is verified exactly. Soft decoders can instead certify inclusion marginals plus a second moment, using log-convexity to bound the \((1+\rho)\)-moment. This is the current clean interface for geometric neighborhoods, facility buckets, AQ cells, and list-decoder output sets.
- Added the explicit fractional dense-filter cover definition. Dense children now appear as asymmetric LSF columns \((U_D,Q_D)\) with certified descendant prices, and the coverage/query/storage constraints are identified exactly with the finite filter-cover LP. This makes overlapping dense recursion a direct LP/rounding object rather than a separate branching process.
- Added the proposal-law filter separator. Any randomized build-time routine now only needs to prove positive expected reduced gain, equivalently a marginal incidence inequality, against the current filter-cover dual. One deterministic seed/filter in the support then has positive reduced gain, so the exact LP verifier retains only certified columns while k-means++, AQ, range-reporting, or list-decoding remain proposal mechanisms.
- Connected the existing scored-label/product-panel AQ verifier to the new proposal-law separator. The paper now explicitly treats additive-IVF, k-means++, and list-decoding panels as proposal laws whose accepted outputs become ordinary filter-cover columns in the recursive fractional layer.
- Added the list-proposal-to-hit-kernel bridge. The remaining paid dense separator can now be stated as: every surplus primitive-hit dual must admit a random list, seeded list, or marginal-incidence list whose expected paid value beats the query/storage list moments. Such a certificate directly contradicts the residual prefix envelope and proves the primitive hit-kernel cover.
- Updated the endpoint chain to include the new list-proposal bridge. The conditional polylog theorem now explicitly factors through either paid-prefix envelope violation or a random/seeded/marginal list proposal certificate before the hit-kernel cover and finite signature compiler.
- Added the query-layer form of random list proposals. Any fractional-query list surplus has a threshold query layer with the same moment surplus, so a future geometric separator can output an ordinary query layer and list rather than a fractional query weighting.
- Added query-layer forms for seeded and marginal list proposals. The same layer-cake reduction works for seeded decoders and soft marginal-incidence decoders because the seed/list-size moments and marginal second-moment bounds do not depend on the threshold. This keeps fractional query weights as a dual-analysis device; accepted dense/list filters can be ordinary Boolean query-layer activations.
- Added the Boolean query-layer list-proposal separator. The random, seeded, and marginal list-proposal hypotheses are equivalent to a form with an ordinary query set \(Q'\subseteq\mathcal Q\); fractional query weights can always be thresholded without losing surplus. This is the cleanest current interface for geometric proposal generators.
- Added the joint query-list proposal separator. A build-time law may now choose the container, query layer, and update list together; if the average paid value beats the average recursive query/storage list-moment price, one deterministic seed already gives a positive Boolean filter column. This connects k-means++/AQ/range-reporting/list-decoder proposal generation directly to the fractional dense-filter LP verifier.
- Added the deterministic query-list verifier. A proposed triple \((W,Q,L)\) is never trusted as the retained core: if its paid value beats its list-moment price, the verifier rounds to an available top-degree prefix for the same Boolean query layer and keeps that exact positive-gain column.
- Added the canonical query-side and query-free list proposal separator. For any proposed list, the best query activation is the positive support-minus-query-price margin set \(Q^+(L)\); therefore a proposal generator only needs to output a container/list pair. If the averaged positive-margin hinge beats storage, the verifier finds an exact positive-gain Boolean column.
- Added the query-free marginal proposal separator. The cleanest proposal interface is now soft: output a certified container, inclusion marginals, and a realizable second-moment bound; the proof derives the positive-margin query layer and then invokes the existing marginal verifier. Hard lists are the special case \(\pi=\mathbf 1_L\), so additive-quantization responsibilities and other overlapping soft buckets fit the same LP column-generation target.
- Added the independent soft proposal separator. A responsibility vector of mass \(\mu\) can be realized by Bernoulli thinning with second moment at most \(\mu+\mu^2\), so for \(\mu\ge1\) the verifier charges the familiar \(\mu^\rho\) query and \(O(\mu^{1+\rho})\) storage costs. This is the most concrete build-time target for soft AQ/k-means-style buckets.
- Added the finite soft-bucket separation oracle. Once preprocessing has any finite family of responsibility buckets, the exact dual objective is the hinge score \(\sum_q(h_B(q)-b\psi\mu^\rho\tau_q)_+-b\psi\sigma\mu^{1-\rho}(\mu+\mu^2)^\rho\); positive score for every surplus dual is precisely enough for the hit-kernel cover. This separates expensive proposal generation from the retained certified Boolean columns.
- Added the fixed-mass soft-bucket extreme-point reduction. For a fixed container and fixed total responsibility mass, the hinge score is convex over the capped simplex, so an optimal soft bucket is a hard list plus at most one fractional point; if the mass is integral, it is exactly a hard list. This confirms that soft responsibilities are an averaging/proposal language, while the exact separator can be driven by ordinary list-like objects.
- Added the positive-soft-gain hard-list rounding. A positive soft bucket can be replaced by a near-hard bucket and then by a two-outcome hard-list law; Jensen and log-convexity show the expected hard-list hinge beats the expected storage charge, so one ordinary hard list has positive canonical query-free gain. This removes the last fractional-materialization issue from the soft-bucket oracle.
- Added the finite hard-list separation oracle. The build-time oracle can now be stated directly over certified container/list pairs with the canonical positive-margin query side; soft AQ, k-means-style responsibilities, and facility weights are proposal mechanisms for finding such lists, but the retained proof object is an ordinary Boolean filter column.
- Added the hard-list-to-prefix reduction. Any positive hard-list hinge certificate can be replaced, for the same dual prices, by the top-degree \(K\)-prefix in the same container for one Boolean query layer. The remaining dense proposal theorem can therefore be phrased as a priced query-layer prefix/range-reporting problem rather than arbitrary clustering.
- Added the fractional-query prefix thresholding reduction. A positive top-\(K\) prefix certificate may be proved with fractional query weights, since the convex top-\(K\) objective attains a positive maximum at a Boolean query layer. Fractional query prices remain a dual-analysis device; retained columns are still ordinary Boolean prefix filters.
- Added the finite prefix separation oracle. For every certified container/cardinality template, the exact scalar objective is the convex top-\(K\) reduced cost \(\max_y D_{W,y}(K)-b\psi K^\rho\tau\cdot y-b\psi\sigma K^{1+\rho}\); a positive value is equivalent to a Boolean query-layer prefix with positive hard-list gain. This is the compact target for LP range reporting, tensor/list decoding, and additive-code proposal separators.
- Clarified primitive-visible support in the fully fractional core-cover section. Formulas that previously said \(p\in V_{z,q}\) "for some \(z\)" now use an explicit \(\exists z\in\mathcal Z\) predicate, so the hit-kernel cover, slice-to-core compression LP, and Pareto-core hinge separator are self-contained rather than relying on an informal unbound \(z\).
- Added the exact-near-set finite-signature compiler. If a signature records the exact unresolved near set, the finite LP may choose one witness per nonempty signature and cover only that witness, rather than every near pair in the signature. This is a strictly weaker ANN certificate with the same recursive query/storage accounting, so pairwise dense covers are no longer required when compact exact near-set signatures are available.
