- Strengthened the exact-near-set compiler to be genuinely existential: the LP now covers a signature when an active filter intersects its exact near set, with no fixed witness selector. Added the matching Farkas dual, so failed exact-signature covers price signatures rather than all near pairs.
- Updated the conditional endpoint accounting for exact signatures: the rounding entropy is \(\log(2+|\Sigma|+|\Sigma^+|)\), not \(\log(2+\sum_\sigma |W_\sigma|)\), because existential materialization has one demand per nonempty exact near-set signature.
- Added the exact-signature coverage-list oracle. Against an exact-signature dual, a candidate list earns a signature once if it intersects that signature's exact near set, so the dense separator becomes a weighted max-coverage/list-decoding problem rather than an additive near-pair degree problem. Seeded proposal laws such as k-means++, AQ cells, or lattice panels are accepted only through this exact reduced-gain inequality.
- Added the hit-probability form of the exact-signature proposal. A randomized list generator may be analyzed through the probability that it intersects each exact near set, or any certified lower bound on that probability, while averaging still exposes a deterministic positive-gain list column.
- Added variable-size and Bernoulli soft-bucket exact-signature proposal criteria. Proposal laws can now pay the recursive prices of the realized list size, and independent responsibilities give exact size-stratified hit probabilities via generating functions, removing the fixed-size artifact from the exact-signature dense separator.
- Added the normalized exact-signature coverage obstruction. If the certified list separator fails, the remaining counterexample is a probability distribution over exact near-set signatures under which every available list's weighted max coverage is dominated by its recursive query/storage price; variable-size and Bernoulli proposals are just averaged versions of this same obstruction.
- Added the capped Bernoulli exact-signature mass certificate. A soft responsibility bucket can now be analyzed only through its total responsibility mass on each exact near set and a tail bound for exceeding the cap; padding turns it into a fixed-size certified list, so the retained proof object remains an ordinary exact-signature filter column.
- Added the normalized capped-mass obstruction. The remaining soft-bucket theorem can now be phrased as ruling out a normalized exact-signature dual for which every responsibility bucket has nonpositive mass-screen value.
- Added the saturated-mass exact-signature screen. The capped-mass obstruction can now be attacked through a thresholded coverage inequality: a responsibility bucket wins if its mass on priced exact near sets beats recursive storage plus overflow tax after saturating each signature at mass \(a\).
- Added the layer-cake saturated-mass screen. The remaining soft-bucket obstruction now has a weak-type form: every responsibility-mass superlevel has priced signature weight bounded by storage plus overflow divided by the level, exactly matching a range-reporting/list-recovery separator target.
- Added the unit-mass weak-type residual. After dual normalization \(W_B^{\rm tot}\le1\), so the remaining separator target is simply one responsibility-mass superlevel whose priced exact-signature weight beats recursive storage plus the certified overflow tail.
- Added the finite exact-signature superlevel separator. The soft-bucket gap is now a finite weighted search over bucket/level pairs, with levels restricted to actual responsibility masses \(m_B(\sigma)\); any real-level violation rounds up to one of those finite levels without loss.
- Added the randomized superlevel proposal criterion. Randomized \(k\)-means++, AQ, lattice/list-decoding, or range-reporting proposals can be analyzed through expected certified surplus, but the proof object retained by the LP is still one deterministic bucket and one deterministic responsibility-mass level.
- Added the multiplicative level-net separator. Exact responsibility-mass levels can be replaced by any from-below \(b\)-dense geometric net at only a factor \(b\) surplus loss; in particular dyadic thresholds lose only a factor two, matching output-sensitive range-reporting/list-decoding implementations.
- Added the output-sensitive reported superlevel separator. A build-time decoder or range-reporting routine may return only a finite panel of bucket-level candidates with certified surplus envelopes; one positive reported surplus, or an approximate report within the available margin, materializes a deterministic filter-cover column while leaving reporter work in preprocessing.
- Added the success-normalized superlevel law. The exact-signature soft-bucket separator is now equivalently a ratio test: captured priced exact-near-set mass must beat recursive query/storage price divided by certified hit probability \(1-e^{-g}\). This aligns the finite LP obstruction with the compressed-bucket Renyi/list-size invariant.
- Added the exact-signature moment separator. A reported superlevel may be certified by captured signature price \(X\), query-price load \(Y\), and child envelopes \(T,S\); proving \(E(1-e^{-g})X\) beats the success-weighted query moment plus storage/overflow moments is sufficient. With compressed size \(K\), this becomes the concrete \(K^\rho/K^{1+\rho}\) Renyi list-size target.
- Added the exact-signature Renyi residual envelope. If a finite reported universe cannot separate a normalized dual, every law over its bucket-level panels obeys the reverse inequality \(E(1-e^{-g})X\le E[(1-e^{-g})\psi K^\rho Y+\theta\psi K^{1+\rho}+\varepsilon]\). This is now the exact obstruction a geometric/list-decoding theorem must rule out.
- Added the observable exact-signature Renyi profile separator. A spherical, AQ, k-means-style, range-reporting, or list-decoding proposal can now be certified only through four observable quantities: a success-weighted hit vector, a success-weighted Renyi query-load vector, a storage moment, and an error moment. Positive dual-priced profile margin implies the existing exact-signature filter-cover LP is feasible; failure leaves the reverse profile inequality as the precise pseudo-random residual.
- Added the observable Renyi profile-cover LP and its single-panel success-normalized corollary. The dense-overlap theorem can now be stated primally as a fractional cover of exact signatures by certified panel profiles: accumulated hit minus certification error covers every signature, accumulated Renyi query-load fits each query budget, and accumulated storage fits the node budget. A one-panel law with hit probability \(a\) may spend \(a\) times the query/storage budgets, making the notes10 success-normalized invariant an immediate sufficient condition for the exact-signature filter-cover compiler.
- Added the local-error observable profile-cover LP. Certification or overflow error can now be represented as a vector over exact signatures, so the cover subtracts error only where it can actually affect the hit certificate instead of charging one scalar error against every signature. The dual obstruction correspondingly prices the error vector by the normalized signature weights, which sharpens the decoded-panel route without adding a new assumption.
- Added the decoded bucket-panel profile certificate. A product/tensor/AQ/range-reporting decoder may output many overlapping bucket records; their superlevel hit contributions, success-weighted Renyi query costs, storage moments, and overflow errors add into one panel profile. If the averaged hit spectrum covers every exact signature by \(a\) after error and the averaged query/storage spectra are at most \(a\) times the local budgets, the exact-signature cover follows. This is the concrete panel-spectrum target for the remaining geometric theorem.
- Propagated local-error accounting to decoded bucket panels and recursive bucket-net ratios. The sufficient hit denominator is now \(\inf_\sigma(H_{\rm net}(\sigma)-E_{\rm net}(\sigma))\) with \(E_{\rm net}\) a signature-local error vector, and the first-moment active-load query certificate still applies with this sharper denominator.
- Added the decoded-panel endpoint certificate. The global polylog theorem now has a direct local sufficient condition: prove the decoded bucket-panel spectrum inequalities at every node with exact-near-set signature entropy and \(N^{\rho_c}\), \(N^{1+\rho_c}\) budgets, and the existing exact-signature LP/rounding induction gives \(d\,n^{\rho_c}\operatorname{polylog} n\) query time and near-optimal space. This makes the fully fractional dense-overlap route explicit as the current proof target.
- Added the local-error decoded-panel endpoint. The global endpoint can now use local-error decoded-panel spectra, local-error bucket-net ratios, or the local-error first-moment active-load sufficient conditions at every node, without changing the recursive query/storage induction.
- Added the recursive compressed-net panel criterion. Bucket records may now carry their own child exponent and price factor, with query spectrum charged as certified hit weight times \(K^{\rho_{\rm child}}\) and storage as \(K^{1+\rho_{\rm child}}\). This is the formal notes10 invariant: large bucket nets are allowed exactly when their success-normalized Renyi query moment and aggregate Renyi storage moment fit the local panel budgets.
- Generalized the compressed-net criterion to separate query-active signatures from captured exact signatures, so false active buckets remain charged in the query spectrum. Added the success-normalized bucket-net ratio form: divide query and storage Renyi moments by the minimum certified hit mass after error. This is now the precise "cost per success probability" theorem target for spherical bucket-net/list-decoding panels.
- Added the first-moment active-load query certificate. Since child exponents are at most one, the query side of the bucket-net ratio follows from the ordinary success-normalized active load, even with false active buckets included. This isolates the actual unresolved geometry to the storage Renyi moment or an even sharper distinct-canonical-net entropy-energy bound.
- Added the shared-canonical panel storage certificate and the distinct-canonical bucket-net specialization. The finite-profile compiler can now charge recursive child storage once per distinct canonical child identifier inside a decoded panel, while decoded occurrence multiplicity remains in query load and explicit metadata. This makes the remaining storage theorem match the actual shared-DAG index rather than an occurrence-weighted sufficient condition.
- Added local-error shared-DAG storage certificates. The signature-local error denominator now propagates through shared-canonical panels, distinct-canonical bucket nets, dyadic distinct-child tails, and tuple-profile storage envelopes, so the storage-side theorem target no longer falls back to a scalar overflow tax.
- Added the dyadic tail certificate for distinct child storage. The shared-DAG storage condition can now be checked by counting, at each recursive price scale, how many distinct canonical child identifiers appear in the decoded panel. This converts the remaining storage theorem from a raw Renyi sum into a large-child counting/entropy-energy statement.
- Added the tuple-witness distinct-storage certificate. A large separated canonical child contains many ordered separated tuples, so the dyadic distinct-child count follows from tuple-incidence envelopes. The remaining geometry can now be targeted as a tuple-profile entropy-energy bound on canonical-child incidence, not merely on occurrence buckets.
- Added the multiplicity-discounted tuple certificate. Occurrence-style tuple counting can now be quotient-normalized by canonical child multiplicity: if many decoded records point to the same stored child, each record contributes only the reciprocal of that multiplicity. This captures the shared-DAG compression that the positive-correlation occurrence obstruction was missing.
- Propagated the multiplicity-discounted storage certificate to the local-error decoded-panel endpoint. The global polylog conditional theorem can now invoke the quotient-aware discounted occurrence-energy route directly, rather than only mentioning it as an audit target.
- Added the finite-prefix local-cover and endpoint corollaries. The paid-prefix route can now be invoked through the concrete finite prefix-template reduced-cost oracle, so the clean theorem target is top-degree prefix separation over certified containers rather than an abstract paid dense separator.
- Added cardinality-net and residual-envelope normal forms for the finite prefix separator. The geometry may now search arbitrary prefix sizes while the retained template pool keeps only dyadic-size prefixes at constant slack, and failure leaves an explicit paid residual satisfying top-degree prefix envelopes for every retained container/cardinality template.
- Added the converse cardinality-net residual envelope. If only dyadic prefix sizes are retained and the finite prefix separator fails, the resulting paid residual still satisfies a top-degree prefix envelope for every analytic prefix size, losing only a constant factor. This removes the discrete-cardinality artifact from the remaining geometric theorem.
- Added the all-size weak-type residual and separator for finite prefixes. A failed dyadic-template separator now forces explicit paid-degree superlevel bounds for every container and every fractional query weighting; conversely, any geometric argument that finds a forbidden middle-density superlevel proves the finite prefix separator and hence the local/global polylog endpoint.
- Added the all-size aggregate list-moment separator. The finite-prefix residual can now be contradicted by an averaged level panel: captured paid degree \(\mathbb E[\delta H_\delta]\) only has to beat the Renyi query term \(A_y(\mathbb E H_\delta)^\rho\) and the storage moment \(\mathbb E H_\delta^{1+\rho}\), with only the dyadic-cardinality constants. This is the list-decoding/range-reporting moment target stripped of exact-level guessing.
- Added all-size random-list and marginal proposal separators. A proposed list, or a soft responsibility vector with a second-moment realization, now proves the finite-prefix separator directly if its expected paid degree beats the dyadic-adjusted \(K^\rho\) query moment and \(K^{1+\rho}\) storage moment. This is the clean build-time interface for k-means++, AQ, range-reporting, or list-decoding proposals.
- Added query-free all-size list and marginal proposal separators. A proposal routine may now output only a container plus a hard list or soft responsibilities; the verifier chooses the positive-margin query layer and then invokes the all-size moment certificate. This removes the last query-layer choice from the retained dense-filter proposal interface.
- Added finite all-size soft-bucket and hard-list separation oracles. The fractional dense-overlap column generator now has explicit \(b_0^\rho\) query and \(b_0^{1+\rho}\) storage losses, and any positive soft bucket with a realizable second moment materializes as a positive ordinary hard-list witness.
- Added the all-size prefix separation oracle. Positive all-size hard-list gain now reduces to a top-degree prefix for one Boolean query layer, and fractional query weights threshold to the same Boolean prefix objective. The remaining build-time target is the scalar range-reporting value \((FAPS1)\) over certified container/cardinality templates.
- Added the normalized all-size prefix obstruction. Failure of the finite all-size prefix oracle now leaves a unit paid-mass dual with query/storage price below \(\pi_1^{-1}\) and all retained templates satisfying the all-size prefix envelope \((APRE1)\). The remaining dense-overlap theorem is exactly to rule out this scale-free obstruction.
- Added the normalized all-size weak-type obstruction and separator. A failed prefix oracle now forces every retained exact superlevel to satisfy the absolute density cap \((NAW2)\), so a range-reporting or list-decoding theorem only has to find one retained exact superlevel above that scale-free cap.
- Added the normalized cardinality-net weak-type obstruction. Dyadic retained cardinalities now remove the exact-superlevel-size artifact at another constant factor, so the remaining range-reporting/list-decoding theorem can search arbitrary positive superlevels against the scale-free cap.
- Added normalized aggregate, random-list, and marginal proposal separators. The scale-free obstruction can now be contradicted by averaged superlevel mass or by build-time proposal marginals whose paid mass beats the absolute \(K^\rho/L\) query and \(K^{1+\rho}/S\) storage moments.
- Added query-free normalized proposal separators and a finite normalized soft-bucket oracle. After replacing query prices by the normalized budget, a build-time bucket can be scored by total captured paid near-pair mass minus absolute recursive query/storage moments, with no query-layer hinge.
- Added normalized soft-to-hard and hard-to-prefix reductions. A successful normalized soft bucket materializes as a hard list, and a successful hard list is dominated by the top total-degree prefix, leaving a scalar normalized prefix oracle over retained container/cardinality templates.
- Added the all-size scalar normalized prefix residual. The final normalized obstruction can now be stated without soft buckets, hard lists, query weights, or exact retained cardinalities: every total-degree superlevel in every retained container lies below the scalar \(K^\rho/L+K^{1+\rho}/S\) envelope.
- Added the scalar aggregate normalized prefix residual and average superlevel panel. A range-reporting/list-decoding reporter may now certify a panel of total-degree levels by aggregate captured mass and Renyi list-size moments instead of guessing one threshold.
- Promoted the fully fractional dense-filter cover route into the abstract and proof-status overview. Dense regions and spherical buckets are now presented up front as one LSF-style LP with update sides, query activations, descendant query prices, and storage prices; the remaining polylog theorem is the weighted dense/spherical separator, not a hard branching or clustering rule.
- Added the paid-prefix local-cover endpoint. A paid-prefix separator for the primitive spherical hit kernel now scales directly into the ordinary finite filter-cover LP, and a nodewise version with finite signatures invokes the existing recursive compiler to give the full \(d\,n^{\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n\) endpoint. This makes the remaining proof obligation exactly the local paid-prefix separator.
- Added the scalar dense-overlap separator target and its local-cover corollary. The fully fractional route now has a shortest clean proof target: every normalized paid residual must admit one retained container and one average total-degree level panel beating the \(K^\rho/L\) and \(K^{1+\rho}/S\) moments. If not, the scalar prefix residual is the exact weighted range-reporting/list-decoding obstruction to rule out or feed back into the spherical reduced-gain test.
- Added the certified scalar superlevel reporter criterion. An LP range-reporting or list-decoding routine can now be analyzed through one-sided certificates: lower bounds on captured scalar mass and upper bounds on superlevel sizes. If those reported quantities beat the Renyi query/storage moments, the exact scalar dense-overlap separator follows, while the retained structure remains the verified top-degree prefix from the finite LP.
- Added the certified scalar-reporter endpoint. The global polylog theorem can now be invoked directly from nodewise primitive spherical hit laws plus certified scalar superlevel reporters, without routing through the older paid-prefix assumption names. This makes the remaining theorem exactly a nodewise one-sided reporter certificate with full descendant prices.
- Added the one-level certified superlevel separator. A single retained smaller-radius container and exact total-degree superlevel beating the scalar \(h^\rho/L+h^{1+\rho}/S\) envelope is already a valid scalar reporter; if no scalar dense-overlap separator exists, every retained container and every superlevel satisfies the strict reverse bound. This is the clean no-concentrated-superlevel residual for the next geometric argument.
- Added the diffuse scalar residual corollary. Failure of the scalar prefix separator now explicitly implies singleton paid-mass caps, a lower bound on paid support size, and Renyi mass bounds inside every retained smaller-radius container. This gives the pseudo-random residual shape that the spherical fallback or tensor reporter should exploit.
- Added the Lorentz scalar residual profile. The normalized residual can now be viewed rank-by-rank: after sorting paid degrees inside any retained container, every prefix lies below the \(K^\rho/L+K^{1+\rho}/S\) envelope. This is the exact diffuse profile a range reporter must violate or a spherical/tensor fallback must exploit.
- Added the low-guard scalar-reporter endpoint. Resident guard sampling now removes high-density query constraints before the scalar dual is formed, provided the guard threshold has inverse at most the local \(N^{\rho_c}\polylog n\) budget. The remaining certified scalar reporter theorem only has to handle low-guard demand systems.
- Added the guard-bounded spherical reduced-gain test. If the high-correlation set in the spherical proof is guard-small and query activation probability is bounded, then the high-correlation co-load cannot block a positive spherical bucket. Thus the remaining scalar reporter theorem only needs to address high-correlation mass that is neither guard-solved nor concentrated in retained smaller-radius containers.
- Added the dual-weighted guard spherical test. The spherical obstruction is now controlled by \(\sum_q\tau_q\Pr[\chi(q)=1]|H(q)|\), not by the worst active query. If high-correlation caps are contained in the resident \(cr\)-guard ball, the low-guard domain removes this obstruction whenever the activation-weighted guard mass is below the surplus dual demand. This narrows the remaining scalar reporter target to high-correlation mass that survives the resident guard in a dual-weighted sense.
- Added the contained high-correlation local-cover corollary. In a low-guard node where \(H(q)\subseteq P\cap B(q,cr)\) and \(\gamma m\max_q\Pr[\chi(q)=1]\lesssim\pi_1L\), the spherical buckets alone separate every surplus dual, so the local filter-cover LP is feasible without invoking dense scalar reporters. The remaining reporter theorem only has to handle guard-leaking or dual-weighted guard-heavy high-correlation mass.
- Added the guarded normalized residual segment. Any surviving infeasible local cover now yields a unit primitive captured-mass potential satisfying every Pareto-core hinge envelope plus a lower bound on activation-weighted high-correlation guard mass. This merges the spherical obstruction and the one-potential dense obstruction into one normalized residual system.
- Added the guard-leakage residual dichotomy. With a budget-compatible low-guard threshold, the inside-guard part of the high-correlation set is too small to support the guarded normalized residual, so any remaining obstruction must put constant normalized mass on annular leakage \(H(q)\setminus B(q,cr)\).
- Added the guard-ball spherical fallback. If the primitive bucket law has the ordinary far collision bound outside \(P\cap B(q,cr)\), then every low-guard node satisfying the activation budget has a feasible local cover using spherical buckets alone. This isolates dense/list-decoding work to failures of the direct guard-ball far bound.
- Added the thresholded annular collision residual. Choosing \(H(q)\) as the guard ball plus annular points with collision \(c_q(x)>\pi_2\) makes the remaining obstruction canonical: any infeasible local cover must put constant normalized activation-weighted mass on those annular threshold violators.
- Added the annular active-collision residual. The thresholded annular obstruction implies a lower bound on the scalar far active-collision load \(\sum_q\bar\tau_q\sum_{\|x-q\|>cr}c_q(x)\), giving the pseudo-random branch a single analytic quantity to upper-bound under the core hinge envelopes.
- Added the activation-relative annular collision residual. Thresholding far points by \(c_q(x)>\beta a_q\) preserves the spherical far-budget argument with \(\pi_2=\beta\max_q a_q\), but converts any surviving low-guard obstruction into an active-collision lower bound with factor \(\beta\) instead of the weaker absolute \(\pi_2\).
- Added the far active-load spherical fallback. If every query's total far active collision load is at most the \(\beta\pi_1 L\) budget scale, the activation-relative residual is impossible, so dense/list-decoding work is needed only when this aggregate pseudo-random load bound fails.
- Added the dyadic relative-load superlevel lemma. Any far active-load overflow is witnessed, up to an \(O(\log |P|)\) loss after a negligible tail, by one dyadic annular conditional-collision superlevel \(c_q(x)>\beta a_q\), making the remaining overflow branch a finite reporter/list-decoding target.
- Added the weighted dyadic annular residual witness. Summing the dyadic layer-cake bound against the residual query prices shows that any surviving infeasible node has one dyadic conditional-collision annulus with large \(\beta\sum_q\bar\tau_q a_q|A_\beta(q)|\), which is the dual-weighted reporter target rather than an unpriced per-query diagnostic.
- Added the weighted annular-to-scalar reporter reduction. Large annular false-positive load is now only a bridge theorem: to close the local LP it must be converted into one-sided scalar superlevel certificates for the actual near-pair degree measure, preventing the proof from treating far annular points as cover columns by themselves.
- Added the primitive lift of annular superlevels. The dual-weighted annular witness now appears as an expected primitive bucket co-load \(\mathbb E_Z\sum_q\bar\tau_q\chi_Z(q)|U_Z\cap A_\beta(q)|\), and with finite primitive support as one concrete high-load seed, giving list decoding and additive quantization a finite reporter object to search.
- Added the single-seed annular query-layer reduction. A finite primitive annular witness now reduces by dyadic layer-cake to one seed \(z\), one collision threshold \(\beta\), and one size threshold \(\Delta\) with large \(\Delta\sum_{q:\chi_z(q)=1,\ |U_z\cap A_\beta(q)|\ge\Delta}\bar\tau_q\), which is the smallest current list-recovery reporter target.
- Added the annular scalar-reporter record criterion. A \(k\)-means++, additive-quantization, lattice/list-decoding, or range-reporting panel may now be analyzed as a finite proposal law over one-sided scalar reporter records; positive expected certified surplus exposes one deterministic retained record by averaging. This keeps annular far points as proposal evidence only, while the retained cover column remains a verified scalar near-pair superlevel.
- Added the annular near-shadow mass certificate. For a single-seed annular layer, a decoder now only needs to certify one retained container's true near-shadow mass and size upper bound; a dyadic layer-cake turns that into a positive scalar reporter with logarithmic loss. This converts the current geometric target into a weighted range-reporting/list-recovery mass certificate rather than an abstract scalar-level panel.
- Added the near-shadow prefix separator. Inside any retained certified container whose top-degree subsets can be materialized, a top-\(K\) prefix by annular near-shadow degree yields the scalar reporter whenever its mass beats the Renyi child-price envelope. Failure leaves the explicit weak-type prefix envelope \((NSP2)\), the current range-reporting/list-recovery obstruction.
- Added the single-seed near-shadow prefix obstruction. Any surviving infeasible residual now has one query-price-heavy annular layer \((SPO1)\) while every retained container and every interpolation point on the residual segment obeys the near-shadow top-prefix envelope \((SPO2)\). The remaining geometric theorem is to rule out this simultaneous tau-load/prefix-diffuseness obstruction.
- Added the query-layer near-shadow hinge envelope. The existing Pareto-core hinge bound implies that, on the same annular query layer \(Q'\), every retained core has near-shadow mass at most its query price times \(\bar\tau(Q')\) plus storage price. The single-seed obstruction now includes this local envelope \((SPO3)\), so the remaining counterexample is diffuse both globally and relative to the actual heavy query layer.
- Added the annular-to-shadow transport reduction. A heavy annular layer now closes the local LP whenever its far incidences can be transported into true near-shadow mass inside a retained container with low congestion; otherwise every surviving residual certifies a Renyi-priced congestion lower bound for every retained smaller-radius container. This makes the next geometric target a pure transport/list-recovery obstruction rather than a vague conversion from far false positives to near-pair mass.
- Added the annular-shadow cut normal form. The low-congestion transport condition is now exactly a Hall-type cut inequality over query sublayers: annular demand in every sublayer must fit the near-shadow neighborhood capacity plus an allowed unshipped tail. Failure of the reporter now leaves a concrete cut \(Y\subseteq Q'\) with excess annular demand, which is a cleaner range-reporting/list-recovery target than the raw transport variables.
- Added the half-mass excess-cut obstruction. If a retained container would make half of the annular mass affordable to transport, infeasibility forces a sublayer with excess \(\sum_{q\in Y}b_q-CD_W^\theta(Y)>B_{\rm tot}/2\). This removes the target-mass parameter from the Hall formulation and leaves a single large-excess cut to rule out or feed back into the spherical far-load branch.
- Added the cut-recycled annular sublayer normal form. Any half-mass Hall cut now contains a dyadic cut-restricted annular layer that preserves a logarithmic fraction of the weighted annular load while the tested retained container has small true near-shadow capacity on that layer. This makes the next separator target a finite list-recovery/range-reporting problem on recycled sublayers rather than a greedy dense-cluster selection problem.
- Added the recycled-sublayer separator sufficiency corollary. The remaining dense theorem can now be stated without all-subset transport language: for each residual single-seed annular layer, find one affordable retained container for which every large recycled sublayer has enough true near-shadow capacity. If such a separator holds, local fractional cover feasibility follows immediately.
- Added the Lagrange recycled-sublayer screen. The all-sublayer near-shadow lower bound is implied by a one-parameter positive-part inequality over query-wise deficits \(((2H/C+\lambda)\Delta'\bar\tau_q-s_{W,\theta}(q))_+\) at each dyadic annular size. This turns the remaining separator target into an additive range-reporting/list-recovery certificate.
- Added the fractional recycled-layer dual normal form. The Lagrange screen is now identified as the positive-multiplier dual of a fractional bad-sublayer knapsack, so a nonpositive scalar dual value rules out every strict-load fractional recycled layer and makes the remaining separator target directly optimizable.
- Added the threshold recycled bad-layer normal form. The fractional knapsack obstruction now has a primal search form: sort annular queries by near-shadow efficiency \(s_{W,\theta}(q)/(\Delta'\bar\tau_q)\), and any bad fractional layer is witnessed by a low-efficiency prefix with only a boundary level fractional.
- Added the threshold recycled-sublayer separator sufficiency corollary. The remaining dense certificate can now be checked by sorting each dyadic annular query layer by near-shadow efficiency and finding one threshold \(t\) with \(tL(t)-S(t)\le(t-A)\Lambda\), which materializes the Lagrange screen without arbitrary sublayer search.
- Added the finite-prefix recycled threshold screen. The continuous threshold search reduces exactly to sorted efficiency breakpoints, the \(A\)-boundary case, or terminal low total load, giving a finite prefix-sum scan for the current dense separator certificate.
- Added the finite-prefix recycled obstruction profile. Failure of the finite scan is now equivalently a large-total-load profile with strict deficit at every positive efficiency breakpoint and either positive \(A\)-boundary deficit or excessive \(A\)-boundary load.
- Added the integrated recycled-threshold obstruction. The finite prefix failure profile is equivalent to a cumulative excess-load area \(A L_0-S_0+\int_A^t(L(u)-\Lambda)du\) staying positive for every threshold, making the remaining range-reporting target an excess-load curve.
- Added the load-quantile recycled threshold screen. Convexity of the excess-load curve reduces the finite prefix scan to the \(A\)-boundary, terminal low-load case, and the first sorted breakpoint where cumulative load reaches \(\Lambda\), so the build-facing dense certificate is a weighted-quantile test.
- Added the load-quantile recycled obstruction profile. Failed recycled threshold screens now have a single strict quantile deficit after excluding the terminal and \(A\)-boundary alternatives, replacing the all-breakpoint failure profile for the remaining geometric target.
- Added the exact-load recycled quantile screen. In the non-boundary case, the weighted-quantile breakpoint condition is equivalent to the lowest-efficiency fractional prefix of exact load \(\Lambda\) having capacity at least \(A\Lambda\); failure is the clean obstruction \(\widehat S_\Lambda<A\Lambda\).
- Added the variational exact-load recycled screen. The exact-load quantile is the minimum-capacity fractional sublayer of load \(\Lambda\), so the dense certificate can be stated without sorting as a universal exact-load capacity lower bound, and failure exposes one deficient fractional query layer.
- Added the exact-load margin extension. Once the exact-load capacity lower bound holds, every larger fractional layer has strict slack \(AH+(a_j-A)(H-\Lambda)\), reconnecting the quantile certificate to the original all-large-layer recycled separator.
- Added the dominated-load recycled screen. Exact-load fractional layers are equivalently probability measures dominated by the normalized query-load measure, so the current dense theorem target is a normalized weak-type inequality: every dominated distribution must have mean near-shadow efficiency at least \(A\).
- Added the dominated-load recycled local-cover endpoint. The local dense theorem can now be invoked directly from one affordable retained container passing the dominated-load mean-efficiency test at every dyadic annular level, with all annular constants substituted.
- Added the finite dominated-load proposal game. In one congestion stratum, a finite affordable proposal pool now has an exact minimax form: either a proposal law passes every dominated-load test in expectation, or a level-weighted family of dominated query distributions prices every retained record below \(A\). This is the fractional dense-overlap column-generation obstruction for list decoding, LP range reporting, AQ, or seeded clustering proposals.
- Added the exact-load dominated proposal obstruction. The finite proposal-game residual can now be written as a level mixture of exact-load cohorts, equivalently as a law over extreme cohorts that are integral except for one boundary query. This removes the probability-vector notation from the remaining target and makes it closer to finite reporter/cohort materialization.
- Added the exact-load cohort-law separator. The finite-pool theorem target is now simply: for every probability law over extreme exact-load cohorts, find one affordable retained record whose expected near-shadow capacity is at least \(A\Lambda\). Failure of this statement is exactly the dominated proposal-game obstruction.
- Added the cohort-law scalar prefix normal form. A cohort law now induces a scalar point degree \(d_\eta^\theta\); beating the cohort law reduces to finding an affordable retained top-\(K\) prefix with \(d_\eta^\theta\)-mass at least \(A\Lambda\). Failure leaves an explicit weak-type prefix envelope for every affordable retained template.
- Added the cohort-law weak-type superlevel normal form. The scalar prefix target can now be checked by any materializable affordable superlevel with height times size at least \(A\Lambda\); failure means every affordable superlevel obeys the strict weak-type cap \(\delta h<A\Lambda\).
- Added the certified cohort-law superlevel reporter. A build-time decoder or finite randomized panel only has to certify a lower bound \(X\le\delta h\) and an upper size certificate \(K\) for an exact retained superlevel; if \(X\ge A\Lambda\), the existing cohort-law minimax chain closes. This turns the latest dense-overlap target into a one-sided reporter certificate rather than exact dense-cluster selection.
- Added the certified cohort-law mass reporter. The build-time target can now certify total cohort degree \(V\) inside a retained container, plus height and size bounds \(B,K\); a dyadic layer-cake extracts a reportable superlevel with yield \(V/O(\log(BK/V))\). Failure of the dominated-load certificate therefore leaves every affordable mass report below this threshold.
- Added the explicit cohort-law mass envelope. The remaining reporter target can now use the direct threshold \(V\ge4A\Lambda L_{B,K}\), with \(L_{B,K}=1+\lceil\log_2\max\{2,2BK/(A\Lambda)\}\rceil\); failure leaves every affordable mass report below this logarithmic envelope.
- Added the capped marginal cohort-law mass reporter. A soft proposal may be analyzed as a finite law over capped hard lists, using only inclusion marginals and total cohort degree; for sublist-closed retained containers, any responsibility vector with total mass at most \(K\) decomposes into reportable hard lists by the capped hypersimplex vertex argument.
- Added the finite capped-marginal cohort oracle. The generated soft-panel pool now has an exact finite matrix-game test: either a randomized panel law beats every cohort law at the explicit logarithmic mass threshold, or one cohort law witnesses that every generated panel is deficient. The unit-height bound for cohort degrees removes the height parameter from this finite game.
- Added the logarithmic top-prefix cohort separator. For sublist-closed retained containers, the remaining dense-overlap theorem reduces to finding a top-\(K\) cohort-degree prefix above the explicit \(4A\Lambda(1+\lceil\log_2\max\{2,2K/(A\Lambda)\}\rceil)\) envelope; failure leaves that prefix envelope for every retained template.
- Added the dyadic-depth cancellation in the cohort prefix threshold. Substituting \(A=2H_m(\Delta)/C\) and \(\Lambda=(1-\xi)\beta_0C_\eta\pi_1\kappa/(16JK_{|P|}H_m(\Delta))\) gives \(4A\Lambda=(1-\xi)\beta_0C_\eta\pi_1\kappa/(2CJK_{|P|})\), so the top-prefix target has no multiplicative \(H_m(\Delta)\) overhead.
- Added the cardinality-net cohort prefix envelope. Exact prefix cardinalities are no longer part of the target: any factor-\(b\) retained cardinality net suffices, with only \(K\) replaced by \(bK\) inside the logarithm; dyadic sizes give \(b=2\).
- Added the finite prefix-template separation game. The remaining dense-overlap search is now an explicit matrix game over verified prefix records with surplus \(V_\eta(T)-\Theta_C L_b(h_T)\); positive value closes the dominated-load certificate, while failure leaves one cohort law with negative surplus for every generated record.
- Added prefix-template column generation. For any finite implicit prefix universe, preprocessing can solve the restricted game, call an exact separator on the minimizing cohort law, and add only verified nonnegative-surplus records; if the full universe satisfies the prefix condition, the process terminates with \((DPG3)\), while a no-prefix certificate identifies the cohort-law obstruction.
- Added the cardinality-net cohort superlevel reporter. The finite prefix separator no longer needs exact top-prefix optimization: any certified threshold list with one-sided value \(X\ge\Theta_C L_b(h)\) is a valid column, while failure leaves the strict weak-type cap for every retained threshold-list report.
- Added one-sided prefix-value column generation. The build oracle can now return any retained record with a certified lower bound \(X\le V_\eta(T)\) above \(\Theta_C L_b(h_T)\); later games still score that record by exact verified payoff, so exact superlevel or top-prefix optimization is only one way to produce a valid column.
- Added the one-sided proposal-law prefix separator. Randomized or batched proposal mechanisms now only need a finite verified support with nonnegative average certified surplus; averaging exposes one deterministic retained column, while an exact no-prefix certificate rules out every such batch.
- Added the sampled one-sided prefix separator. If a sampleable proposal law has nonnegative expected verified surplus against the current cohort law, uncharged preprocessing can sample until a nonnegative-surplus deterministic report appears; only that verified column is retained.
- Added the sampled soft-prefix proposal separator. Soft responsibilities from k-means++, additive quantization, list recovery, or learned proposal panels can now be used as proposal evidence; preprocessing materializes the exact top-degree prefix and retains only the deterministic verified record.
- Added the fractional probing form of the compressed-bucket compiler. The query-side hypotheses can now be stated as one cost-per-success inequality over visible compressed buckets, eliminating the separate fixed near-hit lower bound and Renyi query-moment assumptions.
- Added layer-cake distinct-storage certificates. The shared-DAG storage target can now be proved by an exact integrated tail bound over distinct canonical child prices, with an integrated tuple-tail witness form that avoids dyadic price-class discretization when the geometry permits it.
- Added the price-weighted witness-packing storage certificate. Each distinct canonical child may now pay its full recursive storage price to a randomized witness atom, reducing the storage proof to a bound on price-weighted witness congestion rather than dyadic or tail counts.
- Added the uniform tuple witness-packing specialization. For compressed nets, each child can pay storage to a uniform tuple from its own net; using pairs turns the storage theorem into a distinct-child pair-congestion bound weighted by \(\psi |A|^{\rho-1}\le\psi\).
- Added occurrence-discounted tuple storage congestion. Occurrence-based decoders can now certify shared-DAG storage by weighting each decoded occurrence by the reciprocal multiplicity of its canonical child before measuring pair congestion.
- Added the pair-profile occurrence-discounted storage certificate. The remaining shared-DAG storage theorem can now be stated as a finite entropy-energy inequality over separated pair profiles: profile size times reciprocal-multiplicity congestion must fit the storage budget.
- Added the multiplicity-layer pair-profile certificate. The quotient discount can now be pushed directly into the profile entropy-energy sum: occurrence layer \(2^\ell\) pays only \(2^{-\ell}\) times its undiscounted cap/list-decoding energy, making the remaining geometry a high-multiplicity-or-high-energy dichotomy.
- Added the aggregate multiplicity-profile storage certificate. The remaining storage theorem no longer needs a worst-pair envelope inside each pair profile; it is enough to prove the discounted aggregate entropy-energy sum over profile layers directly.
- Added the two-point Gaussian profile energy and aggregate Gaussian profile-layer storage test. Pair-profile energy is now explicit as \(\beta^2/(1+\theta)\) from shifted threshold margin and maximum pair correlation, so the remaining Gaussian storage inequality can be written as discounted weighted incidence entropy minus this concrete energy.
- Added the high-correlation pair-star reporter. If a Gaussian profile layer has large weighted incidence on genuinely high-correlation pairs, a dyadic layer-cake argument exposes a smaller-radius star superlevel carrying average incidence up to a logarithmic dynamic-range factor, giving the dense-cover separator a concrete proposal.
- Added the star-superlevel column verifier. A reported high-correlation star now becomes an ordinary finite filter-cover column only through a ball-thickened query activation and exact positive reduced gain; if all such stars fail, the paper records the explicit no-star residual inequality left for the diffuse separator.
- Added the star-prefix weak-type residual. A geometric reporter now only has to propose a certified smaller-radius center; the finite verifier can retain the best dual-paid prefix in that ball, and absence of profitable prefixes forces every reported star to satisfy a Renyi query/storage envelope.
- Added the certified star-panel separator. Reporters can now be analyzed with one-sided lower paid-mass and upper size certificates for a whole panel of high-correlation stars; any aggregate surplus over the star Renyi costs forces a positive dense prefix, while failure gives the exact paid residual inequality.
- Added the spherical-or-star-panel local cover criterion. The dense-side target is now concrete: every surplus dual that survives the spherical reduced-gain test must yield a certified paid pair-star panel, and then finite-cover duality forces local LP feasibility.
- Added the hit-weighted star-panel to paid-prefix bridge. The star reporter and the paid-prefix endpoint now share one reduced-cost certificate: certify lower mass against \(\gamma_{p,q}r_{p,q}\), upper list sizes, and fractional query weights; the paid-prefix verifier then thresholds to an ordinary Boolean top-prefix column.
- Added the guarded star-panel local-cover criterion. The reporter theorem is now only required on normalized residuals that survive spherical reduced gain: unit primitive hit mass, below-budget normalized price, large activation-weighted high-correlation guard mass, and no profitable primitive singleton slice.
- Added the guarded no-star residual envelope. If the guarded reporter fails, every hit-weighted degree superlevel in every certified star container satisfies the Renyi weak-type cap; the remaining geometric contradiction is now large guard co-load versus this no-star envelope.
- Added the guard-shadow transport reporter. A large guard co-load now closes the guarded star reporter whenever it can be transported with bounded congestion into hit-weighted near-shadow mass inside one affordable certified star container; otherwise the remaining obstruction is exactly failure of such bounded-congestion transport.
- Added the guard-shadow cut normal form. Bounded-congestion transport is now equivalent to explicit query-cut inequalities, and a no-panel residual with an affordable container must expose a half-mass cut whose guard load exceeds its paid near-shadow capacity.
- Added the guard-shadow credit normal form. The half-mass cut obstruction is exactly the minimum uncredited demand in a fractional bipartite flow from paid near-shadow capacity to guard-load demand, so the next dense-overlap target can be stated without all-subsets cuts: find an affordable smaller-radius container that credits at least half of the guard co-load.
- Added the fractional guard-credit panel reporter. Multiple overlapping certified dense containers can now jointly receive a guard-credit flow, with guard demand used at most once and recursive query/storage costs charged locally to each receiving container; positive aggregate verified surplus still yields an ordinary hit-weighted star panel.
- Added the finite guard-credit flow separator. Once each proposal records a credited-mass scale, the dense-overlap search becomes a finite LP over multiplicities and guard-credit flow variables; any positive LP objective feeds the joint guard-credit cover, while nonpositive value is the exact finite-pool obstruction for column generation.
- Added the dual obstruction to finite guard-credit flow. A failed finite proposal pool is now represented by demand prices, point-capacity prices, and credited-scale prices satisfying explicit edge and template inequalities; after positive-load queries are normalized, the obstruction is a zero-demand-price internal capacity certificate that a proposal oracle must separate.
- Added the one-template guard-credit separator. A new proposal invalidates the zero-objective dual exactly when its internal block infeasibility test fails; after zero-load queries are removed this is the scalar condition that reachable paid-shadow mass \(D_B\) satisfies \(D_B/(4J_B)>R_B\), giving a concrete target for k-means++, AQ, range-reporting, or list-decoding proposal search.
- Added the scale-free guard-credit proposal test. The credited-mass scale no longer has to be a geometric output: a raw container with paid-shadow mass \(D\) sets \(v=\min(D,K)\), or a dyadic scale just below it, and a finite proposal law succeeds as soon as its expected scale-normalized surplus \(D/(4J)-R\) is positive.
- Added the lower-certified scale-free guard panel. A reporter now only has to certify lower paid-shadow masses \(X_i\le D(W_i)\) and upper sizes \(K_i\); positive aggregate surplus \(\sum_i\lambda_i(X_i/(4J_i)-R_i)\) already contains a deterministic separating guard-credit template.
- Added the high-correlation star to guard-credit bridge. A pair-star superlevel now feeds the lower-certified guard panel whenever its reported star multiplicity is dominated by paid near-shadow degree; the remaining geometry can target incidence mass \(X=t|U_{x,t}|\) versus recursive price directly.
- Added the paid-responsibility high-correlation star compiler. If high-correlation star multiplicities are built as subassignments \(\eta_{x,p,q}y_q\gamma_{p,q}r_{p,q}\) of the paid near-shadow, domination by paid degree is automatic; enough total responsibility mass now yields a single separating guard-credit star by layer cake.
- Added the pair-compatible guard responsibility compiler. A high-correlation shipment of guard load into pairwise paid capacities with congestion \(C\) induces responsibility mass \(M/C\), so the old transport picture now feeds the scale-free guard-credit star separator directly.
- Added the pair-compatible guard cut normal form. The high-correlation shipment problem is now an ordinary finite max-flow: failure is exactly a guard-incidence sublayer whose neighboring paid pair capacities are too small, making the remaining geometric obstruction explicit.
- Added the geometric Hall form of pair-compatible guard transport. The max-flow cut is now written as a high-load guard sublayer \(G'(q)\) whose certified high-correlation paid neighborhood \(N_{\rm hc}(G')(q)\) has too little \(\gamma r\)-capacity; a failed half-shipment exposes such a sublayer with load \(>B_{\rm pc}/2\).
- Added the pair-compatible Hall guarded-cover criterion. The full chain is now assembled: a half-shipment satisfying the Hall expansion and scale-normalized layer-cake price inequality yields a lower-certified guard star, positive guard-credit LP, hit-weighted star panel, and local filter-cover feasibility.
- Added the fractional pair-compatible Hall obstruction. The all-sublayer guard-expansion condition is now equivalently a finite fractional LP with guard-incidence variables and paid-neighborhood closure variables; total unimodularity shows no integrality gap, so the dense-overlap frontier can be stated as separating this exact LP rather than as an informal scalar threshold rule.
- Added the exact-load pair-compatible Hall closure profile. Half-shipment failure is now equivalent to a one-parameter lower-envelope deficit \(S_{\rm pc}(\Lambda)<\Lambda-B_{\rm pc}/2\); the profile is the lower convex envelope of integral guard-sublayer load/capacity pairs, so any fractional bad layer is a mixture of at most two ordinary sublayers.
- Added the Hall-profile repair guarded-cover criterion. A guarded residual now closes if either the pair-compatible Hall profile gives a half-shipment, or a proposal oracle converts a lower-hull deficit into a lower-certified guard-credit repair panel on the loaded Hall queries; deficit evidence itself is not retained as a column.
- Added the proposal-law Hall-profile repair form. The lower-hull Hall deficit is now only build-time advice: the accepted certificate is a finite law over raw containers whose expected loaded-query paid-shadow surplus beats the recursive price, after which the existing guard-credit verifier retains ordinary filter columns.
- Added the lower-hull Hall proposal separator target. The current dense-overlap theorem is now explicit: every strict exact-load Hall deficit, priced by a declared pair congestion, must be converted by preprocessing into a positive loaded-query proposal law; the lower-hull optimizer is discarded after certification.
- Added the integral and deficit-restricted Hall proposal normal forms. Any strict exact-load Hall deficit contains one integral positive-excess guard sublayer, and a proposal law only needs to certify paid-shadow surplus inside that sublayer's high-correlation paid neighborhood to close the guarded residual.
- Added the deficient-shadow prefix certificate. The dense separator can now target one certified top prefix inside an affordable smaller-radius container: if its deficient-shadow mass beats the inherited recursive price after the layer-cake factor, the guarded residual closes.
- Added the no-prefix deficient-shadow envelope. If a finite prefix family fails to close a lower-hull Hall deficit, the remaining obstruction is exactly a positive-excess guard sublayer whose top deficient-shadow mass in every searched affordable container obeys the weak-type recursive-price bound.
- Added the tail deficient-shadow envelope. The no-prefix obstruction now also bounds every searched threshold superlevel \(U_{W,t}\), giving range-reporting, list-decoding, and additive-quantization proposal mechanisms a direct superlevel target to violate.
- Added the two-price deficient-shadow screen. Any failed threshold layer is now classified as query-price-limited or storage-price-limited; violating both scalar bounds is enough to close the guarded residual.
- Added the forbidden middle-scale form for deficient-shadow layers. For \(0<\rho_W<1\), a surviving threshold layer must be either below the query-price cardinality threshold or above the storage-price cardinality threshold, so the remaining geometry can target the excluded middle interval.
- Added the cardinality-net deficient-shadow reporter. An arbitrary reported deficient-shadow superlevel in the forbidden interval can be rounded down to a retained certified prefix, so dyadic cardinalities suffice up to constant slack.
- Strengthened the deficient-shadow residual obstruction with the cardinality-net contrapositive: under a dyadic-style retained prefix net, no surviving residual can contain an arbitrary threshold superlevel satisfying the constant-slack forbidden-interval screen.
- Added the layer-cake deficient-shadow reporter. A finite level panel now closes the residual whenever its captured deficient-shadow mass beats the aggregate Renyi query/storage moments on a threshold-height window, giving list-decoding and range-reporting proofs an averaged target.
- Added the integral deficient-shadow moment reporter. The same separator can now be certified by a truncated Lorentz moment of the deficient-shadow degree sequence, with the positive term written as an exact capped power sum over points.
- Added the dyadic-window deficient-shadow moment reporter. Moment surplus can now be summed over dyadic height windows, each paying its own local \(J\)-factor, and any positive total exposes one window that closes the residual.
- Added the dyadic-window deficient-shadow residual envelope. If no retained prefix closes, every capped deficient-shadow moment is bounded by the dyadic sum of Renyi query/storage moments with local \(J\)-charges; this is the explicit inequality the next geometric theorem must violate.
- Added the dyadic log-Lorentz deficient-shadow separator. The \(s=0\) moment specializes the residual target to capped linear deficient-shadow mass versus dyadic threshold cardinalities, giving a cleaner scalar range-reporting/list-decoding objective.
- Added the fractional log-Lorentz deficient-shadow panel. Proposal mechanisms may now be analyzed by positive expected verified surplus over containers and height windows, while the proof still retains one deterministic positive dense column.
- Added the certified log-Lorentz deficient-shadow panel. A reporter now only needs lower certificates for capped captured mass and upper certificates for dyadic superlevel ranks, so exact materialization of the deficient-shadow degree sequence is no longer part of the theorem target.
- Added the mass-support log-Lorentz certificate. A proposed container can now be certified by three coarse scalars--captured deficient-shadow mass, support size, and maximum degree--which choose a canonical interval capturing half the mass.
- Added the average-density mass-support screen. Dividing the coarse certificate by support size leaves a scale-free target: average deficient-shadow density must beat query-price and storage-price terms on opposite sides of the support scale.
- Added the balanced average-density screen. At the stationary support scale, the query/storage density screen collapses to a single threshold proportional to \(\psi_W\mathfrak J A_+^{\rho_W}\sigma^{1-\rho_W}\), up to balance constants.
- Added the balanced top-prefix density screen. When a reporter can materialize a top deficient-shadow prefix near the stationary support scale, the coarse \(\mathfrak J\) dynamic-range charge is replaced by the one-level \(J(\delta)\) charge, and oversized coarse reports can be trimmed to that scale.
- Added the fixed-threshold balanced density screen. The implicit condition \(\delta>\Xi J(\delta)\) is replaced by the noncircular reporter target \(\delta>\Xi J(\Xi)\), so density bucketing can use one fixed level per balanced proposal scale.
- Added the fractional fixed-threshold density panel. Randomized or batched balanced proposals can again be scored by aggregate expected surplus; any positive finite-panel surplus contains a deterministic retained dense column.
- Added sampled proposal-law separation. Positive-margin randomized proposal laws, including fixed-threshold density panels, can now be amplified with extra build-time sampling until a deterministic verified column is found; sampled failures are not retained or charged to query/storage.
- Added the fixed-threshold Hall-deficit oracle. The lower-hull Hall repair law is now replaceable by a positive fixed-threshold balanced density panel on the one exposed deficient Hall shadow, with sampled build-time amplification as an optional separator implementation.
- Added the fixed-threshold Hall-deficit endpoint. Nodewise fixed-threshold Hall-deficit separation now directly implies the fractional filter-cover polylog endpoint, so the remaining geometric theorem is stated in the same language as the current dense reporter target.
- Added the single-record fixed-threshold Hall separator. The remaining deterministic dense theorem no longer needs a panel: one balanced top prefix or trimmable coarse record above the fixed density threshold repairs the exposed Hall deficit.
- Clarified the compressed-bucket tensor route: the worst-case storage target is a low-correlation tuple entropy-energy bound plus a high-correlation dense-star separator, not a false uniform occurrence-moment theorem.
- Added the correlation-split Gaussian profile layer. A profile layer can now be discharged by a low-correlation Gaussian entropy-energy envelope plus a star-scale threshold for high-correlation mass; exceeding the threshold exposes a smaller-radius dense-star proposal.
- Clarified the fully fractional dense-overlap interpretation: proposal mechanisms should be analyzed by reduced gain \(B_A-R_A\) against the filter-cover dual, not by mass-proportional or \(k\)-means++ sampling unless those samplers approximate the separator.
- Added the fixed-threshold star screen for correlation-split layers. High-correlation profile incidence above \(2|P|J_{\rm hi}\Lambda_0\) now forces a one-record guard-credit star whenever \(\Lambda_0\) beats the recursive price after the layer-cake factor; otherwise the high-correlation tail is explicitly chargeable.
- Added the aggregate fixed-tail correlation-split storage certificate. If no profile layer fires its fixed-threshold star repair, the storage compiler may use the discounted sum of low-correlation Gaussian envelopes plus explicit \(2|P|J_i^{\rm hi}\Lambda_i\) high-correlation tails.
- Added the noncircular fixed-tail scale choice. Setting \(\Lambda_i^\circ=8R_iL_i\), with \(L_i\) evaluated at \(8R_i\), automatically beats the layer-cake price test and turns the aggregate high-correlation tail into \(16|P|J_i^{\rm hi}R_iL_i\).
- Added the tuple-profile compressed-bucket storage certificate. A first list-moment bound and a two-point Gaussian tuple-profile energy bound now directly imply the occurrence \(1+\rho\) storage moment, and hence the weaker shared-DAG distinct-net storage target \((CB2)\).
- Added the finite dense/spherical minimax dichotomy. Local feasibility now follows from the exact primal-dual statement: once all dense recursive columns have nonpositive reduced gain, every surplus dual must be separated by a spherical column.
- Added quotient-packing and Pareto dense-core separator endpoints. The local quotient/core-envelope separators now feed directly into the fractional filter-cover polylog induction, so the remaining geometric theorem can be stated nodewise without another recursion argument.
- Added the annular dominated-load separator endpoint. The strongest current recycled-annular/cohort-prefix local separator now feeds directly into the global fractional filter-cover polylog induction, and the finite prefix-template game is recorded as a sufficient nodewise certificate.
- Added the exact top-prefix cohort game. When exact cohort-degree top prefixes are materializable, the dominated-load separator can target the sharp \(A\Lambda\) threshold directly; logarithmic loss is only needed for weaker superlevel, mass, or soft-prefix reporters.
- Added exact-prefix column generation. A finite implicit universe of materializable exact-prefix records can now be searched by arbitrary preprocessing; if its exact game value is nonnegative, ordinary column generation finds a finite verified pool closing \((DPG3)\) at the sharp \(A\Lambda\) threshold, while only the final pool is charged to the index.
- Added the sublist-closed exact-prefix separator. For retained containers closed under \(K\)-sublists, the exact-prefix universe reduces to the scalar build-time objective \(D_\eta^\theta(W,K)-A\Lambda\); the separator materializes only the maximizing subset it returns, preserving the sharp no-log threshold.
- Added sharp exact-prefix proposal-law separators. Randomized or soft build-time panels now only need positive expected certified surplus against \(D_\eta^\theta(W,K)-A\Lambda\) or a one-sided lower bound \(X-A\Lambda\); Las Vegas preprocessing samples until one verified top-prefix column appears, and no logarithmic reporter loss is introduced.
- Added the no-loss exact cardinality net. On the exact-prefix route, monotonicity of \(D_\eta^\theta(W,K)\) means dyadic or factor-\(b\) retained cardinalities replace exact \(K\) without changing the sharp \(A\Lambda\) threshold; the logarithmic cardinality loss is only for weaker reporters.
- Added the exact-template linearization caveat. The payoff \(D_\eta^\theta(W,K)-A\Lambda\) is convex in the cohort law because of the top-\(K\) maximization, so minimax arguments remain over materialized exact records; template objectives are fixed-\(\eta\) separation oracles only.
- Added the average-mass exact-prefix screen. A coarse report certifying mass \(M\) on a support of size \(s\) now feeds the sharp exact-prefix separator whenever \(KM/s\ge A\Lambda\), giving k-means/AQ/list-recovery-style container reports a no-log route to one verified top-\(K\) column.
- Added the average-density exact-prefix scale choice. A coarse support-mass report with \(M\ge A\Lambda\) now chooses \(h_*=\lceil A\Lambda s/M\rceil\) and rounds up to the retained cardinality net, so container proposal mechanisms need not guess \(K\) to feed the sharp exact-prefix separator.
- Added the soft average-density exact-prefix scale choice. Responsibility reports now only need a soft mass \(\mu=\sum_p\pi_p\) and one-sided value \(X\ge A\Lambda\); the verifier chooses \(h_*=\lceil A\Lambda\mu/X\rceil\), rounds to the retained cardinality net, and stores one hard top-prefix column.
- Added the soft exact-prefix tightness corollary. If the exact-prefix route fails for one cohort law, every admissible responsibility vector in every searched container has value below \(A\Lambda\), so AQ/learned/k-means-style soft assignments are proposal languages rather than a weaker relaxation.
- Added the finite soft exact-prefix oracle and column generation. Soft responsibility panels are now scored at the sharp \(A\Lambda\) threshold, but successful panels immediately compile into verified hard exact-prefix records; failed soft proposals are discarded and never charged to the retained index.
- Added one-sided exact-prefix value column generation. Approximate range-reporting, AQ, list-decoding, or seeded-clustering reporters can now stay on the sharp no-log route by certifying a lower value \(X\le V_\eta(T)\) for one hard record with \(X\ge A\Lambda\); later games still score the retained record exactly.
- Added the finite-signature compressed-bucket bridge. The old success-normalized compressed-bucket recursion is now explicitly a local exact-signature filter-cover certificate: useful hit mass, active-load overlap, and distinct canonical storage are all normalized by the same denominator, and the global polylog endpoint follows from the fractional cover induction rather than a separate hard-branching argument.
- Added the finite-signature rate-coverage endpoint. The posterior/facility route now has a fixed-index theorem: once finite tilted-miss envelopes, scan-load envelopes, storage intensity, and the Poisson realization parameter are polylogarithmic, the retained bucket index feeds directly into the smoothed witness-list compiler and gives the \(d n^\rho\operatorname{polylog} n\) bound.
- Added one-sided reduced-gain column generation for rate coverage. Build-time proposal mechanisms may now return certified lower bounds on a point score or whole-label gain; exact verification keeps positive columns and discards failed proposals, while a no-positive answer must certify the universal no-gain inequality.
- Added the one-sided finite-envelope rate-coverage endpoint. The actual finite signature envelopes \(U,V\) now have explicit reduced-price and residual-value formulas; a proposal oracle that certifies one positive envelope score can be composed through column generation, retained Poisson buckets, and the smoothed witness-list compiler to the polylog ANN endpoint.
- Strengthened the finite-envelope endpoint to accept whole-label gain certificates. A facility, code, or lattice proposal can now certify aggregate reduced gain for one bucket label; exact point-score evaluation then materializes all positive point-label columns.
- Added the one-sided finite-signature filter-cover endpoint. The fully fractional dense-overlap route now has a single endpoint-level separator contract: every restricted dual obstruction must either expose one verified positive reduced-gain column or certify universal no-gain; the finite LP and Poisson materialization then give the polylog ANN bound.
- Added the positive finite-signature separator endpoint. The full LP feasibility assumption is now replaceable by the nodewise theorem that every surplus finite-signature dual has one positive reduced-gain column in the unified filter universe.
- Added the dense/spherical separator endpoint composition. If structured overlapping columns have nonpositive reduced gain, it is enough to prove one positive spherical separator; otherwise a structured column is already the column-generation witness.
- Added the proposal-law finite-signature endpoint. A randomized build-time sampler such as facility seeding, AQ, range-reporting proposals, or list/lattice decoding now only needs positive expected reduced gain against the current dual; verified positive samples are retained and failed samples are discarded.
- Added the finite-signature spherical reduced-gain test. The spherical half of the new endpoint now has an explicit signature-level obstruction: absent a positive spherical column, every surplus dual must put large query price on certified high-correlation bucket co-load.
- Added the high-correlation co-load separator endpoint. After the finite-signature spherical calculation, the structured dense theorem only has to separate duals whose certified high-correlation co-load exceeds \(\pi_1A_\alpha/2\); lower-co-load residuals are already spherical.
- Added the finite-signature star-panel co-load endpoint. A high-correlation residual is now discharged by any certified star panel whose lower paid mass beats the Renyi query/storage prefix costs; the retained column is the exact top-degree prefix chosen by the verifier.
- Added the finite-signature responsibility star endpoint. Soft responsibilities from facility, AQ, or list/lattice proposal mechanisms now compile into valid star-panel lower certificates by layer-cake, while the verifier still retains only an exact top-degree prefix column.
- Added the finite-signature co-load transport endpoint. The remaining dense geometry can now be stated as bounded-loss transport from spherical high-correlation co-load atoms to paid responsibility mass inside certified smaller-radius templates.
- Added the finite-signature Hall transport endpoint. A packed co-load shipment has an exact max-flow/min-cut certificate; failure leaves one explicit high-correlation co-load cut whose paid neighborhood is too small by the transport-loss factor.
- Added aggregate finite-signature responsibility and co-load transport endpoints. Responsibility mass may be spread over many templates without a \(|\mathcal X|\) loss, as long as the aggregate Renyi price of the dyadic responsibility panel is below half the transported mass.
- Added the finite-signature Hall-cut repair endpoint. Failed Hall cuts are now explicitly build-time advice only: the retained proof must be either an aggregate transported responsibility panel or a fractional star panel with positive certified surplus.
- Added finite-signature Hall-shadow repair and one-record merge criteria. A failed Hall cut now defines a concrete paid-shadow degree \(d_{A,y}\); any affordable template prefix with enough of this shadow certifies the dense merge column.
- Added the proposal-law Hall-shadow merge criterion. Expensive build-time samplers such as seeded clustering, AQ, range reporting, or list decoding now only need positive expected top-prefix surplus against the Hall-shadow degree; successful samples verify into one retained dense column.
- Added the no-merge Hall-shadow envelope. If every available Hall-shadow prefix fails, the failed cut satisfies an explicit top-prefix envelope and weak-type superlevel bounds, giving the exact residual a geometric separator must rule out.
- Added the balanced Hall-shadow density screen. Near the stationary prefix size \(K^\circ=(1-\rho)A/(\rho\zeta)\), a Hall-shadow prefix closes as soon as its density beats \(\psi C_\rho A^\rho\zeta^{1-\rho}\) up to the balance factor.
- Added fractional and sampled balanced Hall-shadow density panels. Batched proposal mechanisms are now scored by the scalar surplus \(K(\Delta-\Theta)\), and any positive verified surplus collapses to one retained Hall-shadow prefix.
- Added the balanced Hall-shadow separator endpoint. Hall-success or positive balanced Hall-shadow surplus at every high-correlation residual now directly implies the finite-signature dense/spherical separator and the polylogarithmic ANN endpoint.
- Added the clipped Hall-shadow price-density screen and endpoint. The repair scale is now the clipped minimizer of \(A K^{\rho-1}+\zeta K^\rho\), so the same fractional surplus test covers query-dominated, storage-dominated, and internally balanced templates.
- Added the maximal Hall-cut marginal normal form and support-mass clipped reporter. Failed Hall cuts may now be assumed marginally closed, and coarse range/list/AQ/facility reports with only support size plus one-sided shadow mass still verify into exact clipped top-prefix repairs.
- Added the soft-mass clipped Hall-shadow reporter. Fractional facility/AQ/list-decoder memberships now feed the same clipped surplus test by a top-\(K\) rearrangement inequality, leaving exact hard prefixes only to the verifier.
- Added the soft-mass Hall-shadow separator endpoint. The current dense-side target now accepts Hall-success or a positive soft-membership clipped surplus directly, composing through the verifier to the polylogarithmic ANN endpoint.
- Added the exact-scale soft Hall-shadow reporter and endpoint. The verifier may now optimize over prefix cardinalities and charge the exact recursive price, so clipped/balanced scales are proposal screens rather than endpoint restrictions.
- Added shadow-routing exact-scale soft reporting. Any fractional routing of paid Hall-shadow atoms into certified templates now becomes a verified soft panel, and failure even for cut-center routings leaves the explicit cut-center obstruction \((CCO2)\).
- Added the thresholded shadow-routing screen. Routed mass supported on \(d_{A,y}\ge\delta\) now contributes the simple benefit \(\min\{X,K\delta\}\), making range-reporting/list-decoding proposal certificates closer to the final Hall-shadow target.
- Added layered thresholded shadow routing and its endpoint. Paid shadow can now be split fractionally across many superlevel layers of overlapping templates, each scored by \(\min\{X,K\delta\}\) minus exact recursive price; the cardinality-optimized split identifies the local benefit-per-cost test below or above \(X/\delta\), and positive layered surplus directly implies the finite-signature polylog endpoint.
- Added the thresholded routing residual envelope. If no thresholded route fires, every degree-superlevel whose density beats recursive price must lie above its routed-mass scale \(X/\delta\) and have total routed mass bounded by that recursive price, giving range-reporting/list-decoding proposals an exact inequality to violate.
- Added the cumulative superlevel Hall-shadow reporter. For a proposed template \(y\), a one-sided lower bound on \(M_A(y,\delta)=\sum_{d_{A,y}(p)\ge\delta}d_{A,y}(p)\) now directly verifies a thresholded route; if every such report fails, the remaining obstruction is the weak-type envelope \(\min\{M_A(y,\delta),K\delta\}\) bounded by the exact recursive price.
- Added cumulative price-density and clipped cumulative Hall-shadow screens. A superlevel report now closes whenever both its height \(\delta\) and average cumulative mass \(M_A(y,\delta)/K\) beat the recursive price density, and the clipped version reduces the search to the canonical scale \(K_y^\natural\).
- Added the tail-mass cumulative Hall-shadow screen. At the clipped scale, threshold search can be removed entirely: a one-sided lower bound on strict tail mass \(M_A^+(y,\Theta_y)\) above the price-density level closes whenever it exceeds \(K\Theta_y\); otherwise every surviving residual satisfies that tail bound.
- Added support-limited tail and cut-center tail obstructions. If paid shadow assigned to a template is supported on at most \(K\) points and exceeds \(2K\Theta_y\), the tail-mass screen must fire; hence any full assignment to cut centers that survives all such screens carries at most \(2K_x\Theta_x\) mass through each support-\(K_x\) center.
- Added support-sensitive tail and cut-center accounting. Without assuming small support, any surviving assigned shadow submeasure has mass at most \((K+s)\Theta_y\), so the only remaining cut-center escape is an explicit large-support term \(s_x\Theta_x\).
- Added the support-adaptive exact tail screen and cut-center price obstruction. Choosing a retained cardinality comparable to the assigned support size turns the surviving mass bound into the recursive price scale \(\psi(A s^\rho+\zeta s^{1+\rho})\), absorbing the previous large-support escape up to cardinality-net constants.
- Added the support-price assignment separator and endpoint. A failed Hall cut can now be reduced to minimizing the recursive support price \(\Phi_b(r)\) over full fractional routings of its paid shadow; if the shadow mass beats that price, a support-adaptive Hall-shadow merge follows and the dense/spherical separator endpoint applies.
- Added the independent support-law assignment certificate. Pointwise independent routings are now analyzable through marginal support counts \(\mu_x\), using the self-contained bounds \(\mathbb E S^\rho\le\mu^\rho\) and \(\mathbb E S^{1+\rho}\le\mu+\mu^{1+\rho}\); this gives a cleaner target for randomized facility, AQ, or list-decoding proposal laws.
- Added the atom-marginal support-law certificate. The marginal endpoint now also accepts local stochastic atom-routing weights \(z_{x,p,\sigma}\), bounding each point-center support probability by \(\min(1,\sum_\sigma z_{x,p,\sigma})\), so proposal mechanisms can certify a routing without materializing a distribution over pointwise set covers.
- Added the partial support-price and partial atom-marginal certificates. The Hall-shadow repair target no longer has to route all paid atoms: any routed submeasure with paid mass \(X_z\) exceeding its recursive support price \((b+1)\overline\Phi_b(z)\) already verifies into a dense merge, which restores the intended benefit-per-cost separation.
- Added the centerwise partial atom screen. Positive partial atom-routing surplus is separable by cut center, so the remaining dense theorem can search for one center with \(X_x>(b+1)\Pi_x\) instead of coordinating a multi-center routing.
- Added centerwise atom hardening. Any one-center atom-routing surplus now hardens to an ordinary top-\(K\) Hall-shadow prefix at a comparable cardinality, so atom weights remain only a proposal language and the retained dense column is again the standard certified prefix.
- Added the centerwise atom no-relaxation obstruction. If all retained top prefixes fail, then every centerwise atom certificate and every partial atom-marginal routing certificate fails as well; the dense-side residual is exactly the prefix no-merge envelope.
- Added the maximal no-prefix Hall-cut normal form. A failed Hall transport can now be replaced by an inclusion-maximal surplus cut; either its top-prefix shadow degree verifies a one-record dense merge, or it satisfies both Hall marginal closure and the top-prefix weak-type no-merge envelope.
- Added the Hall-shadow/finite-prefix bridge.
