A failed Hall cut induces a paid-shadow demand system on signatures, and every Hall-shadow top-prefix test is exactly a finite-prefix reduced-cost test with the template activation as the query layer. This lets future range-reporting, list-decoding, or additive-code proposals target the generic finite-prefix oracle directly.
- Updated the audit trail to make the dense-side status explicit: the surviving obstruction is now an inclusion-maximal Hall cut plus the finite-prefix no-merge envelope, not a separate clustering, routing, or atom-relaxation problem.
- Added the maximal Hall frontier expansion consequence. Once the failed cut is inclusion-maximal, every co-load atom outside the cut must expose enough novel paid shadow outside \(N(A^\star)\); otherwise it would enlarge the Hall surplus. Thus the residual splits into a closed-shadow no-prefix envelope and an expanding outside frontier.
- Updated the audit trail to mention the frontier expansion split explicitly, so the current dense-side target is recorded as closed-shadow finite-prefix failure plus outside paid-shadow expansion.
- Added the outside-transport consequence of maximality. After deleting the closed cut and its shadow, the remaining co-load atoms satisfy Hall against the novel paid shadow, so the transport failure is fully localized to the closed cut.
- Updated the audit trail to strengthen the frontier split: the outside frontier is already packable by Hall, so the remaining dense repair theorem only has to eliminate the closed-shadow finite-prefix obstruction.
- Added the closed-shadow query-layer relaxation. Fractional signature-side query weights are now only a proposal language: any positive fractional closed-shadow prefix objective convexifies to a certified Boolean query layer, so the retained repair remains an ordinary Hall-shadow prefix.
- Updated the audit trail to record the same query-layer relaxation in the dense-side status summary.
- Added the closed-shadow soft proposal hardening step. A proposal may now certify only fractional signature weights, soft point memberships, and a normalized lower mass; the verifier hardens any positive certificate to a Boolean signature layer and top-\(K\) Hall-shadow prefix.
- Updated the audit trail to mention the closed-shadow soft proposal hardening in the dense-side status summary.
- Added the closed-shadow soft-proposal separator endpoint. After maximal Hall-cut localization, one positive certified soft proposal for the closed shadow now directly implies the finite-signature Hall repair alternative and hence the dense/spherical polylog endpoint; a sampled Las Vegas variant only charges successful verified proposals.
- Updated the audit trail to make the closed-shadow soft-proposal endpoint the compact current dense-side target, with the older exact-scale, thresholded, clipped, and balanced endpoints recorded as sufficient forms.
- Added the closed-shadow soft-proposal residual envelope. If no certified soft proposal fires after maximal Hall localization, every fractional signature layer and soft update membership in the proposal universe satisfies the normalized price envelope \((CSSR1)\), so future facility/AQ/list-decoding/k-means-style proposal mechanisms have an exact residual inequality to violate.
- Updated the audit trail to identify this residual envelope as the current compact dense-side obstruction, rather than only recording the positive-proposal endpoint.
- Added the closed-shadow fractional superlevel envelope. Choosing the soft membership vector to be a paid-degree superlevel converts \((CSSR1)\) into the weak-type bound \(\min\{M,K\delta\}\le R_C(y,K)\), and a \(b\)-dense cardinality net yields the cumulative mass envelope \((CSW6)\)--\((CSW7)\) for every fractional signature layer.
- Updated the audit trail to state the remaining closed-shadow obstruction in this searchable superlevel form.
- Added the closed-shadow dyadic layer-cake separator. A build-time reporter can now certify the dyadic superlevel profile of one container/layer; if total paid degree exceeds twice the aggregate recursive price of those layers, the verifier extracts one positive superlevel repair.
- Updated the audit trail to record this finite reporter form as the next closed-shadow target.
- Added the reported closed-shadow layer-cake separator. The reporter now needs only one-sided certificates: a lower bound on total paid degree and upper bounds on dyadic superlevel sizes; positive certified surplus compiles through the exact verifier, with a Las Vegas sampled form.
- Updated the audit trail to make this one-sided reported certificate the build-facing closed-shadow target.
- Added the reported closed-shadow residual dichotomy. For any finite certified report universe, either one report has positive certified surplus and repairs the cut, or every report satisfies the scalar reverse inequality \((CRR3)\); ruling out that residual is now the compact geometric reporter theorem.
- Updated the audit trail to name \((CRR3)\) as the finite reported closed-shadow obstruction.
- Added the routed-support closed-shadow report bridge. A support-price, atom-routing, list-decoding, or facility report that certifies a routed support and lower paid mass now compiles into the reported layer-cake interface; if only support size is reported, the loss is the dyadic dynamic-range factor.
- Updated the audit trail to fold routed-support mechanisms into the current reported closed-shadow obstruction language.
- Added the aggregate routed-support closed-shadow panel. Multi-center support-price or atom-routing reports now need only aggregate routed paid mass beating the sum of support prices; the verifier extracts one positive reported layer-cake repair.
- Updated the audit trail to include this aggregate routed-support panel as the support-price bridge to the current \((CRR3)\) obstruction.
- Added the reported support-price routing residual. Any partial routing whose paid mass beats its reported support price now repairs the cut; if no report fires, every partial routing satisfies \((RPR3)\), and every full routing gives the scalar obstruction \((RPR4)\).
- Updated the audit trail to record \((RPR4)\) as the support-price optimization form of the closed-shadow residual.
- Added the reported marginal support-law certificate. A stochastic proposal law now only has to beat the marginal reported support price \(\widehat\Psi_{\rm rep}\); independent rounding over data points hardens positive expected surplus into one deterministic reported routing, so facility/AQ/list-decoding/lattice-style proposal mechanisms can be analyzed by support probabilities rather than explicit hard clusters.
- Updated the audit trail to record this marginal-law bridge as the current build-facing route into the reported support-price residual.
- Added the certified one-slot marginal support search. Positive aggregate marginal surplus now separates to one reported support slot, and a randomized build-time proposal only has to sample one slot with positive conservative benefit-per-cost surplus before the verifier materializes an ordinary deterministic report.
- Updated the audit trail to record the one-slot screen as the cleanest current target for k-means++/AQ/list-decoding/lattice-style dense proposals.
- Added the point-coupled one-slot support law. Soft cluster memberships can now couple all routed atoms of one data point to a single Bernoulli support event, so the reported support price uses point support mass \(\sum_p q_p\) rather than the atom-union upper bound.
- Updated the audit trail to identify the point-coupled form as the sharper soft-cluster interface for dense proposals.
- Added reported soft-support prefix hardening. Any soft dense proposal that beats the exact cardinality price now materializes as one deterministic top-\(K\) reported support, with a simple coarse scale test when all cardinalities are available.
- Updated the audit trail to record that the retained object is again an exact top-prefix support, not a stochastic or fractional support.
- Added the soft-support price envelope and panel screen. A finite or sampled soft proposal panel now closes when aggregate certified mass beats the scalar support-mass price \(\mathsf P_i^{\rm soft}\); the verifier then extracts one deterministic top-prefix report.
- Updated the audit trail to make \((RSP3)\)--\((RSP5)\) the build-facing scalar target for soft dense proposals.
- Added the soft-support no-relaxation residual. Failure of the scalar soft screen is now exactly recorded as the ordinary top-prefix envelope \((RSN2)\); every positive soft or sampled panel must contain one deterministic prefix violation.
- Updated the audit trail to identify \((RSN2)\) as the current residual form behind the soft-support proposal interface.
- Added the exact-prefix support-mass no-layer-cake screen. When the verifier can materialize exact top-prefix columns, hard and soft support-mass proposals close at the finite-prefix price without the reported \(2bJ\) layer-cake toll.
- Updated the audit trail to distinguish the sharper exact-prefix route from the support-size reported layer-cake route.
- Added the aggregate exact-prefix support-mass panel. Finite or sampled panels of hard and soft support reports now close when aggregate certified mass beats aggregate exact-prefix price, and the verifier extracts one deterministic top-prefix repair.
- Updated the audit trail to make the exact-prefix panel price the build-facing no-layer-cake dense proposal target.
- Added the optimized exact-prefix support price. Hard support reports now pay \(s\inf_K\psi(AK^{\rho-1}+\zeta K^\rho)\), and soft reports pay the analogous \(\inf_K\max\{\mu,K\}\psi(AK^{\rho-1}+\zeta K^\rho)\), letting the verifier choose the recursive scale after seeing the proposal.
- Updated the audit trail to make the optimized price-density expression the sharpest current no-layer-cake dense proposal target.
- Added the clipped optimized exact-prefix price. The discrete optimized support price is now upper-bounded by the closed clipped threshold \(\Theta_i(m)=\psi_i f_i(K_i^\natural(m))\) up to the cardinality-net factor, so support reports can target one canonical scale.
- Updated the audit trail to record the clipped threshold as the current scalar no-layer-cake dense proposal screen.
- Added the clipped exact-prefix density residual. If no exact-prefix dense repair exists, every hard or soft support proposal has average paid density at most \(\beta_i\Theta_i\) at its clipped scale; any violation materializes one deterministic top-prefix repair.
- Updated the audit trail to make this density envelope the current residual inequality for no-layer-cake dense proposals.
- Added the benefit-per-price proposal normal form. Fractional dense-filter proposal laws now have an exact size-biased reduced-gain interpretation: after biasing by certified recursive price, useful proposals are precisely those with mean dual benefit per price above one, clarifying why mass-only \(k\)-means++ seeding is only a heuristic unless it approximates the dual-priced separator.
- Updated the audit trail to connect this benefit-per-price criterion to the fully fractional dense-overlap LP.
- Added the centerwise clipped exact-prefix proposal law. Hard supports and soft memberships from facility, AQ, list/lattice decoding, or \(k\)-means++-style build-time routines now close the no-layer-cake dense screen whenever their expected clipped surplus is positive; the verifier still retains only one deterministic exact top prefix.
- Updated the audit trail to identify this as the centerwise/facility interface for the clipped exact-prefix residual.
- Added the shared-canonical panel reduced-gain separator. A decoded bucket/list/AQ/facility panel can now be scored directly against the normalized exact-signature dual, with query load summed over records but canonical child storage charged once per retained panel.
- Updated the audit trail to identify this as the dual-facing, fully fractional bucket analogue of the dense benefit-per-price score.
- Added one-sided shared-panel column generation. Restricted profile covers can now be grown by verified positive-gain decoded panels; a no-positive certificate is sound only when it rules out the full finite panel universe.
- Updated the compressed-bucket audit to identify the shared-panel oracle as the build-time search procedure behind the success-normalized bucket route.
- Added the shared-panel positive-separator endpoint. A nodewise theorem that every normalized exact-signature dual has positive shared-panel gain now directly implies the exact-signature covers needed for the polylogarithmic fractional-filter induction.
- Updated the audit trail to name this as the endpoint-level compressed-bucket theorem target.
- Added the weighted shared-panel Renyi separator. Positive shared-panel gain is now expanded as priced useful hit mass beating active Renyi query load plus shared canonical child storage, with a first-moment query-load sufficient form.
- Updated the compressed-bucket audit to identify this as the concrete weighted list-decoding theorem target.
- Added the certified-storage weighted panel separator. The weighted shared-panel inequality can now use any certified upper envelope for shared child storage, letting tuple-profile, dyadic-tail, or multiplicity-discounted storage analyses feed the same endpoint.
- Updated the audit trail to record this as the storage-envelope interface for the compressed-bucket/list-decoding route.
- Added the certified-storage weighted residual envelope. Failure of the finite panel separator is now exactly a normalized dual under which every seed law has useful hit bounded by active load plus certified storage envelope.
- Updated the audit trail to make this residual inequality the current counterexample format for the weighted shared-panel route.
- Added the query-storage split for weighted residuals. A failed separator now has an explicit dichotomy for every proposed seed law: either active first-moment/Renyi load is already comparable to useful priced hit, or the certified shared-storage envelope carries the missing mass. This makes the fully fractional dense-overlap target a pair of ratios \(Q/A\) and \(W/A\), rather than a branching-count problem.
- Added the dual-priced witness-storage separator. Distinct canonical child storage can now be certified by distributing each child's storage price over witnesses and bounding dual-priced witness congestion; combined with an active-load gap, this immediately gives the certified-storage panel separator.
- Added the witness-law shared-panel endpoint. The compact nodewise theorem is now: for every normalized exact-signature dual, produce a finite panel law with useful hit \(A\), active load \(Q\), and dual-priced witness storage \(W\) satisfying \(Q/A+W/A<1\). This implies the shared-panel positive separator and the full polylogarithmic ANN endpoint.
- Added the witness-law residual obstruction. For any fixed certified report class, failure of the witness-law endpoint is now exactly a normalized residual where every report satisfies \(A\le Q+W\); therefore any query-efficient report in a surviving residual must pay comparable dual-priced witness storage.
- Added sampled witness-report separation. A build-time sampler for list/range/AQ/facility reports only needs positive expected verified surplus \(A-Q-W\); with bounded positive surplus, uncharged repetition finds one positive report and reduces it to an ordinary positive shared-panel column.
- Added witness-report panel hardening. Batched list/range/AQ/facility proposal output can be analyzed by aggregate surplus over a finite weighted panel of certified reports; any positive aggregate surplus contains one ordinary positive witness report.
- Added the benefit-allocated witness-density screen. Inside one witness report, net useful benefit can be allocated over tuple/profile/range/list classes; if aggregate allocated benefit beats certified witness congestion, one class has positive benefit-minus-congestion surplus and the report separates the residual.
- Added the class-local witness-density screen. When a tuple profile, range cell, or decoder output type can be materialized as an admissible witness report, the surviving residual must satisfy the entropy-density envelope \(B_\ell\le N_\ell e^{-I_\ell}\); a violation is an ordinary positive shared-panel column.
- Added the Gaussian tuple-profile witness-density residual. For a materialized low-correlation pair profile, the surviving residual must satisfy \(B_\pi\le M_\pi\exp(-\beta_\pi^2/(1+\theta_\pi))\), with the summed-margin variant \(s_\pi^2/(4(1+\theta_\pi))\); violations are positive shared-panel columns.
- Added the correlation-split witness-profile residual. A split profile now has one fractional accounting inequality: useful benefit is bounded by the low-correlation Gaussian congestion, an explicit unreported high-correlation tail, and the paid recursive price of reported high-correlation star prefixes. This records the dense-overlap principle at the witness-law level: high-correlation overlap is either verified into one positive dense prefix or charged as panel price, never as a branching multiplier.
- Added split-profile panel hardening. A finite weighted panel of low/high correlation profile reports with positive aggregate surplus now hardens to either one low-correlation witness-density violation or one paid high-correlation dense-prefix violation. This is the proof-facing interface for build-time list/range/facility/AQ/lattice proposal oracles: analyze aggregate surplus, retain one deterministic column.
- Added the min-cost split-profile envelope. The chosen high-correlation routing is now optimized out: each profile pays the infimum over certified star routings plus explicit tail allowance, and a surviving residual must obey the aggregate scalar envelope \(B_\pi\le C_\pi^{\rm lo}+\Phi_\pi^{\rm hi}\). This makes the remaining dense theorem a fractional min-cost routing problem rather than a selected-decomposition argument.
- Added the min-cost split-profile separator endpoint. If every normalized exact-signature dual has a finite split-profile panel violating the optimized scalar envelope, and the produced low records or dense prefixes live in the certified shared-panel universe, the positive shared-panel separator holds at every node and the \(n^{\rho_c}\operatorname{polylog}n\) endpoint follows. The remaining theorem is now a direct fractional geometric separator over \(B_\pi,C_\pi^{\rm lo},\Phi_\pi^{\rm hi}\).
- Added the atomized high-correlation routing dual. A concrete \(\Phi_\pi^{\rm hi}\) can now be computed by a finite atom-tail/star-record covering LP, whose dual is a weighted no-star condition \(0\le y_a\le1\) with every certified star's weighted atom mass below its recursive price. If this atomized routing costs less than raw high-correlation mass, a paid dense prefix is forced; if no prefix fires, the all-ones dual is feasible and the high-correlation mass receives no discount.
- Added the layer-cake atom-cut normal form. Any fractional no-star dual for the atomized high-correlation routing LP thresholds to a dyadic atom cut whose scaled benefit captures a logarithmic fraction of the dual value while every certified star still sees scaled atom mass below its recursive price. This turns the high-correlation residual into a single cut-style geometric obstruction.
- Added the high-correlation atom-cut star screen. A threshold atom cut represented by high-correlation pair incidences now feeds the pair-star reporter directly: if scaled cut benefit exceeds \(2|P|J_{\rm cut}P_0\), one certified smaller-radius dense prefix must be positive; otherwise the cut is charged by that explicit fixed tail.
- Added the atomized high-correlation fixed-tail bound and explicit split-profile tail separator. Truncating any atomized no-star dual at level \(\varepsilon\), applying the layer-cake cut, and screening the cut by pair-stars gives \(\Phi_\pi^{\rm hi}\le \varepsilon B_\pi^{\rm hi}+4J_\varepsilon\mathfrak P_\star\) unless a dense prefix already fires, where \(\mathfrak P_\star\) is the aggregate recursive price of the reported stars. The older \(|P|J_\star P_0\) term is only the uniform-price specialization.
- Added the aggregate star-price cut screen behind that bound. A high-correlation atom cut surviving all star-prefix tests pays \(2\sum_{x,s}P_{x,s}\), so dense proposal mechanisms can be analyzed by the total recursive price of the centers/levels they actually report rather than a worst-case center count.
- Added the reported-center atom-cut screen. High-correlation cut mass assigned to unreported centers is now an explicit tail \(E_C(t)\), while the reported centers pay \(2\sum_{x\in C,s}P_{x,s}\); the fixed-tail bound and explicit split-profile separator now use \(\varepsilon B^{\rm hi}+2J_\varepsilon\mathfrak E_\star+4J_\varepsilon\mathfrak P_\star\). This is the direct proof interface for expensive build-time center proposals such as k-means++, facility search, AQ, or list/lattice decoders.
- Added the reported-center benefit-price screen. For any fixed atom cut, a center proposal that captures scaled mass \(M_C(t)\) larger than twice its aggregate reported star price \(P_C\) necessarily verifies a smaller-radius dense prefix; randomized center laws only need positive expected \(M_C(t)-2P_C\).
- Added the singleton reported-center reduced-cost screen. At a fixed atom cut, additive center mass and star price imply any positive center-set or sampled-law surplus contains one singleton center with \(m_x(t)>2P_x\), so the cut-local proof oracle can search centers one at a time.
- Added the one-sided singleton center certificate. Heuristic or sampled build-time center proposals now only need lower certified captured mass and upper certified star price with \(\underline m_x>2\overline P_x\); with an exact no-positive singleton check, this is complete for the cut-local center universe.
- Added the clipped singleton center-cover formula. With additive reported-center prices and singleton panels, the cut-local fractional center-cover LP has value \(\sum_x\min\{m_x(t),2P_x\}\), so any strict improvement over tailing all mass is already one positive singleton.
- Added the fractional reported-center cover dual. For a fixed atom cut, preprocessing can solve a finite LP that either pays unit tail for center mass or pays twice the recursive star price of reported center panels; any strict improvement over tailing everything hardens to one positive dense-prefix column, while failure leaves the all-ones no-center dual feasible. This makes k-means++/facility/AQ/list-decoding center proposals an exact reduced-cost search.
- Added the sampled reported-center tail certificate. A finite proposal law over center panels satisfies the surviving residual only if its expected captured scaled mass is at most twice its expected aggregate star price; positive expected surplus immediately contains one deterministic verified dense-prefix column. Thus randomized center generation contributes no extra rounding or set-cover loss.
- Added the sampled-center high-correlation fixed-tail bound. The atomized high-correlation dual now accepts a randomized center-panel law at each layer-cake cut: expected unreported tail \(\mathfrak E_\star\) and expected star price \(\mathfrak P_\star\) give the same \(\varepsilon B^{\rm hi}+2J_\varepsilon\mathfrak E_\star+4J_\varepsilon\mathfrak P_\star\) bound unless a sampled panel verifies a dense prefix.
- Added the clipped-singleton high-correlation fixed-tail bound. A cut-wise scalar envelope \(\Gamma_{\rm clip}=E_{\mathcal X}+\sum_x\min\{m_x,2P_x\}\) now substitutes for sampled center tail/price envelopes, with \(A^{\rm clip}=2\Gamma_{\rm clip}\); any strict clipping gain is one verified singleton dense prefix.
- Added the clipped-singleton logarithmic density endpoint. The full polylog endpoint now follows from one profile with polynomial dynamic range and clipped singleton price \(A^{\rm clip}\le D/\log^{1+\Omega(1)}n\), replacing sampled center laws by a cut-wise scalar clipping certificate.
- Added the one-sided clipped-singleton profile oracle. Build-time search may now certify lower residual benefit and upper high-correlation/clipped-price bounds; randomized proposals become Las Vegas by retaining only verified \((OCD1)\)--\((OCD3)\) certificates.
- Added the integrated clipped-singleton high-correlation bound and endpoint. The layer-cake loss now charges the dyadic sum of actual clipped singleton cut prices rather than \(J_\varepsilon\) times the worst cut; the polylog endpoint follows when this integrated price is a constant fraction of residual benefit after polynomial dynamic-range truncation.
- Added the one-sided integrated clipped profile oracle. The sharper integrated target now has a verifier form: certify \(\varepsilon\overline B^{\rm hi}+2\overline G_\Sigma<\underline D\), with failed randomized proposals discarded in Las Vegas build time.
- Added the integrated singleton reduced-cost screen. A center can now be scored across all dyadic layer-cake cuts by \(M_x^\Sigma-2P_x^\Sigma\); positive integrated surplus forces one individual cut with \(m_{x,t}>2P_{x,t}\), and a one-sided certificate \(\underline M_x^\Sigma>2\overline P_x^\Sigma\) is Las Vegas-searchable.
- Added the integrated clipped-price surplus identity. The dyadic clipped price equals total dyadic cut area minus positive singleton surplus, so any strict integrated clipping improvement is exactly a verified center-cut surplus.
- Added the sampled-center split-profile separator. The endpoint-level split-profile surplus condition can now use randomized center-panel laws for the high-correlation side directly; positive aggregate surplus yields either a low-correlation witness record or one sampled dense-prefix column, so no deterministic dense decomposition is needed before the verifier runs.
- Added the sampled-center split-profile residual envelope. A surviving normalized residual must satisfy the explicit inequality \(B\le C^{\rm lo}+\varepsilon B^{\rm hi}+2J_\varepsilon\mathfrak E_\star+4J_\varepsilon\mathfrak P_\star\) for every finite sampled-center panel; violating this scalar envelope is now the exact geometry/list-decoding target.
- Added the optimized sampled-center split-profile residual. When the sampled-center tail/price envelopes are uniform over truncation levels, the residual can optimize out \(\varepsilon\), replacing \(\varepsilon B^{\rm hi}+J_\varepsilon A\) by \(\Theta(B^{\rm hi},A)\le C A(1+\log_+(B^{\rm hi}/A))\), where \(A=2\mathfrak E_\star+4\mathfrak P_\star\). This is the cleaner logarithmic high-correlation toll to target.
- Added the logarithmic sampled-center separator endpoint. The full polylog endpoint now follows if every normalized exact-signature dual admits a sampled-center split-profile panel whose residual benefit beats the optimized toll \(\Theta(B^{\rm hi},A^{\rm samp})\); a sufficient benefit-density form is \(\sum(B-C^{\rm lo})>C_0H\sum A^{\rm samp}\) when the sampled-center dynamic range is at most \(H\).
- Added the single-profile logarithmic density screen. Any weighted panel satisfying the logarithmic sampled-center density inequality contains one profile with \(B-C^{\rm lo}>C_0H A^{\rm samp}\), so the build-time oracle can search profile-by-profile for a high residual-benefit/sampled-price ratio rather than coordinating a whole dense panel.
- Added the inverted sampled-center density residual. In any surviving residual, a profile with residual benefit \(D=(B-C^{\rm lo})_+\) must have sampled center price \(A^{\rm samp}\gtrsim D/(1+\log_+(B^{\rm hi}/D))\); therefore a proposal below this inverse-log threshold immediately separates.
- Added the polylog sampled-center density endpoint. If every normalized dual has one sampled-center split profile with polynomial global dynamic range \(B^{\rm hi}\le n^{K_{\rm dyn}}D\) and sampled price \(A^{\rm samp}\le D/\log^{K_{\rm den}}n\) for any \(K_{\rm den}>1\), then the shared-panel separator and hence the \(n^{\rho_c}\operatorname{polylog}n\) endpoint follow.
- Added the one-sided polylog sampled-center profile oracle. Build-time proposal mechanisms may now be verified by lower-bounding residual benefit and upper-bounding high-correlation mass and sampled-center price; if every normalized residual has one certified profile satisfying the polylog density tests, the endpoint follows.
- Added the Las Vegas sampled-center profile oracle. Randomized build-time profile proposals now only need positive probability of producing a one-sided certificate satisfying \((OSP1)\)--\((OSP3)\); failed or unverifiable proposals are discarded and never enter retained query/storage accounting.
- Added the shared integrated star-prefix screen. A single certified star-prefix record can now accumulate layer-cake mass across all dyadic cuts while paying its recursive price once, using the dyadic inequality \(\sum_{t\le y_a}t\le2y_a\) and the ordinary paid pair-star verifier.
- Added the shared integrated high-correlation tail bound and endpoint. The high-correlation residual can now be charged by \(2\mathfrak E_\Sigma+4\mathfrak P_\Sigma\), where \(\mathfrak P_\Sigma\) is a one-time shared recursive price rather than a sum of cut-local prices; the remaining theorem is to find shared records with small integrated unassigned tail plus one-time price.
- Added the finite shared integrated cover dual. The new LP minimizes integrated unassigned tail plus twice the one-time recursive price over shared star-prefix records; its dual is the exact no-shared-record obstruction, and the cover-cost endpoint states the remaining dense-overlap theorem as \(\varepsilon B^{\rm hi}+2\mathfrak C_\Sigma<D\).
- Added shared integrated cover column generation and a one-sided oracle. Restricted shared-cover solves now expose the exact reduced-cost score \(\sum_a h_{i,a}u_a-2P_i\); positive one-sided certificates can be found by uncharged randomized proposals, while a no-positive result remains an exact finite-universe certificate.
- Added the shared top-prefix reduced-cost compiler. For any certified smaller-radius container, shared-cover separation is now the scalar top-prefix test \(\sum_{j\le K}d(p_j)>2P(K)\), so facility, AQ, range, list/lattice, or \(k\)-means++ proposals only need to certify one container whose sorted shared-degree prefix beats its one-time recursive price.
- Added the shared prefix weak-type and price-density residual. If every shared top prefix in a certified container fails, the shared degrees obey \(\delta\le2\psi(b^{1-\rho}Ah^{\rho-1}+\zeta h^\rho)\); conversely, a height-and-mass density violation immediately materializes a positive shared prefix.
- Added the shared prefix density-panel hardening. A finite weighted panel of facility/AQ/range/list/lattice-style shared-prefix proposals now closes whenever every active record beats its local price density and aggregate captured shared degree beats aggregate prefix price; the verifier still retains one deterministic top prefix, with one-sided lower-mass and upper-price certificates allowed.
- Added the sampled shared-prefix density search wrapper. Randomized build-time panels with positive expected certified surplus can be amplified until one verified panel appears, then immediately hardened to a deterministic shared top prefix; this is useful plumbing, but the next real proof work should attack the weak-type residual/separator theorem rather than add more wrappers.
- Added the clipped shared-prefix price-density screen. For a shared container with price density \(A K^{\rho-1}+\zeta K^\rho\), the core dense separator can now test near the clipped stationary scale \(K^\natural\) instead of all cardinalities; a violation at that scale immediately materializes a deterministic shared top prefix.
- Added the tail-mass clipped shared-prefix residual. If no shared prefix fires, the total shared degree above the clipped threshold \(\Theta=2a\psi f(K^\natural)\) is at most \(K\Theta\) for any available \(K\) near \(K^\natural\), giving the geometric separator a direct tail-mass target.
- Added the cardinality-net clipped shared-tail residual. Under the usual dense available-cardinality net, the no-prefix residual for one shared container is now the single scalar cap \(M^+(\Theta^{\rm net})\le2K^\natural\Theta^{\rm net}\), eliminating both threshold and prefix-size choices from the geometric obstruction.
- Added the single-tail shared separator endpoint. The current dense-overlap target is now: every normalized exact-signature dual either has a positive low-correlation profile record or one certified shared container violates the clipped tail cap \(M^+(\Theta^{\rm net})\le2K^\natural\Theta^{\rm net}\); such a violation materializes a deterministic shared top prefix and implies the polylog endpoint.
- Added the subset form of the clipped shared-tail residual. The same no-prefix cap now implies \(\sum_{p\in U}d(p)\le(|U|+2K^\natural)\Theta^{\rm net}\) for every certified subset \(U\), so the remaining geometric/transport theorem may target any smaller-radius subset with paid mass above this fixed affine threshold rather than explicitly constructing the tail.
- Added the transported-subset clipped-tail certificate. A bounded-congestion shipment of mass \(M\) into a certified subset \(U\) now separates whenever \(M/C>(|U|+2K^\natural)\Theta^{\rm net}\), avoiding the dyadic layer-cake dynamic-range loss when the geometric reporter already supplies a single subset.
- Strengthened the guard-shadow mass and transport reporters. Since hit-weighted star panels accept arbitrary certified subsets, a transport into one certified container now separates at \(M/C>\psi(K^\rho A+\sigma K^{1+\rho})\) by submitting the whole container, removing the previous \(4J_{K,V}\) layer-cake loss from this route.
- Propagated the no-layer-cake guard-credit simplification through the finite guard-credit LP, its dual, the one-template proposal test, and the Hall-profile repair interfaces. The verified dense route now uses direct surplus \(D-R\) or \(X-R\) for whole certified containers; credited mass scales and their \(4J\) dynamic-range factors are no longer part of this guard-credit path.
- Propagated the same direct-surplus correction through the deficient-shadow prefix pipeline. A retained deficient-shadow prefix now closes at \(X>R(h)\), so the no-prefix, tail, balanced-prefix, and fixed-threshold Hall-deficit screens lose the artificial one-level \(J(\delta)\) factor; only coarse mass/support/max-degree certificates still pay their genuine dyadic window count.
- Updated the Hall-deficit audit wording to match the sharpened statements: the remaining local target is a direct mixed-density repair inequality, not a layer-cake price inequality.
- Added the transported deficient-shadow certificate. A bounded-congestion shipment into one certified subset now gives the same direct prefix mass certificate \(M/C>R(h)\), so range-reporting, list-decoding, AQ, or facility-style geometry can close a Hall-deficit residual without materializing the full deficient-shadow degree order or introducing a dyadic extraction loss.
- Added the restricted deficient-shadow transport cut. For one proposed certified subset, pair-compatible shipment from the exposed Hall sublayer either certifies the transported prefix mass directly or exposes a localized Hall cut whose paid-pair capacity is measured only inside that subset.
- Added the flow-value deficient-shadow screen. The Hall-deficit reporter can now score proposed subsets by exact max-flow value \(\Phi_U(C)/C-R_W(|U|)\); positive value closes, while a surviving residual has nonpositive value for every proposed subset/congestion scale and hence a localized cut certificate for every supercritical target.
- Added the flow-value Hall-deficit oracle and endpoint. The global polylog theorem may now assume the exact max-flow reduced-gain separator \((FVS1)\) directly, replacing the balanced fixed-threshold density panel by a build-time flow solve whose retained artifact is still one deterministic smaller-radius prefix.
- Collapsed the unrestricted flow-value Hall screen back to the deficient-shadow mass separator. For a fixed certified subset \(U\), \(\sup_C \Phi_U(C)/C=\sum_{p\in U}d_{G'}(p)\): the full-left cut gives the upper bound, and sufficiently small congestion saturates every reachable paid-pair sink. Thus congestion optimization is not an independent proof ingredient; max-flow is only a way to lower-certify the same subset shadow mass when degrees are not materialized.
- Added the shadow-mass Hall-deficit oracle and endpoint. The clean current conditional theorem no longer mentions congestion scales: every exposed positive-excess Hall shadow must have one certified smaller-radius subset, or finite positive average of subsets, with \(D_{G'}(U)>R_W(|U|)\). The global polylog endpoint now references this reduced separator directly.
- Hardened the shadow-mass Hall endpoint from finite panels to one subset. Since all panel weights are nonnegative, positive average shadow-mass surplus already contains a deterministic certified \(U\) with \(D_{G'}(U)>R_W(|U|)\). Randomized or finite proposal panels are only build-time search devices for this one retained certificate.
- Status audit after the drift check: the useful progress is the collapse from flow/panel endpoints to the single shadow-mass separator. Additional quotient, sampled-center, or shared-panel endpoints are only useful if they produce that separator or a strictly stronger equivalent condition. The next proof work should attack the separator directly: from a positive-excess Hall sublayer plus the weak-type deficient-shadow envelopes, derive either a certified smaller-radius subset with \(D_{G'}(U)>R_W(|U|)\) or a concrete geometric obstruction showing which extra richness hypothesis is missing.
- Added the algebraic obstruction for Hall excess. A positive-excess Hall sublayer can coexist with no shadow-mass surplus in the incidence-only model, even when every guard incidence reaches the same paid point; choosing many duplicate guards makes \(\Delta(G')>0\) while \(D_{G'}(\{p\})\) stays fixed. Therefore the missing separator cannot be a formal consequence of Hall excess and weak-type envelopes alone. It must use Euclidean/container richness, low-correlation list-size energy, or dense-star geometry.
- Added the local list-size guard-shadow criterion. A proposed container can now be certified from captured guard load \(H_{G'}(U)\) and a local paid-pair multiplicity bound \(\kappa_U\): if \(H_{G'}(U)/\kappa_U>R_W(|U|)\), then \(D_{G'}(U)>R_W(|U|)\) and the Hall residual closes. This is the positive counterpart to the duplicate-guard obstruction and turns the next geometric target into a capture/list-size/price trilemma.
- Strengthened local list-size certification from pointwise multiplicity to a fractional guard-to-paid assignment. A max-flow inside one proposed container either routes all captured guard load to paid pairs with congestion \(\kappa\), yielding the same \(H_{G'}(U)/\kappa>R_W(|U|)\) shadow-mass certificate, or exposes a local duplicate-guard Hall cut. The surviving obstruction is now explicit: captured guard load whose paid-pair neighborhood is too light.
- Added the intrinsic local list-size \(\kappa_U^*\), the maximum duplicate-guard cut ratio inside a proposed container. This removes the guessed congestion parameter: the exact reduced gain is \(H_{G'}(U)/\kappa_U^*-R_W(|U|)\), and positive gain for one container or finite panel hardens to the same shadow-mass certificate.
- Added slack duplicate-cut descent. If a proposed container captures guard load \(H\) at recursive price \(R\) but still does not close, then for every slack \(\varepsilon\) it contains a guard-incidence cut with duplicate ratio \(>(1-\varepsilon)H/R\) (or a zero-shadow cut when \(R=0\)). Thus a failed high-capture proposal is reduced to a weighted high-correlation incidence cut at the container's own price density.
- Added the local transport-to-star bridge. A feasible local guard-to-paid assignment now canonically induces a high-correlation responsibility tensor with total mass \(H_{G'}(U)/\kappa\); every responsibility superlevel is a certified smaller-radius star whose mass lower-bounds deficient-shadow mass. Hence the positive side of the duplicate-cut obstruction feeds the existing dense-star verifier directly, while a surviving residual must satisfy a concrete star weak-type envelope.
- Added the integrated responsibility-star residual. If no responsibility superlevel closes, then \(H_{G'}(U)/\kappa\) is bounded by twice the sum of the recursive prices of the dyadic responsibility levels that actually appear. This replaces a coarse global center-count loss by a cost-sensitive local accounting target.
- Added the local close-pay-cut trichotomy. At any proposed congestion \(\kappa\), the local max-flow either returns a duplicate guard cut with ratio \(>\kappa\), or returns a feasible responsibility decomposition with dyadic levels defined from its positive responsibility masses; in the latter case the residual closes unless \(H_{G'}(U)/\kappa\) is paid both by the whole-container price and by twice the integrated responsibility-star price.
- Added canonical duplicate-cut closure. For fixed \(U,\kappa\), the duplicate excess \(B(\mathcal A)-\kappa P(\mathcal A)\) has a unique inclusion-maximal maximizer; after deleting this one closure and its paid shadow, the remaining incidences satisfy Hall and route with congestion \(\kappa\). Thus the duplicate branch has a canonical merge object instead of an arbitrary branching family of cuts.
- Strengthened the local close-pay-cut trichotomy to return the canonical duplicate closure in its infeasible branch. The trichotomy now preserves the complement-routing certificate directly, so later algorithms can branch on one merged duplicate object rather than on an arbitrary min-cut witness.
- Added duplicate-closure shadow repair. Any canonical duplicate closure now induces its own closed paid-shadow degree on the points of the proposed container; a certified prefix whose cut-shadow mass beats recursive price closes the original deficient-shadow residual, while failure leaves a no-prefix envelope localized to that closed shadow plus an already-routable complement.
- Added the query-layer duplicate-closure prefix compiler. A duplicate closure can now be searched by fractional query layers with price \(\sum_q\tau_qa_qy_q\); any positive fractional surplus hardens to one Boolean query layer and top prefix through the existing paid-prefix verifier, so the duplicate branch need not pay the all-loaded-query price when a narrower layer certifies the shadow mass.
- Added the duplicate-closure finite-prefix normal form. The duplicate shadow \(N_U(\mathcal A)\) is now an ordinary finite-prefix demand system after the substitution \(\widehat\gamma_{p,q}=a_q\gamma_{p,q}\), \(\widehat\tau_q=a_q\tau_q\); a surviving canonical duplicate branch is therefore exactly a finite-prefix residual plus the already-routable complement, so the existing cardinality-net, layer-cake, and reported-support finite-prefix reporters apply without a parallel duplicate-specific proof stack.
- Added the local finite-prefix residual normal form. A local max-flow failure now either yields one verified paid-prefix repair from the canonical duplicate closure or leaves the explicit finite-prefix envelope \((LFP1)\) on that closure, while the complement is already packed at the tested congestion; this removes duplicate-cut branching from the local algorithmic state.
- Added the deficient-shadow finite-prefix normal form and finite-prefix Hall-deficit oracle. A Hall-deficient shadow now uses the same weighted demand substitution \(\widehat\gamma_{p,q}=a_q\gamma_{p,q}\), \(\widehat\tau_q=a_q\tau_q\), so the repair target is one query-layer finite-prefix surplus rather than the all-loaded-query shadow-mass test; the all-query certificate is just the special case \(y\equiv1\).
- Added the query-layer deficient-shadow residual obstruction. The named current bottleneck is now the finite-prefix envelope \(D_{G',W,y}^{\widehat\gamma}(h)\le\psi_W(h^{\rho_W}\sum_q\widehat\tau_qy_q+\sigma h^{1+\rho_W})\) for every searched container/cardinality/query layer, with all-loaded shadow mass only the \(y\equiv1\) specialization. This is the sharper target for LP range reporting, list decoding, AQ, or center proposals.
- Added the query-layer random-list deficient-shadow repair and matching finite-prefix Hall-deficit endpoint. A build-time randomized list or marginal center law now closes an exposed Hall shadow whenever its expected captured query-layer mass beats the corresponding Renyi query/storage moments; positive expected surplus hardens to one Boolean query layer and one top prefix, giving the global \(n^{\rho_c}\operatorname{polylog}n\) endpoint under this finite-prefix separator.
- Added the query-free deficient-shadow list-gain scorer. A proposal no longer has to choose a fractional query layer: the verifier scores a hard list by \(\sum_q(h_L(q)-\psi K^\rho\widehat\tau_q)_+-\psi\sigma K^{1+\rho}\), and scores soft marginals by the analogous Jensen/log-convex bound. Any positive score hardens to one Boolean query layer and one finite-prefix repair, giving k-means++/facility/AQ/list-decoding proposals an exact reduced-gain objective.
- Added the low-diameter query-free Hall-shadow seeding bridge. If a hidden positive-gain deficient-shadow list is shell-compact and carries an \(\alpha\)-fraction of the chosen residual or dual sampling weight, residual/k-means++-style cap seeding finds a certified cap containing it with miss probability \(e^{-\alpha T}\), while exhaustive center enumeration finds it deterministically. This turns the empirical k-means signal into a provable route under an explicit compact-shadow existence hypothesis, without claiming that hypothesis is automatic.
- Added the certified-ball repair cap-enumeration corollary. A hidden repair contained in a Euclidean ball of radius \(R_A\) inside a shell of lower radius \(s_{\min}\) has normalized angular diameter at most \(4R_A/s_{\min}\), so exhaustive resident-center enumeration deterministically proposes a cap containing it. This uses the user's build-time allowance to remove sampling mass assumptions for already-certified small-ball repairs; the remaining gap is existence of such a positive-gain ball repair.
- Added the price-compatible query-free refinement lemma. A positive Hall-shadow list can be split into compact certified pieces without losing the proof exactly when the refined prices satisfy \(\sum_i\psi_iK_i^\rho\le\psi K^\rho\) and \(\sum_i\psi_iK_i^{1+\rho}\le\psi K^{1+\rho}\); then subadditivity of the hinge forces one child piece to have positive query-free gain. This pinpoints how radius contraction must pay for the concave \(K^\rho\) splitting loss.
- Added the bounded-fanout radius-discount refinement screen. If a positive Hall-shadow list is split into at most \(J\) certified pieces and every piece has price factor at most \(\theta\psi\), then \(\theta J^{1-\rho}\le1\) makes the refinement price-compatible. For the padded radius potential this is exactly \(\Psi(R_\star)/\Psi(R)\le J^{-(1-\rho)}\), giving a concrete fanout-versus-radius-contraction target for k-means/facility/range-reporting ball decompositions.
- Added the metric-entropy Hall-shadow closure. A positive-gain Hall shadow with certified covering number \(N_\star(L,R_\star)\le(\Psi(R)/\Psi(R_\star))^{1/(1-\rho)}\) at a smaller radius closes by the bounded-fanout refinement lemma; conversely an unclosed shadow must have covering number above that threshold. The low-doubling specialization shows that, away from the terminal radius, any shadow of certified doubling dimension \(<4/(1-\rho)\) closes after a constant radius drop. This turns the next separator target into a high-entropy-residual-versus-spherical/list-decoding problem.
- Added the Renyi-spread Hall-shadow obstruction. For any proposed smaller-radius partition with common child price \(\psi_\star\), if no child closes then \(\sum_i K_i^\rho>(\psi/\psi_\star)K^\rho\), equivalently the normalized child-size distribution has Renyi-effective support above \((\psi/\psi_\star)^{1/(1-\rho)}\). This strengthens the cover-number obstruction for unbalanced clusterings and identifies the theory-native objective as radius-discounted Renyi fanout rather than Lloyd/k-means distortion.
- Added the reduced-gain quotient refinement theorem in the finite dense/spherical LP language. A positive quotient column whose demand-preserving smaller-radius refinement is price-compatible must contain a positive child quotient; with common child price this gives the same Renyi-spread contrapositive \(\sum_iK_i^\rho>(\psi/\psi_\star)K^\rho\). This moves the high-spread obstruction from the Hall-shadow normal form to the actual reduced-gain quotient columns consumed by the spherical/dense minimax dichotomy.
- Added the quotient-spread packing-entropy handoff. If a positive quotient survives every radius-\(R_\star\) child and point-centered \(R_\star\)-balls are certified, then every \(R_\star\)-ball cover has size above \((\Psi(R)/\Psi(R_\star))^{1/(1-\rho)}\), so the quotient contains an \(R_\star\)-separated set of that size. The uniform law on this set has exact Renyi effective support equal to its cardinality, and the separated pair count is \(M(M-1)\). This turns the high-spread residual into the concrete packing-entropy input used by the tuple-profile/list-decoding storage estimates.
- Added the spread-packing entropy-energy screen. For the separated set extracted from a surviving quotient, the distinct-pair second moment is bounded by \(\sum_\pi |\pi|e^{-I_\pi}\); if every profile energy satisfies \(I_\pi\ge\log|\pi|+\Lambda\), the profile contribution is at most \(|\Pi|e^{-\Lambda}\). This makes the remaining spherical/list-decoding payment explicit: either energy dominates packing entropy, or the offending profile is the dense high-correlation signal for the quotient separator.
- Added the spread-packing star-signal bridge. A weighted profile contribution split into low-energy and high-correlation edges now has an exact dyadic layer-cake alternative: either the high-correlation weight is at most \(2|E|J\Lambda\), or some smaller-radius star superlevel has scale \(t|U_{x,t}|\ge\Lambda\). Thus entropy-energy failure on the packing witness is handed directly to the existing pair-star dense-column verifier instead of remaining an informal "high-correlation signal."
- Added the paid integrated spread-star residual. When the high-correlation pair weights are dominated by paid near-shadow degrees, failure of all smaller-radius paid prefixes bounds the high-correlation profile mass by twice the sum of the Renyi prices of all dyadic star superlevels. This removes the coarse worst-case \(|E|J\) handoff in the paid setting: a profile either pays through low-correlation energy plus integrated star price, or a positive dense prefix is certified.
- Added the paid cover residual for quotient stars. For any assignment of a quotient's update side to smaller balls, the parent paid degree of an assigned point is dominated by the ball-thickened paid degree of its assigned center by the triangle inequality. Therefore, if all smaller-radius paid prefixes fail, the parent quotient demand is at most the integrated Renyi price of the assigned cover. A positive quotient can survive such a cover only when the cover's integrated paid price exceeds the parent price, giving a paid analogue of the earlier cover-number obstruction.
- Added the paid Renyi-cover obstruction. Combining positive parent reduced gain with the paid cover residual shows that any surviving assigned cover must have integrated paid price strictly above the parent price. If the child activations are query-compatible with the parent activation, ordinary Renyi price compatibility would close the quotient; hence every surviving query-compatible cover has \(\sum_x |U_x|^\rho>(\psi/\psi_\star)K^\rho\). The remaining paid obstruction is therefore either query-activation inflation or the same high-Renyi-spread condition as before.
- Added the activation-inflation assigned-cover screen. The vague "not query-compatible" branch is now a precise toll \(\sum_x\psi_x|U_x|^\rho E_x\): if a Renyi-price-compatible assigned refinement survives, this toll must exceed the parent Renyi/storage slack. This confirms the current work is still narrowing the actual dense/spherical separator rather than adding endpoint wrappers.
- Added the inherited-activation paid-cover closure. If a parent quotient has a retained safe activation, smaller-radius refinements can inherit that activation and low Renyi fanout closes directly; activation inflation is only the measurable toll for replacing inherited finite-demand activation by standalone geometric child wrappers. This is a real simplification of the recursive algorithm: standard certified-ball recursion is now just another same-activation filter-cover refinement.
- Added the inherited-activation cover-spread packing corollary. For safe parent activations, every low-fanout certified smaller-ball cover would close by the same-activation refinement; if all such prefixes fail, the quotient directly contains the separated packing needed by the entropy-energy/list-decoding screen. This removes a separate child-activation closure assumption from the high-spread handoff.
- Added the inherited-activation spread-star residual. Entropy-energy high-correlation failures can now be paid using the parent activation \(a\) whenever their multiplicities are dominated by the parent paid degree; no ball-thickened activation is needed in this same-activation branch. A violation exposes an exact positive inherited star prefix through the finite-cover verifier.
- Added the clipped inherited-activation spread-star residual. The domination assumption \(M_x(y)\le b(y)\) is now replaced by an exact decomposition into a clipped paid part and an overload \(W_+\). The clipped part is paid by inherited star-prefix prices; paid-product edge laws \(\lambda_x\theta_{x,y}b(y)\) have zero overload. This is a sharper progress checkpoint: the remaining proof should either run the entropy-energy packing with paid-product weights or prove that any overload creates a duplicate/activation dense obstruction.
- Added the paid-product spread-packing handoff. The entropy-energy/profile side can now be stated with weighted cross moments \(\sum_{x,y}\lambda_xb(y)\Pr[x,y\in U_G]\): low-correlation pairs use the usual energy bound and high-correlation pairs are automatically dominated by the parent paid degree for each center. This removes the overload term on the preferred route and makes the next geometric target a paid-product profile-entropy lower bound.
- Added the bipartite paid-product spread handoff. The separated packing now only needs to provide center entropy; the second coordinate can range over all of the parent update side with weight \(b(y)\). This fixes the mismatch where a sparse separated packing might carry little paid mass, and turns the next theorem into a center-spread versus paid-measure cross-moment estimate.
- Added the paid level-set spread packing lemma. Center spread alone is too weak because the paid mass can be disjoint from the unweighted packing witness. If all inherited paid prefixes fail, a dyadic paid superlevel carries a \(1/O(\log)\) fraction of the parent paid mass, and every smaller-ball cover of that superlevel has Renyi price at least its level mass. The entropy-energy target should now use centers from this paid superlevel.
- Added the paid-superlevel entropy-energy handoff. Uniformly averaging over centers in a paid superlevel keeps the product moment normalized by the actual paid mass: low-correlation profiles pay by their certified energy, while high-correlation profiles either pay inherited star-prefix prices or expose a positive inherited dense prefix. This finally puts center entropy, paid mass, and the dense fallback into one local statement.
- Added the fiberized paid-superlevel exposure identity. The low-correlation payment \(\sum_\pi e^{-I_\pi}L_\pi\) is now exactly a \(b\)-weighted average of the low exposure seen from the separated center set, so the remaining geometric/list-decoding target is one paid-fiber exposure bound rather than a profile-indexed accounting problem.
- Added the uniform-energy and Gaussian-margin paid-fiber screens. After fiberization, the low branch only needs a uniform energy floor \(I\ge\Lambda\), giving \(e^{-\Lambda}B_Y\) without any profile-entropy factor; for Gaussian caps, all nondefective low-correlation pairs get this floor from a combined margin threshold, high-correlation pairs feed inherited prefixes, and the only remaining explicit term is the margin-defect mass.
- Added the margin-defect first-moment payment. The Gaussian margin defect is bounded by truncated one-point active loads on the center or paid side, and also by the Cauchy bound \(\sqrt{B_Y\bar p_XP_Y}\); hence it is not a new second-moment/list-decoding obstruction, but an ordinary first-moment active-load term.
- Added the composed Gaussian paid-fiber closure. The paid-product moment is now bounded by \(e^{-\Lambda}B_Y\), a one-point active-load term, and inherited high-correlation prefix price; choosing \(\Lambda=A\log\log n\) and inverse-polylog one-point loads closes the non-high branch without profile entropy or layer-cake overhead.
- Added the Gaussian paid-fiber separator criterion. The branch now has an explicit contrapositive target: prove a paid-product lower bound, inverse-polylog truncated margin loads, and an inherited-prefix price bounded by a strict fraction of the paid-product mass; then a positive radius-contracting prefix must exist.
- Corrected the Gaussian paid-fiber separator criterion to use truncated margin loads rather than the untruncated Cauchy product. The total first-moment product upper-bounds the entire paid-product mass, so it is useful for closure but tautological as a positive separator; the real separator signal is a margin split with small low-margin \(X\) and \(Y\) tails.
- Added the margin-tail obstruction normal form. Optimizing over the split \(\gamma\) gives one scalar defect \(\mathsf M_G\); after paid-product mass and inherited-prefix price are controlled, any surviving residual must have large \(\mathsf M_G\), i.e. every split leaves significant low-margin mass on one side.
- Collapsed the margin-tail defect back to one-point active-load accounting. The optimized split defect satisfies \(\mathsf M_G\le\min\{B_Y\bar p_X,P_Y\}\), and the margin-defect closure now uses \(\min\{\eta_X,\eta_Y,\sqrt{\eta_X\eta_Y}\}\) rather than only the Cauchy product. Thus a surviving Gaussian margin tail must be a simultaneous endpoint-load obstruction, not a separate geometric branch.
- Added the total-load Gaussian paid-fiber separator as the algorithmic screen. Once paid-product mass and inherited-prefix price are controlled, small total active load on either endpoint forces a positive inherited prefix, so the retained data structure need not optimize over margin splits in this branch; the split-optimized margin-tail form is only a diagnostic strengthening.
- Drift audit after the user's challenge: the work is making progress only insofar as it narrows the finite-prefix Hall residual. The current lead route is finite-prefix Hall residual \(\to\) price-compatible smaller-radius prefix or high-Renyi-spread packing; the first branch is closed by the refinement/metric-entropy lemmas, and the second must feed the entropy-energy/list-decoding screen plus the total-load Gaussian paid-fiber separator. New wrappers, clustering heuristics, or posterior/channel variants are non-lead unless they produce either a verified finite-prefix repair or a high-spread paid-fiber exposure bound.
- Added the Renyi-cover Hall-shadow closure. A positive query-free Hall-shadow list now closes under any certified overlapping cover plus assignment whose assigned Renyi query price \(\sum_i\psi_iK_i^\rho\) and storage price fit under the parent price; if child price factors are no larger than the parent, storage is automatic and every surviving residual must violate this assigned Renyi cover inequality. This sharpens the k-means/facility target from balanced fanout or Lloyd distortion to radius-discounted Renyi cover cost.
- Added the soft Renyi-cover assignment hardening. Any randomized or fractional feasible assignment whose marginal child loads satisfy \(\sum_i\psi_i\mu_i^\rho\le\psi K^\rho\) hardens by Jensen to one deterministic assigned cover, with storage automatic when child price factors are no larger than the parent. This turns k-means++/facility/AQ soft memberships into a direct certificate target rather than another heuristic branch.
- Added the inherited-activation Renyi center law. When same-activation smaller-ball prefixes all fail, every assigned point-centered cover has normalized cell masses \(z_x\) with \(\sum_x z_x^\rho>\psi/\psi_\star\); a maximal separated cover therefore gives a weighted center law of large Renyi effective support, and one dyadic center level keeps the mass-entropy product up to a logarithmic loss. This lets the high-spread branch feed paid-product screens with natural center weights instead of immediately flattening to raw packing cardinality.
- Added the weighted Gaussian paid-fiber separator. The Gaussian margin/active-load closure now accepts any probability law \(\lambda_x\) on separated centers, bounding \(A_\lambda=\sum_x\lambda_x\sum_y b(y)\kappa_{x,y}\) by the same inverse-polylog energy term, weighted center active load, paid-side load, and inherited high-correlation prefix price. This lets the Renyi center law enter the final Gaussian screen directly, avoiding a forced dyadic flattening step.
- Added the weighted margin-tail obstruction. The weighted Gaussian branch now has the same contrapositive normal form as the unweighted screen: after a lower bound \(A_\lambda\ge aB_Y\) and inherited-price bound \(\mathsf P_{\rm hi}^\lambda\le\xi A_\lambda\), any surviving residual must have both \(\bar p_\lambda\) and \(P_Y/B_Y\) at least \((1-\xi)a-e^{-\Lambda}\). This makes the remaining lower-bound task exact rather than hidden in a margin split.
- Status audit after the drift challenge: the current proof is making progress only if the next theorem proves a weighted paid-product lower bound for the Renyi center law, or shows that failure of that lower bound is itself a finite-prefix repair. The main obstruction is the mismatch between cell-size center weights \(\lambda_x=|U_x|/K\) and paid mass \(b(y)\); spread alone does not imply \(A_\lambda\) unless the paid mass is sufficiently exposed to the center law or the mismatch is certified as a dense prefix.
- Added the paid-transport Gaussian handoff. The Gaussian paid-fiber screen no longer has to force the center and paid measures into the product form \(\lambda_xb(y)\): any transport \(w_{x,y}\le b(y)\) has the same high-correlation prefix accounting, and an assigned cover \(w_{c(y),y}=b(y)\) gives \(A_w\ge aB_w\) from a pointwise co-activation lower bound on the assigned edge. This directly addresses the size-law versus paid-law mismatch; the remaining theorem can target assigned-cover co-activation and endpoint active-load control rather than a product lower bound.
- Added the assigned Gaussian transport lower bound. For assigned edges with \(\langle c(y),y\rangle\ge\theta_->0\) and both Gaussian margins at most \(M\), the common-factor Gaussian event gives \(\kappa_{c(y),y}\ge\frac14\overline\Phi(M/\sqrt{\theta_-})\), hence \(A_w\ge a_{\theta_-,M}B_w\). The remaining geometric task is now to produce such assigned paid transports with acceptable margin/active-load monitors, not to prove an abstract product co-activation conjecture.
- Added the windowed assigned-transport load certificate. The active-load issue in the assigned Gaussian branch is not absolute one-point load: if assigned margins lie in a window with \(M=O(\sqrt{\log\log n})\), then \(P_X^w/A_w\) and \(P_Y^w/A_w\) are only \(\log^{O_c(1)}n\). The remaining target is therefore sharper: construct windowed assigned transports and charge their shared storage, or show that failure exposes a finite-prefix dense repair.
- Strengthened the assigned-transport load certificate from a two-sided window to an upper-margin cutoff. Since endpoint probabilities are always at most one, \(m(c(y)),m(y)\le M=O(\sqrt{\log\log n})\) already gives \(P_X^w/A_w,P_Y^w/A_w\le\log^{O_c(1)}n\). The truncation alternative is now exact: either inverse-polylog paid mass lies below the cutoff, or almost all assigned mass is on a high-margin center or paid endpoint.
- Added the high-margin assigned-prefix/spread normal form. The complement of the upper-margin assigned transport is now routed through the same finite-prefix verifier: if its paid mass beats the inherited cell prices, a positive prefix closes; if not, the residual is explicitly high Renyi spread across assigned high-margin cells. This removes "high margin" as a separate informal branch.
- Added the assigned Gaussian transport trichotomy. A positive-correlation assigned transport now has exactly three proof-facing outcomes: inverse-polylog upper-margin hit mass with polylog normalized endpoint load; a positive inherited finite prefix inside an assigned cell; or a priced high-margin Renyi-cover residual. This keeps the next target honest: construct shared-storage assigned transports or pay the priced residual via the existing entropy/list-decoding route.
- Added the high-margin assigned storage split. For deterministic assigned cells, \(\sum_x h_x^{1+\rho}\le(\sum_xh_x)^{1+\rho}\) makes the recursive storage charge automatic once child price factors do not exceed the parent. Therefore the surviving high-margin residual is a Renyi query-price/list-decoding obstruction, not an unaccounted storage-branching obstruction.
- Added the high-margin endpoint split. The residual now separates into paid-high mass, whose one-point paid activity is suppressed by \(\overline\Phi(M)\), and center-high mass, whose Renyi query price is active only with the same Gaussian tail factor. With \(M^2=\Theta(\log\log n)\), the high-margin branch is tail-suppressed; the remaining theorem is to route that tail-suppressed high-spread price through the entropy/list-decoding certificate.
- Added the high-margin tail-rate screen. Restricting the assigned transport to either endpoint side and applying the transport Gaussian separator shows that, after inherited high-correlation prefix prices are controlled, the restricted paid-product rate is at most \(e^{-\Lambda}+\overline\Phi(M)\). Thus the remaining list-decoding/entropy theorem needs to produce a paid-product lower bound above that tail scale, or certify that the branch's actual activity is already inverse-polylogarithmically budgeted.
- Added the high-margin inverse-polylog exposure threshold. Summing the center-high and paid-high tail-rate screens gives \(A_{\rm hm}\le(e^{-\Lambda}+\overline\Phi(M))B_{\rm hm}+\mathsf P_{{\rm hi},{\rm hm}}\). Therefore, once inherited high-correlation price is a fixed fraction of \(A_{\rm hm}\), any exposure \(A_{\rm hm}\ge(\log n)^{-K}B_{\rm hm}\) forces a positive prefix by taking \(M^2,\Lambda=\Theta_K(\log\log n)\). The remaining list-decoding theorem only needs inverse-polylog exposure, not constant exposure.
- Added the two-cutoff high-margin window reduction. Given \(M<U\), the portion of the high-margin residual with both endpoint margins at most \(U\) is just another upper-margin assigned transport and has hit mass \(\Omega(\overline\Phi(U/\sqrt{\theta_-}))\) times its paid mass with polylog normalized load when \(U^2=O(\log\log n)\). If that window carries too little mass, almost all residual mass is above \(U\), where the endpoint-tail and active Renyi query price are suppressed by \(\overline\Phi(U)\). This turns high-margin handling into an iterative cutoff process rather than a monolithic residual.
- Added the high-margin margin ladder reduction. A polylogarithmic number of margin bands between \(M\) and \(U=O(\sqrt{\log\log n})\) is enough: either one band carries inverse-polylog mass and is already an upper-margin assigned transport, or a constant fraction of the residual reaches the final very-high cutoff and inherits the stronger \(\overline\Phi(U)\) tail suppression. This removes the free cutoff choice from the high-margin branch.
- Added the compressed-bucket rung checklist. Bucket-net recursion is now an explicit deterministic geometry lemma, and the query hypothesis has been simplified to the ordinary success-normalized candidate-load first moment because \(|N_F|^\rho\le |N_F|\le |B_F|\). Therefore the compressed-bucket route's remaining nonstandard theorem is shared/canonical-net storage, not query-side Renyi list decoding.
- Added the reciprocal-multiplicity pair-congestion storage certificate. In the pair-witness specialization, a canonical child of size \(K\) and exponent \(\rho\le1\) contributes at most its price factor \(\psi\) per contained separated pair after dividing decoded occurrences by the child's multiplicity. Thus the shared-DAG storage theorem can be targeted as a weighted pair-congestion/list-decoding estimate rather than a higher-order tuple moment.
- Added the reciprocal-multiplicity fixed-tail storage endpoint. This composes the occurrence-multiplicity discount with the existing correlation-split/star machinery: after dyadic layers, storage closes if the low-correlation aggregate Gaussian energy plus \(4|P|J_i^{\rm hi}R_i\) fixed high-correlation tails fit the storage budget; otherwise a profile layer must expose a verified smaller-radius dense/star repair.
- Added the local entropy-slack screen for reciprocal storage. The aggregate fixed-tail condition can be certified profile-by-profile: with \(M_{\mathcal I}\) active layers, each low-correlation layer gets budget \((aS-\mathbb E\Gamma)/(2M_{\mathcal I})\), so polylogarithmically many layers cost only an \(O(\log\log n)\) additive entropy-energy margin. This isolates the remaining geometric task as proving those local margins or triggering the dense repair.
- Added the profile-entropy local storage screen. A low-correlation layer may now be refined into subprofiles with weights \(W_h\) and two-point energies \(I_h\); if \(I_h\ge\log_+W_h+\Lambda\), the whole layer costs only \(|\mathcal H|e^{-\Lambda}\). Thus the reciprocal-storage branch depends on the number of retained entropy profiles rather than raw pair mass, and failures are exactly the spread-packing star-signal cases already routed to dense repair.
- Added the entropy-deficit tail form of the local storage screen. The witness-density residual compares benefit to congestion and does not by itself bound storage congestion; the storage side instead needs a direct tail bound for \(X_h=W_he^{-I_h}\). The new screen says the low branch closes if the dyadic entropy-deficit sum \(\sum_j2^j|\{h:X_h\asymp2^j\}|\) fits the local budget, making a tail theorem sufficient and avoiding a worst-profile margin requirement.
- Added the positive-moment entropy-deficit screen. Choosing a cutoff \(\tau\) and exponent \(\nu>0\), it is enough to bound \(\sum_h[((X_h/\tau)^{1+\nu}-1)_+]\) plus the baseline \(\tau|\mathcal H|\). This is the list-decoding-friendly form of the storage tail theorem: prove a small positive moment of entropy excess rather than an all-profile or all-bin bound.
- Added the Renyi-moment entropy-deficit screen. Optimizing over the cutoff reduces the diffuse storage branch to \(C_\nu|\mathcal H|^{\nu/(1+\nu)}(\sum_h X_h^{1+\nu})^{1/(1+\nu)}\), so the remaining low-correlation theorem can be attacked as one average \(L_{1+\nu}\) list-size/entropy-energy estimate per active layer.
- Added the near-first-moment entropy-deficit screen. Taking \(\nu=1/\max\{1,\log|\mathcal H|\}\) makes \(C_\nu|\mathcal H|^{\nu/(1+\nu)}=O(1)\), so the local storage check no longer pays an explicit profile-count factor; the remaining theorem only needs a barely-superlinear \(L_{1+\nu}\) moment of \(X_h=W_he^{-I_h}\).
- Added the variational near-first-moment obstruction. The barely-superlinear moment condition is exactly a Gibbs variational bound \(\sup_\mu \mathbb E_\mu(\log W_h-I_h)+h(\mu)/(1+\nu)\), so failure produces one tilted low-correlation profile law. This is the clean dual object for the remaining geometric proof: dominate its entropy-energy value or convert it to the existing dense/star repair.
- Added the Gibbs water-level storage screen. A failing active layer is now represented by the single law \(\mu_h\propto(W_he^{-I_h})^{1+\nu}\) and scalar water level \((1+\nu)^{-1}\log\sum_h(W_he^{-I_h})^{1+\nu}\); storage closes if the level fits the local budget, otherwise the geometric separator only has to repair this one tilted profile law.
- Added the Gibbs-weighted guard-repair criterion. A positive dense/star proposal surplus averaged under the failing Gibbs law flattens into the existing lower-certified guard panel and materializes as one deterministic repair; a surviving obstruction is now exactly high water plus no positive Gibbs-weighted guard panel.
- Added the partition-local Gibbs residual. Any chosen finite partition of the positive profiles localizes a failing water level to one class after only a \(J^{1/p}\) budget loss, and any positive proposal surplus under that class Gibbs law still hardens to one guard-credit repair. This gives range-reporting/list-decoding/tuple-profile separators a finite cell target instead of an arbitrary global Gibbs law.
- Added the count-energy cell screen. Inside a partition cell, the class water level is sandwiched between \(d^-+p^{-1}\log|C|\) and \(d^++p^{-1}\log|C|\), so bounded dynamic-range cells reduce the Gibbs residual to a concrete count-energy excess plus the existing no-positive guard-panel condition.
- Added the singleton profile Gibbs residual. With the singleton partition, a failing Gibbs water level exposes one profile \(h\) with \(\log W_h-I_h-\ell\log2\) above the local budget up to the explicit \(H^{1/p}\) toll; if that profile has any positive certified guard-panel surplus, it hardens to one repair.
- Added the singleton spread-star repair dichotomy. A violating singleton profile is now forced into a single explicit price overflow \(L_h+2\sum_b R_b>\Theta_h\) unless the existing guard verifier fires; thus the next storage theorem is to prove the certified low envelope plus reported star prices fit each local water threshold, not to introduce another Gibbs reformulation.
- Added the partition-cell spread-star repair dichotomy. The same price-overflow bridge now works after grouping profiles into reporter cells: if a class \(\ell_p\) split has low envelope plus reported star prices below its \(J^{1/p}\)-discounted water threshold, the class passes or a one-record guard repair fires. This keeps the live obstruction at the level of a chosen polylog partition instead of forcing singletonization of all raw profiles.
- Added the shared-record partition-cell compiler. A dense/star record shared by many profiles in one cell is now charged through the \(\ell_p\) norm of its profile allocations and one recursive price, rather than duplicate profilewise prices. This connects the existing shared-record LP machinery to the new partition-cell water obstruction and makes the next target a shared allocation certificate.
- Added the aggregate shared-cell water budget. The uniform \(J^{1/p}\) partition toll is no longer part of the target: shared cell costs only need to fit in layer-level \(\ell_p\) aggregate. This is a real overhead reduction for uneven reporter partitions and makes the current storage theorem a single aggregate water-fill inequality.
- Added the layer-global shared-record water compiler. Reporter partitions are now only a certification aid: if allocations to one dense/star record can be certified over the whole active layer, its recursive price is charged once globally instead of once per cell. This removes cross-cell duplicate charging and makes the live storage target a global shared allocation certificate.
- Added the no-discount layer-global water corollary. Under the no-repair invariant, every shared record contributes no more \(\ell_p\)-allocation norm than its recursive price, so the optimal global shared-water certificate is exactly the Gibbs water level. This redirects the storage target from allocation optimization back to proving \((GWL2)\) itself or materializing a positive guard repair.
- Added the dyadic top-prefix water screen. A high Gibbs water level now implies one sorted dyadic prefix with \(x_k k^{1/p}\) above the local budget up to a logarithmic rank-net toll; ruling out all such prefixes proves \((GWL2)\). This turns the corrected direct-water target into an exact prefix/range/list-decoding search problem.
- Added the dyadic prefix spread-star repair bridge. A violating top-prefix floor now feeds back into the same lower-certified guard verifier: if its low envelope plus reported star prices fits the dyadic floor budget, the prefix is ruled out; otherwise the surviving obstruction is one explicit prefix-price overflow rather than a new branching process.
- Added the dyadic prefix low-price residual split. The prefix overflow is now normalized into two scalar branches: either the certified low envelope exceeds its chosen slack share of the dyadic floor budget, or the one-time recursive price of the reported star records exceeds the complementary share. This makes the next proof target explicit instead of leaving an unstructured bad prefix.
- Added the dyadic prefix high-star mass residual. If the low envelope is below its slack share and a dyadic prefix still violates the water envelope, the reported star panel must carry large lower-certified paid mass; in a no-repair residual that panel is price-saturated record by record. This turns the high branch from "large price" into the concrete mass-versus-price saturation object for shared-prefix and integrated-cover compression.
- Added the dyadic prefix shared-capture compression screen. A shared reporter may cover the forced high-star mass by assigning original star records into coarser lower-certified records and leaving explicit tail; if tail plus one-time shared recursive price is below the forced mass, the existing guard verifier fires. Thus the surviving high branch is an exact shared-cover obstruction rather than recordwise duplicate price.
- Added the finite dyadic shared-capture LP. The high-star shared-cover obstruction is now an explicit finite primal/dual program over original star records and candidate shared records; a low LP value closes the dyadic prefix, while a surviving residual gives a dual with no positive-dual-surplus shared record. This makes range/list/AQ/facility proposals exact column-generation oracles.
- Added the one-sided dyadic shared-capture oracle and top-prefix compiler. Candidate generation is now verifier-facing: a seeded cluster, AQ cell, range cell, or decoded list must materialize a lower-certified shared record whose current dual-weighted top prefix beats its one-time recursive price; otherwise the residual gets an explicit weak-type superlevel envelope.
- Added the generated-cell dyadic shared-cover dichotomy. Any finite family of generated cells now either gives a budgeted fractional cover of the high-star mass or exposes one dual weighting whose top-prefix tests fail in every generated cell; the remaining geometry can target that exact no-cell-dual residual.
