- Added the dyadic dual-mass low-cover closure. In a no-cell residual, any set of star records with large \(X_bu_b\)-mass must lack a compatible low-Renyi-price cover by generated cells; otherwise one generated cell already contains a positive top-prefix column.
- Added the no-cell dual entropy witness. A large no-cell dual mass now yields an almost-uniform dyadic layer whose compatible generated-cell cover number is provably large unless the storage-price term already dominates; this turns the remaining residual into a single-layer entropy/list-decoding target.
- Added the no-cell metric-cover screen. A low-doubling, range, AQ, or list-decoded cover of the exposed dyadic layer now closes the residual once each cover part has a generated-template copy; otherwise the layer carries a certified high generated-cover number.
- Added the low-doubling no-cell density screen. Generated-cover growth \(N_{\rm gen}(L,\lambda)\le C\lambda^{-\delta}\) now forces an explicit upper bound on the no-cell layer density \(\theta\); a denser low-doubling layer immediately yields a positive shared top-prefix column.
- Added the polylog low-correlation co-load cutoff. Centered spherical caps at threshold \(T^2=2\log m+O(\log\log m)\) now have only \(m^{1+\rho}\operatorname{polylog}m\) two-point co-load from pair correlations up to \(O(\log\log m/\log m)\), so any polynomial storage obstruction must live in the tiny positive-correlation branch routed to dense/star or quotient separators.
- Added the tiny-positive cap-chain depth bound. A cap margin \(K\log\log n/\log n\) still gives enough certified radius contraction that any genuine recentering lineage inside the localized polylog-radius ball has length \(O_K(\log^2 n/\log\log n)\); the remaining positive-correlation issue is therefore local paid-prefix separation, not superpolylogarithmic geometric depth.
- Added the quadratic cap-margin budget. Along any certified cap-recentering lineage, \(\sum_i\gamma_i^2=O(\log(R_0/r))\), hence only \(O(\log\log n)\) on localized instances. This makes the sharper positive-correlation target a margin-squared local toll: if dense/star shared-prefix repairs can be charged by \(O(\gamma^2)\) rather than by a unit toll, the whole lineage contributes only a polylogarithmic multiplicative factor.
- Added the quadratic positive-correlation toll compiler. This packages the status after the drift audit: the telescoping part is now proved, and the live endpoint obligation is a same-prefix dense/star or shared-prefix repair with local loss \(\exp(O(\gamma^2))\) at cap margin \(\gamma\). A unit-cost repair per recentering is explicitly not enough for the polylog endpoint.
- Added the padded-radius reprice and cap-centered pair-star reporter. A positive-correlation star superlevel should be treated as a cap-centered child of radius \(R\sqrt{1-\gamma^2}\), not merely as a point-centered guard ball. Repricing the padded recursive potential between parent radius and that child costs only \(\exp(O(\gamma^2))\), so the remaining local theorem is the paid-prefix/shared-prefix reduced-gain inequality inside the cap-centered child rather than another radius-depth problem.
- Added the cap-centered paid-prefix quadratic criterion. If a cap-centered prefix has paid degree above its \(e^{B\gamma^2}\)-padded child price, the finite-cover verifier returns a same-prefix dense column and the padded-radius lemma makes it a \((B+3)\)-quadratic repair. If no such prefix exists, all cap-centered paid superlevels obey the explicit weak-type envelope \((CPQ9)\). The positive-correlation endpoint is now reduced to forcing a violation of that envelope, or proving that its residual is already paid by the low-correlation/list-decoding certificate.
- Added the high-correlation star to cap-prefix handoff. Under the standard domination of star multiplicity by cap-centered paid degree, any dyadic high-correlation star whose layer-cake mass beats its cap-centered child price fires as a quadratic local repair. If no repair fires, the whole high-correlation layer is bounded by the cap-centered price envelope \((HCP5)\)/\((HCP7)\). This ties the positive-correlation incidence accounting to the same child scale used by the quadratic radius toll.
- Added the clipped cap-centered handoff and responsibility compiler. Arbitrary high-correlation multiplicity is now split into the part clipped by the cap-centered paid degree \(d_x^+\), which automatically obeys the quadratic cap-prefix price envelope, and the explicit overload \(\sum(M_x-d_x^+)_+\). Any bounded-congestion assignment of high-correlation mass into \(d_x^+\) gives the dominated part by construction, so the remaining positive-correlation obstruction is a concrete overload/transport theorem rather than an unsupported domination hypothesis.
- Added the cap-centered transport Hall normal form. High-correlation atoms now have a finite max-flow/min-cut certificate for assignment into cap-centered paid query capacities \(C\alpha_{p,q}a_x^+(q)\). If the Hall inequalities hold, the flow feeds the responsibility compiler and hence the quadratic cap-prefix verifier; if not, the residual is one explicit Hall cut with atom mass exceeding its paid neighborhood plus allowed tail. This makes the overload theorem a concrete separator target.
- Added the pure-overload star reduction for cap-centered Hall cuts. Any failed cap-centered transport cut, after selecting one admissible center per atom, contains dyadic cap-centered star mass in the positive part \((B_x/C-d_x^+)_+\). This removes the arbitrary set-system shape from the live obstruction: the next separator only has to handle pointwise overload stars whose raw high-correlation atom load exceeds the cap-centered paid degree.
- Added the weighted overload-storage handoff. A pure overload star canonicalized to one shared child can be treated as weighted occurrences with reciprocal weighted multiplicity, so the cohort contributes exactly one child-storage price to the tuple witness congestion. This removes the integer-materialization issue for Hall atoms: the remaining overload branch is a weighted shared-DAG tuple-congestion theorem, not a per-atom recursive branching cost.
- Added the weighted low-correlation co-load cutoff and made the \(|P|\le m\) hypothesis explicit in the original cutoff. The Gaussian low branch is homogeneous in cap multiplicity: weighted or fractional cap panels satisfy the same polylog co-load bound with total cap weight in place of an integer occurrence count. This pairs with the overload-storage handoff, so both the low-correlation and overload-storage branches now accept weighted Hall atoms directly.
- Added the weighted overload-cohort correlation split. For canonical overload children with fixed price class and a Gaussian/list-decoding cap certificate, reciprocal weighted storage now decomposes into a proved low-correlation polylog co-load plus explicit high-correlation cap-centered stars. This is the concrete next interface: deterministic overload stars still need a cap/list-decoding certificate, but once they have one the remaining work is only the high-star paid-prefix/shared-child separator.
- Added the cap-prefix pricing tail for overload-cohort high stars. The high-correlation side now has a no-repair inequality \(\mathcal W_{\rm hi}\le \mathcal W_{\rm over}+2mJ_{\rm pay}\Lambda\): cap-paid high mass is either a quadratic paid-prefix repair or a fixed tail, and the only potentially polynomial survivor is the explicit cap-centered overload already targeted by Hall transport and weighted shared storage.
- Identified the overload-cohort tail with an actual Hall cut. Atomizing each high-cohort incidence \((\kappa,a,b)\) as a singleton-center Hall atom makes \(\mathcal W_{\rm over}\) exactly the cut excess of overloaded point-center pairs. Therefore any large tail yields the existing dyadic pure overload star, and once canonicalized it is charged once by reciprocal weighted storage rather than creating a new residual notion.
- Added exact Hall-tail storage closure. Summing the reciprocal weighted pair congestion of the canonical Hall-tail overload stars over ordered pairs equals the total distinct child storage price, so the remaining Hall-tail storage theorem is precisely the geometric bound on distinct child price rather than another integer-materialization or pair-envelope problem.
- Added benefit-priced Hall-tail materialization. If every retained Hall-tail child has recursive price sponsored by the Hall atom mass that exposed it, then the exact storage closure reduces to a bounded-overlap Hall-mass estimate; otherwise the residual is a concrete unsupported child with too little Hall mass per unit recursive price.
- Added the disjoint sponsored Hall-tail schedule. A build-time materializer that reserves disjoint Hall-atom cohorts for retained benefit-priced children has overlap one, so storage is paid by total Hall-tail atom mass; the remaining proof target is whether enough Hall-tail obstruction can be captured by this disjoint sponsor schedule or whether the unreserved residue triggers another dense/prefix repair.
- Added the fractional sponsor Hall cut. Sponsoring retained Hall-tail children by compatible weighted Hall atoms is now an exact max-flow/min-cut condition: either a fractional sponsor flow pays all child storage from total Hall mass, or a concrete child subset has recursive price larger than all compatible sponsor mass by the chosen \(\beta\) factor.
- Added the sponsor-cut generated-column screen. A failed Hall-tail sponsor cut now feeds directly into generated-column search: any generated cell, range/list-decoded container, additive-code bucket, or seeded cluster whose top child-price prefix captures more cut price than its one-time recursive price is a positive column; if none exists, every dyadic child-price layer satisfies the same metric-entropy lower bound as the no-cell residual.
- Added the sponsor-cut inflate-or-layer reduction. If every sponsor Hall cut is only an \(\varepsilon\)-fractional violation, inflating \(\beta\) to \(\beta/(1-\varepsilon)\) restores the fractional sponsor flow; otherwise a strong cut exposes one dyadic child-price layer carrying a logarithmic fraction of the cut price, which is the concrete generated-cover or spherical-separator target.
- Added the strong sponsor-layer generated-entropy bound. For the exposed dyadic layer, any generated cover whose Renyi price falls below the layer mass yields a positive generated top-prefix column; absent such a column, the layer has an explicit generated-cover lower bound in terms of the sponsor-cut excess, range factor, and child count.
- Added the low-doubling sponsor-layer closure. A low-doubling/range/list-decoded generated cover now closes the exposed sponsor layer whenever its growth exponent satisfies the density or cut-excess threshold; otherwise the surviving non-storage layer has an explicit generated-dimension lower bound.
- Tightened the low-doubling closure statement: the closure screen allows the degenerate scale \(\lambda=1\), but the inverted dimension lower bound explicitly requires \(\lambda<1\).
- Added the sponsor-layer packing bridge. A no-column, non-storage strong sponsor layer now yields a separated packing witness in any certified generated metric, and representatives of that packing feed the same spherical/list-decoding entropy-energy second-moment screen used for quotient spread residuals.
- Added the sponsor-profile star signal. Any weighted sponsor-packing profile whose low-correlation energy payment is insufficient now exposes a dyadic high-correlation star of retained children, giving the generated-column, dense-star, or shared-prefix verifier a finite object to test.
- Added the generated sponsor-star envelope. If those high-correlation sponsor stars inside the failed sponsor cut are certified generated containers, each either fires as a sponsor-cut generated column or the entire high-profile mass is bounded by an integrated generated-price sum weighted by the dyadic coactivation levels.
- Added the no-column sponsor-star price gap. Under the common generated price envelope, any surviving sponsor star is either bounded-cardinality on the sublinear \(Ah^\rho\) side or storage-dominated on the \(\zeta h^{1+\rho}\) side; medium-cardinality high-correlation stars would already be positive generated columns.
- Added the balanced sponsor-star scalar gate. If the forbidden medium-star interval disappears, then \(A^\rho\zeta^{1-\rho}\) is already at the capture scale; otherwise every medium-cardinality sponsor star is a positive generated column and the remaining residual splits cleanly into small-star and large-storage tails.
- Added the bounded sponsor-star fixed-tail reduction. The bounded-cardinality side contributes only \(O(|E|J_\omega\omega_{\max}H_-)\) high-profile mass; any larger high-correlation residual must contain a large storage-dominated sponsor star.
- Added the fractional storage tail for storage-dominated sponsor stars. The large-star branch no longer has to select one dyadic superlevel: after the price-gap split, bounded stars cost the fixed tail, while every storage-dominated star is charged as a fractional child with price factor \(t\psi_\star\zeta\). The remaining sponsor-star theorem is now an integrated distinct-storage tail for these fractional star children, not integer materialization or another dense-branching rule.
- Added the shared-cover LP for the fractional sponsor tail. Duplicated large storage-dominated stars can now be compressed by residual fractional copies plus one-time shared records; failure exposes a dual weighting \(y_s\le u_s^{\rm frac}\) on star children. A generated range/list/AQ/facility template is useful exactly when its current \(y_s\)-weighted top prefix beats the certified shared-child price, otherwise it satisfies an explicit weak-type envelope.
- Added the generated-cover closure for the fractional sponsor tail. A finite generated template family now either covers the fractional storage tail at the desired allowance or exposes one dual \(y_s\) whose top-prefix tests fail in every generated cell. Any dual-heavy layer with a low-Renyi generated cover would fire as a shared-cover column, so the surviving fractional-tail obstruction is a generated entropy/list-decoding witness rather than another large-star materialization branch.
- Added the fractional sponsor-tail packing bridge. A no-cover, non-storage-dominated fractional-tail layer now contains a large separated packing in any certified generated metric; that packing feeds the same spherical/list-decoding second-moment energy screen as the earlier sponsor layers. The fractional large-star branch therefore terminates at the existing low-correlation energy versus high-correlation dense-star signal.
- Added the high-correlation return path for fractional sponsor-tail packings. An energy-deficient high-correlation profile now either contains a generated shared-cover prefix with positive reduced cost, or its dyadic cap stars are bounded by the integrated generated-price envelope. This makes the fractional large-star loop explicit rather than leaving the handoff to dense-star/shared-prefix verifiers in prose.
- Added the cap-star price gap for the fractional-tail return. The returned high-correlation cap stars now have the same no-medium-scale normal form: absent a positive generated shared-cover prefix, every star is bounded-cardinality or storage-dominated, and the whole high branch is a fixed tail plus an integrated fractional storage subtail.
- Added the cap-star storage interface for the fractional-tail return. The storage-dominated cap-star subtail is now an ordinary finite shared-cover LP with the same dual obstruction and generated-prefix target as the fractional sponsor tail, after absorbing dyadic intensity into the child price factor.
- Added the generic fractional child-panel shared-cover compiler. Sponsor-star and cap-star storage tails now share one finite LP, generated-prefix, generated-cover, and weak-type residual interface, so the remaining storage theorem can target priced child panels directly rather than separate star families.
- Added the generic child-panel low-cover and packing screen. The low-cover/entropy handoff is no longer sponsor-specific: any no-prefix priced child-panel residual with a low-price generated cover fires a positive prefix, and otherwise exposes a separated packing in the chosen generated metric.
- Added the generic child-panel packing energy screen. The separated-packing endpoint is now the same second-moment entropy-energy test for any priced child panel; an energy-deficient profile is the only remaining high-correlation return signal.
- Added the generic child-panel high-correlation return. The return uses only cap geometry, dyadic layer cake, and the generic generated-prefix test, so the sponsor-tail high-correlation corollary is now explicitly a specialization rather than a separate mechanism.
- Added the generic returned cap-star storage split. A high-correlation child-panel return now has a branch-independent normal form: positive generated prefix, bounded fixed tail, or storage-dominated priced child panel routed back through the shared-cover and distinct-storage interfaces.
- Added the generic child-panel pair-witness storage identity. For any priced child panel with separated witness sets, total shared storage is exactly the ordered-pair congestion sum, with reciprocal weighted multiplicity for repeated decoded records; this states the remaining list-decoding storage theorem directly in child-panel notation.
- Added the generic sponsor-flow compiler and the unsponsored child-panel obstruction. Exact pair-witness storage is paid whenever exposed Hall/guard/detector atoms fractionally sponsor the child prices; without sponsorship or generated merging, identical witness-set children can duplicate storage arbitrarily, so the remaining theorem must target unsponsored child-price cuts rather than a geometry-only pair-congestion bound.
- Added the generic unsponsored-cut generated-column screen. Weakly unsupported cuts close by constant sponsor inflation; a strong cut exposes one dyadic child-price layer, and absent a positive generated merge that layer has an explicit generated-cover entropy lower bound. This is the cut-local target for range/list-decoding, AQ, facility, or lattice separators.
- Added the generic unsponsored-cut packing and energy handoff. A strong no-merge child-price layer with certified generated-metric balls yields a separated packing; a bucket, spherical, code, or list-decoding law either pays that packing by profile energy or exposes a dyadic high-correlation star for the generated-merge/dense-prefix verifier.
- Added the centered-cap closure for unsponsored-cut packings. At the large-range Gaussian threshold, the exposed packing's first list moment and low-correlation co-load are automatically \(m^{1+\rho}\operatorname{polylog}m\); any remaining polynomial cap-panel residual must be a dyadic high-correlation star routed to the generated-merge or dense-prefix verifier.
- Added the cap-prefix tail for unsponsored-cut cap stars. Clipping the high-correlation cap-panel weights by cap-centered paid degree makes the paid part either fire a same-prefix quadratic repair or shrink to a fixed dyadic tail; the only potentially polynomial survivor is the explicit overload \((\omega-d^+)_+\), which rejoins the Hall/sponsor-flow and shared-child storage interfaces.
- Added the unsponsored cap-overload Hall-tail adapter. The explicit overload \((\omega-d^+)_+\) has a dyadic cap-centered pure-overload star; after canonicalization, reciprocal weighted multiplicity charges one shared child, so the residual enters the generic sponsor-flow/generated-column pipeline rather than a new high-correlation branch.
- Added the generated-column normal form for cap-overload children. Canonical overload children are now either paid by a fractional sponsor flow, merged by a positive generated prefix, or reduced to one strong child-price cut whose dyadic layer has an explicit generated-cover entropy lower bound; the remaining separator target is therefore a concrete range/list/AQ/facility cover theorem.
- Added the average generated-cell separator for cap-overload layers. A randomized tensor/range/AQ/facility/list-decoding proposal law whose layer hit mass beats twice its expected generated-record price contains a deterministic positive merge; a surviving strong layer therefore forces a normalized average Renyi list-size lower bound for every such proposal law.
- Added the fractional generated-cover LP for cap-overload layers. If generated records fractionally cover the exposed layer below half its cut mass, a deterministic positive generated prefix exists; otherwise finite LP duality exposes one weight vector on the layer that every generated record captures only below its certified price.
- Added the cap-overload dual low-cover screen. The LP dual can be dyadically unweighted: any dual-heavy layer with a low-Renyi-price generated cover violates the dual constraint and fires a positive prefix, so a surviving cap-overload obstruction must have high generated-cover entropy unless the explicit storage term dominates.
- Added the cap-overload dual centered-cap endpoint. A dual-heavy layer with certified generated balls now feeds the weighted low-correlation Gaussian cap cutoff; any polynomial excess is forced into a dyadic high-correlation cap star, and such a star fires immediately when its generated record price is below its dual mass.
- Added the cap-overload dual cap-star storage split. The returned high-correlation stars now have no surviving medium-cardinality regime after generated-prefix search; the residual is a bounded fixed tail plus a storage-dominated priced child panel routed back to the generic shared-cover/distinct-storage interfaces.
- Added the cap-overload storage-tail shared-cover reduction. The storage-dominated overload stars now have their own finite shared-cover LP, exact dual, and generated top-prefix column rule, so the cap-overload high-correlation return exposes the same weighted no-prefix residual as the sponsor-tail pipeline.
- Added the cap-overload storage-tail generated-cover handoff. A failed generated cover for the overload storage tail now yields one dual-heavy layer with the same low-cover entropy lower bound and separated generated-metric packing witness as the generic child-panel pipeline.
- Added the cap-overload storage-tail packing endpoint. A separated overload-tail packing is now paid by the common entropy-energy screen, or an energy-deficient high-correlation profile either fires a generated prefix or returns only a bounded fixed tail plus another priced child panel.
- Added the common child-panel residual endpoint. The generic child-panel, unsponsored-cut, sponsor-tail, and cap-overload branches are now explicitly routed to one conditional min-cost split-profile certificate; the paper no longer presents these as separate live theorem obligations, and it keeps the warning that unsponsored duplicate children cannot be bounded by geometry alone.
- Added the common child-panel single-profile density endpoint. After sponsor/generated-prefix and low-correlation alternatives are removed, one sampled-center split profile with polynomial dynamic range and inverse-polylog sampled-center price now suffices for the global polylog endpoint, so the live theorem is a single-profile density statement rather than an aggregate panel-coordination problem.
- Added reduced-cost singleton-center and top-prefix router search primitives. This turns the k-means/k-means++ practical intuition into a proof-facing oracle format: clustering methods may propose centers or containers, but only certified benefit-minus-recursive-price surplus is retained, and exact finite reduced-cost maximization is complete for the certified family.
- Added the common child-panel shared-integrated endpoint. The sharper live target is now a constant-fraction shared integrated cover for one split profile, where a dense record can capture mass across many layer-cake cuts while paying recursive price once; the sampled-center inverse-polylog density theorem remains a fallback formulation.
- Added the template-complete shared-cover obstruction and fixed the shared-integrated truncation constants. The \(D/4\) template-cover target now has an exact finite dual: a high-value no-prefix vector whose every certified container satisfies the top-prefix/clipped-tail envelope. The previous \(1/(2n^{K_{\rm dyn}})\) truncation only gave a non-strict endpoint margin, so it is now \(1/(4n^{K_{\rm dyn}})\).
- Added the transported clipped-tail panel separator and transport form of the template obstruction target. The high-value no-prefix dual can now be attacked by a weighted panel of bounded-congestion transported subsets; any positive aggregate affine-tail surplus hardens to one deterministic shared top-prefix column, so the remaining geometric theorem can be stated as finding such a transport panel for every \(TCO\) obstruction.
- Added the flow-value clipped-tail screen and max-flow form of the template obstruction target. Explicit transported loads can now be replaced by finite max-flow values; a positive aggregate flow-value surplus fires a shared top-prefix column, while a failed flow proposal leaves the localized min-cut certificate \((CTV6)\).
- Added the opened-support flow screen and endpoint. Flow proposals now pay \(\Theta\) only for sink points that actually receive transported mass, so support selection, cohort materialization, and bounded-congestion transport are scored by one branch surplus \(\Phi/C-\Theta|\operatorname{supp}|\); the older fixed-subset max-flow screen is a corollary.
- Added the fractional opened-support flow LP and endpoint. The nonconvex opened-support search now has a sound fractional relaxation with exact dual \(\sum_i(\mu_i/C)\alpha_i+\sum_p(d_p\beta_p-\Theta)_+\); positive LP surplus still forces a clipped-tail violation, while failure leaves finite source/point prices as the next obstruction.
- Added the thresholded dual normal form for failed fractional opened-support flow. Any failed LP now splits into at most \(L/\eta\) normalized source load paid by large \(\alpha\)-prices plus a low-\(\alpha\) source neighborhood whose shared-degree tail is clipped at \(\Theta/(1-\eta)\), giving the next geometric target a flat residual rather than an arbitrary failed proposal.
- Added the source-retention form of failed fractional flow. If the proposed source system has normalized load \(M_{\rm src}\), then failure still leaves a low-\(\alpha\) subsystem of load at least \(M_{\rm src}-L/\eta\) with the raised clipped-tail cap; in the no-prefix half-threshold case this loses only \(4K\Theta\) source load.
- Added the common retained residual lemma for many failed flow screens. When many proposal families share the same normalized source measure, failed LP screens delete only \(\sum_j L_j/\eta_j\) source mass, leaving one common residual that satisfies every raised clipped-tail cap; this makes heavy build-time proposal search additive instead of another branching overhead.
- Added the adaptive retained residual lemma for sequential failed flow screens. Later proposal families may depend on earlier failures; as long as each screen uses the current retained source measure, the final residual loses only \(\sum_t L_t/\eta_t\) source mass and inherits every tried screen's raised clipped-tail cap.
- Added the value-retained failed-screen normal form. The adaptive retained residual now carries obstruction value explicitly: failed screens spend \(\Delta_T=\sum_t L_t/\eta_t\), and if this does not pay down the dual value, a high-value residual remains that satisfies every transcript tail cap.
- Added the adaptive fractional-flow exhaustion endpoint. The remaining build-time theorem is now a finite transcript-exhaustion statement: every high-value no-prefix obstruction must either yield a half-threshold fractional flow screen or admit no high-value residual satisfying all failed-screen caps.
- Added the obstruction-sourced flow exhaustion specialization. The adaptive source measure can now be forced to be the exposed no-prefix dual mass \(b_a u_a\); proving that no half-value atom residual survives the failed-screen caps is enough to close the adaptive flow endpoint.
- Added the failed-transcript residual LP certificate. Surviving residual subsets are relaxed to a finite LP, and its dual pays source mass either directly or through weighted failed-screen tail capacities; an LP value below half the exposed \(b_a u_a\) mass certifies transcript exhaustion.
- Added the fully fractional failed-transcript accounting lemma. A failed opened-support screen now shrinks source intensities by \((1-2\alpha_i)_+\) using its own LP dual, losing only \(4K\Theta\) value and preserving a weighted tail cap; after any all-failed transcript the final intensity vector is feasible for the residual LP. This removes the integer-retained-subset interpretation from the adaptive accounting layer.
- Added capped residual-LP continuation. The adaptive build loop can now solve the current capped residual LP, use its optimizer as the next source measure, and update the cap after a failed screen; this prevents previously paid source mass from reappearing while proving each failure decreases residual LP value by at most \(4K\Theta\).
- Added the LP-guided capped-flow exhaustion endpoint. The ANN endpoint now follows from a finite build loop that repeatedly solves the capped residual LP, proposes a screen against its optimizer, and either finds a half-threshold opened-support screen or certifies the capped LP has fallen below half the obstruction value before half that value has been spent.
- Added the downward residual normal form for LP-guided search. A capped residual optimizer \(z\) now becomes the smaller no-prefix dual \(u^z\), so the geometric separator no longer has to reason about the whole failed transcript, only about the current downward residual dual and the cap LP that selected it.
- Added the capped atom-capture support screen. For a downward residual \(u^z\), any proposed support \(S\) whose monotone-submodular capped capture \(\sum_a u_a^z\min\{b_a,\sum_{p\in S}h_{B,p,a}\}\) beats the affine clipped support price yields the opened-support LP surplus and hence one positive top prefix; no-prefix residuals obey the reverse inequality for every materializable support.
- Added the fractional capped-capture threshold screen. A proposal can now output fractional point weights \(x_p\); if the layer-cake average of capped atom capture over threshold supports beats \(\Theta\sum_p x_p+2K\Theta\), scanning thresholds produces one deterministic support and hence a positive shared top prefix.
- Added the routed capped-capture support screen. A geometric reporter may now certify atom-to-support routed mass \(r_{a,p}\le h_{B,p,a}\) with per-atom cap \(\sum_p r_{a,p}\le b_a\); routed mass above the same affine support price lower-bounds capped capture and therefore exposes a positive shared top prefix.
- Added averaged routed capped-capture proposals. Randomized build-time proposal laws can now be analyzed by expected one-sided routed surplus; positive expectation implies some deterministic sample verifies, with a Las Vegas repetition bound \(1-\exp(-N\Gamma/M_{\rm prop})\).
- Added the low/high compressed-cap storage screen. For a centered cap bucket net, \(K^{1+s}\le K^2\) reduces the shared-DAG storage moment to the diagonal, low-correlation ordered-pair co-load, and one realized high-correlation pair tail. The first two are polylogarithmic at threshold \(T^2=2\log m-O(\log\log m)\), so any polynomial compressed-bucket storage survivor is exactly a high-correlation tail that must be sponsored or repaired by the dense/star verifier.
- Added cap-prefix pricing for compressed-cap high tails. The realized high-correlation tail is clipped by cap-centered paid near-pair degree; the paid part either fires a quadratic same-prefix dense repair or contributes only a fixed dyadic tail. The only potentially polynomial survivor is now the overload \(H_{\rm over}\), the same Hall/sponsor object handled by the later overload-storage machinery.
- Added the compressed-cap overload Hall-tail handoff. The overload \(H_{\rm over}\) is exactly the excess of a cap-centered Hall cut whose atoms are ordered high-correlation pairs; failed transport exposes a dyadic pure overload star, and canonicalizing that star charges its weighted incidences by one shared child price through reciprocal multiplicity.
- Added the compressed-cap generated-column endpoint. After canonicalizing overload stars, raw \(M_x(y)\) remains the occurrence weight for exact shared-child storage, while the smaller excess \(O_x(y)\) is the sponsor mass. A successful sponsor flow pays storage by \(H_{\rm over}\); otherwise the residual is a single strong generated-cover LP dual layer.
- Added the compressed-cap low-cover endpoint. The generated-cover LP dual layer now dyadically unweights to a dual-heavy subset; any certified low-price range/list/AQ/facility/tensor cover or generated-metric ball cover would violate the dual and produce a positive generated prefix, so a survivor must exhibit a concrete entropy/packing lower bound.
- Added the compressed-cap packing-energy endpoint. A dual-heavy compressed-cap packing now feeds into the usual second-moment entropy-energy screen; centered cap panels pay one-point and low-correlation pair mass polylogarithmically, while any remaining high-correlation star must either be a positive generated prefix or have generated price at least its dual mass.
- Added the compressed-cap cap-star storage split. Nonmergeable high-correlation stars now obey the standard forbidden-interval rule: medium stars would be positive generated prefixes, small stars form a fixed tail, and large storage-dominated stars become a priced child panel for the generic shared-cover and distinct-storage certificates.
- Added the diffuse compressed-cap rung certificate. The random-position/low-correlation case now closes directly: success-normalized active load plus absence, or certified polylog boundedness, of the high-correlation net-pair tail implies the full compressed-bucket rung checklist after verified resampling. The remaining worst-case work is explicitly the positive-correlation tail.
- Added the compressed-cap high-tail storage-only corollary. The paid positive-correlation side either closes the compressed rung or fires same-prefix quadratic repairs whose lineage product is polylogarithmic; after this screen the only live positive-correlation storage object is the overload \(H_{\rm over}\).
- Added the flattened returned child-panel closure. Storage-dominated high-correlation returns can now be enumerated into one finite build-time generated-cover LP; either the returned panels are paid collectively, or a single no-prefix dual satisfies every reachable top-prefix test, so the remaining overload work is one geometric/list-decoding separator rather than an adaptive return loop.
- Added the flattened dual panel screen. Against the single no-prefix dual produced by returned-panel closure, any lower-certified weighted proposal panel with positive aggregate surplus hardens to one deterministic generated top-prefix column; failed panels become exact dual budget inequalities for range/list/AQ/facility/lattice separators.
- Added the flattened panel-separator endpoint. The overload/returned-panel branch now has a conditional nodewise endpoint: each flattened closure is either paid by its generated-cover LP value or a lower-certified proposal panel hardens to one positive top-prefix column; if this holds for all returned residuals, the shared-panel separator gives the polylog query and space bounds.
- Added the flattened dual dyadic low-cover screen. A surviving flattened no-prefix dual can now be reduced to one almost-uniform heavy layer; any certified assigned range/list/AQ/facility/lattice cover of that layer must satisfy an explicit cover-number/list-size lower bound, otherwise the lower-capture panel hardens to a positive generated top prefix.
- Added the flattened dyadic packing witness. Once generated-metric balls are materializable templates, the almost-uniform flattened layer either has a low-cover violation that hardens to a positive prefix or contains a separated packing at the same entropy scale for the spherical/list-decoding energy screen.
- Added the flattened dyadic entropy-energy handoff. The flattened packing now feeds directly into the standard second-moment screen: a certified bucket/list-decoding law either pays the separated packing by profile energy, or a finite high-correlation star is exposed for the generated-prefix and smaller-radius dense verifiers.
- Added the flattened high-correlation star screen. A star returned by the flattened entropy-energy handoff now either forms a one-record lower-certified panel for the flattened dual, contributes only a bounded fixed tail, or becomes a storage-dominated returned child record in the format handled by the flattened closure LP.
- Added the budgeted flattened star closure. After prefix search, a high-correlation profile now has an explicit scalar bound by its low part, bounded star tail, and returned-storage price; failure to pay the profile certifies proportional returned-storage price for the closure/sponsor separator.
- Added the returned-star sponsor-cut normal form. An unpaid flattened high-correlation profile now either has its returned-star storage paid by sponsor flow, fires a generated shared merge, or exposes a strong dyadic returned-star price layer with the same generated-cover/list-size obstruction as the generic unsponsored-cut branch.
- Added average and fractional generated-cover screens for returned-star layers. A strong returned-star sponsor cut is now closed by any proposal law whose marginal hit beats expected generated price, or else exposes one dyadic dual-heavy sublayer with an explicit low-price generated-cover lower bound.
- Added the returned-star dual packing-energy handoff. The dyadic dual-heavy returned-star layer now has the same terminal interface as the flattened and cap-overload duals: materializable generated balls force a separated packing, entropy-energy pays low-correlation profiles, and a high-correlation survivor is a finite star whose generated price either violates the returned-star LP dual or is charged as an explicit bounded/storage tail.
- Added the returned-star tail shared-cover adapter. The second returned-star storage tail is now routed back into the ordinary finite shared-cover LP interface, so it is either paid at shared-canonical price or exposes a top-prefix dual obstruction already covered by the flattened closure universe.
- Added the sharp LP/SA soft-decoder reduction. A branch-and-bound decoder whose relaxation upper bounds are within \(O(\sqrt{\log\log n})\) of the true subproblem optimum visits only a polylogarithmic enlargement of the Gaussian threshold list; the remaining theorem is now explicitly the Gaussian pseudocodeword-spectrum bound for a low-rate code relaxation.
- Added the anchored Gaussian-width certificate for LP/SA sharpness. If every fractional pseudocodeword in a branch subproblem can be anchored to a true codeword with excess directions of Gaussian width \(O(\sqrt{\log\log n})\) and summable concentration loss, the existing branch-and-bound decoder has the required polylog overhead. The entry also records the warning that uniform certification over a polynomial prefix universe with constant-radius excess sets is too weak, so a real code theorem must either shrink the anchored radius, work on the near-threshold node process, or use a build-certified small node universe.
- Added false-survivor accounting for LP branch-and-bound. Visited nodes now split into ancestors of true codewords above a lowered threshold plus false survivors whose relaxation gap beats the remaining slack; the sufficient LP/SA theorem is a joint tail bound on anchored excess exactly on subproblems whose true maximum is below the relevant slack level. This removes the need to union-bound sharpness over the full prefix universe.
- Added the exponential false-survivor certificate. Under anchored excess domination, every low-maximum surviving node must satisfy \(M(u)+X_u\ge T\), so a single tilted moment bound on these crossings controls the entire false-survivor contribution. The remaining LP/SA theorem is now a Palm-style moment estimate below the \(T-\gamma\) true-codeword frontier, rather than either a full-prefix union bound or a binned probability sum.
- Added the conditional tilted-excess certificate. The false-survivor moment follows if, at the Gaussian tilt \(\lambda=T/b\), each branch subproblem has \(\E[\exp(\lambda X_u)\mid W(c),c\in C_u]\le\log^{O(1)}n\); the true maximum part is then paid by \(\sum_u e^{\lambda M(u)}\le(H+1)\sum_c e^{\lambda W(c)}\), requiring only one-codeword Gaussian marginals and no independence among codeword scores.
- Added the conditional Gaussian projection certificate. Conditioning on true codeword scores decomposes anchored pseudocodeword excess into projected bias plus independent orthogonal Gaussian width/radius; proving these are polylogarithmic at \(\lambda=T/b\) is now enough for the conditional tilted-excess LP/SA target.
- Added the false-survivor projected-bias and Gram-form projection certificates. The LP/SA target no longer needs uniform projected-bias control over all formal prefixes: it suffices to bound the tilted projected-bias weight on false-surviving nodes, and this bias has an exact expression through the codeword-overlap Gram matrix and the anchored excess overlap vectors.
- Added the shifted overlap-coefficient and well-conditioned Gram certificates. Projected bias on false-survivor nodes now splits into scalar leakage plus negative coefficient mass times score gaps below the frontier, and a well-conditioned overlap Gram matrix turns small excess-overlap norm and sparse coefficients into small projected radius and negative mass.
- Added the balance-witness scalar-leakage certificate. For q-ary simplex relaxations, each coordinate-block all-ones vector is a witness with \(A^\top e_i=\mathbf 1\) and \(\langle e_i,h\rangle=0\), so scalar leakage \(\kappa\) is zero when the witness lies in the local codeword span and otherwise is paid by the existing orthogonal residual.
- Added the frontier-gap cancellation certificate for negative coefficients. If the negative coefficient mass is at most one, the false-survivor exponential weight cancels the gap from \(M(u)\) to \(T-\gamma\), leaving only internal score gaps \(M(u)-W(c_j)\) to control in the LP/SA projected-bias target.
- Added the leader-shift near-leader LP/SA certificate. The clean terminal route shifts the overlap-coefficient bound at the true local maximum \(M(u)\), so exact scalar balance preserves the usual \(\sum_u e^{\lambda M(u)}\) codeword-tail charge while reducing the projected-bias theorem to negative coefficients supported only on near-leader true codewords, plus the already separated orthogonal width/radius terms.
- Added the Boltzmann negative-tail LP/SA certificate. Near-leader support is no longer a hard requirement: it suffices to bound the internal partition function \(\sup_h e^{\lambda M\kappa(h)}(1+\sum_j a_j^-e^{\lambda(M-W_j)})\), so far-below-leader codewords may carry negative coefficient mass only if their exponentially weighted tail remains polylogarithmic at the Gaussian tilt.
- Added the cumulative internal-tail LP/SA certificate. The Boltzmann negative-tail condition is now reduced by layer-cake to an exponential decay bound on \(A_{u,h}(s)=\sum_{M-W_j\ge s}a_j^-\), or a finite dyadic shell sum, making the remaining low-rate code theorem a concrete cumulative tail-decay statement.
- Added the support-function internal-tail certificate. Cumulative negative tail mass is exactly \(\sup_{0\le r\le1_{S(s)}}\Phi_u(-A_uG_u^+r)\), so the remaining LP/SA theorem can be stated as a finite separation bound for down-score selectors rather than direct bookkeeping of negative Gram coefficients.
- Added the anchor-fiber internal-tail certificate. After fixing the anchor map, each down-score selector tail check decomposes into explicit LP objectives over anchor fibers \(P_{u,c}\), so a failed negative-tail theorem returns a concrete selector, anchor, and fractional point witness.
- Added the LP gap-scale sharpness corollary and no-good cut clarification. The Gaussian tail ratio shows that \(O(\sqrt{\log\log n})\) additive LP sharpness is tight up to constants for polylog overhead, and no-good cuts are only an implementation of duplicate-free enumeration; each residual node still needs the same sharpness or false-survivor certificate.
- Added the anchor-fiber LP/SA compiler. The chain from fiber separation to cumulative tails, Boltzmann negative-tail control, orthogonal residuals, and conditional tilted excess is now one local checklist: balance/negative-mass normalization, anchor-fiber LP separation, and orthogonal residual bounds with polylog slack imply polylog branch-and-bound overhead.
- Added the singleton down-influence certificate. The arbitrary selector \(r\) in the anchor-fiber LP separation can be eliminated by support-function subadditivity: it suffices to bound per-codeword influences \(I_{u,c,j}\) and prove their cumulative exponential decay below the local leader.
- Added the singleton Boltzmann-influence certificate. The dyadic selector-tail target can be weakened further to one tilted influence partition function \(\sum_j e^{\lambda(M-W_j)}I_{u,c,j}\) per anchor fiber, which plugs directly into the Boltzmann negative-tail certificate and is better aligned with planted/tilted LP or SA analyses.
- Added the simplex-envelope influence compiler. For ordinary q-ary product-simplex fibers, each singleton influence is bounded by the coordinate envelope of the inverse-Gram objective \(-AG^+e_j\), so the Boltzmann influence target reduces to envelope decay by distance/overlap shells plus a planted tilted score-enumerator bound.
- Added the mobility-envelope influence compiler. The unrestricted product-simplex envelope exposes a likely barrier: diagonal inverse-Gram overlap terms are too large under Boltzmann gap weights unless anchor fibers are contracted. The new certificate replaces the full simplex envelope by per-symbol off-anchor mobility bounds \(\mu_{c}(i,a)\), making the remaining LP/SA target either mobility decay on dangerous symbols or aggregate selector cancellation.
- Added the aggregate mobility selector certificate. Instead of singletonizing \(-AG^+r\), it bounds the whole selector objective through the same mobility constraints before taking positive parts. This preserves cancellations among inverse-Gram columns and gives both a dyadic selector certificate and a direct Boltzmann-box certificate \(\sup_{0\le r\le e^{\lambda(M-W)}}\mathfrak M_c(r)\le\Xi\).
- Added the box-dual mobility certificate. The aggregate selector box is exactly dual to an off-anchor mobility multiplier box: the remaining condition is a tilted positive-part enumerator over true codewords for every mobility pattern \(\beta\), preserving aggregate inverse-Gram cancellation while returning a concrete obstruction if the bound fails.
- Added the Laplace-tail mobility enumerator compiler. The box-dual positive-part enumerator is exactly a layer-cake integral over weighted tails, and it is implied by a standard Laplace/Chernoff bound \(\sum_j w_j e^{\nu L_\beta(j)}\). The remaining low-rate code theorem is now a familiar tilted tail or moment bound for every certified mobility pattern.
- Added the balanced Gram form of mobility scores. Each off-anchor mobility pattern \(\beta\) is now an ambient block-balanced direction \(d_\beta\), and the scalar obstruction is exactly \(L_\beta(j)=-(G^+A^\top d_\beta)_j\). The live LP/SA theorem is therefore a one-sided Laplace or negative-part bound for inverse-Gram coefficients of balanced directions under the Boltzmann tilt, rather than a vague pseudocodeword-support condition.
- Added score-leader anchoring and the diagonal-preconditioned leader-mobility split. The LP/SA certificates may anchor a node at its current true score leader because the leader is measurable under the conditioned codeword scores; this removes the large Boltzmann penalty from the anchor self-column. Expanding \(G^+\) around \(b^{-1}I\) then splits the mobility obstruction into explicit self-mobility \(B_\beta/b\), an off-anchor planted enumerator for \((A^\top d_\beta)_-\), and a residual inverse-Gram conditioning error.
- Added the coordinate-overlap load closure. The off-anchor planted enumerator is now bounded by weighted list-recovery loads \(\Lambda_i=\sum_{j:c_j(i)=c_\star(i)}e^{\lambda(M-W_j)}\) times the remaining per-coordinate mobility, plus the inverse-Gram preconditioning residual. This exposes the next code theorem as small score-leader coordinate overlap on mobile coordinates, not an arbitrary positive-part moment.
- Added the convex-hull residual mobility certificate. The score-leader closure is now explicitly treated as diagnostic: it can charge real low-score codewords as movement from the leader. The new certificate compares each LP point to a point in the true codeword convex hull first, so integral codewords have zero residual and only genuinely fractional positive mass is charged through a transported balanced-direction inverse-Gram tail bound.
- Added the residual Hall verifier. A proposed residual budget \(\mu\) is valid iff every nonnegative symbol-score objective has relaxation advantage over true codewords at most \(\langle y,\mu\rangle\); a failed budget returns the exact pseudocodeword separator \((y,z)\). This turns the convex-hull witness assumption into a finite oracle check using codeword maximization plus relaxation maximization.
- Added priced residual-gap duality. The minimum weighted positive residual distance from a relaxation point to the true codeword convex hull equals a clipped nonnegative score-table gap \(0\le y\le r\); for inverse-Gram selector directions this gives an objective-wise separator and avoids committing to one global coordinate residual budget.
- Added the direct coefficient projection certificate. Instead of anchoring a relaxation point to a leader or a convex witness, represent its projection in the true-codeword span by coefficients \(b\) with \(Ab=\Pi z\). True codewords and convex combinations can choose nonnegative coefficients, so the projected LP/SA obstruction is exactly negative score-span coefficient mass with large Boltzmann internal-gap tail, plus the orthogonal Gaussian width of \(P_u\).
- Added the negative coefficient tail dual verifier and scalar-leakage witness. The minimum weighted negative coefficient mass over representations \(Ab=\Pi z\) is dual to a finite-dimensional clipped score-span separator \(-\omega_j\le\langle y,c_j\rangle\le0\); using the combined price \(\omega^\lambda+\Xi\mathbf 1\) forces one representation to have both total negative mass at most one and Boltzmann tail at most \(\Xi\). The scalar coefficient sum is handled by the coordinate-block balance witness and the existing orthogonal residual.
- Added the near-optimal score-gap form of the tail verifier. Any signed clipped separator from the coefficient-tail dual can be block-shifted into a nonnegative score table whose true-codeword deficits are bounded by the same prices; hence the negative-tail theorem follows from an ordinary LP-decoding gap bound on these near-optimal score bands. This is the cleaner finite code target for LP/SA: rule out fractional optima above the best true codeword for tilted nonnegative score tables, rather than analyze signed separators directly.
- Added the finite score-band LP/SA compiler. The local theorem now has one input: for combined prices \(\Omega_j=e^{\lambda(M-W_j)}+\Xi\), every \(\Omega\)-near-optimal nonnegative score band must have relaxation gap at most \(\Xi\). Together with the coordinate-balance witness and orthogonal Gaussian width, this directly implies the conditional tilted-excess moment and hence polylogarithmic branch-and-bound overhead.
- Added the band-limited residual-budget compiler. The score-band gap condition follows from a valid one-sided residual budget \(\mu\) whose mass against every \(\Omega\)-near-optimal nonnegative score band is at most \(\Xi\). Thus the LP/SA code target can be phrased as residual localization: fractional mass must be Hall-coverable by true codewords and invisible to all score bands competitive with the local leader.
- Added the min-chart verifier for band-limited residuals. The remaining band-mass supremum over block-shifted near-optimal score tables is now exactly the maximum of \(q^b\) ordinary LPs, one for each block-minimum chart. This is a useful build-time-only reduction: a failed residual localization certificate exposes a concrete chart and score-span table, so the live LP/SA obstruction is finite and inspectable rather than a loose universal quantifier.
- Added the deficit-transport dual for min charts. Each chart LP now has an explicit positive certificate: the block-centered residual budget is neutralized, modulo the true-codeword orthogonal complement, by free upward moves away from chart minima plus a zero-total signed combination of true codewords whose positive part is paid by the near-leader prices. This turns the residual-localization theorem into a concrete finite transport/majorization target for low-rate code relaxations.
- Added the codeword-score normal form of the min-chart dual. The positive certificate is equivalently the Gram-system equation \(h_\tau(\mu)+h_\tau(\alpha)+G_u\theta=0\), with \(\alpha\ge0\), zero-total \(\theta\), and positive \(\theta\)-mass paid by near-leader prices. This removes ambient projection notation from the residual-localization target and leaves a finite codeword-incidence/Gram majorization problem.
- Added the off-chart majorant form of the min-chart certificate. Since the centered residual \(d_\tau(\mu)\) is itself a chart-cone move \(K_\tau(\mu)\), the auxiliary upward variables can be absorbed into one majorant \(\zeta\ge_\tau\mu\). The live residual-localization target is now: for every chart, find \(h_\tau(\zeta)+G_u\theta=0\) with zero-total \(\theta\) and small near-leader-priced positive mass.
- Added the canonical zero-sum inverse certificate for min-chart residuals. The off-chart target can be checked by the minimum-norm zero-sum inverse \(-G_{u,0}^\dagger h_\tau(\zeta)\); in a coordinate-balanced subcode with scalar Gram action on \(1^\perp\), this reduces further to an \(\Omega\)-weighted lower-tail bound for the chart-incidence scores \(h_\tau(\zeta)\). This isolates the diagonal/random-code model that would close the band residual condition.
- Added the perturbed scalar zero-sum certificate. Exact scalar Gram action is not necessary: if the balanced codeword Gram matrix is within spectral error \(\delta\) of \(\chi I\) on \(1^\perp\), the canonical inverse certificate pays the scalar lower tail plus the explicit perturbation term \(\delta\|\Omega\|_2\|h_\tau(\zeta)\|_2/(\chi(1-\delta))\). This gives a realistic finite random-code/Tanner-code target: prove coordinate balance, row regularity, a Gram spectral bound, and a chart-incidence lower-tail bound.
- Added the range-restricted inverse and chart-majorant dual certificates. The Gram condition no longer has to hold on the full zero-sum coefficient space, which is impossible for large panels because the codeword Gram has rank at most \(bq\); it only has to give a scalar-like right inverse on selected chart-score vectors. The majorant choice itself is now optimized by an LP whose dual obstruction is a price-capped selector with chart-dominated coordinate histograms, turning the remaining theorem into a weighted list-recovery/shadow bound.
- Added the penalized chart-majorant dual and uniform residual shadow form. The inverse-error norm term can be absorbed into the majorant optimization by allowing an \(\ell_2\)-bounded signed blur of the chart-dominated selector. For symbol-uniform residual budgets \(\mu_{i,a}=\nu_i\), the diagonal obstruction is exactly the soft chart excess \(\sum_i\nu_i(qs_i(\tau_i)-\sum_j s_j)\), making the next target an explicit weighted list-recovery bound rather than an inverse-Gram expression.
- Added the posterior plurality and collision-energy form of the chart shadow. The chart supremum is exactly the weighted plurality bias of coordinate posteriors induced by a price-capped selector \(s\le\Omega\). A Cauchy bound reduces this to the centered overlap quadratic form \(s^\top B^\nu s\), so the current random-code/Tanner-code target is a box-restricted second-moment bound over the internal-gap price box.
- Added the positive-overlap tail certificate for chart shadows. The box-restricted quadratic form is bounded by the layer-cake integral of weighted above-random codeword-pair overlaps, with a Laplace pair-enumerator sufficient condition. This turns the selector supremum into a concrete tilted pair-tail theorem under the same internal-gap prices.
- Added the singleton shadow obstruction. A singleton selector at codeword \(c_j\) costs exactly \(\omega_j\) times the residual mass off the symbols of \(c_j\); uniform residual budgets therefore require bounded prices. The full internal-gap proof must use localized/mobility residual budgets around high-price codewords, or split off the far score tail with a separate near-leader certificate.
- Added the localized posterior-mismatch form. For arbitrary residual budgets, the chart supremum is exactly \(S(s)\sum_i m_i^\mu(\max_a\pi_i^s(a)-\langle r_i^\mu,\pi_i^s\rangle)\). This turns the next proof target into matching residual coordinate distributions to high-price selector posteriors, which is the right interface for anchor-fiber mobility bounds.
- Added the finite dual for localized residual feasibility. A residual budget must satisfy Hall lower bounds and posterior-mismatch upper bounds; Farkas duality says failure is exactly a mixture of Hall gap score tables dominated coordinatewise by chart-mismatch directions but with value exceeding the chart budget. This makes the next localized-residual theorem a concrete dominated-mixture separation target.
- Added the source-level localized residual LP. Instead of arbitrary Hall score-table mixtures, the shared residual budget can be optimized directly over relaxation sources and true-hull witnesses; its dual is a convex combination of chart-mismatch directions dominating source-local Hall score tables. This is a cleaner finite normal form for the remaining Tanner/SA/list-recovery theorem.
- Added the shared-envelope distance form. For a fixed chart-mismatch aggregate \(D\), the residual value is the \(D\)-weighted coordinatewise positive envelope left after projecting every exposed relaxation source to the true codeword hull; maximizing this over convex combinations of chart selectors recovers the source-level LP. The remaining theorem can now be stated without Hall score tables.
- Froze the LP/SA route at the finite score-band / chart-incidence target instead of adding another certificate form. Added a formal uniform full-price chart-shadow barrier: singleton charts force any uniform residual budget to pay \((q-1)V_\nu\max_j\Omega_j\), so full internal-gap prices are impossible at polylog cost unless the score range is already truncated. Added `impl/chart_tail_stress.py` and tests; small balanced random-panel sweeps show the full positive-overlap tail is almost entirely diagonal while off-diagonal clipped tails stay constant-scale, pointing to localized residual matching or a formal impossibility result as the next real step.
- Extended the chart-tail diagnostic to test the tempting single shared Boltzmann-posterior residual budget. It reduces the singleton lower bound only by a constant factor at full prices (about \(1.2\cdot10^{10}\) mean singleton cost on the \(512\times32,q=8\) sweep), and the new corollary proves why: a single posterior residual is cheap only if high-price codewords almost agree with the global price posterior in every coordinate. The next real target is anchor/source-local residual matching, not a shared posterior budget.
- Tested exact anchoring of the largest internal-gap prices as a possible escape from the singleton barrier. This also fails in the random-panel diagnostic: reaching the clipped polylog singleton budget needed about \(74/128\), \(172/256\), and \(361/512\) exact anchors. The new exact-anchor truncation corollary explains the issue: largest \(\Omega_j\) means far below the leader, so anchoring large prices tries to cover almost the whole low-score tail. The remaining theorem must instead prove that residual/negative coefficient mass avoids that far tail.
- Added the Gaussian clipped near-leader size lemma and matching diagnostic counters. In the iid Gaussian score model, clipping \(e^{\lambda(M-W_j)}\) at \(\log^A N\) leaves only \(O(\log^{A+2}N)\) expected near leaders. The stress test agrees qualitatively: near-leader counts were about \(23/128\), \(34/256\), and \(64/512\), but the complementary far tail still carried more than \(0.9996\) of the Boltzmann price mass. This pins the unresolved theorem to tail-avoidance by LP/SA coefficients, not list-size.
- Added the necessary Boltzmann tail-decay lemma and diagnostic mass budgets. If \(\sum b_j e^{\lambda g_j}\le\Xi\), then cumulative coefficient mass beyond gap \(s\) must be at most \(\Xi e^{-\lambda s}\). On the \(512\times32,q=8\) sweep with \(\Xi\) equal to the polylog price cap, the allowed mass is about \(3.6\cdot10^{-2}\) at the median gap, \(6.7\cdot10^{-4}\) at the 90th percentile gap, and \(2.7\cdot10^{-6}\) at the maximum gap. This is the concrete decay rate a low-rate LP/SA theorem must prove.
- Added the finite-range Boltzmann threshold and toy coefficient-tail diagnostic. The exact layer-cake identity shows three regimes: decay exponent \(\alpha>\lambda\) gives a range-free budget, \(\alpha=\lambda\) pays the score window length, and \(\alpha<\lambda\) must spend \(e^{(\lambda-\alpha)G}\). On the \(512\times32,q=8\) sweep, normalized profiles \(b_j\propto e^{-\alpha g_j}\) had mean budgets \(4.4\cdot10^6\), \(2.7\cdot10^4\), \(1.3\cdot10^2\), and \(6.7\) at \(\alpha/\lambda=0,1/2,1,3/2\); meeting the \(\log^3N\) cap needed mean \(\alpha/\lambda\approx0.90\). This refines the barrier: the theorem must prove near-\(\lambda\) cumulative decay or explicitly use finite score-range slack.
- Added a direct tiny score-band LP verifier for \((LNN2)\), using min-chart/max-chart enumeration and a dependency-free simplex solver. The sanity checks pass: the full binary cube has zero product-simplex gap, while deleting one binary corner exposes a unit gap. Random balanced toy panels show that the bare product-simplex relaxation is not the theorem: with \(\Xi=(\log N)^3\), worst gap ratios were about \(10.0\) for \(6\times3,q=2\), \(2.5\) for \(8\times3,q=2\), and \(3.0\) for \(9\times2,q=3\). This is a concrete barrier, not a reformulation: the clean ANN route must use actual LP/SA/Tanner constraints to kill missing-chart pseudocodewords.
- Extended the score-band verifier to fixed-source and local-projection pseudowords. True codewords have zero fixed-source gap, while the missing binary corner has the same unit gap as the product-simplex search. Pairwise local checks are not enough in the parity toy model: the length-3 even-parity code has four odd integral pseudowords passing every pair projection, with worst unit-price score-band gap \(1/2\). For the binary random toy panels, all pair projections left integral local pseudowords that attained the same worst \(\text{gap}/\Xi\) ratios as product-simplex (\(10.0\) and \(2.5\) in the two sweeps). The next real code theorem needs local list-recovery/Tanner expansion strong enough to exclude high-price missing charts.
- Added the integral score-band obstruction corollary. Any integral pseudocodeword surviving a proposed LP/SA/Tanner relaxation must have fixed-source score-band gap at most \(\Xi\); otherwise \((LNN2)\) fails immediately. A useful toy distinction emerged: for \(N=8,b=4,q=2\), all triple projections removed integral local pseudowords, but the product-simplex score-band gap still had worst ratio about \(7.4\). After integral missing charts are removed, the next diagnostic has to solve the fractional local-marginal score-band LP.
- Added the fractional local-marginal score-band diagnostic. The stress harness now has a dependency-free phase-I equality simplex for local check assignment variables with shared unary marginals, then evaluates sampled local-marginal sources using the fixed-source score-band LP. Pairwise local checks still reproduce the integral obstruction exactly. Triple projections behave differently in the toy panels: for \(N=8,b=4,q=2\), sampled local-marginal ratios stayed below one (max about \(0.91\) over sixteen seeds) while product-simplex stayed at \(7.4\); in an \(N=12,b=4,q=2\) seed scan, the clean seeds with no integral local pseudowords had sampled fractional ratio at most \(0.41\), and over-budget local-marginal cases coincided with integral pseudowords. This is evidence, not proof, but the next barrier now has to be an explicit fractional local-marginal over-\(\Xi\) source.
- Added exact tiny local-marginal vertex enumeration. The reduced checker eliminates explicit unary variables, enumerates basic feasible local-check marginals under consistency, projects them to unary sources, and then maximizes the fixed-source score-band gap over all projected vertices. This is exact for tiny panels because the gap is convex in the source. It confirms the weak pairwise barrier exactly, and on the worst sampled clean triple seed \(N=8,b=4,q=2,\text{seed}=46\) it enumerated \(646646\) bases, \(145\) local vertices, and \(37\) projected sources; the exact local-marginal ratio was the sampled \(0.906\), while product-simplex was \(6.87\) and no integral local pseudoword existed.
- Formalized the local-marginal projected-vertex obstruction in `paper.tex`. For a finite local-marginal relaxation \(Q_u\), the score-band gap \(\Gamma_u(x;\Omega_u)\) is convex in the projected unary source \(x\), so \((LNN2)\) for \(S_uQ_u\) is equivalent to checking all projected local vertices. This justifies the exact verifier as a theorem rather than a heuristic. An independent-basis enumeration shortcut was tested, but it was slower than the simple basis loop on the \(N=8,b=4,q=2,\text{seed}=46\) exact case, so it was discarded.
- Added the convex-hull dominance screen for local vertices. If every projected local vertex \(v\) contains a true-code convex-hull submass \(1-\delta(v)\), then its score-band gap is at most \(\delta(v)\) times the product-simplex gap. This is a real sufficient condition for \((LNN2)\), but the seed-46 diagnostic shows it is not sharp enough alone: the worst sampled fractional source has dominance deficit \(0.25\), product ratio \(6.87\), and dominance bound \(1.72\Xi\), while the exact local-marginal ratio is \(0.906\). The remaining proof needs either smaller dominance deficits from stronger local constraints or a localized product-gap bound for the residual direction.
- Added a localized residual dominance diagnostic. For the seed-46 worst exact source \(((1,0),(1/2,1/2),(1/2,1/2),(1/2,1/2))\), the max-mass dominance residual is \(0000\), with residual ratio \(3.62\), so the localized charge \(0.25\cdot3.62=0.906\) matches the exact source gap and closes that source. But scalar dominance still fails as a theorem: the sampled source \(((1/2,1/2),(1/2,1/2),(0,1),(1/2,1/2))\) has exact ratio \(0.664\), while an arbitrary max-mass residual can be \(1111\) and charges \(1.72\Xi\). The next proof obligation is residual orientation or score-aware dominated-mass selection, not another dominance-deficit reformulation.
- Added clean-seed reporting to the fast sampled local-marginal scan and reran the triple-projection panels. For \(N=8,b=4,q=2\), a 64-seed scan with 64 random objectives and four closure rounds had 55 seeds with no integral local pseudoword, zero clean over-budget fractional cases, and clean maximum \(0.906\Xi\) at seed 46; the overall sampled maximum \(1.17\Xi\) was integral. For \(N=12,b=4,q=2\), the analogous 64-seed scan had 25 clean seeds, zero clean over-budget fractional cases, and clean maximum \(0.533\Xi\); the overall sampled maximum \(1.62\Xi\) was again integral. This supports the frozen target and says the next counterexample search should optimize fractional vertices after integral pseudowords are excluded, not keep rechecking the product simplex.
- Replaced the primary exact local-marginal source enumerator with a projected-source H-representation path. Each local check projection is converted from allowed assignments to facets in reduced unary coordinates, then the global projected source polytope is enumerated by active facets. This is the exact object needed for the convex fixed-source gap, and it avoids enumerating local-check bases that project to non-vertices. Seed 46 now checks \(58905\) projected active sets, finds 8 projected-source vertices, and reproduces the exact \(0.906\Xi\) gap in about 4.6s; the old local-basis enumeration checked \(646646\) bases and found 37 projected images. For \(N=12,b=4,q=2,\text{seed}=48\), exact projected enumeration checks \(73815\) active sets, finds 9 projected-source vertices, and confirms the sampled clean ratio \(0.533\Xi\) in about 3.5s.
- Tightened the formal local-marginal obstruction from projected images of all local-check vertices to vertices of the projected unary source polytope \(S_uQ_u\). This matches the exact verifier and is the right normal form: convexity of \(\Gamma_u(\cdot;\Omega_u)\) only requires checking \(\operatorname{vert}(S_uQ_u)\); projected images of non-exposed local vertices are harmless extra points. The convex-hull dominance screen now uses the same projected-source vertex set.
- Made the projected-source facet converter affine-aware, so lower-dimensional local projection hulls add their affine hull as paired halfspaces instead of falling back to slow local-basis enumeration. With exact clean reporting enabled, the \(N=8,b=4,q=2\) 64-seed triple-projection scan had 55 clean seeds, zero clean over-budget exact fractional cases, and clean maximum \(0.918\Xi\) at seed 24; the overall exact maximum \(1.17\Xi\) was integral. The \(N=12,b=4,q=2\) 64-seed exact scan had 25 clean seeds, zero clean over-budget exact fractional cases, and clean maximum \(0.601\Xi\) at seed 4; the overall exact maximum \(1.62\Xi\) was integral. This upgrades the previous sampled evidence to an exact finite check for these panels.
- Added clean-seed reporting for the dominance and residual-dominance screens and found a finite barrier to that sufficient proof route. In the \(N=8,b=4,q=2,\text{seed}=3\) triple-projection panel, there are no integral local pseudowords and the exact projected-source ratio is \(0.750\Xi\), but max-mass residual dominance charges \(1.483\Xi\) on the source \(((1/2,1/2),(1/2,1/2),(0,1),(1/2,1/2))\) by leaving residual \(((1,0),(1,0),(0,1),(0,1))\). Along the natural maximum-mass subtraction ray the residual bound only increases from the exact \(0.750\Xi\) to \(1.483\Xi\), so mandatory true-code subtraction is counterproductive here. This does not falsify the frozen projected-source target; it says a proof cannot be just scalar dominance deficit plus max-mass residual orientation.
- Probed the next binary size \(N=10,b=5,q=2\) with all 4-coordinate checks. Exact projected-source enumeration initially hit the active-set cap: one seed would need \(13019909\) raw active sets at the \(10^6\) limit. A sampled local-marginal closure run with 32 seeds, 128 random objectives, and six closure rounds had no integral local pseudowords, no sampled clean over-budget fractional source, and clean maximum \(0.541\Xi\). This was only sampled obstruction-search evidence; it was not certifying and the exact verifier still needed a better vertex enumeration strategy.
- Added global halfspace deduplication to the projected-source enumerator, keeping the tightest duplicate normal. This resolves the \(b=5\) enumeration cap for the random panels: \(N=10,b=5,q=2,\text{seed}=0\) drops from \(71\) raw halfspaces and \(13019909\) active sets to \(27\) halfspaces and \(80730\) active sets. More importantly, exact enumeration finds the sampled search had missed a clean fractional obstruction. In an 8-seed exact scan with all 4-coordinate checks, every seed had zero integral local pseudowords but one seed was exact over budget. For seed \(3\), the projected source \(((1/2,1/2),(1/2,1/2),(0,1),(1/2,1/2),(1,0))\) is feasible in the original local-marginal equality formulation and has exact score-band ratio \(18.36\Xi\). Thus all \((b-1)\)-projection consistency is not a viable low-rate theorem by itself; the next proof must use stronger Tanner/SA structure or exclude this fractional pattern directly.
- Added a full-overlap local-marginal diagnostic. On the same \(N=10,b=5,q=2,\text{seed}=3\) panel, requiring the 4-coordinate check marginals to agree on their complete pairwise intersections gives a 47-variable, rank-38 equality LP. The over-\(\Xi\) projected source above is infeasible in that LP, and optimizing its exact separating score table over the overlap-consistent relaxation gives no positive gap over the true codeword maximum. This suggests the missing ingredient is genuine overlap consistency between local views, not a new score-band reformulation.
- Added sampled score-band closure for the overlap-consistent relaxation. On \(N=10,b=5,q=2\) with all 4-coordinate checks, a 64-seed overlap-only scan with 128 random objectives and six closure rounds had 63 clean seeds, zero clean sampled gap, zero clean over-budget cases, and zero unary integrality defect for every sampled overlap source. The only nonzero overlap gap was \(0.293\Xi\) on the one non-clean seed with an integral local pseudoword. This supports the next concrete theorem target: prove projected-unary integrality, or find an exact fractional overlap vertex, for a real Tanner/SA low-rate construction.
- Added the q-ary caveat for that theorem target. Binary random panels and parity toys had integral unary projections under full-overlap consistency, but a ternary \(b=4\) random panel has a feasible overlap-consistent source with unary integrality defect \(4/3\). Therefore the overlap route should be stated as a binary or construction-specific integrality theorem plus integral-pseudoword exclusion, not as a blanket q-ary local-marginal fact.
- Added the projected-unary integrality reduction. If a local relaxation's projected unary vertices are integral, then \((LNN2)\) reduces exactly to the integral score-band obstruction on those words; if those words are all true codewords, the local score-band gap is zero. This makes the overlap route precise: prove binary/construction-specific projected-unary integrality and integral-pseudoword exclusion. Added diagnostics around this statement: a helper for unary integrality defect, a binary parity regression where sampled full-overlap unary sources are integral but include missing pseudowords, and the ternary fractional-source regression showing the theorem cannot be q-ary without extra structure.
- Tightened the overlap-integrality target again after finding a tiny binary caveat: the \(b=3\) panel \(\{001,100,010\}\) admits the all-half unary source under pair-overlap consistency, with integrality defect \(3/2\). Thus even binary overlap consistency is not enough in full generality. The theorem target has to use construction-specific structure of the low-rate/Tanner/SA panel, plus integral-pseudoword exclusion.
- Added an exact support-oracle projected-hull diagnostic for the overlap relaxation. It optimizes unary source objectives over the overlap LP, enumerates facets of the hull of found projected sources, and re-optimizes each facet normal; if no facet is violated, the found projected hull is certified. Regressions now distinguish the tiny binary fractional vertex \(\{001,100,010\}\) from the parity case where projected vertices are integral but include missing pseudowords. The exact \(N=10,b=5,q=2\) leave-one-out sweep certified all 64 seeds: 63 clean seeds had only true-codeword projected vertices and exact overlap score-band gap zero, while the only nonzero gap was the known non-clean seed with one integral pseudoword at \(0.293\Xi\). The previous seed-3 \(18.36\Xi\) unary-glued obstruction collapses to 10 true-codeword overlap vertices. A larger \(N=12,b=6,q=2\) leave-one-out hull sweep certified all 64 seeds, all clean, with no fractional projected vertices and no integral pseudowords (max source count 12, max facet count 46). This is now the theorem target rather than another certificate form: prove projected-unary integrality and integral-pseudoword exclusion for the leave-one-out overlap/Tanner construction, or find a certified fractional projected vertex in that model.
- Proved a concrete sufficient version of that theorem target. If every two-coordinate deletion of the local panel is injective, equivalently the panel has minimum Hamming distance at least 3, then the leave-one-out full-overlap relaxation is exactly the true codeword hull: overlap consistency identifies the punctured local distributions by their common codeword labels. Therefore all projected vertices are true codewords and the finite local score-band gap is zero by the projected-unary integrality reduction. Added a regression with a length-5 binary distance-3 panel whose certified overlap hull has exactly the true codeword vertices. This is a real construction path, not another certificate form; the remaining question is how cheaply the ANN construction can enforce this low-rate distance condition while preserving the intended exponent.
- Closed the combinatorial cost side of that construction path. A greedy packing in \([q]^b\) gives minimum-distance-3 panels of size at least \(q^b/(1+b(q-1)+\binom b2(q-1)^2)\). Thus a requested local list size \(N\) can be supported with \(q^b\le N\operatorname{poly}_q(\log N)\) for fixed alphabet. Combined with the two-deletion overlap closure, the low-rate leave-one-out separator has zero local score-band gap and only polylogarithmic ambient-size slack. The remaining subpolynomial-overhead question should now move to the score-tail truncation/routing layer, not to projected local pseudowords.
- Updated the front matter to reflect the current proof boundary. The low-rate structured-panel separator is no longer presented as an open LP/SA certificate: distance-three leave-one-out overlap panels close the finite score-band target with polylogarithmic slack. The old mixed-frontier work assumption is also no longer the preferred endpoint, because the canonical budget-thinned one-tree route already pays activated geometric work by optional stopping. The remaining clean \(n^{\rho_c}\operatorname{polylog} n\) target is now frozen as saturated aggregate value delivery for the budget-thinned tame-route trie: every route cut should carry inverse-polylogarithmic activation mass, equivalently a route law should have polylogarithmic cut energy against the activation prefix weights.
- Froze the aggregate-value target in a finite verifier form rather than adding another certificate reformulation.
