Added `impl/route_value_verifier.py` with unit tests for the exact finite algebra: saturated Bellman value equals route-cut min-cut on tiny tries, least superharmonic majorants compute the antichain obstruction, superharmonic potentials induce prefix-dominating route laws, and water-filled allocations obey the transition-density bound. In `paper.tex`, added `prop:accepted-route-certificates-imply-value`: an accepted route trie or compressed DAG with the existing superharmonic antichain check, transition-aware water-filled allocation, and \(H_0\log C=O_c(\log\log n)\) directly implies the saturated route value required by `asm:aggregate-value-delivery` and has \(F_4(m,R)\operatorname{polylog}n\) reporter work. The remaining theorem is now concrete: construct such decoded route skeletons for the tame-route process, or find an antichain-charge counterexample.
- Proved the first finite barrier for that accepted-route verifier. In a complete \(B\)-ary balanced prefix trie of depth \(H\), with homogeneous child charge \(a_*(u)=B^{-\rho(\ell+1)}\) at level \(\ell\), the deepest internal level is an antichain of total charge \(B^{H-1-\rho H}\). Therefore the superharmonic root check \(\Phi^*(\varnothing)\le C/H\) would require \(C\ge H B^{H-1-\rho H}\), exponential in \(H\) for \(\rho<1\). Added a regression showing the finite verifier computes this obstruction. This confirms that the full balanced prefix skeleton is not the construction to certify; the route skeleton must be contracted, leader-quotiented, or posterior-decoded before applying the aggregate-value proposition.
- Connected the existing boosted leader-quotient calculation to saturated aggregate value. Added the harmonic Bellman recursion to `impl/route_value_verifier.py` and regressions for the harmonic-versus-saturated depth comparison and a boosted single-leader chain. In `paper.tex`, added `cor:boosted-leader-skeleton-value-delivery`: `cor:boosted-leader-skeleton-toll` gives \(W(r_{s_0})\ge e^{-2}/L(s_0)\), and `lem:harmonic-saturated-comparison` gives \(W\le U\), so a polylog-depth boosted leader skeleton has the inverse-polylogarithmic saturated value required by `asm:aggregate-value-delivery`. This sharpens the remaining constructive theorem: build output-sensitive leader reporters with inverse-polylog hidden pure value at each nonunit posterior handoff, instead of trying to certify the full balanced prefix trie.
- Updated the finite-channel numerical stress test to match that sharpened target. `impl/finite_channel_sweep.py` now reports the boosted-leader repetition proxy \(\log(1/\varepsilon)/H_\eta(p,q)\), with \(\varepsilon=(H+1)^{-2}\), at median, 90th, and 99th percentiles; zero hidden value is preserved as an infinite obstruction rather than being averaged away. The paper now says this is the diagnostic quantity for the leader-reporter route: lower-tail failures of \(H_\eta\) are precisely local boosted-leader obstructions.
- Ran the boosted-leader diagnostic instead of adding another certificate form. Commands: `python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 300,1000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels gaussian,whitened_gaussian,cross_polytope,pca,landmark --scales 0.5,1,2,4,8 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-leader-critical.csv`; `python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 300,1000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels gaussian,whitened_gaussian,cross_polytope,pca,landmark --scales 0.5,1,2,4,8 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-leader-isolated.csv`; `python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 3000,10000 --d 32 --queries 240 --panels whitened_gaussian --scales 4,8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-leader-isolated-focused.csv`. The critical random-sphere run had all 150 boosted-overhead quantiles finite, but mean guard density \(0.502\) and best \(90\%\) overhead proxy \(30.1\), so it is guard-masked. In isolated planted-pair runs, whitened Gaussian scale 8 gave \(90\%\) overhead \(8.97\)--\(10.68\) at \(m=300\) and \(10.37\)--\(11.84\) at \(m=1000\), with \(H_\eta\) lower \(5\%\) quantiles \(0.452\)--\(0.539\) and \(0.446\)--\(0.502\). Focused \(m=3000,10000\) whitened scale 8/12 runs kept \(90\%\) overhead \(11.57\)--\(16.57\), \(99\%\) overhead \(19.89\)--\(39.75\), and \(H_\eta\) lower \(5\%\) quantiles \(0.289\)--\(0.406\). The support lower quantile was essentially one and Hessian medians were \(10^8\)--\(10^{10}\), so the positive signal is posterior contraction/leader selection rather than well-conditioned curvature. Cold scale 0.5 is a real obstruction: non-whitened panels had \(90\%\) overhead about \(10^3\)--\(2.3\cdot10^3\), and whitened panels still had \(1.6\cdot10^2\)--\(3.0\cdot10^2\). This points the frozen theorem target at tuned balanced-whitened output-sensitive leader reporters, or at a barrier showing the observed posterior contraction cannot be made worst-case uniform.
- Added the exact soft-surplus decomposition to the finite-channel diagnostic: \(H_\eta=A_\beta\mathbf E_W(1-(\tau/R_p)^\beta)_+\). This is not a new certificate target; it prevents over-reading the hard margin test. `impl/finite_channel_sweep.py` now reports soft surplus, tilted top-outcome mass, and the fraction of \(H_\eta\) carried by the top tilted outcome. Regression tests check that the decomposition exactly reproduces the original \(H_\eta\) formula. A compact isolated sweep (`python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 1000,3000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels whitened_gaussian --scales 4,8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-leader-winner.csv`) shows the tuned positive regime is typical-winner-led but not uniformly argmax-only: for scales \(8,12\), lower \(5\%\) soft surplus is \(0.468\)--\(0.793\), lower \(5\%\) tilted-top mass is \(0.433\)--\(0.611\), and median top-outcome contribution is \(0.950\)--\(0.997\), while the lower \(5\%\) top-outcome contribution is only \(0.441\)--\(0.632\). The next proof route should therefore be a winner reporter plus a certified lower-tail backup, not a theorem requiring all useful mass to satisfy a large fixed posterior margin.
- Extended the same diagnostic to cumulative top-\(k\) tilted prefixes and added `cor:top-k-tilted-backup`: if the top \(k\) hidden tilted outcomes carry a \(\gamma\) fraction of the soft-surplus value, then reporting those \(k\) top-\(\xi\) posterior children preserves \(\gamma H_\eta\) while paying only \(k\)-label work. This is a proof hook, not a new certificate form; the query implementation still has to recover the witness-dependent prefix. The compact isolated sweep (`python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 1000,3000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels whitened_gaussian --scales 4,8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-leader-topk.csv`) showed that for the tuned scale \(8,12\) regime, top two outcomes captured lower \(5\%\) contribution fraction \(0.724\)--\(0.962\), top four captured \(0.988\)--\(1.000\), and top eight captured all measured value. The next constructive target is now sharper: a constant-size hidden tilted-prefix reporter, plus a proof that the top-four-style lower-tail capture is uniform or a counterexample showing it is not.
- Added query-visible top-\(k\) contribution diagnostics and `cor:query-top-k-backup`. This is the first check that turns the hidden prefix into an implementable object: \(Q_k(q)\) is the top \(k\) outcomes under \(K_q\), so a retained top-\(k\) softmax reporter could produce it without knowing the near witness. The compact isolated sweep (`python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 1000,3000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels whitened_gaussian --scales 4,8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-leader-query-prefix.csv`) showed query top four matches the hidden top-four lower tail in the tuned regime: for whitened scales \(8,12\), query top four captured lower \(5\%\) contribution fraction \(0.988\)--\(1.000\), and query top eight captured all measured value. Query top two was weaker (\(0.724\)--\(0.962\)). The remaining theorem target is now concrete and query-facing: prove a uniform lower tail for the query top-four softmax prefix, and implement/report those four labels output-sensitively for a structured balanced-whitened panel.
- Added `prop:query-top-k-channel-compiler`, which does the missing arithmetic instead of just naming a reporter: if a structured finite channel can report the query top \(k=\operatorname{polylog}m\) outcomes in \(d\operatorname{polylog}m\) time and those labels carry \(H_{\xi,k}^{\rm qry}\ge\operatorname{polylog}^{-1}m\), then \(O(\xi^{-\rho}\operatorname{polylog}m)\) groups give constant local success, stored child space remains \(m^{1+\rho}\operatorname{polylog}m\), and recursive work stays \(d m^\rho\operatorname{polylog}m\) for every \(\xi\ge1/m\). Thus the \(B\simeq\xi^{-1}\) enumeration factor is gone from query work. A larger query-prefix check (`python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 10000,30000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels whitened_gaussian --scales 8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-leader-query-prefix-large.csv`) preserved the signal: query top four captured lower \(5\%\) contribution fraction \(0.963\)--\(0.999\), query top eight captured all measured value, \(H_\eta\) lower \(5\%\) quantiles were \(0.273\)--\(0.374\), and the boosted-overhead \(90\%\) proxy was \(12.21\)--\(17.56\). The remaining proof is now very specific: build a structured balanced-whitened panel with output-sensitive top-four softmax outcome reporting and prove the query-prefix lower tail uniformly.
- Added the query-to-tilted prefix stability lemma and diagnostic fields. The lemma proves a concrete sufficient route: if the query top-\(k\) log-probability gap \(g_k(q)\) beats twice the centered tilt radius \(\Delta_{p,q}\), then the hidden tilted top \(k\) labels are exactly the query top \(k\) labels. This also gives a barrier certificate: if tilted top-\(k\) leaves the query prefix, the query boundary gap is small. The focused stress tests (`python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 1000,3000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels whitened_gaussian --scales 4,8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-query-stability.csv` and `python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 10000,30000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels whitened_gaussian --scales 8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-query-stability-large.csv`) show this simple proof route is too conservative: for tuned scales \(8,12\), query top four still captured lower \(5\%\) contribution fraction \(0.988\)--\(1.000\) at \(m=1000,3000\) and \(0.963\)--\(0.999\) at \(m=10000,30000\), but the lower \(5\%\) stability margin \(g_4-2\Delta\) stayed negative (about \(-1.86\) to \(-0.91\) in the compact run and \(-1.44\) to \(-0.70\) in the large run), with only \(55.0\%\)--\(69.4\%\) and \(55.0\%\)--\(68.1\%\) of rows certified stable. Thus the lower-tail proof cannot be just worst-case top-\(k\) order invariance; it must use weighted surplus concentration, top-eight backup, or extra retained-panel structure.
- Strengthened the same stability lemma to a containment certificate and tested the natural top-eight backup. If the query rank-\(k\) to rank-\(L+1\) gap \(g_{k,L}\) beats \(2\Delta_{p,q}\), then the hidden tilted top \(k\) labels are contained in the query top \(L\) labels. The focused sweeps (`python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 1000,3000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels whitened_gaussian --scales 4,8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-query-containment.csv` and `python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 10000,30000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels whitened_gaussian --scales 8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B-mult 4 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-query-containment-large.csv`) show that this is the right explanation for top-eight: despite top-four equality stability certifying only \(55\%\)--\(69\%\) of rows, hidden top-four-in-query-top-eight containment certified \(100\%\) of compact tuned rows and \(99.4\%\)--\(100\%\) of large tuned rows. The lower \(5\%\) containment margin \(g_{4,8}-2\Delta\) was \(2.83\)--\(7.60\) in the compact tuned runs and \(2.23\)--\(4.30\) in the large tuned runs; hidden top-four already carried \(0.970\)--\(0.999\) lower \(5\%\) value in the large tuned runs. The proof target is now narrower and positive: prove the hidden top-four surplus lower tail and the \(g_{4,8}-2\Delta\) lower tail for the structured balanced-whitened panel, while implementing an output-sensitive query top-eight reporter.
- Added the first implementable top-eight finite-channel candidate. `product_sign` and `whitened_product_sign` panels have \(B=2^r\) sign labels and factorized logits, so `product_sign_topk_indices` reports exact query top \(k\) labels by enumerating the \(k\) smallest bit-flip penalty subset sums; tests compare it against explicit score sorting. The sweep preserves this structure by using zero label offsets for product-sign panels. The unwhitened product-sign panel is a barrier: in `python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 1000,3000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels product_sign,whitened_product_sign,whitened_gaussian --scales 4,8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B 16 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-product-sign.csv`, unwhitened scale \(8,12\) had \(\mathcal H_\eta\) lower \(5\%\) only \(0.041\)--\(0.159\) and boosted-overhead \(90\%\) proxies \(33.5\)--\(117.4\). Whitened product-sign is viable but weaker than fully balanced whitened Gaussian: in the compact run, scale \(8,12\) had \(\mathcal H_\eta\) lower \(5\%\) \(0.348\)--\(0.475\), query top-eight lower \(5\%\) \(0.996\)--\(1.000\), and exact containment certification \(99.4\%\)--\(100\%\). The larger check (`python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 10000,30000 --d 32 --queries 160 --panels whitened_product_sign,whitened_gaussian --scales 8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B 16 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16 --csv /tmp/lsh-product-sign-large.csv`) kept \(\mathcal H_\eta\) lower \(5\%\) at \(0.212\)--\(0.333\), query top-eight at \(0.997\)--\(1.000\), hidden top-four at \(0.908\)--\(0.994\), and top-four-in-top-eight containment at \(99.4\%\)--\(100\%\). Thus the current constructive branch is no longer an unstructured reporter assumption: prove the hidden top-four and containment lower tails for whitened product-sign panels with bit-additive offsets, or find a worst-case obstruction to that structured panel.
- Resolved the fixed-width product-sign containment subproblem instead of adding another certificate form. In a product-sign channel, the query gap \(g_{k,L}\) is exactly the difference between the \((L+1)\)-st and \(k\)-th subset sums of the bit flip penalties \(2|u_a(q)|\), while the centered query-to-tilted perturbation radius is exactly \(\beta\|u_p-u_q\|_1\). A bounded-density subset-sum anti-concentration union bound therefore gives inverse-polylogarithmic containment failure for fixed \(B\) random Gaussian bit panels at \(\beta=\Theta(1/\log m)\). A targeted width stress test using `python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 10000,30000 --d 32 --queries 200 --panels whitened_product_sign --scales 8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B B0 --near-corr 0.95 --route-depth 16` with \(B0\in\{8,16,32,64\}\) says to keep the constructive branch fixed-width or grow \(k\): \(B=16\) had \(H_{q05}=0.237\)--\(0.394\), hidden top-four lower \(5\%\) \(0.921\)--\(0.998\), query top-eight \(0.999\)--\(1.000\), and containment \(100\%\); \(B=32\) had \(H_{q05}=0.299\)--\(0.467\), hidden top-four \(0.821\)--\(0.954\), query top-eight \(0.973\)--\(0.999\), and containment \(98.5\%\)--\(100\%\); \(B=64\) improved \(H_{q05}\) to \(0.354\)--\(0.501\) but fixed top-eight leaked, with hidden top-four \(0.700\)--\(0.882\), query top-eight \(0.896\)--\(0.986\), and containment \(95.5\%\)--\(99.0\%\). The unresolved fixed-top-eight theorem is now the posterior-surplus lower tail for hidden top-four labels of the whitened product-sign panel, not the query/tilted containment event.
- Added direct restricted-value tails to the finite-channel sweep (`top4_value_q01`, `top4_value_q05`, `query_top8_value_q01`, `query_top8_value_q05`) so the frozen top-eight theorem is measured pointwise instead of by separate \(H_\eta\) and contribution-fraction quantiles. The focused stress test `python impl/finite_channel_sweep.py --n 10000,30000 --d 32 --queries 220 --panels whitened_product_sign --scales 8,12 --seeds 0,1,2 --B B0 --near-corr corr --route-depth 16` with \(B0\in\{16,32\}\) and `corr` in \(0.90,0.95,0.98\) kept hidden top-four direct value \(q05\) in \(0.116\)--\(0.579\) and query top-eight direct value \(q05\) in \(0.124\)--\(0.602\). The weakest 1% case was \(B=16,\text{corr}=0.90,m=30000,\text{scale}=12,\text{seed}=1\): hidden top-four value \(q01=0.0340\), query top-eight value \(q01=0.0353\), and \(H_\eta q01=0.0353\). Thus fixed-width prefix truncation is not the measured bottleneck; the remaining proof has to lower-bound product-sign posterior rank thresholds and soft surplus, i.e. \(H_\eta\) itself.
- Decomposed the same direct-value stress rows into affinity and soft surplus. Across the \(B0\in\{16,32\}\), `corr` in \(0.90,0.95,0.98\) runs, affinity \(q05\) stayed in \(0.444\)--\(0.942\), while soft-surplus \(q05\) ranged from \(0.180\) to \(0.700\). In the weakest row, the top-four value was essentially \(H_\eta\), and \(H_\eta\)'s lower tail came from the soft-surplus/posterior-threshold term rather than from tilted affinity. This pins the next proof obligation to a fixed-width product-sign posterior rank threshold lower tail.
- Added posterior-threshold margin-curve columns to `finite_channel_sweep.py` for \(\Delta=0.25/\alpha\) and \(0.5/\alpha\), with 1% and 5% lower quantiles of both tilted good mass and certified value. The focused product-sign runs (`B=16,32`, near correlations `0.90,0.95`, `n=10000,30000`, scales `8,12`) show the old \(\Delta=1/\alpha\) margin certificate is too cold: its value lower 5% is zero in several rows. The \(\Delta=0.5/\alpha\) margin is still intermittent, also hitting zero for \(B=16\). The first robust-looking scale is \(\Delta=0.25/\alpha\): good-mass lower 5% \(0.277\)--\(0.997\), certified-value lower 5% \(0.036\)--\(0.175\). The 1% tail remains fragile in the hardest \(B=16,\text{corr}=0.90\) run (certified value \(0.0012\)), so the next theorem is specifically a quarter-log product-sign posterior-rank lower tail, not a full-log margin statement.
- Added an exact product-sign posterior-score lemma: \(\log R_s(x)=\Psi_s(x)-\log(2^r\pi_s)\), with \(\Psi_s(x)=\sum_a(s_au_a(x)-\log\cosh u_a(x))\). Thus \(R_s(p)\ge e^\Delta\tau_s\) is exactly \(\Psi_s(p)\ge Q_{s,\eta}^{\rm node}+\Delta\), and the output-mass imbalance cancels from rank margins. Added `finite_channel_threshold_stress.py` to compare empirical node thresholds with held-out spherical thresholds for the same panel. On the focused product-sign cases (`B=16,32`, correlations `0.90,0.95`, `ref_samples=100000`), held-out threshold log errors were modest (median \(0.025\)--\(0.138\), 90% \(0.085\)--\(0.310\)), and replacing empirical thresholds by held-out thresholds left \(H_\eta\), soft-surplus, hidden top-four value, and quarter-log certified value nearly unchanged. The quarter-log theorem should therefore be distributional for the bit score \(\Psi_s\), with empirical quantile transfer handled separately.
- Corrected the margin scale needed for the clean endpoint. `prop:query-top-k-channel-compiler` only needs \(h_m=\log^{-O(1)}m\), so a fixed posterior-rank margin \(\Delta_0=O(1)\) already gives the allowed \(1/\log m\) factor through \(1-e^{-\beta\Delta_0}\). Added `cor:bounded-rank-margin-polylog-value` to make this explicit. This also resolves the fixed-width ceiling: \(\Psi_s(x)\le r\log2\), so quarter-log margins cannot be the right asymptotic obligation at fixed \(r\). New sweep columns for constant margins show the bounded target is stable: at \(\Delta_0=1\), tilted good-mass q05 was \(0.624\)--\(0.998\), certified-value q05 was \(0.0368\)--\(0.0831\), and certified-value q05 divided by \(\alpha\) was \(0.38\)--\(0.79\) across `B=16,32` and correlations `0.90,0.95`. The fixed-width product-sign theorem target is now distributional bounded-margin posterior rank for \(\Psi_s\), plus top-eight containment and empirical-quantile transfer.
- Stopped extrapolating the fixed-width product-sign evidence. Added `impl/product_sign_margin_stress.py` and a regression test for the frozen \(\Psi_s\) normal form: it measures the distributional top-\(\eta\) score quantile, the ceiling gap \(r\log2-Q_{r,\eta}\), tilted bounded-margin good mass, affinity, and the certified bound. The new `lem:product-sign-ceiling-barrier` proves a real obstruction: with bounded Gaussian bit variance, if \(r(1+\log^2 r)=o(\log m)\), then the top \(m^{-\gamma}\) score quantile is within any fixed margin of the ceiling, so a fixed positive posterior-rank margin is eventually impossible. The focused ideal-Gaussian sweep (`python impl/product_sign_margin_stress.py --m 1000000,1000000000,1000000000000,1000000000000000000,1000000000000000000000000 --r 4,8,12,16,24,32,48,64 --sigma 3.5 --corr 0.9 --margin 1 --quantile-samples 160000 --pair-trials 800 --label-samples 384 --seeds 0 --csv /tmp/lsh-product-sign-margin-growth.csv`) showed the same transition. A selected repeat (`python impl/product_sign_margin_stress.py --m 1000000000000000000000000 --r 8,16,24 --sigma 3.5 --corr 0.9 --margin 1 --quantile-samples 300000 --pair-trials 1500 --label-samples 768 --seeds 0,1,2 --csv /tmp/lsh-product-sign-margin-selected.csv`) had \(r=8\) ceiling-blocked in every seed, \(r=16\) tilted good-mass q05 \(0.033\)--\(0.039\), and \(r=24\) q05 \(0.585\)--\(0.599\). A scale sweep over \(\sigma\in\{1.5,2.5,3.5,5.0\}\) put the first tested `good_mass_q05 >= 0.1` bit counts at \(r=12,16,24\) for \(m=10^{12},10^{18},10^{24}\). The product-sign target remains the same bounded-margin lower tail for \(\Psi_s\), but now with growing \(r\) beyond the ceiling-barrier scale; this is still compatible with the top-\(k\) reporter and the finite-channel compiler because they need \(O(dr+k\log k)\) query work and allow \(2^r\le\xi^{-1}\operatorname{polylog}m\).
- Added the positive side of the same frozen normal form. `lem:product-sign-mean-gap-certificate` computes the exact conditional tilted mean \(M_{p,q}\) and variance \(V_{p,q}\) of \(\Psi_s(p)\); if \(M_{p,q}\) clears the posterior threshold by \(\Gamma\), Cantelli gives tilted good mass at least \(\Gamma^2/(V_{p,q}+\Gamma^2)\). `cor:gaussian-growing-width-product-sign` then reduces the ideal growing-width theorem to a concrete Gaussian rate gap: the fixed-label upper threshold must lie below the per-bit tilted mean. The stress harness now reports `mean_gap_*`, `tilted_variance_q95`, `cantelli_good_*`, and `cantelli_bound_over_alpha_q05`. In `python impl/product_sign_margin_stress.py --m 1000000000000,1000000000000000000,1000000000000000000000000 --r 12,16,24,32,48 --sigma 3.5 --corr 0.9 --margin 1 --quantile-samples 220000 --pair-trials 1200 --label-samples 512 --seeds 0,1,2 --csv /tmp/lsh-product-sign-cantelli-growth.csv`, the \(m=10^{24}\) transition was: \(r=24\) borderline with mean-gap q05 \(0.262\)--\(0.837\), Cantelli q05 \(0.003\)--\(0.026\), empirical good-mass q05 \(0.545\)--\(0.568\); \(r=32\) certified with mean-gap q05 \(9.07\)--\(9.95\), Cantelli q05 \(0.734\)--\(0.769\), empirical q05 \(0.939\)--\(0.959\). At critical-ish correlation \(0.75\) (`--m 1000000000000000000,1000000000000000000000000 --r 24,32,48,64 --corr 0.75`), \(m=10^{24}\) needed \(r=64\) for a strong crude certificate: Cantelli q05 \(0.776\)--\(0.783\), empirical q05 \(0.959\)--\(0.967\). The next theorem is now the Gaussian rate-gap inequality, not another certificate form.
- Closed the ideal i.i.d. Gaussian rate-gap theorem. `cor:cramer-product-sign-closure` uses Stein's identity to show the \(\beta\to0\) tilted per-bit mean is \(\mu_X+a\sigma^2\mathbf E\operatorname{sech}^2 Z>\mu_X\), and then Cramer's theorem gives the bounded-margin target with \(r=\kappa\log m\) whenever \(\kappa I_\sigma(t)>\gamma\). The deterministic helper `gaussian_rate_gap_summary` in `impl/product_sign_margin_stress.py` reproduces the compiler feasibility constants. Running `python impl/product_sign_margin_stress.py --sigma 2.5,3.5,5.0 --corr 0.75 --c 2.0 --rate-level-slack 0.1 --rate-quadrature 80 --rate-theta-grid 1200 --rate-summary-csv /tmp/lsh-product-sign-rate-c2.csv` gives Cramer rates \(0.150,0.163,0.143\) and minimum label exponents \(0.577,0.531,0.606\), all below the \(B\le m\) envelope. At correlation \(0.9\), the same command gives exponents \(0.339,0.309,0.363\). Thus the ideal Gaussian product-sign channel is now closed; the remaining work is transfer to actual whitened product-sign panels and empirical quantiles, not another certificate reformulation.
- Added the focused ideal-to-panel transfer diagnostic instead of another certificate form. `impl/product_sign_transfer_stress.py` measures the actual whitened product-sign bit logits in the frozen \(\Psi_s\) normal form: fitted bit variance, cross-bit correlations, near-pair bit correlation, reference/node fixed-label score thresholds, fitted Gaussian Cramer slack, Cantelli mean gaps, and exact tilted good mass when labels can be enumerated. Regression tests cover the top-\(\eta\) order statistic, exact tilted enumeration, and a real whitened trial. Compact sweeps at \(d=32\), near correlation \(0.95\), target bit sigma \(3.5\), and \(m=10000\) are positive for whitened panels: exact \(B=16,32\) runs have per-bit Cramer slack \(0.91\)--\(1.24\), exact tilted good-mass lower \(5\%\) \(0.813\)--\(0.981\), and certified value over \(\alpha\) \(0.648\)--\(0.778\); exact \(B=256\) has good-mass lower \(5\%\) \(0.9988\)--\(0.9996\). A sampled \(B=4096\) run also stays positive, but is not an all-label theorem. The unwhitened \(B=256\) control fails for the right reason: fitted bit sigma is only \(0.57\)--\(0.63\), minimum label exponent is \(1.95\)--\(2.73\), and lower \(5\%\) mean gap is negative. `paper.tex` now states `lem:product-sign-transfer-check`, which pins the remaining theorem to reference-threshold, empirical-threshold, and tilted mean/variance transfer for random whitened product-sign bits.
- Made the product-sign row-law transfer exact. `lem:whitened-product-sign-row-law` proves that the implemented rows \(u_a(x)=\lambda d^{-1/2}\langle g_a,Hx\rangle\) are iid bivariate Gaussian bits conditional on the whitening map and the near pair, with variance/correlation given by the whitening metric. The transfer diagnostic now reports these metric parameters and has a Monte Carlo regression test for the formula. In the \(m=10000,d=32,B=256\) whitened sweep above, the metric bit sigmas were \(3.497\)--\(3.502\), metric pair-correlation q05 was \(0.9483\)--\(0.9486\), sampled finite-row pair correlation matched at \(0.944\)--\(0.950\), and the q95 score level still sat \(1.35\)--\(1.50\) below the fitted Gaussian rate level. This discharges the iid-row part of the transfer; the live theorem is now metric calibration plus empirical/reference \(\Psi_s\)-quantile concentration, not another certificate form.
- Closed the iid empirical-threshold part of the same transfer. `lem:product-sign-empirical-quantile-transfer` is just Hoeffding plus a union bound: if reference thresholds have tail at most \(\eta-\delta\), then all \(B\) empirical node top-\(\eta\) thresholds lie below them except with probability \(B\exp(-2m\delta^2)\). With \(\eta=m^{-\gamma}\), \(\gamma<1/2\), and \(B\le m^{O(1)}\), this is superpolynomially small. The remaining product-sign transfer theorem is therefore sharply localized to reference all-label \(\Psi_s\)-threshold control for the random whitened panel and whitening-metric calibration of near pairs.
- Reduced the reference all-label threshold and near-pair calibration to spectral events. `lem:product-sign-reference-covariance-transfer` couples a nearly scalar Gaussian bit covariance to the iid Gaussian score, paying an explicit \(2A\sigma\varepsilon r\) threshold slack; `cor:random-row-reference-threshold-transfer` gets the needed scalar covariance from the standard row-Gram/Wishart bound. `lem:whitening-metric-calibration` shows that if \(d^{-1}H^\top H\) is \(\delta\)-close to identity then bit variances and near-pair correlations are calibrated. The transfer diagnostic now reports `ref_cov_rel_op`; the compact \(B=256\) runs show the generic spectral route is conservative at small dimension but has the right trend: at \(d=32\), relative covariance operator deviation was \(0.73\)--\(1.11\); at \(d=128\), \(0.355\)--\(0.387\); at \(d=512\), a smoke run gave \(0.248\). The observed exact good mass stayed near one in all these rows, so the spectral theorem is a sufficient asymptotic route, while sharper finite-dimensional comparison remains optional rather than a new certificate target.
- Closed the Gaussian-to-spherical transfer piece for the frozen product-sign normal form. `lem:product-sign-spherical-shell-transfer` couples \(Y/\|Y\|\) to \(Y/\sqrt d\) and pays only the Lipschitz score slack \(2L\delta(1-\delta)^{-1}r\); `cor:spherical-random-row-reference-threshold-transfer` combines that shell coupling with the scalar-covariance comparison and row-Gram event, while `lem:empirical-spherical-whitening-calibration` proves the exact spherical whitening metric event from standard sample-covariance concentration. This gives a real sufficient closure for iid spherical local nodes when \(r=\Theta(\log m)\), \(d/\log m\to\infty\), and the empirical whitening covariance concentrates. It also exposes the concrete low-dimensional barrier: in \(d=\Theta(\log m)\), the row-Gram, whitening, and shell slack must fit inside the per-bit Cramer gap. The transfer stress tool now reports coupled shell deviations and spherical-minus-Gaussian threshold gaps; a compact check (`python impl/product_sign_transfer_stress.py --n 10000 --d 128 --queries 80 --panel whitened_product_sign --bit-sigma 3.5 --B 256 --ref-samples 8000 --threshold-label-samples 256 --tilted-label-samples 512 --ideal-samples 30000 --margin 1.0 --near-corr 0.95 --seeds 0,1 --rate-quadrature 48 --rate-theta-grid 600 --csv /tmp/lsh-product-sign-shell-transfer.csv`) had shell relative q95 \(0.120\)--\(0.123\), maximum spherical-over-Gaussian threshold gap \(0.026\)--\(0.034\), exact tilted good-mass q05 \(0.9995\)--\(0.9997\), and per-bit score-level gap \(1.45\)--\(1.48\).
- Found and proved the next real product-sign barrier instead of adding another threshold certificate. `lem:growing-product-sign-top-k-barrier` shows that for a product-sign tilted law with parameters \(v_a\), any reported label set \(Q\) has restricted value at most \(W(Q)\le |Q|\exp(-\sum_a\log(1+e^{-2|v_a|}))\). Hence with iid fixed-entropy bit logits, \(r=\kappa\log m\), and \(|Q|=\operatorname{polylog}m\), every polylog top-\(k\) reporter carries only \(m^{-\Omega(1)}\) value. The deterministic Gaussian rate summary now reports `mode_deficit` and `min_topk_mass_exponent`. At the same \(c=2\), level-slack \(0.1\) Cramer points, \(\sigma=2.5,3.5,5.0\), correlation \(0.75\) gives top-k mass exponents \(0.096,0.059,0.040\); correlation \(0.9\) gives \(0.057,0.035,0.024\). This means the all-label product-sign transfer is genuinely closed, but it does not discharge the output-sensitive endpoint: the remaining branch must compress/store the growing output layer without enumerating it, use a channel with \(O(\log\log m)\) tilted entropy, or accept this as a product-sign route barrier.
- Closed the obvious sampled escape hatch for the same product-sign panel. `lem:sampled-product-sign-rate-obstruction` shows that if a query-Gibbs-sampled product-sign panel stores \(x\) in labels with \(\Psi_s(x)\ge rt\), then storage and sampled far-load force \(I_\sigma(t)/\log2\ge1-\rho\). For \(c=2\) this required fraction is \(6/7\). The deterministic rate summary now reports `sampled_required_rate_fraction`, `sampled_rate_fraction`, and `sampled_rate_gap`; at the same level-slack \(0.1\) points, correlation \(0.75\), \(\sigma=2.5,3.5,5.0\) gives sampled rate fractions \(0.217,0.236,0.206\), and correlation \(0.9\) gives \(0.369,0.404,0.344\), all far below \(0.857\). Thus direct sampling from the product-sign query Gibbs law cannot bypass the top-k entropy barrier. The remaining viable directions are a bucket quotient/compressed all-output layer that changes storage/far-load accounting, a low-tilted-entropy channel, or a proof-level barrier for the product-sign branch.
- Tightened the quotient escape hatch instead of changing normal forms. `cor:stable-small-quotient-product-sign-obstruction` shows that if product-sign labels are merged into ordinary union buckets with only polylogarithmically many hidden labels per quotient class, and the union scan is stable in the sense of the existing quotient lemma, then the hidden unquotiented sampled panel inherits the quotient storage/far-load bounds up to polylog factors and the near mass only loses a polylog factor. Therefore the sampled rate obstruction still forces \(I_\sigma(t)/\log2\ge1-\rho\). A product-sign quotient can only remain viable if it uses superpolylogarithmic aggregate classes with a genuine aggregate/responsibility sampler, or if the channel itself has low tilted entropy.
- Quantified the ordinary quotient barrier. `cor:ordinary-product-sign-quotient-class-size` proves that if product-sign labels are merged into ordinary union buckets, storage and sampled far-load alone force the maximum hidden class size exponent to satisfy \(\delta\ge(1-\rho)/(I_\sigma(t)/\log2)-1\). The deterministic rate summary now reports `ordinary_quotient_required_class_exponent` and its gaps to unit and minimum-label budgets. At the same \(c=2\), level-slack \(0.1\) points, correlation \(0.75\), \(\sigma=2.5,3.5,5.0\) requires class exponents \(2.956,2.638,3.158\); correlation \(0.9\) requires \(1.325,1.121,1.492\). Thus ordinary union quotienting would need polynomially huge hidden classes, typically more than an \(m\)-sized label budget, before it even addresses near mass. The only remaining product-sign escape is a genuinely nonordinary aggregate/responsibility sampler, not a small or stable quotient.
- Closed that nonordinary aggregate escape for the same thresholded product-sign score. `cor:aggregate-responsibility-product-sign-obstruction` gives the strongest one-bucket version: even if all hidden labels are aggregated into one stored bucket and query output is responsibility-thinned pointwise, far-load plus inverse-polylog near emission force \(I_\sigma(\mu_{\sigma,a})/\log2\ge1-\rho\), where \(\mu_{\sigma,a}\) is the near tilted per-bit mean. The deterministic rate summary now reports `near_mean_rate_fraction`; at the same \(c=2\) rows it is only \(0.262,0.270,0.226\) for correlation \(0.75\) and \(0.445,0.464,0.378\) for correlation \(0.9\), versus the required \(0.857\). Thus the product-sign branch is not rescued by top-k, direct sampled labels, ordinary quotienting, or one-bucket responsibility thinning; the remaining positive route must change the channel/structured sampler so the far rate reaches the near mean.
- Froze the next branch at the existing smoothed-Gibbs mass-capture target and found a concrete barrier rather than another equivalent certificate. `lem:medium-valid-plateau-gibbs-obstruction` shows that if \(m^a\) already-valid points sit on a plateau with \(\rho<a<1-\rho\), then the guard density is still below \(m^{-\rho}\operatorname{polylog}^{-1}m\), but every charged list of size \(m^\rho\log^A m\) has Gibbs mass at most \(m^{\rho-a}\log^A m=o(\log^{-B}m)\). The new `impl/smoothed_gibbs_stress.py` diagnostic checks the same necessary top-\(K\) condition: for \(c=2,\rho=1/7\), \(a=0.5\), list log power \(2\), and \(m=10^{24}\), the top-\(K\) upper mass is \(8.19\cdot10^{-6}\) versus the \(\log^{-2}m\) threshold \(3.27\cdot10^{-4}\); for \(a=0.7\) at \(m=10^{12}\), it is \(1.57\cdot10^{-4}\) versus \(1.31\cdot10^{-3}\). A sampled \(m=20000,a=0.7\) plateau with no polylog output slack has invalid Gibbs mass numerically zero and top-\(K\) mass \(5/1024\), while the guard still fails. This is not an ANN lower bound, because the plateau points are valid; it is a barrier to proving `asm:smoothed-gibbs` verbatim for all low-guard nodes. The next useful smoothed route must either handle medium valid plateaus by direct valid output or prove a stronger score-max/Gumbel primitive that returns one certified high-score valid point.
- Tested the obvious direct-output escape from that barrier: use the existing smooth cumulative-score filter as a candidate generator for the smoothed score-max primitive by querying it at \(y=Y/t\). The new `impl/smoothed_scoremax_stress.py` keeps the normal form fixed and reports both the oracle score gap and the candidate-list loss. In planted spherical promised instances with \(c=2\), far correlation at most \(-0.05\), and \(t=64\log m/r^2\), the oracle gap is clean: score-gap failure \(0\), planted rank \(1\), and oracle top-one valid rate \(1\). But the smooth-filter implementation is not viable as-is. At \(n=400,d=16\), work-safe thresholds \(\theta=1.0,1.2,1.4\) returned zero candidates and never captured the score maximizer. At \(n=100,d=12,\theta=0.4\), four of five seeds captured the score leader, but candidate means were \(10.7\)--\(52.0\), i.e. \(5.5\)--\(26.9\) times \(n^\rho\), and storage was \(20.5n\)--\(44.4n\); one seed still missed entirely with loss about \(4.37\) margins. At \(n=200,d=12,\theta=0.4\), the capturing seeds needed candidate means \(116.5\)--\(118.0\), about \(55n^\rho\), and storage \(348n\)--\(488n\). Thus the plateau barrier is bypassed by exact score maximization, but the currently implemented smooth leaf filter does not supply the needed additive score-max oracle; the next positive route needs a different additive NN/MIPS primitive or a structured reporter with a real \(m^\rho\operatorname{polylog}m\) candidate bound.
- Added and ran the corresponding centroid/IVF score-max diagnostic (`impl/smoothed_scoremax_ivf_stress.py`). It keeps the same planted promised spherical instances and exact smoothed-score reranker, but routes \(y=Y/t\) to top cosine centroid buckets with \(C=m^{1-\rho}\). This gives a useful split. Random sampled data-centers are not enough: at \(n=1000\), two of three seeds put the planted bucket at median ranks \(39.5\) and \(113.5\), and at \(n=3000\), two of three seeds put it at ranks \(128.5\) and \(364.5\), so even eight probes usually missed. Lloyd-refined centers are much better at \(n=1000,3000\): most rows had planted bucket rank \(1\), scoremax capture \(1.0\), and candidate means \(1.0\)--\(5.3\) for one/two probes at \(n=1000\) and \(1.0\)--\(5.1\) at \(n=3000\), i.e. \(O(n^\rho)\) scan load. But Lloyd is initialization-sensitive: at \(n=10000,\text{seed}=0\), the planted bucket median rank stayed \(41.5\), and even \(64\) probes captured only \(55\%\) of trials with candidate mean \(116.7\), while seed \(1\) captured with one probe and candidate mean \(4.0\). Exact \(k\)-means++ initialization repairs the compact regime: at \(n=1000\), all three seeds had planted bucket rank \(1\), scoremax capture \(1.0\), and one-probe candidate means \(1.0,3.0,4.0\) (\(0.37\)--\(1.49\,n^\rho\)). The remaining positive target is therefore concrete: prove or implement a facility-initialized centroid decoder with top-few bucket routing, \(m^\rho\operatorname{polylog}m\) scan load, and without explicitly scoring all \(m^{1-\rho}\) centers at query time.
- Froze that facility-IVF target and stress-tested the missing compact centroid-decoder layer instead of adding another certificate form. Added `impl/smoothed_scoremax_centroid_decoder_stress.py` plus regression tests. The exact scorer remains a positive control: on \(n=3000,d=24,c=2\) planted \(k\)-means++ facility instances, the assigned bucket of the smoothed score maximizer had exact centroid rank \(1\) in all tested trials. A balanced random-projection beam tree over the centroids did not give a compact replacement. With beam \(2\), it spent only about \(0.146C\) center-score equivalents but missed the score-max bucket completely in two of three seeds; beam \(8\) still missed one seed completely while spending about \(0.457C\); the hard seed needed beam \(32\) to recover only \(74\%\) while spending \(1.24C\). A flat multiprobe hyperplane decoder over centers showed the same tradeoff: eight 8-bit tables with 64 probes recovered only \(0\%\)--\(22\%\); eight 6-bit tables with 64 probes spent about \(0.51C\)--\(0.54C\) and recovered \(28\%\)--\(96\%\); eight 6-bit tables with 128 probes recovered all tested trials only after spending \(0.79C\)--\(0.82C\). Thus the facility cover itself still looks promising, but ordinary centroid ANN/LSH routing is a real bottleneck; the next positive route needs a stronger decoder that exploits the facility construction, or a proof-level barrier showing top facility-bucket search costs a constant fraction of the retained centers.
- Strengthened the same compact-decoder stress test with an exact-neighbor pivot graph over the retained centroids, so the negative result is not merely a weak tree/hash artifact. The new `pivotgraph` decoder in `impl/smoothed_scoremax_centroid_decoder_stress.py` builds the centroid graph from exact all-pairs centroid similarities, scores a query-visible pivot set, and best-first expands graph neighbors by the true query score. In `python impl/smoothed_scoremax_centroid_decoder_stress.py --n 3000 --d 24 --routers kmeanspp --decoders pivotgraph --probes 1 --query-trials 80 --seeds 0,1,2 --far-corr-max -0.05 --t-scale 64 --lloyd-iters 4 --center-graph-degree 16 --center-graph-pivots 172 --center-graph-entries 8 --center-graph-ef 256 --csv /tmp/lsh-centroid-pivotgraph-focused.csv`, exact all-centroid scoring still had score-max/additive success \(1.0\), but the graph decoder had success \(0.075,0.0875,0.0\), \(90\%\) loss \(4.24\)--\(4.27\) margins, and work \(0.39C\). Increasing to `--center-graph-ef 512` raised success only to \(0.30,0.2167,0.0167\) while spending about \(0.61C\). This points to a real local-navigation barrier for ordinary centroid proximity graphs: the top score bucket need not sit in the query-visible graph basin before the search is already near-dense.
- Added centroid score-margin fields to the compact-decoder diagnostic to check whether the graph/tree/hash failures are just tiny top-center ties. They are not in the focused planted \(k\)-means++ rows. Running `python impl/smoothed_scoremax_centroid_decoder_stress.py --n 3000 --d 24 --routers kmeanspp --decoders exact --probes 1 --query-trials 200 --seeds 0,1,2 --far-corr-max -0.05 --t-scale 64 --lloyd-iters 4 --csv /tmp/lsh-centroid-margin-focused.csv` gave exact scoremax success \(1.0\) in every seed, normalized top-minus-second center-gap \(10\%\) quantiles \(0.159,0.080,0.117\), and top-minus-rank-\(32\) gap \(10\%\) quantiles \(0.233,0.155,0.188\). So the ordinary decoders are not failing because the winning center is an infinitesimal tie; they are failing to enter the right high-score basin without near-dense center enumeration.
- Looked at the existing code/lattice hooks and froze a structured-decoder candidate map rather than adding another certificate form. The useful interface is already in `lem:soft-list-recovery-reporter`, `cor:euclidean-list-decoder-reporter`, `lem:reliability-erasure-sweep`, and `cor:unified-soft-decoded-rate-coverage`: a native decoder lists high-score labels under the actual Fano/soft score, then the verifier charges reported buckets. The best next experiment is a polar/Reed--Muller-constrained product-sign panel: use SCL/top-codeword decoding on bit LLRs, measure query-top-\(L\) tilted value, sampled far-load, and rate-coverage feasibility, and compare to the full-cube product-sign entropy barrier. Other promising but more theorem-heavy routes are Euclidean soft-decision blocks (Koetter--Vardy RS, Barnes--Sloane/Barnes--Wall, Construction-A/D) when the score is an embedded radius, and high-rate expander/Tanner/tensor list recovery for reduced-cost separation after dyadic discretization. Lattice enumeration/BDD/Gaussian sampling should be imported only as Fano-biased pruning or compact quotient sampling, not as black-box nearest-center search.
- Added `impl/coded_product_sign_panel_stress.py` plus regression tests for the first exact structured-code experiment. The harness enumerates binary linear panels, reports top-\(L\) codewords by query bit LLR, stores a point when the product-sign score exceeds the calibrated far threshold \(m^{\rho-1}/L\), and compares near hit, oracle hit, top-list mass, storage, and far load. In \(r=16,m=8192,c=2,\sigma=3.5,\operatorname{corr}=0.95,L=8\), \(RM(2,4)\) has the right label exponent \(11/13\approx0.846\), median query top-list mass about \(0.997\), and tilted lower \(5\%\) mass \(0.79\)--\(0.84\), but near hit is only \(0.107\)--\(0.126\) and matches the oracle over the whole code. Thus the list decoder is not the bottleneck; sparse-code coverage at the far threshold is. The one-label mode is the only positive finite signal: \(RM(2,4)\) at \(L=1\) stores \(0.908\) labels per point, keeps far load at budget, and hits with probability \(0.324\)--\(0.436\); \(RM(2,5)\) at \(m=2^{19}\) stores \(0.852\) labels per point and hits \(0.242\)--\(0.258\), with a random \([32,16]\) code essentially indistinguishable. At bit correlation \(0.99\), \(RM(2,5)\) rises to \(0.375\)--\(0.425\). This is not a special RM theorem yet; it is a concrete boostable sparse-code target, or a barrier if the constant decays with blocklength.
- Added a random-code saddlepoint layer and iid-label controls to the coded product-sign diagnostic. The finite top-one hit was not RM-specific: iid labels at \(r=16,k=11\) hit \(0.292\)--\(0.366\), and iid labels at \(r=32,k=16\) hit \(0.188\)--\(0.250\), close to the random-linear/RM rows. The asymptotic Cramer-calibrated picture is negative. For a constant-rate sparse code with \(R=k/r=1/2\), \(\sigma=3.5\), and bit correlation \(0.95\), shape-only saddlepoint rows \([32,16]\), \([64,32]\), and \([96,48]\) have median-max mean gaps \(-0.636\), \(-0.563\), and \(-0.549\) per bit, with normal hit estimates \(0.067\), \(0.026\), and \(0.010\). Even at correlation \(0.99\), the gaps remain \(-0.586\), \(-0.513\), and \(-0.499\). A \([96,48]\) sigma sweep at correlation \(0.95\) stays negative even at \(\sigma=1.0\) (gap \(-0.113\)). The finite constants are explained by finite-block empirical thresholds lying below the Cramer level; once the far threshold is calibrated at the asymptotic rate, iid/random-linear sparse product-sign top-one decays. The positive code/lattice route now has to use non-iid geometry or a different score channel, not a random sparse subcode of product signs.
- Added `impl/sparse_subset_channel_stress.py` plus tests for the ordered-statistics sparse-subset channel. Labels are signed width-\(w\) coordinate subsets with centered score \(\sum_{i\in A}s_iu_i-\log e_w(2\cosh u)+\log(2^w\binom rw)\); query decoding chooses the \(w\) largest absolute LLR coordinates and signs, so this is an OSD/information-set channel rather than a random sparse subcode. The diagnostic computes \(e_w\) by dynamic programming, calibrates a sampled Cramer far threshold, and compares the near selected score. At bit correlation \(0.95\), narrow controls are negative: \([16,1]\) gaps \(-0.030,-0.033\) for \(\sigma=1,2\), \([16,2]\) gaps \(-0.047,-0.056\), and \([32,2]\) gaps \(-0.022,-0.022\). The ordered-statistics regime turns positive: \([32,4]\) gaps \(0.003,0.031\) with hit estimates \(0.551,0.795\), \([64,4]\) gaps \(0.015,0.043\) with hits \(0.868,0.976\), and \([64,8]\) gaps \(0.060,0.119\) with hits essentially one. Empirical finite thresholds are optimistic on the tiny tails, so the saddlepoint gap is the theorem target: prove the large-deviation gap for \(r=\Theta(\log m)\) and suitable \(w\), or find the hidden asymptotic correction. This is the first non-random product-channel signal after the sparse-code barrier.
- Froze the ordered-statistics sparse-subset theorem target instead of adding another harness. For \(w=\lambda r\), the denominator is the quenched elementary-symmetric coefficient free energy \(\mathcal F_\lambda(\nu)=\inf_{\theta>0}\{\int\log(1+2\theta\cosh z)\,d\nu-\lambda\log\theta\}\), not the annealed \(\log\E(1+2\theta\cosh Z)\) shortcut. The fixed-label far threshold is the Sanov variational rate over active/inactive empirical laws, and exposed tails reduce to a two-scalar Legendre saddle in \((\beta,\theta)\) with a tilted denominator-occupancy equation. The near OSD selected score is \(M_{\lambda,\sigma,\chi}=2\chi\sigma\varphi(z_\lambda)-\mathcal F_\lambda(\gamma_\sigma)+H(\lambda)+\lambda\log2\). The next real question is the single inequality \(M_{\lambda,\sigma,\chi}>t_{\lambda,\sigma}\), where the far threshold solves \(\mathcal J_{\lambda,\sigma}(t)=H(\lambda)+\lambda\log2\). Any Tanner/polar/RS/Construction-A/lattice replacement must preserve the reliability-ordered numerator while not raising this far variational threshold; random sparse subcodes fail because they replace the order statistic by a random-code extreme average.
- Important caution from scratch Gauss-Hermite checks of the new saddle: the finite positive sparse-subset rows are not yet evidence of an asymptotic gap. Using the exposed-tail saddle with asymptotic \(R_\lambda\), I get negative preliminary gaps for \(\lambda=4/64\) and \(8/64\) at \(\sigma=1,2,\chi=0.95\), while the retained sampled script still reports positive finite gaps because it estimates an \(\exp(-rR_\lambda)\) tail from far fewer than \(\exp(rR_\lambda)\) samples. Treat the OSS branch as unresolved: either find parameters with \(M_{\lambda,\sigma,\chi}>t_{\lambda,\sigma}\) under the scalar saddle, or record a barrier.
- Proved the small-width OSS barrier. As \(\lambda\downarrow0\), \(\mathcal F_\lambda(\gamma_\sigma)=H(\lambda)+\lambda\log2+\lambda\sigma^2/2+o(\lambda)\), and the near OSD mean is \(M_{\lambda,\sigma,\chi}=\lambda\chi\sigma a_\lambda-\lambda\sigma^2/2+o(\lambda)\), where \(a_\lambda=\varphi(z_\lambda)/\bar\Phi(z_\lambda)\). A far active-coordinate tilt to mean \((\chi+\eta)\sigma a_\lambda\) has entropy cost \(((\chi+\eta)^2+o(1))R_\lambda<R_\lambda\) for any \(\chi+\eta<1\), while its denominator change is \(o(\lambda a_\lambda)\) and its score exceeds the near mean by \(\eta\lambda\sigma a_\lambda+o(\lambda a_\lambda)\). Hence \(t_{\lambda,\sigma}\ge M_{\lambda,\sigma,\chi}+\Omega_{\sigma,\chi}(\lambda\sqrt{\log(1/\lambda)})\): OSS cannot work by taking very sparse information sets. The only remaining OSS possibility is a fixed moderate active fraction or a construction that changes the far variational threshold.
- Closed the raw coordinatewise OSS channel for every fixed \(0<\lambda<1\). Let \(L\in\{-,0,+\}\) be the query threshold/sign decision and \(U\) the near logit. Use in the far Sanov problem the active law \(U\mid L=+\) and inactive law \(U\mid L=0\). Its numerator is exactly the OSD near numerator, and its denominator free energy is the typical \(\mathcal F_\lambda(\gamma_\sigma)\) because the coefficient integrand is even and the \(L=-\) law is the reflection of \(L=+\). Its cost is \(\lambda D(U\mid L=+\|\gamma_\sigma)+(1-\lambda)D(U\mid L=0\|\gamma_\sigma)=I(L;U)<I(L;Q)=H(L)=R_\lambda\) for \(\chi<1\). Therefore \(\mathcal J_{\lambda,\sigma}(M_{\lambda,\sigma,\chi})<R_\lambda\), so \(t_{\lambda,\sigma}\ge M_{\lambda,\sigma,\chi}\) and the strict OSS gap is impossible. The next positive code/lattice route must change charged label entropy, the far spectrum, or add a non-ternary Fano/decoder bias; raw reliability-ordered subsets are closed as a barrier.
- Generalized the OSS obstruction to a raw top-one entropy barrier. For any symmetric Gaussian block panel with deterministic top-one query label \(A=f(Q)\), far law \(P_U\), and storage score \(\Phi_a(U)\), the conditional near law \(P_{U\mid A=a}\) is a valid far variational witness at the selected near mean. Its cost is \(D(P_{U\mid A=a}\|P_U)=I(A;U)\le I(A;Q)=H(A)\), strictly below \(H(A)\) for a nondegenerate noisy near channel. Therefore a far threshold calibrated at full label entropy cannot sit below the selected near mean. This closes the whole family of "better raw top-one code/lattice decoder" attempts; positive constructions must lower charged entropy through output-sensitive lists/prefix moments, quotient or aggregate samplers, or a certified Fano bias, or else change the far spectrum.
- Turned the raw top-one barrier into a reusable code/lattice screening rule. `lem:information-charge-survivor-test` says that for any retained transcript \(T=f(Q)\), score \(\Phi_T(U)\), and fixed-transcript far rate \(J_T\), the conditional near law \(P_{U\mid T=t}\) is a far witness at the selected mean with cost \(D(P_{U\mid T=t}\|P_U)\). Hence any threshold charged at rate \(C_t>D(P_{U\mid T=t}\|P_U)/r\) cannot lie below that selected mean. This makes the necessary survivor condition explicit: the charged rate of the reported transcript must be at most the conditional information retained by the near data block, up to polylog slack. The practical map is now sharper: polar/RM/RS/Tanner/list-recovery blocks should be used as threshold or reduced-cost reporters; GMD/Chase/OSD only through min-entropy information-set enumeration; Construction-A/D, Barnes--Wall, and lattice enumeration only through Fano-biased prefix moments or compressed aggregate samplers. Raw ML/top-one codeword decoding remains blocked regardless of algebraic structure.
- Strengthened the information-charge screen with the Gaussian strong data-processing constant. `lem:gaussian-information-charge-contraction` proves \(I(T;U)\le\chi^2 I(T;Q)\) for the near Gaussian block \(U=\chi Q+\sqrt{1-\chi^2}G\) by Ornstein--Uhlenbeck entropy contraction. Thus an equivariant raw transcript of entropy rate \(R\) has only \(\chi^2R\) information-charge rate in the data block, while ordinary stored labels still pay the full rate \(R\). `cor:polylog-raw-list-entropy-barrier` extends this to top-\(\operatorname{polylog}m\) raw lists: the list length contributes only \(o(r)\) entropy and cannot close the constant \((1-\chi^2)R\) charge gap. This rules out "top few codewords" for polar/RM/RS/lattice panels unless the retained object changes to output-sensitive work, quotient aggregation, reduced-cost rate coverage, or Fano prefix moments.
- Closed the cosmetic-Fano escape for raw labels. `cor:raw-label-fano-price-no-rescue` observes that subtracting a deterministic Fano/pruning price from the raw label score shifts the conditional near mean and the far score by the same deterministic amount, but leaves the conditional divergence \(D(P_{U\mid A=a}\|P_U)\) unchanged. If the fixed-label threshold is still charged at full raw-label rate \(R\), the Gaussian contraction gap remains. Therefore Fano prices help only when the proof charges the prefix/list work moment, quotient bucket entropy, aggregate sampler entropy, or rate-coverage load; they do not rescue full-rate raw codeword buckets by themselves.
- Quantified how much raw list decoding would have to spend to get past the same information barrier. `cor:raw-list-rate-lower-bound` combines the Gaussian contraction \(I(A;U)\le\chi^2H(A)\) with ordinary list-work accounting: if a top-\(K_r\) raw codeword list only lowers the per-label far charge from \(R\) to \(R-r^{-1}\log K_r\), then beating the selected near mean requires \(r^{-1}\log K_r\ge(1-\chi^2)R-o(1)\). Thus polynomial block-list decoders such as standard SCL, Chase/GMD, and bounded-radius lattice enumeration still behave like the polylog-list barrier when \(r=\Theta(\log m)\); an exponential raw list spends a real query exponent unless quotienting, aggregation, or a prefix-moment certificate compresses the charged object.
- Instantiated the surviving aggregate-sampler route with a concrete coding algorithm. `cor:bcjr-trellis-quotient-sampler` says that a compact acyclic code/lattice trellis with multiplicative query edge weights can run the usual BCJR backward recursion and sample stored bucket classes in exact proportion to aggregate Gibbs path mass, in linear trellis work. This is the useful role for finite-state convolutional/tail-biting trellises, recursive Barnes--Wall/Barnes--Sloane graphs, and polar/Tanner/Construction-A residue decoders when they expose an exact acyclic sum-product state graph: they do not rescue raw nearest-codeword decoding, but they can implement the quotient bucket law required by `(BQ1)`--`(BQ3)`.
- Checked the tempting escape from the product-sign top-k entropy barrier: increase the bit scale so the query/tilted product-sign law becomes spiky. The deterministic large-\(\sigma\) asymptotics show the tradeoff is fatal. `cor:product-sign-scale-tradeoff` proves that for \(r=\Theta(\log m)\) and \(\beta=\Theta(1/\log m)\), a polylog query list is blocked at low scale by tilted min-entropy \(\Omega(\log m/\sigma)\), while high scale makes the affinity factor \(\mathsf A_\beta=\sum_sK_p(s)^\beta K_q(s)^{1-\beta}\) at most \(\exp(-\Omega(\sigma))\). Optimizing the scale still leaves superpolylogarithmically small restricted value. Thus variable-scale product-sign top-k tuning is not a route; the remaining possibilities are a genuinely different low-tilted-entropy channel, or a quotient/aggregate construction that changes the storage and far-load arithmetic rather than just sharpening product-sign reporting.
- Added a deep code/lattice audit rather than another decoder formulation. `rem:deep-code-lattice-audit` sorts the usable constructions by the certificate they can actually satisfy: algebraic/list-recovery codes as reduced-cost reporters; polar/convolutional/low-state trellises as exact aggregate samplers; Tanner/LDPC only after exact small-junction-tree or certified-BP error control; Construction-A/D through theta residue-code partition functions; Barnes--Wall/Barnes--Sloane through recursive aggregate states; sphere enumeration/extreme pruning through Fano-prefix moments; and discrete-Gaussian samplers only with a retained bucket quotient spectrum. The concrete next target is therefore a Construction-A or trellis residue sampler with transfer-matrix verification of `(BQ1)`--`(BQ3)`, not raw nearest-codeword or polynomial-list decoding.
- Made the trellis quotient target directly checkable. `lem:marked-trellis-spectrum-verifier` says that for any additive storage score on a layered trellis, the sampled far-load and near-mass terms in `(BQ2)`--`(BQ3)` are exactly marked partition-function ratios \(Z_{q,x}^{\ge T}/Z_q\), computable by a score-augmented forward recursion when scores are discretized. This leaves storage as the true remaining issue: either raw incidences already fit, or the construction must prove a designed distinct-bucket canonicalization theorem. `lem:random-sparse-bucket-no-quotient` records the matching barrier: independent sparse buckets at the ANN scale almost never coincide, so random-looking code/lattice filters do not get quotient storage compression by accident.
- Added the scalar homogeneous-trellis rate audit. `cor:finite-state-trellis-rate-test` specializes the marked verifier to a fixed-state trellis of depth \(h=\kappa\log m\): raw storage is controlled by \(\inf_\theta(\Gamma_x(\theta)-\theta t)\), sampled far load by \(\sup_\theta(\theta t-\Lambda_{q,x}(\theta)+\Lambda_q)\), and near mass by the tilted lower-tail gap \(\Lambda'_{q,p}(0)>t\). Thus a proposed Construction-A residue trellis or convolutional sampler now has three Perron--Frobenius curves to compute against \(\rho/\kappa\) and \((1-\rho)/\kappa\); failure is a spectrum barrier, while success leaves only designed bucket canonicalization.
- Extended the information-charge barrier from deterministic code/lattice reporters to randomized aggregate samplers. `cor:randomized-aggregate-sampler-information-screen` treats a BCJR path, Construction-A residue, discrete-Gaussian lattice point, or bucket id as a randomized transcript \(T\) generated from the query and proves the same necessary condition \(C_t\le r^{-1}D(P_{U\mid T=t}\|P_U)+o(1)\), with average information bounded by \(r^{-1}I(T;U)\le\chi^2r^{-1}I(T;Q)\). In a transitive full-entropy raw-path panel this blocks any fixed \(\chi<1\), so exact sampling alone is not enough; the retained construction must certify a quotient bucket, responsibility law, or Fano-prefix charge below the conditional information budget.
- Applied that screen directly to the finite-state trellis route. `cor:raw-trellis-path-information-barrier` says that a transitive equivariant BCJR/polar/convolutional/Construction-A/recursive-lattice sampler with \(\exp(rR)\) hidden paths cannot charge each hidden path as an ordinary raw bucket at rate \(R\): Gaussian contraction gives only \(\chi^2R+o(1)\) conditional information in the near data block, so the fixed-path far threshold cannot lie below the selected near mean. Thus exact path sampling is not the missing theorem; the missing theorem is a lower charged object, namely quotient buckets, responsibility thinning, or a Fano/list-work prefix moment.
- Made the responsibility escape precise for trellises. `lem:marked-trellis-responsibility-verifier` says that after sampling a designed trellis bucket \(\ell\), the exact hidden responsibility of point \(x\) is the marked partition ratio \(Z_{q,x,\ell}^{\ge T}/Z_{q,\ell}\). If an output-sensitive reporter emits points within a polylog factor of these ratios, then far emitted load is bounded by the marked far spectrum and near emission is at least the marked near mass. Thus the remaining trellis/code/lattice positive theorem is no longer "sample a path"; it is compute or approximate these bucket-local marked partition ratios and report their heavy/responsible points without scanning the whole quotient bucket.
- Added the matching full-scan barrier. `cor:full-bucket-scan-responsibility-barrier` shows that ordinary union scanning of a sampled trellis bucket is polylog-stable only if every emitted point has marked responsibility at least inverse-polylog; the pointwise overcharge is exactly \(Z_{q,\ell}/Z_{q,x,\ell}^{\ge T}\). Thus quotienting by itself is insufficient when buckets contain low-responsibility passenger points, even if the aggregate path sampler is exact.
- Quantified the same obstruction in the random sparse model. `lem:random-sparse-aggregate-responsibility-barrier` groups \(H\) iid sparse hidden filters, each storing a point with probability \(s\), into one forced aggregate bucket. The hidden marked load is \(ms\), while full union scanning costs \(m(1-(1-s)^H)\), so the expected overcharge is \((1-(1-s)^H)/s=\Omega(\min\{H,1/s\})\). At ANN scale \(s=m^{\rho-1+o(1)}\), superpolylogarithmic hidden classes have superpolylog full-scan overhead, while polylog classes give only polylog compression. This pins the remaining positive route to true responsibility-thinned reporting or non-random bucket structure.
- Froze the coding/lattice route at the marked responsibility ratio instead of introducing a new certificate. `cor:heavy-responsibility-trellis-reporter` proves that listing all points with \(Z_{q,x,\ell}^{\ge T}/Z_{q,\ell}\ge1/K\), then exact-filtering by that same ratio, charges far load by only \(K\) times the marked spectrum and gives near recall equal to the sampled mass of buckets where the witness is heavy. `rem:code-lattice-responsibility-audit` translates this into construction tests: Construction-A theta quotients need soft list recovery for the heavy-ratio inequality; GMD/Chase only helps in high-margin buckets; OSD gives a concrete minimum-information-set entropy test; Tanner/polar/BCJR and recursive lattice decoders still need a reverse point-indexed heavy posterior lister, not just a hidden-label sampler.
- Added the complementary random-model sanity check. `lem:random-sparse-heavy-responsibility-screen` shows that the same iid sparse aggregate that overcharges union scans is statistically clean after responsibility thresholding: far passengers with \(r_x\ge\tau\) have expectation at most \(m\exp(-H d(\tau\Vert s))\), while a near point with membership probability \(u>\tau\) is missed with probability at most \(\exp(-H d(\tau\Vert u))\). At \(s=m^{\rho-1+o(1)}\), \(\tau=1/K\), \(K=\log^{O(1)}m\), taking \(H\gtrsim K/(1-\rho)\) already kills far heavy passengers in expectation. So the random aggregate barrier is specifically a full-scan barrier; the useful code/lattice problem is to find the heavy residents output-sensitively.
- Removed a spurious extra hypothesis from the heavy-responsibility route. `cor:marked-heavy-lister-closes-trellis` shows that if the marked near mass is \(L_m^{-1}\), then buckets with witness responsibility at least \((2L_m)^{-1}\) already have sampled mass at least \((2L_m)^{-1}\), by the elementary bound \(E R\le\tau+\Pr[R\ge\tau]\). Thus `(MTS4)`--`(MTS5)` plus a lister for \(r_{q,x,\ell}\ge(2L_m)^{-1}\) closes the trellis/code/lattice sampled ANN branch with only polylog overhead. The remaining task is exactly the point-indexed heavy lister.
- Added a concrete OSD compiler for that remaining lister. `cor:osd-heavy-responsibility-compiler` says that if every point with \(Z_{q,x,\ell}^{\ge T}/Z_{q,\ell}\ge(2L_m)^{-1}\) has a linear-code signature whose soft score exceeds a threshold \(\Theta_{q,\ell}\), and the minimum-information-set bound satisfies \(\beta(M_{q,\ell}-\Theta_{q,\ell})+\Lambda_*(q,\ell)\le\log W_m\), then ordered-statistics decoding lists all heavy codeword signatures before exact marked-ratio filtering. The resident lookup expansion is charged separately by \(R_{q,\ell}\), with the sampled condition \(\mathbb E[W_m+R_{q,\ell}]\le m^\rho\polylog m\). This turns the Construction-A/OSD route into a checkable entropy-plus-expansion inequality rather than a vague decoder hope.
- Added a direct conditional-hidden-sampling implementation of the heavy lister. `cor:conditional-hidden-sampling-heavy-lister` samples \(N=O(L_m)\) hidden paths from \(W_q(\pi)/Z_{q,\ell}\) inside the already sampled quotient bucket, scans only their raw subbuckets, and exact-filters by \(Z_{q,x,\ell}^{\ge T}/Z_{q,\ell}\ge(2L_m)^{-1}\). A heavy witness is scanned with constant probability on heavy buckets, far emissions still charge to the marked spectrum, and the raw-scan work is exactly \(N\sum_x Z_{q,x}^{\ge T}/Z_q\). Thus conditional sampling is a positive route precisely when the all-resident marked raw-scan load is \(m^\rho\polylog m\); it does not rescue constructions whose raw subbuckets are themselves too large.
- Froze the conditional-hidden-sampling branch as a raw-spectrum diagnostic. `cor:conditional-hidden-sampling-unquotiented-test` defines \(\mathcal R_q^{\rm raw}=\sum_x Z_{q,x}^{\ge T}/Z_q\) and observes that any implementation paid by conditional raw-subbucket enumeration has expected work \(N\mathcal R_q^{\rm raw}\), independent of the quotient map. Therefore a superpolylogarithmic far/raw hidden load is a real barrier for this direct sampler; a code or lattice construction then has to supply stable quotient scanning, point-indexed heavy-responsibility listing, or a Fano/list-work prefix certificate instead of another conditional-sampling reformulation.
- Answered the tuple-subset pseudorandomness question with a concrete star obstruction. `lem:additive-mask-star-obstruction` shows that retaining tuples by an independent scalar additive mask \(\sum_j m_j(i_j)\ge s\) has the right marginal density but can miss an additive top-tuple star with probability \(1-p^{c_k+o(1)}\) even when the star has expected retained size \(1\). Thus scalar additive masking is not random enough uniformly over additive superlevel sets. `rem:decodable-pseudorandom-tuple-subsets` records the plausible replacement: decomposable syndrome/tabulation hashes \(h_1(i_1)+\cdots+h_k(i_k)=\sigma\), which preserve conditional entropy on stars and remain decodable by a syndrome-state trellis, with the \(|G|\) state cost explicitly charged.
- Proved the positive counterpart for pseudorandom tuple thinning. `lem:pairwise-independent-syndrome-thinning` shows that independent per-coordinate pairwise-independent hashes into a finite group give pairwise-independent retained-tuple indicators for every fixed tuple set \(A\), so \(\mathbb E|A\cap R|=|A|/|G|\), \(\operatorname{Var}|A\cap R|\le |A|/|G|\), and a set of size \(\Omega(|G|)\) is hit with constant probability. Thus 2-independence is enough for per-query subset randomness once the query/top set is fixed independently of the seed; it is not a simultaneous guarantee over exponentially many seed-adaptive sets. `rem:multiply-shift-syndrome-masks` records the algebraic route: affine/multiply-add-shift style hashes give a random linear parity equation, decodable by lookup for \(k=2\), by a syndrome-state trellis for constant \(k\), or by meet-in-the-middle residue lists/max-plus convolution, with \(|G|\) state or residue-frontier cost still explicit.
- Turned the syndrome mask idea into decoder accounting. `lem:syndrome-state-product-decoder` augments the additive product-panel prefix decoder with a partial-syndrome state and residue-aware suffix bounds \(R_{\ell+1}(q,\tau)\), yielding the exact prefix moment \(P_\beta^{\rm syn}\) and terminal moment \(T_\beta^{\rm syn}\). `cor:residue-blind-syndrome-prefix-leakage` is the useful caveat: if the suffix bounds ignore the residual syndrome, the nonterminal prefix moment is exactly the unthinned product-panel moment, while pairwise-independent hashing only thins the terminal moment in expectation by \(|G|^{-1}\). Thus large syndrome thinning is not a free route to polylog overhead; the construction must keep \(|G|=\operatorname{polylog}m\), use direct lookup/meet-in-the-middle residue frontiers, or prove genuinely residue-aware suffix contraction.
- Added the concrete affine lookup compiler for the multiply-shift branch. `cor:affine-syndrome-lookup-compiler` shows that one affine parity check over a field determines a forced coordinate whenever some multiplier is nonzero, so the syndrome-constrained threshold query becomes a corrected-prefix enumeration. For two blocks this costs \(O(|\mathcal C_1|+L)\), eliminating the \(|G|\)-state factor; for more blocks it replaces state cost by an \((h-1)\)-coordinate frontier or meet-in-the-middle residue frontier. The same corollary records the near-mass limitation: large \(|G|\) thinning is useful only when the acceptable top-tuple set has \(\Omega(|G|)\) mass, otherwise recall is killed and comparable syndrome repetition is necessary.
- Applied that near-mass condition to the actual smooth product terminal filter. `cor:oversampled-product-syndrome-thinning` oversamples each tensor block by \(K^{1/k}\), with \(K=\operatorname{polylog}m\), then keeps one pairwise-independent syndrome class. The tuple universe grows by \(K\), terminal query/storage/far moments are thinned back by \(K^{-1}\), and the near pair has \(\Omega(K)\) common-good tuples with constant probability, so Paley--Zygmund gives constant retained recall. Thus polylog pseudorandom tuple thinning is genuinely implementable with 2-independent syndrome masks; the precise boundary is that polynomial-rate thinning would require polynomially broad near witness sets or comparable repetition/frontier cost.
- Proved the matching first-moment barrier for terminal tuple thinning. `cor:no-free-terminal-syndrome-thinning` shows that the original smooth product terminal filter, without \(K^{1/k}\) per-block oversampling, has only \(O(1)\) expected common-good near tuples. Any retained tuple subset independent of the Gaussian columns with marginal density at most \(K^{-1}\) therefore hits the near witness set with probability only \(O(K^{-1})\). This makes the oversampling factor in `cor:oversampled-product-syndrome-thinning` necessary up to constants for blind polylog syndrome thinning.
- Tightened the multiply-shift answer. `cor:difference-universal-syndrome-thinning` shows that exact pairwise independence is stronger than necessary if the target syndrome is sampled as part of the seed: a per-coordinate difference-universal bound \(\Pr[h(a)-h(b)=\tau]\le C_\Delta/|G|\) gives the same first/second-moment hit guarantee up to \(C_\Delta\). This is the right proof interface for multiply-shift-like hashes. Full affine field hashes and carry-less multiply-plus-linear-projection satisfy it cleanly; ordinary integer high-bit multiply-shift must be checked for two-adic difference bias. The revised `rem:multiply-shift-syndrome-masks` also records the decoding distinction: full syndromes can force a coordinate, while projected polylog syndromes force a dictionary bucket of expected size \(|\mathcal C_h|/|G|\), so the bucket/frontier cost remains part of the certificate.
- Added a concrete positive screen for random sparse aggregate buckets. `cor:random-sparse-hidden-sampling-positive` combines the binomial heavy-responsibility screen with conditional hidden-path sampling: at sparse ANN membership rate \(ms\le m^\rho\polylog m\), a polylog responsibility threshold \(K^{-1}\), and aggregate size \(H=\Theta(K)\), far heavy passengers vanish in expectation while a near point with membership probability \((1+\epsilon)/K\) is heavy with constant probability. Sampling \(N=\Theta(K)\) hidden labels and exact-filtering responsibilities then scans only \(K\,ms=m^\rho\polylog m\) raw residents and emits the near point with constant conditional probability. This distinguishes the viable path--conditional hidden sampling plus exact heavy filtering--from full union scanning, whose overcharge is \(\Theta(\min\{H,s^{-1}\})\).
- Tightened the multiply-shift branch around a real algebraic distinction. `lem:two-adic-multiply-mask-obstruction` shows that low-bit integer multiplication modulo \(2^r\) has difference-universal constant \(2^{\nu_2(x-y)}\) on pairs with even differences, so ordinary integer multiply-shift is not automatically the proof-safe 2-independent object. The positive object is `cor:projected-affine-residue-compiler`: field or carry-less multiply plus a linear projection turns the retained subset into a projected affine residue equation, so for pairs the decoder can force a residue bucket and list above threshold in bucket-output-sensitive time. This algebra removes a state factor only when the residue dictionaries/frontiers are cheap; it does not hide the large kernel cost of projected syndromes.
- Added the pair-prefix accounting payoff of random residue masks. `cor:random-residue-pair-prefix-bound` shows that, for two blocks, a random target residue makes first-coordinate prefix \(a\) live with probability at most \(\min\{1,N_2(\Theta-\phi_1(a))/|G|\}\), where \(N_2\) is the number of high enough partners before hashing. So the algebraic hash thins live prefixes by partner counts, not by wishful terminal randomness: it gives a \(K\)-fold prefix saving only where the compatible suffix set has size \(O(K)\), and it saturates on broad suffixes. This is the exact branch-and-bound benefit to test in tuple experiments.
- Generalized the residue-prefix accounting to every product level. `lem:syndrome-prefix-occupancy` proves that a prefix \(u\) is live with probability exactly the number of occupied suffix residues divided by \(|G|\), hence at most \(\min\{1,|A_u|/|G|\}\), where \(A_u\) is the high-score suffix set. Under pairwise-independent suffix syndromes this is tight up to a factor two: the expected live probability is at least \(|A_u|/(|G|+|A_u|)\). So 2-independence is enough for recall, but branch-and-bound speed requires a residual-entropy profile; broad suffix sets cover nearly all residues and cannot be thinned further by algebra alone.
- Added the operational residual-entropy audit. `cor:syndrome-residual-entropy-audit` defines \(\mathsf C_\ell(K,\Theta)=\sum_u\min\{1,N_\ell(u;\Theta)/K\}\) and proves that this is the live-prefix work scale for syndrome branch-and-bound, tight up to constants under pairwise-independent suffix residues. Terminal outputs are always thinned by \(K\), while prefix work is reduced to this suffix-count profile. This gives a concrete experiment: measure \(\mathsf C_\ell\) and, more importantly, the cost of the suffix-residue reporter needed to realize it; more hash independence will not replace meet-in-the-middle, max-plus, trellis quotienting, or responsibility-thinned suffix reporting.
- Sharpened the residual-entropy audit: `cor:retained-terminals-dominate-prefixes` observes that \(\mathsf C_\ell(K,\Theta)\le L(\Theta)/K\) at every level, because each high-scoring terminal contributes to one suffix count and \(\min(1,N/K)\le N/K\). Thus for constant-depth smooth product filters, syndrome live-prefix count is already within the retained terminal-output budget. The actual implementation bottleneck is not the number of live prefixes; it is building/querying suffix-residue occupancy summaries without scanning broad suffix sets.
- Closed the ordinary multiply-shift question with a high-bit obstruction. `lem:high-bit-multiply-shift-obstruction` shows that for \(h_{a,b}(x)=\operatorname{hi}_r(ax+b\bmod 2^w)\), the pair \(x,x+2^{w-1}\) has deterministic output difference \(2^{r-1}\) for every odd multiplier. Thus high-bit multiply-add-shift can have useful collision/load behavior but still fails the difference-universal law needed by the syndrome second moment, with \(C_\Delta=\Omega(|G|)\) in the worst case. The proof-safe algebraic version remains affine hashing over a field, or carry-less multiplication plus a linear projection.
- Added the code/lattice positive version of the same idea. `cor:native-linear-syndrome-code-lattice` uses random linear maps \(A_j:V_j\to\mathbb F_q^r\) on the native finite-field block space, giving exact difference-universality because \(A_j(a-b)\) is uniform for every nonzero difference. The retained tuple condition is then a genuine random syndrome coset; after fixing a prefix, the remaining block is decoded inside \(A_h c=\tau\). This turns the useful algebraic property into standard soft list recovery / threshold decoding for linear, Tanner, polar, convolutional, or Construction-A quotient blocks, with quotient collisions charged as bucket multiplicity.
- Removed the terminal-product decoder caveat for the polylog thinning regime. `cor:polylog-terminal-syndrome-filtering` observes that when \(K=\log^{O(1)}m\), the oversampled smooth product filter can simply enumerate all unthinned oversampled active tuples and then apply the 2-independent syndrome test. The unthinned oversampled active count is only \(K\) times the retained count, hence still \(m^{\rho(a,b)}\polylog m\); storage and far candidates remain syndrome-thinned because only retained buckets are stored/looked up. Thus suffix-residue occupancy or code/lattice reporters are only needed for polynomial-rate thinning or nonterminal frontiers, not for the terminal polylog table-recall fix.
