- Added a finite-skeleton implementation bridge for the boosted leader route. `cor:expected-affinity-leader-seed` shows that deterministic almost-unit outgoing affinity at every nonunit skeleton state is not necessary as a primitive: on a decoded skeleton with \(N\) nonunit states and route length \(H\), a randomized leader reporter with expected missed affinity at most \(1/(40NH^2)\) per state can be fixed, by Markov plus a union bound, so all states satisfy the deterministic boosted-leader condition and the total reporter work is within a constant factor of its expectation. For \(N,H=\polylog n\), this costs only an extra \(O(\log(NH))\) local repetition factor.
- Weakened the exact Voronoi-cell posterior primitive to a standard list-decoding target. `cor:additive-nearest-cell-list-compiler` shows that the max-tilted leader sampler does not need exact nearest-cell decoding: for \(Z\sim N(q,I/t)\), it suffices to output a candidate list containing the exact nearest resident, for example all residents within additive squared-distance \(\Delta_{\rm list}\) of the nearest cell, with expected list-generation and verification work \(T_{\rm list}\). The same scalar rejection proof succeeds because the exact nearest valid cell's reweighted mass is still present in the list. Thus code/lattice decoders can aim for output-sensitive additive near-cell list decoding under the smoothed query law instead of exact Voronoi-cell identification.
- Clarified the multiply-shift takeaway in `rem:multiply-shift-syndrome-masks`: black-box 2-independence is enough for the per-query second-moment hit/load calculation, but it is not enough for fast decoding. The useful extra property is that, after fixing a prefix, the residual bucket is an output-sensitive algebraic object. Field-affine and carry-less multiply-plus-projection hashes give linear cosets; ordinary integer multiply-shift with carries/truncation remains a heuristic unless it separately proves the difference-universal law and a residual-bucket reporter bound.
- Added a concrete OSD/code route for the smoothed score-max shortcut. `cor:osd-smoothed-score-max-compiler` groups residents by a linear-code or quotient signature, upper-bounds each bucket score by a separable reliability score, and tries a polylogarithmic grid of OSD thresholds \(M_Y-\Gamma\). If one tried \(\Gamma\) satisfies \(\Gamma\ge M_Y-\operatorname{OPT}_Y\) and \(\exp(\beta\Gamma+\Lambda_*(Y))+R_{Y,\Gamma}\le m^\rho\polylog m\), exact reranking over the listed buckets returns the smoothed score maximizer. Thus the code/lattice pass condition is no longer vague nearest-cell decoding: prove small product-optimum deficit plus small minimum-information-set entropy, with charged quotient-bucket expansion.
- Added the matching raw-list barrier for that compiler. `cor:raw-osd-scoremax-barrier` applies the Gaussian information-charge screen to the OSD score-max setting: a transitive raw label family with top-\(e^{o(r)}\) equivariant labels, charged at full fixed-label far rate, cannot prove the needed near-vs-far threshold margin for any fixed noisy correlation \(\chi<1\). Therefore ordinary SCL/Chase/GMD/OSD nearest-codeword or nearest-lattice top lists are not enough; a viable code/lattice construction must lower the charged object through quotient buckets, aggregate sampling, responsibility listing, reduced-cost reporting, or a Fano/prefix-moment certificate.
- Added the responsibility quotient score-max compiler. `cor:responsibility-quotient-scoremax-compiler` specializes the responsibility-thinned quotient lemma to the smoothed score-max shortcut: if the score maximizer has inverse-polylog hidden mass, total hidden load is \(m^\rho\polylog m\), and sampled quotient classes can list all points with conditional responsibility at least inverse-polylog, then polylog repetitions return the exact smoothed score maximizer. This is a concrete escape from the raw OSD list barrier: the charged object is the heavy point responsibility inside a quotient, not the full raw label.
- Added the large-alphabet obstruction for ordinary low-bit multiply hashing. `cor:large-alphabet-two-adic-multiply-barrier` observes that any \(N\)-symbol subset of \(\mathbb Z/2^r\mathbb Z\) contains a pair with two-adic difference at least \(\lceil\log_2 N\rceil-1\), so cyclic multiply masks have difference-universal constant \(\Omega(N)\) on that alphabet. Thus integer multiply-shift has useful bucket algebra only as a heuristic or with an explicitly paid bias; the proof-safe algebraic primitive remains field-affine or carry-less linear syndromes.
- Added a concrete implementation path for the point-indexed responsibility lister. `lem:linear-sketch-heavy-responsibilities` is a nonnegative linear heavy-hitter sketch: from aggregate bucket/bit counters for the responsibility vector \(r_x\), it recovers every \(r_x\ge\tau\) point with work \(O((\|r\|_1/\tau)\log^2m)\). `cor:sketched-responsibility-lister` applies this to the score-max quotient compiler; since the sampled bucket mass averages to \(\sum_x\mu_Y(x)\), the sketch work is \(m^\rho\polylog m\) under the existing load bound. The new concrete obligation is the reverse data-access problem in `rem:sketched-responsibility-aggregation`: a code/lattice/trellis decoder must aggregate the point-hash terminal statistics, not just compute scalar partition functions or sample hidden labels.
- Added the trellis positive case for that reverse data-access problem. `cor:trellis-sketch-responsibility-aggregation` shows that when hidden labels are acyclic paths and both quotient class and resident incidence are terminal-measurable, each point-hash responsibility counter is an ordinary backward partition function with a different terminal reward. Thus a BCJR/polar/convolutional/Construction-A state graph with \(\polylog m\) edges can implement the sketched heavy-responsibility lister provided terminal sketch rewards are initialized output-sensitively. The pass/fail condition is now concrete: number of requested sketch counters times graph size, plus terminal reward initialization, must be \(m^\rho\polylog m\).
- Quantified the multiply-shift bias budget instead of changing normal forms. `cor:difference-bias-repetition-loss` shows that if a decomposable syndrome hash is only \(C_\Delta\)-difference-universal, then a near-witness set of size \(\eta |G|\) is hit with probability at least \(\eta/(1+C_\Delta\eta)\), so \(O((C_\Delta+\eta^{-1})\log(1/\delta))\) independent panels recover the same failure probability. This means polylogarithmic difference bias is absorbable in the target endpoint, while the two-adic obstructions for ordinary power-of-two multiply-shift are fatal exactly when they make \(C_\Delta\) superpolylogarithmic on the charged alphabet.
- Closed the raw terminal-list side of the responsibility-sketch implementation question. `cor:sparse-terminal-sketch-initialization` shows that if a sampled quotient's terminal posting lists have total incidence \(I(\ell)\), then all heavy-hitter terminal rewards can be initialized in \(O(D(b_0+1)I(\ell))\) sparse updates, and the subsequent trellis recursions stay inside \(m^\rho\polylog m\) whenever \(\mathbb E_{\ell\sim\Pi_Y}I(\ell)\le m^\rho\polylog m\) and the graph is polylogarithmic. It also records the matching raw-access barrier: without precomputed sketch summaries or factorized incidence, exact reward initialization for arbitrary counters requires reading the active incidences. Thus dense terminal incidence must be handled by real code/lattice structure, not by another BCJR partition-function reformulation.
- Added the fixed-sketch precomputation escape hatch for dense terminal lists. `cor:fixed-terminal-sketch-histograms` chooses the heavy-hitter sketch hashes during preprocessing and stores, for each terminal, sparse bucket and bucket-bit histograms. Query-time terminal reward initialization then costs the active histogram mass \(J_W(\ell)\), not the raw incidence \(I(\ell)\), and the number of backward recursions is at most \(\min\{D(b_0+1)W,J_W(\ell)\}\). This turns the remaining dense-incidence question into a concrete storage/query tradeoff: prove \(\mathbb E[J_W(\ell)+K_W(\ell)|E|]\le m^\rho\polylog m\) and global histogram storage \(\mathsf H_W\le m^{1+\rho}\polylog m\), or supply a more structured code/lattice reporter.
- Evaluated the fixed-histogram escape hatch on the random sparse aggregate model. `cor:random-sparse-terminal-histogram-barrier` computes \(\mathbb E J_W=\Theta(DH\min\{ms,W\})\) up to identifier-bit factors. Since the heavy-responsibility sketch at threshold \(K^{-1}\) needs width \(W\gtrsim K\,ms\) on the typical event, fixed histograms do not compress iid sparse terminal incidence at the width needed for recovery: active mass remains \(\Theta(DHms)\). Thus a superpolylogarithmic aggregate size \(H\) is still a barrier in the ANN sparse scale unless the incidences have deterministic sharing/factorization or a stronger code/lattice reporter.
- Added the positive deterministic-sharing compiler matching that barrier. `cor:shared-terminal-signature-histograms` assumes terminal resident sets factor through a signature map \(C_t=C_{\sigma(t)}\), stores sketch histograms once per signature, and charges query initialization to active distinct signature mass \(J_W^\Sigma(\ell)\) rather than active terminals or raw incidence. The pass condition is now explicit: \(\mathbb E[J_W^\Sigma(\ell)+K_W^\Sigma(\ell)|E|]\le m^\rho\polylog m\) and signature-histogram storage \(\mathsf H_W^\Sigma\) fits preprocessing. This is the concrete code/lattice target left after the iid sparse barrier: create shared terminal signatures or an equivalent factorized incidence reporter.
- Specialized shared signatures to code/lattice residues. `cor:linear-residue-signature-histograms` takes resident signatures \(A\phi(x)\) and terminal residues \(\sigma(t)\), with incidence \(U_x(t)=1\{A\phi(x)=\sigma(t)\}\). Then all terminals with the same residue share the same resident histogram, so the responsibility sketch is implemented whenever the active residue-signature histogram mass \(J_W^G(\ell)+K_W^G(\ell)|E|\) is \(m^\rho\polylog m\) in expectation and the residue partition storage \(D(b_0+1)\sum_\alpha\min\{|C_\alpha|,W\}\) fits \(m^{1+\rho}\polylog m\). This is the direct Construction-A/Tanner/polar/lattice pass/fail test for the current branch.
- Balanced the residue-signature pass/fail test. `cor:balanced-residue-histogram-budget` assumes \(|C_\alpha|\le\lambda m/|G|\) and reduces the shared-histogram conditions to \(\mathbb E R_G(\ell)\min\{\lambda m/|G|,W\}\le m^\rho\polylog m\) and \(|G|\min\{\lambda m/|G|,W\}\le m^{1+\rho}\polylog m\), up to sketch/graph polylogs. This connects the 2-independent/multiply-style syndrome idea to a concrete code/lattice audit: the algebraic hash is useful only if the sampled quotient activates few distinct balanced residues, or if a stronger reporter avoids paying active residue mass.
- Separated the positive low-entropy residue case from the random-residue barrier. `cor:affine-active-residue-budget` shows that if a sampled quotient's active residue signatures lie in \(B_\ell\) affine subspaces of dimension \(s_\ell\), then the query test is \(\mathbb E[B_\ell q^{s_\ell}\min\{\lambda m/|G|,W\}]\le m^\rho\polylog m\). `cor:random-active-residue-barrier` shows the opposite model: \(H\) iid uniform active residues occupy \(\Theta(\min\{H,|G|\})\) signatures and have expected histogram mass \(\Theta(D\min\{H,|G|\}\min\{m/|G|,W\})\). Thus the code/lattice route needs affine/subspace collapse, concentrated residue dictionaries, or a reporter that avoids paying per occupied residue; random-looking terminal residues do not remove the overhead.
- Turned the affine-collapse condition into a rank audit for linear quotients. `cor:linear-quotient-rank-active-residues` covers terminal charts with linear quotient constraints \(Bz=b\) and residue observer \(Cz+\gamma\). The active residue dimension in a chart is \(\operatorname{rank}\binom{B}{C}-\operatorname{rank}B\), so the shared-histogram query test is \(\mathbb E[(\sum_a q^{r_{\ell,a}})\min\{\lambda m/|G|,W\}]\le m^\rho\polylog m\). This is the concrete check for Tanner/polar/convolutional/Construction-A/lattice quotients: after sampling the quotient, the resident-signature observer may only see a small residual rank unless a different reporter avoids per-residue initialization.
- Added the matching generic-rank obstruction. `cor:residue-row-span-alignment` identifies low residual rank with row-span alignment: \(C=LB+E\) with \(\operatorname{rank}E\) small. `cor:generic-linear-observer-rank-barrier` shows that for a random observer \(C:\mathbb F_q^N\to\mathbb F_q^r\), the residual rank is the rank of a random \(r\times(N-\operatorname{rank}B)\) matrix and \(\Pr[R\le s]\le4q^{-(r-s)(N-\operatorname{rank}B-s)}\). Therefore generic linear residues expose almost all remaining quotient degrees of freedom and give near-linear active histogram mass in the full-width balanced regime; the positive route must align \(C\) with the quotient constraints, concentrate terminals on a small residue dictionary, or use a non-per-residue reporter.
- Isolated the surviving aligned-quotient parameter window. `cor:aligned-quotient-residual-rank-window` shows that in the full-width balanced regime the exact query condition is \(\mathbb E Q_\ell\le |G|m^{\rho-1}\lambda^{-1}\polylog m\). Thus even a one-residue quotient needs \(|G|\gtrsim \lambda m^{1-\rho}/\polylog m\), and if \(\mathbb E Q_\ell\le q^sL_Q\) then \(q^sL_Q\le |G|m^{\rho-1}\lambda^{-1}\polylog m\). The natural positive design point is therefore \(|G|=m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\) with only polylogarithmically many residual observed signatures; any polynomial residual rank must be paid by a proportionally larger residue group or by abandoning per-residue histogram initialization.
- Added the alignment-vs-randomness audit. `cor:aligned-residue-no-leftover-randomness` shows that any posterior, decoded-path sampler, or internal reporter supported on one cheap aligned quotient has residue law supported on at most \(Q_\ell=\sum_a q^{r_{\ell,a}}\) signatures, hence has total variation distance at least \(1-Q_\ell/|G|\) from a fresh uniform residue mask. At the surviving design point \(|G|=m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\), \(Q_\ell=\polylog m\), the decoded active residues are almost singular relative to uniform. This is the sharp takeaway for pseudo-random subsets: pairwise-independent external syndrome masks can certify hit/load, but the efficiently decoded residue support itself must be low entropy; random-looking leftover terminal residues are exactly what the histogram budget cannot afford.
- Added the positive aligned-residue compiler. `cor:balanced-aligned-residue-lister` combines the native residue histograms, balanced classes, linear quotient rank audit, and nonnegative heavy-responsibility sketch: if \(\mathbb E_{\ell\sim\Pi_Y}Q_\ell\lambda m/|G|\le m^\rho\polylog m\), then every fixed sketch width has aggregation cost \(m^\rho\polylog m\), dyadic histogram storage is only \(\lambda m\polylog m\), and exact filtering of decoded candidates is just one additional residue terminal-reward recursion per candidate, charged by `(RQS1)`. Thus at the design point \(|G|\ge m^{1-\rho}/\polylog m\), \(\lambda=\polylog m\), and \(Q_\ell=\polylog m\), the responsibility-sketch implementation is no longer the blocker. The remaining theorem obligations are now explicitly geometric/structural: `(RQS1)`, `(RQS2)`, residue balance, and row-span-aligned residual rank.
- Reduced the remaining near/load conditions to a residue-posterior target. `cor:balanced-residue-near-prediction` defines the terminal residue posterior \(p_Y(\alpha)=\Pr_{\pi_Y}[\sigma(a)=\alpha]\), proves \(\mu_Y(x)=p_Y(A\phi(x))\), and shows balanced classes give \(\sum_x\mu_Y(x)\le\lambda m/|G|\). Hence `(RQS1)` is automatic at \(|G|\ge\lambda m^{1-\rho}/L_m\). The only near condition is \(p_Y(A\phi(x_*))\ge L_m^{-1}\). If the near residue is an independent uniform residue, this happens with probability at most \(L_m/|G|\), so random balanced partitioning gives the right load but not recall. The actual code/lattice obligation is now a soft residue predictor for the near point, plus the aligned-rank condition.
- Added the information audit for that soft residue predictor. `cor:near-residue-list-fano-cost` turns any posterior \(p_Y\) with \(p_Y(\alpha_*)\ge L^{-1}\) into a size-\(L\) residue list and applies list-Fano: if the near residue is \(\lambda_*\)-balanced over \(|G|=M\) residues and the predictor succeeds with probability \(\delta\), then \(I(\alpha_*;Y)\ge \delta\log(M/L)-\log\lambda_*-\log2\). At the aligned endpoint \(M=m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\), a code/lattice block must therefore transmit roughly \(\delta(1-\rho)\log m\) nats about the near residue, modulo balance and constant losses. This gives a concrete pass/fail information budget for proposed soft decoders.
- Added the matching Gaussian-channel cap. `cor:gaussian-residue-prediction-cap` combines the list-Fano lower bound with the existing Gaussian information contraction: if the near residue is generated from query block \(Q\) and the decoder sees only \(U=\chi Q+\sqrt{1-\chi^2}G\), then \(I(\alpha_*;Y)\le\chi^2H(\alpha_*)\le\chi^2\log|G|\), so any \(L\)-list posterior predictor obeys \(\delta\le(\chi^2\log|G|+\log\lambda_*+\log2)/\log(|G|/L)\). For uniform residues and \(L=\polylog |G|\), typical prediction has ceiling \(\delta\le\chi^2+o(1)\). Therefore the aligned-residue positive route must either use rare informative score directions, a nonuniform conditioned near residue, or a quotient whose charged residue entropy is genuinely lower.
- Quantified the independent auxiliary-weight idea rather than treating it as generic randomness. `lem:scalar-mask-entropy-profile` shows that retaining tuples by an independent scalar additive score \(\sum_jm_j(i_j)\ge s_p\) at density \(p=e^{-L}\) hits a product witness profile \(|A_j|=\exp(\theta_jL+o(L))\) according to the threshold \(\sum_j\sqrt{\theta_j}\) versus \(\sqrt k\). A total-size \(p^{-1}\) witness is only at the hit threshold when its entropy is balanced across coordinates; star-like or collapsed witnesses lose exponentially/polynomially. This cleanly separates scalar auxiliary weights from syndrome/tabulation masks: scalar masks can work for balanced product witnesses, but not for arbitrary top-tuple sets after conditioning; syndrome masks need only pairwise/difference independence for the fixed set, then must pay the algebraic decoding/frontier cost.
- Refined the balanced boundary for that scalar-mask route. `cor:critical-scalar-mask-log-tax` uses the Gaussian maximum quantile correction to show that a density-\(K^{-1}\) scalar additive mask needs \(|A_j|\simeq K^{1/k}(\log K)^\alpha\) with \(\alpha>(k-1)/(2k)\) in each coordinate to hit a balanced \(k\)-product witness whp, while \(\alpha<(k-1)/(2k)\) still misses whp. So the auxiliary-weight idea is not dead, but it carries a genuine \((\log K)^{(k-1)/2}\) product-witness tax at the critical boundary. Pairwise-independent syndrome/tabulation thinning avoids that particular extreme-value tax because it thins the fixed witness set directly by a second moment.
- Added the positive scalar-mask terminal compiler. `lem:scalar-mask-lagrange-decoder` observes that tuples satisfying both the query score threshold \(W\ge T\) and independent mask threshold \(M\ge S\) are listed by an ordinary product heap on the combined score \(W+(S/T)M\), and the heap superset has only polynomial tail overhead because the tangent halfspace has exponent \((T^2+S^2)/(2k)\). `cor:scalar-auxiliary-polylog-terminal-thinning` applies this to the smooth product terminal filter: for \(K=\polylog m\), oversample each block by \(K^{1/k}(\log K)^\alpha\) with \(\alpha>(k-1)/(2k)\), retain tuples with \(\sum_i\xi_{i,j_i}\ge s_K\), and enumerate by the combined heap. Near recall stays constant, retained query/update/far work and storage stay \(m^\rho\polylog m\), and no syndrome-state decoder is needed. The limitation is explicit: raw block sorting costs \(K^{1/k}J_a(u)^{-1}\), so this is a clean polylog-thinning construction, not a free superpolylog thinning method without an additional block reporter.
- Added the matching long-route boundary for scalar masks. `cor:scalar-mask-long-route-barrier` turns the critical balance tax into a depth statement: at density \(K^{-1}\), a balanced \(k\)-product scalar mask needs at least \(K(\log K)^{(k-1)/2-o(k)}\) unmasked witnesses before thinning. For \(K=\log^{\Theta(1)}m\), this is already superpolylogarithmic once \(k\gg\log\log m/\log\log\log m\), and is enormous for the cumulative-score depth \(k=\Theta(\log m)\). Thus the second-random-weighting idea is a useful terminal constant-depth polylog-thinning tool, but it cannot discharge the long branch-and-bound / aggregate-value overhead by itself. The long route still needs aggregate value delivery, a low-residual-entropy residue/syndrome reporter, or another structured output-sensitive decoder.
- Tested a different scalar random-weight law instead of only Gaussian auxiliary weights. `lem:exponential-mask-profile-tax` uses iid \(\mathrm{Exp}(1)\) coordinate masks: a density-\(K^{-1}\) threshold is \(s_K=\log K+(k-1)\log\log K+O(1)\), and every product witness of total leading entropy \(K\) is hit exactly when its total extra log factor exceeds \((\log K)^{k-1}\), independent of whether the witness is balanced or star-like. `cor:exponential-mask-terminal-decoder` shows the Lagrange heap still has only polynomial tail overhead for terminal Gaussian thresholds, so exponential masks are a real terminal polylog-thinning option. They do not solve the long route: for \(K=\log^{\Theta(1)}m\) and \(k=\Theta(\log m)\), the \((\log K)^{k-1}\) tax is far beyond polylogarithmic.
- Closed the broader "try a different scalar mask law" loophole for Weibull tails. `lem:weibull-scalar-mask-trichotomy` proves that if a coordinate mask has tail \(\Pr[X\ge t]=\exp(-t^a+o(t^a))\), then \(a>1\) has the Gaussian/light-tail obstruction and misses one-coordinate stars at density \(K^{-1}\), while \(0<a<1\) is a one-big-jump/heavy-tail law and misses balanced \(K^{1/k}\)-per-coordinate products. The exponential scale \(a=1\) is the only leading-order scalar compromise, and the previous exponential lemma shows that this critical compromise still pays the \(K(\log K)^{k-1}\) witness tax. So changing the scalar random weighting distribution cannot remove the long cumulative-score overhead within the standard Weibull family.
- Added a fixed-form aggregate-value barrier rather than another certificate reformulation. `lem:level-mass-mincut-barrier` builds a depth-\(H\) route trie whose every deterministic level has total prefix mass at least \(H-1\), and whose leaf mass is also large, but whose saturated value is only \(\varepsilon\). The small cut is the antichain of first bottlenecks in each cohort. This confirms that the remaining budget-thinned theorem must prove route-cut or stopping-frontier mass, not merely terminal output count, average complete-route count, or mass on the generation levels.
- Translated the raw code/lattice list information barrier into the ANN query exponent budget. `cor:raw-list-ann-exponent-budget` says that a raw label universe of entropy \(m^\alpha\) needs at least \(m^{(1-\chi^2)\alpha-o(1)}\) reported raw labels before ordinary list-size accounting can overcome Gaussian information contraction. Therefore a raw-label decoder that pays per label fits an \(m^\rho\polylog m\) local query budget only if \((1-\chi^2)\alpha\le\rho\); at the terminal LSF scale \(\alpha=1-\rho\), this requires \(\chi^2\ge1-\rho/(1-\rho)\). This is a concrete pass/fail rule for SCL/Chase/GMD/OSD/nearest-lattice top lists: outside that high-correlation window they must use quotient aggregation, responsibility listing, reduced-cost reporting, or Fano/prefix-moment charging.
- Specialized the raw-list exponent budget to the native critical Euclidean Gaussian channel. `cor:native-critical-raw-list-budget` plugs in \(\rho_c=1/(2c^2-1)\), terminal label entropy \(m^{1-\rho_c}\), and native channel correlation \(a_0=1-1/c^2\). The necessary raw list size becomes \(K\ge m^{2(c^2-1)/c^4-o(1)}\), which fits \(m^{\rho_c}\polylog m\) only when \(c^2\le1+1/\sqrt3\). At \(c=2\), the list exponent is \(3/8\) while the target is \(1/7\). This rules out native-channel raw nearest-codeword/nearest-lattice/top-list decoding as the missing polylog primitive for ordinary approximation factors; a positive code/lattice route has to change the charged object.
- Added the matching positive-side information audit for aligned residues. `cor:native-aligned-residue-information-window` plugs the same native channel into the residue-prediction Fano bound at the aligned design point \(|G|=m^{1-\rho_c}\polylog m\). A one-panel posterior predictor has success cap \(\delta\le a_0^2+o(1)\), but for any fixed \(\delta<a_0^2\) the Gaussian information budget is not violated. At \(c=2\), this cap is \(9/16\). Thus the aligned-residue route remains information-feasible; the real work is constructing the soft residue predictor together with balance and low residual rank, not producing more raw top-list decoders.
- Closed a tempting but invalid shortcut for the soft residue predictor. `cor:independent-residue-no-channel-reliability` applies the Gaussian residue-prediction cap after conditioning on the independent preprocessing seed: a legal seed-independent residue map cannot recover an \(m^{1-\rho}\)-scale residue with channel-code reliability; for uniform residues and polylog lists its success fraction is at most \(\chi^2+o(1)\). `rem:forward-awgn-code-shortcut-invalid` explains the source of the mistake: a forward Gaussian code \(A\to Q\to U\) couples the codebook seed to the source block when reversed into a residue map, and making the seed-conditioned mixture resolve the Gaussian source costs the covering rate \(\frac12\log(1/(1-\tau^2))\), strictly larger than the noisy decoding rate \(\frac12\log(1/(1-\chi^2\tau^2))\). The aligned-residue target is therefore an erasure/list-posterior common-randomness problem, not ordinary reliable AWGN decoding.
- Strengthened the aligned-residue obstruction with the hypercontractive list bound. `lem:balanced-gaussian-residue-list-bound` says that for any seed-independent \(\lambda\)-balanced Gaussian residue partition into \(M\) cells and any query-side list of size \(L\), the agreement probability is at most \(\lambda^{1/(1+\chi)}L^{1/(1+\chi)}M^{-(1-\chi)/(1+\chi)}\). `cor:balanced-aligned-residue-list-barrier` plugs in the aligned endpoint \(M=m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\): polylog lists succeed with only polynomially small probability, and inverse-polylog success needs \(L\ge M^{1-\chi}\lambda^{-1}\polylog^{-1}m\). At the native \(c=2\) point this is \(L\ge m^{3/14-o(1)}\). Thus balanced seed-independent residue prediction is closed; the remaining escape must lower charged residue entropy, tolerate/load-pay nonuniform residues, or move to point-indexed responsibility/leader/rate-coverage reporters.
- Closed the "rare informative score direction" loophole for balanced residue lists. `cor:rare-balanced-residue-direction-barrier` localizes the same hypercontractive argument to a query-visible event \(E\): if a polylog-size list is used only on \(E\), its conditional hit probability is at most \(\lambda^{1/(1+\chi)}L^{1/(1+\chi)}\Pr[E]^{-\chi/(1+\chi)}M^{-(1-\chi)/(1+\chi)}\). Therefore inverse-polylog conditional success at the aligned scale \(M=m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\) requires \(\Pr[E]\le m^{-(1-\rho)(1-\chi)/\chi+o(1)}\). At the native critical point this is \(m^{-2\rho_c+o(1)}\), so amplifying such rare events would cost \(m^{2\rho_c-o(1)}\) trials, already beyond the \(m^{\rho_c}\polylog m\) local budget. Balanced aligned residue prediction cannot be rescued by waiting for inverse-polylog rare good directions.
- Closed the nonuniform residue-list escape by charging far mass rather than list size. `lem:residue-list-hit-load-tradeoff` proves that any Gaussian residue list with correlated hit probability \(\pi\) and independent far residue mass \(\beta\) satisfies \(\pi\le M^{\chi/(1+\chi)}\beta^{1/(1+\chi)}\), hence \(\beta\ge\pi^{1+\chi}M^{-\chi}\). `cor:nonuniform-residue-scan-load-barrier` plugs in \(M=m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\): inverse-polylog near hit forces class-scan load \(m\beta\ge m^{1-\chi(1-\rho)-o(1)}\). At the native critical point this exponent is \((3c^2-2)/(c^2(2c^2-1))\), which is strictly larger than \(\rho_c\); for \(c=2\) it is \(5/14\) versus the \(1/7\) target. Thus nonuniform residue cells only buy prediction by increasing far mass; a positive path must reduce charged entropy before residue listing or use point-indexed responsibility/leader reporting that does not scan whole listed classes.
- Combined the balanced-list and far-load inequalities into a residue entropy squeeze. `cor:target-residue-load-superbudget-list` says that any balanced seed-independent residue-list reporter with inverse-polylog hit and target independent far mass \(\beta\le m^{\rho-1}\polylog m\) must have \(M\ge m^{(1-\rho)/\chi-o(1)}\) residue classes and list size \(L\ge\lambda^{-1}m^{(1-\rho)(1-\chi)/\chi-o(1)}\). At the native critical point this is \(M\ge m^{1+\rho_c-o(1)}\) and \(L\ge \lambda^{-1}m^{2\rho_c-o(1)}\), e.g. \(M\ge m^{8/7-o(1)}\), \(L\ge m^{2/7-o(1)}\) for \(c=2\). Thus enlarging the residue dictionary enough to fix scan load makes the query list itself superbudget; the only surviving code/lattice route must stop paying per listed residue class.
- Closed balanced native residue class listing as an implementation class. `cor:native-balanced-residue-class-listing-barrier` specializes the entropy squeeze to \(\rho_c,a_0\): with subpolynomial residue balance, inverse-subpolynomial near hit and target far mass force \(L\ge m^{2\rho_c-o(1)}\) listed residue classes, contradicting any native local query budget that pays per class and allows only \(m^{\rho_c+o(1)}\) work. This turns the residue branch into a clean dichotomy: balanced class listing is impossible; useful code/lattice residues must use point-indexed responsibility, leader mass, reduced-cost reporting, or aggregate bucket sampling.
- Extended the same obstruction to point-specific Poisson replication on a single residue label. `lem:poisson-residue-hit-load-tradeoff` allows arbitrary retained intensity \(a(x)\) but still only one reported residue \(f(x)\): the Poisson-retained near hit \(\pi_a\) and retained far incidence mass \(\beta_a\) satisfy the same inequality \(\pi_a\le M^{\chi/(1+\chi)}\beta_a^{1/(1+\chi)}\). The proof uses hypercontractivity with \(g_\alpha(x)=(1-e^{-a(x)})1_{f(x)=\alpha}\) and \((1-e^{-u})^{1+\chi}\le u\). `cor:single-residue-rate-coverage-barrier` therefore blocks the naive rate-coverage escape: at \(M=m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\), inverse-polylog near hit still forces retained scan load \(m\beta_a\ge m^{1-\chi(1-\rho)-o(1)}\), i.e. \(m^{5/14-o(1)}\) at \(c=2\). Thus rate coverage must give points multiple query-visible labels or compute point-indexed responsibilities inside a merged hidden class; nonuniform replication of one class label is not enough.
- Quantified the surviving multi-label escape instead of adding another certificate. `lem:multilabel-effective-hit-load-tradeoff` allows arbitrary finite point-label Poisson intensities \(b_\alpha(x)\) and arbitrary query label sets \(B(Y)\). If \(z_\alpha=(\mathbb E b_\alpha(X))\Pr[\alpha\in B(Y)]\), \(r=1/(1+\chi)\), and \(N_r(z)=(\sum_\alpha(z_\alpha/\beta)^r)^{1/(1-r)}\) is the Renyi effective support of the independent far-load weights, then the near hit and far load obey \(\pi\le N_r(z)^{1-r}\beta^r\), hence \(\beta\ge\pi^{1+\chi}N_r(z)^{-\chi}\). `cor:multilabel-effective-entropy-budget` plugs this into the ANN scale: if \(N_r\le m^\eta\polylog m\), inverse-polylog near hit forces scan load \(m^{1-\chi\eta-o(1)}\). At the native critical point, target \(m^{\rho_c}\polylog m\) requires \(\eta\ge(1-\rho_c)/a_0=1+\rho_c-o(1)\); for \(c=2\), any multi-label scheme with effective support \(m^{8/7-\epsilon}\) still pays \(m^{1/7+(3/4)\epsilon-o(1)}\). Thus a true multi-label rate-coverage construction must use essentially storage-scale effective label entropy or switch to point-indexed responsibility/leader/aggregate reporting; an \(m^{1-\rho_c}\)-scale residue dictionary remains polynomially overloaded.
- Added the matching rigidity statement for any union-scanned multi-label construction that actually hits the ANN target. `cor:multilabel-storage-incidence-rigidity` rearranges the effective-support inequality under \(m\beta\le m^{\rho_c}\polylog m\) and inverse-polylog near hit to get \(N_r(z)\ge m^{1+\rho_c-o(1)}\). Thus the marginal query-visible label support must be storage-incidence scale, and its average far-load contribution is at most \(m^{-2+o(1)}\). Under the usual storage budget \(\sum_\alpha A_\alpha\le m^{\rho_c}\polylog m\), the average materialized bucket size over this active support is only \(m^{o(1)}\). This makes the remaining positive target sharper: a union-scanned rate-coverage proof must be almost singleton/incidence-level and spread over the whole storage budget; a compact code/lattice/residue dictionary can work only by aggregate sampling, leader reporting, or point-indexed responsibility rather than by scanning reported labels.
- Added the exact small-treewidth compiler for the code/lattice sampler branch. `lem:junction-tree-quotient-sampler` generalizes the BCJR trellis sampler to arbitrary finite factor graphs with a tree decomposition: a query law \(W_q(z)=\prod_a\psi_a(z_{\partial a};q)\) can be sampled exactly, and pushed forward to quotient buckets, in \(O(|\mathcal J|A^{w+1})\) arithmetic. Thus Tanner/LDPC/polar/Construction-A/lattice factor graphs are proof-useful only when they expose a polylogarithmic exact junction tree, or when an approximation error certificate replaces exactness; generic loopy BP is not a theorem object. `cor:junction-tree-marked-spectrum-verifier` adds the marked version: local additive storage scores are handled by score-polynomial messages, so \(Z_{q,x}^{\ge T}\) and bucket-local ratios \(Z_{q,x,\ell}^{\ge T}/Z_{q,\ell}\) are exact observables when the score range is polylogarithmic. This gives a concrete pass/fail condition for deep code/lattice constructions before invoking heavy-responsibility listing.
- Added the matching exact-inference barrier for expander factor graphs. `lem:expander-factor-graph-junction-barrier` applies the standard treewidth/separator argument to the primal graph of a Tanner/LDPC-style factorization: if every \(h/3\)--\(2h/3\) variable set has external neighborhood at least \(\epsilon h\), then every exact junction tree has width at least \(\epsilon h-1\). For block length \(h=\kappa\log_A m\), exact sum-product therefore costs \(m^{\epsilon\kappa-o(1)}\), before bucket scanning. This rules out generic expanding Tanner/LDPC graphs as the missing polylog aggregate sampler unless the exponent is explicitly budgeted or the construction supplies a certified approximation, quotient aggregation, or point-indexed heavy-responsibility reporter.
- Specialized that barrier to ordinary Tanner expansion. `cor:tanner-check-boundary-junction-barrier` assumes a Tanner graph with maximum variable degree \(\Delta_v\) and linear crossing-check boundary \(|\partial_CU|\ge\eta h\) for every \(h/3\)--\(2h/3\) variable set. Assigning each crossing check to an outside variable shows the primal external neighborhood is at least \(\eta h/\Delta_v\), hence every exact junction tree has width at least \(\eta h/\Delta_v-1\). At block length \(h=\kappa\log_A m\), constant-degree middle-expanding LDPC/Tanner blocks cost \(m^{\eta\kappa/\Delta_v-o(1)}\) for exact aggregate inference. The practical conclusion is sharper: expansion/list-recovery machinery remains useful only as an output-sensitive reporter, low-rank quotient, or certified approximation; it cannot be imported as free exact loopy sum-product.
- Added the certified-approximation interface for the surviving loopy/BP branch. `lem:certified-approximate-quotient-sampler` says that if approximate aggregate bucket weights satisfy \(K^{-1}W_q(b)\le\widehat W_q(b)\le K W_q(b)\) uniformly, the normalized bucket law is distorted by at most \(K^2\). Hence the bucket storage is unchanged, the far sampled co-load in `(BQ2)` is multiplied by \(K^2\), and the near sampled mass in `(BQ3)` is divided by \(K^2\). A polylogarithmic \(K\) still gives the clean polylog ANN sampler, while \(K=m^{o(1)}\) exactly reintroduces the subpolynomial loss. This makes the approximate Tanner/LDPC route falsifiable: local BP marginal accuracy is not enough unless it certifies aggregate bucket weights or equivalent marked sums.
- Added a raw product-grid obstruction that explains why simple finite scalar quadrature is not the missing code/lattice construction by itself. `lem:coordinate-spike-product-grid-barrier` considers labels \(\mathcal A^d\) with one bucket per product label and storage rule \(\langle g,x\rangle\ge T\). If a coordinate spike \(e_i\) is a possible terminal point, its incidence is exactly \(H_TA^{d-1}\), where \(H_T=|\{a\in\mathcal A:a\ge T\}|\). Nonzero spike recall forces \(H_T\ge1\), so the natural critical per-resident incidence cap \(m^\rho\polylog m\) (or the total cap on a worst-case data set with many spike residents) implies \(A\ge M/(m^\rho\polylog m)\) for \(M=A^d\). At near-linear label count \(M=m^{1+o(1)}\), this forces \(d\le(1-\rho)^{-1}+o(1)\), e.g. \(d\le7/6+o(1)\) at \(c=2\). Thus a raw Cartesian product grid cannot have logarithmic depth, near-linear label count, critical storage, and uniform coordinate-spike coverage simultaneously; it must certify terminal \(\ell_\infty\)-flatness or use quotient aggregation/code-lattice structure.
- Clarified what random rotations do and do not rescue for that product-grid branch. `lem:rotation-data-flat-query-spike` proves that a Haar preprocessing rotation makes any fixed \(m\)-point resident set \(\ell_\infty\)-flat at scale \(\sqrt{2\log(2md/\delta)/d}\), so the coordinate-spike storage obstruction can be removed for fixed data. But for every rotation, every resident \(p\), and every target correlation \(a_0\), there is a valid near query \(q\) with \(\langle p,q\rangle=a_0\) and \(\|Rq\|_\infty\ge\sqrt{1-a_0^2}\sqrt{1-1/d}\). Thus the product-grid theorem left after rotation is not an all-flat theorem: it must handle flat resident vectors against arbitrary correlated queries, or add a certified query signature/quotient mechanism for the nonflat coordinate.
- Added a one-dimensional Cramer audit for raw flat product grids. `lem:flat-product-grid-cramer-audit` reduces the raw uniform product-grid sampler on the flat all-ones resident/query and a flat orthogonal far direction to three scalar conditions: storage \(I(\theta)\), near tilted mean \(\psi'(s)\), and far rate \(J_s(\theta)\). `cor:binary-product-grid-height-barrier` shows binary signs at near-linear label count cannot even reach the critical sampled height at \(c=2\): nonzero flat near mass requires \(\nu\ge2(1-\rho)\log2\), i.e. \(1.188\ldots\) when \(\rho=1/7\). Thus growing raw binary product grids are blocked before decoder issues; larger alphabets, different normalization, or quotient/responsibility aggregation are required.
- Found a live finite-alphabet product-grid candidate inside the same frozen audit. `cor:spiked-ternary-flat-audit` shows that at \(c=2\), \(A=3\), \(M=3^d=m^{1+o(1)}\), a tiny perturbation of the centered variance-one multiset \(\{\sqrt2,-1/\sqrt2,-1/\sqrt2\}\) passes the flat all-ones scalar Cramer test with slack: the storage rate is \(D_{\rm Ber}(0.980\ldots\Vert1/3)=0.987\ldots>6\log3/7\), the tilted near mean can be made larger than the threshold, and the flat orthogonal far event is deterministically impossible because the half-range \(3/(2\sqrt2)=1.060\ldots\) is below \(\sqrt{12\log3/7}=1.372\ldots\). This redirects the product-grid branch from "finite alphabets impossible" to the sharper uniform question: does the spiked ternary grid survive all rotated flat residents and arbitrary correlated queries, or does it need quotient/responsibility aggregation?
- Closed that spiked ternary candidate as a uniform raw construction. `cor:spiked-ternary-orientation-barrier` observes that the same half-range \(3/(2\sqrt2)\) is also the maximum score per \(\sqrt d\) on a sign-balanced flat resident \(p_\pm\). Since this is below the critical height \(\sqrt{12\log3/7}\), the storage incidence of \(p_\pm\) is identically zero, robustly under small distinct perturbations of the alphabet. Thus the all-positive ternary pass is orientation-fragile: random rotated residents are sign-balanced, so a usable product-grid construction needs alphabet/sign symmetrization, a data/query signature to align the spike, or quotient/responsibility aggregation.
- Found the first sign-symmetric sparse product-grid candidate that clears the immediate flat screens. `cor:signed-sparse-seven-flat-audit` uses the limiting seven-symbol law \(\{-\sqrt{7/2},0,0,0,0,0,\sqrt{7/2}\}\). At \(c=2\), \(A=7\), \(M=7^d=m^{1+o(1)}\), the height is feasible \(\sqrt{7/2}=1.870\ldots>\sqrt{12\log7/7}=1.826\ldots\), storage has slack \(I_7(\theta)=1.795\ldots>6\log7/7=1.667\ldots\), and with \(s=2.86\) the tilted mean and flat orthogonal far rate pass: \(\psi'(s)=1.827\ldots>\theta\), \(J_s(\theta)=5.113\ldots\). Sign symmetry makes this uniform over all coordinate sign orientations of a flat resident. The remaining test is now precise: flat residents against arbitrary correlated rotated queries and all flat far residents, plus an efficient tilted sampler for the sparse product law.
- Blocked that signed sparse seven-symbol candidate on a native mixed near query. `cor:signed-sparse-seven-mixed-query-barrier` takes a flat resident and a unit query with coordinate profile \(-1/4\) on \(7/23\) of coordinates and \(19/16\) on \(16/23\), giving correlation \(3/4\) and second moment \(1\). For every nonnegative query temperature, negative coordinates have nonpositive tilted storage mean and positive coordinates contribute at most \(\sqrt{7/2}\), so the normalized mean is at most \((16/23)\sqrt{7/2}=1.301\ldots<\sqrt{12\log7/7}=1.826\ldots\). Hoeffding gives near mass \(e^{-\Omega(d)}=m^{-\Omega(1)}\). Thus all fixed sparse sign grids tested so far fail the uniform near-query condition; the surviving product-grid escape needs a richer alphabet with uniform mean over all profiles \(E U=3/4,E U^2=1\), or query-signature/quotient adaptation.
- Generalized the sparse-seven failure into a reusable mixed-profile screen. `lem:mixed-profile-product-grid-mean-test` fixes the raw product-grid normal form and shows that a flat resident against any two-point query profile has tilted storage mean \((1-\gamma_x)\mu(sx)+\gamma_x\mu(sy_x)\); inverse-polylog near mass requires this to reach the normalized height. `cor:low-peak-sign-symmetric-product-grid-barrier` then proves that a sign-symmetric alphabet with top atom \(a_*\) fails some valid near query whenever \(a_0^2a_*<\theta\). At the native \(c=2\), near-linear \(A^d=m\) scale this forces \(a_*\ge(16/9)\sqrt{12\log A/7}\); since variance-one sign-symmetric \(A\)-symbol laws have \(a_*\le\sqrt{A/2}\), all such alphabets with \(A\le39\) are blocked. The product-grid escape, if it exists, must now use a fairly large/heavily spiked alphabet or a query-signature/quotient mechanism, not another small symmetric scalar quadrature.
- Closed the large-peak loophole for sign-symmetric raw product grids. `cor:sign-symmetric-product-grid-dichotomy` combines the mixed-profile screen with a big-jump storage lower bound: if \(a_*<\theta/a_0^2\), mixed near recall is exponentially small; if \(a_*\ge\theta/a_0^2\), forcing \(r>\theta/a_*\) coordinates to the top atom stores the flat resident in at least \(m^{1-r-o(1)}\) labels. Since at the native exponent \(a_0^2<1-\rho_c\), one can take \(r<1-\rho_c\), so storage exceeds \(m^{\rho_c+\Omega(1)}\). Thus no sign-symmetric one-bucket-per-label near-linear product alphabet can simultaneously meet critical storage and full near-query recall. The remaining product-grid escapes must break sign symmetry with a certified signature, quotient labels, or move to point-indexed responsibility/aggregate reporting.
- Removed the fixed asymmetric scalar-alphabet escape as well. `lem:asymmetric-product-grid-mean-top-squeeze` works with an arbitrary fixed finite alphabet: mixed two-point near queries force \((1-a_0^2)\bar a+a_0^2a_+\ge\theta_A\), while flat-resident storage forbids \((1-r)\bar a+ra_+>\theta_A\) for every \(r<1-\rho_c\). Since \(a_0^2<1-\rho_c\), the only possible limit is \(a_+=\bar a=\theta_A\). `cor:fixed-alphabet-product-grid-barrier` then observes that a finite alphabet whose maximum equals its mean is constant, so either every raw label stores the flat resident or none do, violating storage or near recall. This closes near-linear one-bucket-per-label raw product grids for every fixed scalar alphabet; any remaining product-grid route must use growing/knife-edge alphabets, query signatures, or quotient/responsibility aggregation.
- Extended the product-grid squeeze to regular growing alphabets. `lem:regular-growing-product-alphabet-knife-edge` assumes \(A_m^d=m^{1+o(1)}\), \(d\to\infty\), and bounded-difference concentration scale \(\eta_m=R_m\sqrt{\log L_m/d}=o(1)\), where \(R_m\) is the alphabet range and \(L_m=\polylog m\). Mixed two-point near recall gives \((1-a_0^2)\bar a_m+a_0^2a_{+,m}\ge\theta_m-O(\eta_m)\); storage at \(r_0=(a_0^2+1-\rho_c)/2\) gives \((1-r_0)\bar a_m+r_0a_{+,m}\le\theta_m+O(\eta_m)\). Hence \(a_{+,m}-\bar a_m=O(\eta_m)\) and \(\bar a_m=\theta_m+O(\eta_m)\). So the only remaining raw scalar product-grid escape is a true knife-edge alphabet whose mean, top atom, and threshold coalesce at the concentration scale, or else a query signature / quotient / responsibility mechanism.
- Closed that regular growing-alphabet escape once sign-balanced residents are included. `cor:regular-product-grid-sign-balanced-barrier` pairs positive and negative coordinates of \(p_\pm=d^{-1/2}(1,\ldots,1,-1,\ldots,-1)\), turning any scalar alphabet into the symmetric difference alphabet \((X-X')/\sqrt2\) with mean zero, label count unchanged, and concentration scale \(2R_m\sqrt{\log L_m/d}\). Two-point block query profiles lift to valid original near queries for \(p_\pm\). Applying the knife-edge lemma to the block panel would force the zero mean to equal the positive critical threshold \(\sqrt{2(1-\rho_c)\log A_m^2}\), a contradiction. Thus regular one-bucket-per-label scalar product grids are closed under the orientation-uniform condition left by random rotation; surviving product-grid routes must be nonregular, signature-adaptive, quotient-based, or responsibility-based.
- Weakened the boosted leader skeleton burden from pointwise state guarantees to the actual route-average value quantity. `cor:average-affinity-leader-skeleton` applies the typical route-law toll certificate to the affinity-proportional skeleton: if \(D(V)=\sum_{s\prec V}\log_+(1/\Omega_s)\) has expectation \(B\) under the decoded route law, then the original harmonic value is at least \(\frac12e^{-2B}/L(s_0)\). A randomized reporter seed with expected route toll \(B\) and expected work \(W_0\) can be fixed with work \(4W_0\) and value \(\frac12e^{-8B}/L(s_0)\). This is a genuine relaxation of the earlier `cor:expected-affinity-leader-seed` union-bound criterion: bad low-route-mass states no longer need \(H^{-2}\) affinity loss, so the remaining construction can allocate leader/reporter effort along the route law like pruned lattice enumeration rather than uniformly over every exposed skeleton state.
- Added the positive side of the scalar auxiliary-mask story. `lem:additive-mask-overlap-spectrum` computes the second moment of an additive Gaussian mask on an arbitrary top-tuple set \(A\) in terms of its coordinate-overlap spectrum \(N_r(A)\): pairs sharing \(r\) coordinates contribute at scale \(p^{2k/(k+r)}\). `cor:scalar-mask-spread-criterion` says scalar masks are usable when the query-conditioned top set has small high-overlap pair energy, with Lagrange-heap decoding and only a polylog repetition loss. This cleanly separates the randomness question from the decoder question: additive scalar masks work for overlap-spread witnesses, fail for stars/entropy-collapsed witnesses, while pairwise-independent syndromes remove the overlap spectrum entirely at the cost of an explicit syndrome-state or residue-list decoder.
- Audited that overlap criterion on the actual additive Gaussian top sets \(A_T=\{\sum_jW_{j,i_j}\ge T\}\). `cor:additive-top-set-overlap-tax` shows \(\mathbb E N_r(A_T)\) contributes \(B^{r(k-r)/(k+r)}A^{-2r/(k+r)}\) (up to fixed-\(k\) polynomial tail factors) to the normalized scalar-mask second moment when the auxiliary density is \(p=A/(B^kq_T)\). The singleton-overlap term alone forces \(A\ge B^{(k-1)/2}\) up to logs to pass the spread certificate. Conclusion: a second additive weighting is not a proof-safe critical random subset for broad product alphabets; it is acceptable for polylog-sized alphabets or after an independent overlap-flattening mechanism, while field-affine/tabulation syndromes remain the clean way to get true pairwise-independent thinning with algebraic decoding.
- Added the matching nonterminal syndrome decoder barrier. `cor:materialized-residue-summary-barrier` observes that for a prefix suffix set \(A_u\), a pairwise-independent syndrome occupies at least \(K|A_u|/(K+|A_u|)\) residues in expectation. Thus any exact oracle that materializes all completable residual classes costs \(\Omega(K)\) per broad prefix with \(|A_u|\ge K\). This rules out hiding a superpolylogarithmic syndrome range inside branch-and-bound by explicit residue summaries: to be endpoint-useful, polynomial/subpolynomial thinning must come from an implicit reporter such as a polylog-state trellis/junction tree, certified meet-in-the-middle or max-plus frontier, heavy-responsibility lister, or leader/quotient rule.
- Composed the heavy-responsibility route with the average-affinity leader skeleton instead of adding another certificate form. `cor:heavy-responsibility-average-affinity` shows that a local marked near mass \(\mu_s\) gives one-trial useful leader affinity at least \(\mu_s/2\), and \(R_s\) independent trials give \(\Omega_s\ge1-\exp(-R_s\mu_s/2)\). Plugging this into `cor:average-affinity-leader-skeleton` gives the exact route toll \(\mathbb E_\Lambda\sum_{s\prec V}\log(1/(1-\exp(-R_s\mu_s/2)))\). The useful consequence is also the barrier: inverse-polylog local marked mass closes a route only after boosting/allocating along the route, or when the nonunit skeleton is shallow; without boosting it pays \(\mathbb E|V|\log L_m\), so long cumulative-score trees still need contraction, quotienting, stronger marked mass, or route-average repetition.
- Operationalized that bridge with route-local boosting accounting. `cor:route-local-heavy-reporter-boosting` chooses \(R_s=\lceil2\mu_s^{-1}\log(1/\varepsilon_s)\rceil\), obtains average affinity toll at most \(2\sum_s\pi_s\varepsilon_s\), and charges expected reporter work by \(\sum_s\pi_sT_s\mu_s^{-1}\log(1/\varepsilon_s)\) plus lower-order terms. The continuous optimum for a fixed toll budget has \(\varepsilon_s=\min\{1/2,2T_s/(\lambda\mu_s)\}\). This makes the next pass/fail quantity concrete: route-occupied cost per marked near mass \(T_s/\mu_s\). If its water-filled boosting cost is superpolylogarithmic, the current heavy-lister route still has the old overhead and must improve marked mass, lower lister cost through code/lattice structure, or contract the skeleton.
- Composed aligned residues with the route-local heavy reporter budget. `cor:aligned-residue-route-heavy-reporter` shows that a balanced residue trellis with active residual-rank budget \(\mathcal Q_s\), residue class load \(\eta_s=\lambda_s m/M_s\), and local near-residue posterior mass \(\mu_s\) has one-trial lister work \(T_s\le d\polylog(m)\eta_s(\mathcal Q_s+\mu_s^{-1})\) and far marked spectrum \(F_s\le\eta_s\). Plugging this into the route-local boost gives the exact sufficient sum \(\sum_s\pi_s\eta_s(\mathcal Q_s\mu_s^{-1}+\mu_s^{-2})\log(1/\varepsilon_s)\le m^\rho\polylog m\). At the aligned design point this isolates the remaining constructive target: \(M_s\gtrsim\lambda_sm^{1-\rho}\), \(Q_s=\polylog m\), \(\mu_s^{-1}=\polylog m\), and only polylogarithmic expected nonunit route length.
- Added the route-level consequence of random-looking active residues. `cor:random-active-residue-route-barrier` plugs the existing iid active-residue lower bound into the route-local aligned reporter: in the full-width balanced regime, one local trial already costs \(\Omega(D_sm\mathcal H_s/M_s)\), where \(\mathcal H_s=\mathbb E\min\{H_s,M_s\}\) is the occupied active-residue count. After boosting, the histogram work is at least \(\sum_s\pi_sD_sm\mathcal H_s M_s^{-1}\mu_s^{-1}\log(1/\varepsilon_s)\). At the aligned point \(M_s=m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\), \(\mu_s^{-1}=\polylog m\), this forces \(\sum_s\pi_s\mathcal H_s\log(1/\varepsilon_s)=\polylog m\). Thus 2-independent affine/carry-less syndrome randomness is statistically enough but not structurally enough: route-occupied decoded residues must collapse to polylog degrees of freedom, or the construction needs a point-indexed responsibility/leader/aggregate reporter not charged by occupied residue count.
- Added the symmetric rank barrier for the low-rate-code branch. `cor:generic-linear-quotient-rank-barrier` fixes the residue observer \(C\) and samples the quotient row space \(U=\operatorname{row}(B)\) uniformly. The residual rank \(R=\dim((U+\operatorname{row}C)/U)\) satisfies \(\Pr[R\le s]\le16q^{-(r-s)(d_*-s)}\), \(d_*=N-\operatorname{rank}B\). Therefore at the aligned point \(q^r=m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\), getting \(R=O(\log\log m)\) from a random quotient with \(d_*-O(\log\log m)=\Omega(\log m)\) has probability \(q^{-\Omega((\log_q m)^2)}\). Generic random parity checks/Tanner quotients do not create the row-span alignment needed for \(Q_\ell=\polylog m\); the quotient must structurally contain the residue observer up to polylog residual directions, or the reporter must stop paying per active residue.
- Composed the multi-label entropy squeeze with actual route-local boosting. `cor:route-local-union-effective-entropy-barrier` applies the hypercontractive hit/load tradeoff to one route state and then multiplies by \(R=\lceil\mu^{-1}\log(1/\varepsilon)\rceil\) repetitions: a union-scanned reporter with near hit \(\mu\), Gaussian correlation \(\chi\), and effective label count \(N_*\) has boosted far-incidence load at least \(m\mu^\chi N_*^{-\chi}\log(1/\varepsilon)\). At the native endpoint this fits \(m^{\rho_c}\polylog m\) only when \(N_*\ge m^{1+\rho_c-o(1)}\) for inverse-polylog \(\mu\); if \(N_*\le m^{1+\rho_c-\delta}\), even one inverse-polylog occupied state costs \(m^{\rho_c+a_0\delta-o(1)}\). This closes the "small but cleverly decoded code dictionary, then scan labels" route: sub-storage-scale code/lattice/facility labels must switch to point-indexed responsibility, leader mass, aggregate sampling, or reduced-cost reporting.
- Bridged the rate-coverage certificate to the surviving query-facing access primitive. `cor:point-mass-rate-coverage-reporter` shows that a retained structure which can list all points with heavy aggregate coverage \(b_x(B(q,Z))\ge\theta\), for inverse-polylogarithmic \(\theta\), implements the smoothed witness-list endpoint whenever the exponential miss bound has constant slack and the usual rate-load bound holds. The proof is just \(1_{\{b<\theta\}}\le e^\theta e^{-b}\), so this is not a new certificate form: it is the operational point-mass interface for the existing \((RI3)\)-\((RI4)\) normal form. `lem:rate-coverage-expansion-access` adds the matching access accounting: if those heavy point masses are obtained by expanding reported labels, the expected work is exactly \(\sum_x b_x(B)\), so the route-local effective-entropy barrier still applies. Random syndromes or multiply-shift hashes are therefore useful only if their algebra exposes the heavy point masses through a compact aggregator, sketch, quotient, trellis, or reduced-cost reporter rather than by scanning the selected label union.
- Composed the existing heavy-hitter sketch with the new point-mass rate-coverage interface. `cor:sketched-rate-coverage-point-mass` applies the nonnegative linear sketch lemma directly to the vector \(r_x=b_x(B(q,Z))\): pairwise-independent rows list every point with \(b_x(B)\ge\theta\) using \(O(\theta^{-1}\sum_x b_x(B)\log^2m)\) decoded-candidate work, plus the time to compute the sketch counters. Thus 2-independent multiply-shift/affine hashes are random enough for the identifier-recovery step. The remaining code/lattice obligation is exactly the aggregate counter problem \(\sum_{a\in B}\sum_x c_xb_{x,a}\) for hash-bucket indicators \(c_x\), without expanding the selected label union. This turns the pseudorandom-subset question into a sharper algebraic pass/fail test: can the construction compute these label-linear point-mass sketches at the rate-load scale?
- Added a concrete positive compiler for those aggregate counters. `cor:shared-signature-rate-coverage-sketch` assumes the rate-coverage intensities factor through a resident signature partition, \(b_{x,a}=\omega_a1\{x\in C_{\sigma(a)}\}\). If the query decoder outputs the sparse weighted active-signature list \(u_\alpha(B)=\sum_{a\in B:\sigma(a)=\alpha}\omega_a\), fixed pairwise-independent hash histograms over the resident signature classes compute every heavy-point sketch counter by \(T_{s,j}(B)=\sum_\alpha u_\alpha(B)H_{\alpha,s}(j)\). The histogram storage is only \(m\polylog m\) for a partition, and the query cost is active signature histogram mass. In a \(\lambda\)-balanced residue partition with \(M\gtrsim\lambda m^{1-\rho}\polylog^{-1}m\), polylogarithmically many active weighted residues are enough to compute the point-mass reporter at the \(m^\rho\polylog m\) scale; iid-looking polynomially many active residues fall back to the route-local union entropy barrier. This gives the first crisp positive target for multiply-shift/affine sketch rows: not more randomness, but a decoder that collapses the selected labels to a polylog weighted residue-signature support.
- Added the matching occupancy obstruction for the shared-signature compiler. `cor:random-active-signature-support-barrier` says that if a decoded label set \(B\) of size \(N\) is assigned pairwise-independent uniform signatures in a group of size \(K\), then the expected number of occupied weighted active signatures is at least \(KN/(K+N)\); in particular \(N\ge K\) forces \(\Omega(K)\) active signatures. Thus random affine/tabulation/carry-less signatures are sufficient randomness for second-moment thinning but not for sparse materialization of the aggregate-counter input. To use `cor:shared-signature-rate-coverage-sketch`, the decoder must target a small residue set in advance or structurally collapse labels to few signatures; "select a broad set, then hash it again and list occupied signatures" is just the usual occupancy barrier.
- Added the positive aligned-linear specialization for the frozen point-mass interface. `cor:aligned-linear-chart-rate-coverage-sketch` shows that if rate-coverage labels are uniform affine code/lattice charts \(B_az=b_a\) with resident signature \(\gamma_a+C_az\), then the exact weighted active-signature list has size \(\sum_a q^{R_a}\), where \(R_a=\operatorname{rank}(B_a;C_a)-\operatorname{rank} B_a\). In a balanced residue partition with \(M\gtrsim\lambda m^{1-\rho}\polylog^{-1}m\), polylogarithmic expected residual observed rank budget and polylogarithmic linear-algebra setup are enough to compute the heavy point-mass sketches at \(m^\rho\polylog m\) query cost. This is the concrete constructive target for code/lattice charts: hide many labels in each quotient, but leave only polylog observed residue degrees of freedom.
- Blocked the tempting Fourier/transform aggregation escape for point-mass coverage. `lem:sparse-fourier-point-mass-uncertainty` proves that a nonnegative residue weight \(u\) with Fourier support \(R\) and total mass \(U\) has \(\|u\|_\infty\le RU/M\). `cor:sparse-fourier-rate-coverage-barrier` combines this with two-sided balanced resident classes: if some residue has point mass at least \(\theta\) and the total rate-coverage load is \(m^\rho\polylog m\), then the Fourier support must be \(R\ge m^{1-\rho}\polylog^{-1}m\). Thus Walsh/Fourier/carry-less transform histograms can compute broad laws, but they cannot replace the sparse active-residue list for nonnegative heavy point masses unless they use storage-scale spectral support.
- Added a positive alternative to active-support materialization. `cor:weighted-signature-sampler-point-mass` shows that in the shared-signature model it is enough to sample from the active signature law \(u_\alpha(B)/U_B\), evaluate sampled weights, and expand the sampled signatures whose mass is at least \(\theta\). With \(O((U_B/\theta)\log(m+U_B/\theta))\) samples, all heavy signatures are recovered with failure \(O(m^{-2})\), and the class expansion cost is \((\lambda m/M)(U_B/\theta)\). At the aligned residue scale \(M\gtrsim\lambda m^{1-\rho}\polylog^{-1}m\), a polylogarithmic total-mass log moment and a polylog/target-cost exact sampler implement the point-mass reporter. This is the main surviving positive algebraic path: broad active laws are allowed if they are sampleable and have small log-moment total mass; sparse support is sufficient but not necessary.
- Weakened the sampler access primitive further. `cor:unfiltered-signature-sampler-witness` observes that exact sampled signature-mass evaluation is not needed for the ANN witness-list endpoint: draw \(O((U_B/\theta)\log(m+U_B/\theta))\) signatures from \(u_\alpha(B)/U_B\) and expand every sampled class. Heavy signatures are still hit with \(O(m^{-2})\) conditional failure, while false positives cost only the sampled class-expansion load \((\lambda m/M)(U_B/\theta)\log(m+U_B/\theta)\). `cor:acyclic-aggregate-signature-reporter` plugs in the existing compressed-DAG/BCJR/junction-tree samplers: backward partition functions supply \(U_B\), top-down sampling supplies signatures, and no active-support enumeration or per-signature partition evaluation is required. The remaining constructive target is now just a code/lattice/trellis quotient with polylog total-mass log moment and rate-budget sampled class expansion.
- Collapsed the sampler total-mass condition to the original rate load under two-sided signature balance. `cor:two-sided-balance-sampler-load` proves \(U_B\le (M/\kappa m)L_B\) and \((\lambda m/M)U_B\le(\lambda/\kappa)L_B\), where \(L_B=\sum_x b_x(B)\), whenever active resident classes satisfy \(\kappa m/M\le |C_\alpha|\le\lambda m/M\). Therefore the unfiltered sampler expansion cost, and the BCJR/DAG top-down sampling cost when paths are polylogarithmic and \(M/m\) is polylogarithmic, are both charged by the same \(L_B\) in `(PMR2)` up to polylogs. This removes "prove polylog total active mass" as a separate burden in balanced code/lattice quotients; the actual obstruction is active mass leaking into empty/tiny signatures, where rate load can be small but raw aggregate samples are wasted.
- Removed even that empty/tiny-signature leakage by changing the sampling law. `cor:resident-size-biased-sampler` samples signatures proportional to \(|C_\alpha|u_\alpha(B)\), then samples a uniform resident in the class, so the emitted point has exactly law \(b_x(B)/L_B\) with \(L_B=\sum_x b_x(B)\). With \(O((L_B/\theta)\log(m+L_B/\theta))\) draws it hits every point with \(b_x(B)\ge\theta\), and `(PMR2)` pays the sample count directly. `cor:acyclic-resident-size-biased-sampler` implements this in a DAG/BCJR/junction-tree sampler by using terminal boundary \(H(t)=|C_{\alpha(t)}|\). This is the sharpest access primitive so far: no active-support enumeration, no per-signature mass evaluation, no two-sided balance requirement for online sampling; the remaining code/lattice task is exact resident-size-weighted aggregate sampling plus the original tilted miss/load certificate.
- Added the implementation bridge for the case where that resident-count boundary is not naturally part of the trellis state. `cor:balanced-rejection-resident-sampler` starts from the raw aggregate sampler \(u_\alpha(B)/U_B\): draw a raw signature, accept it with probability \(|C_\alpha|/C_+\), then draw a uniform resident in \(C_\alpha\). One proposal emits \(x\) with probability \(b_x(B)/(C_+U_B)\), so \(O((C_+U_B/\theta)\log(m+C_+U_B/\theta))\) proposals hit all \(b_x(B)\ge\theta\) points. Under active two-sided balance \(C_-\le |C_\alpha|\le C_+\), \(C_+U_B\le(C_+/C_-)L_B\), so the proposal work is charged by the same `(PMR2)` load. This means a BCJR/junction-tree/lattice quotient can use its native raw sampler plus a class-count rejection filter instead of installing the arbitrary resident-count table as a terminal potential; the only price is the balance ratio, and empty/tiny active classes are now explicitly the failure mode.
- Clarified exactly where pairwise-independent hashing is enough. `lem:pairwise-oblivious-active-load` fixes a nonnegative active signature law \(u\) before the resident-signature seed is drawn and shows that a pairwise-independent uniform map \(h:S\to\Sigma\) has \(\mathbb E L_h=(m/M)U\), \(\operatorname{Var}L_h\le(m/M)U^2\), and lower-tail failure at most \(4M/m\). Thus multiply-shift/affine 2-independence is enough for the annealed average resident mass of one seed-oblivious active law. It is not enough, by itself, for the quenched rejection-sampler condition: we still need an upper cap \(C_+\) and protection against query-adaptive active laws concentrating on empty/tiny buckets. That extra condition must come from stronger concentration, deterministic balancing, or code/lattice alignment.
- Quantified how much stronger the resident-balance randomness has to be. `lem:limited-independence-resident-balance` uses the standard bounded-independence Chernoff moment bound: for a \(2r\)-wise independent uniform resident signature map into \(M\) classes, with \(\mu=m/M\), all class sizes lie in \([\mu/2,3\mu/2]\) except with probability at most \(2M(Cr/\mu)^r\). At the aligned scale \(M\le m^{1-\rho}\polylog m\), constant \(r=O_c(1)\) already gives high-probability two-sided balance. This separates the randomness from the decoder: balance itself needs only limited independence, but a polynomial/tabulation hash is useful for LSF only if the query-side code/lattice sampler can still compute the active law \(u_\alpha(B)\) and predict the near residue. Linear affine/carry-less maps keep the decoder algebra but give only the weaker second-moment guarantee unless additional concentration or deterministic balancing is proved.
- Added the exact linear-source boundary for those algebraic maps. `lem:linear-source-resident-balance` shows that if the residents are a complete affine source \(z_0+W\) with uniform multiplicity and \(A(W)=G\), then every linear residue class has exactly \(m/|G|\) residents, and uniform class sampling is just sampling \(\ker(A|_W)\). This is the clean positive case for Construction-A/code panels where the resident cloud is itself a complete linear source. The matching obstruction `cor:low-rank-linear-resident-balance-barrier` says that if \(\phi(S)\) lies in affine dimension \(d<r\), then a linear observer into \(\mathbb F_q^r\) has at most \(q^d\) nonempty classes; at the aligned scale it needs \(d\ge(1-\rho)\log_qm-O_c(\log\log m)\) before full two-sided balance is even possible. Thus affine/carry-less algebra is not enough on low-rank resident data: either the resident source must be linearly rich and regular, or the sampler must use explicit resident counts/nonlinear balancing.
- Weakened the rejection sampler condition from pointwise active lower balance to weighted active balance. The updated `cor:balanced-rejection-resident-sampler` only needs \(C_+U_B\le\Lambda_BL_B\), so the active law's average resident class size, not the minimum active class size, pays for proposals. Pointwise two-sided balance is now just the sufficient case \(\Lambda_B=C_+/C_-\). This matters for random or algebraic signatures: empty/tiny classes are not automatically fatal if the raw aggregate sampler assigns them negligible mass, and the quantity to prove for a concrete code/lattice quotient is the weighted leakage ratio \(C_+U_B/L_B\).
- Sharpened the raw expansion branch rather than adding another certificate. `cor:raw-expansion-resident-load` shows that if the native sampler draws raw signatures from \(u_\alpha/U_B\) and expands sampled classes, the expected scan is exactly charged by \(L_B=\sum_\alpha |C_\alpha|u_\alpha\), with no upper class-size cap and no two-sided balance. The only extra sampling term is \(U_B\) times the path-sampling cost, so the sufficient leakage condition is just \(U_B\le\Gamma_BL_B\); if every active signature is nonempty this holds with \(\Gamma_B=1\). This corrects the earlier intuition: tiny nonempty classes hurt rejection point-sampling, but they do not hurt raw class expansion. The real raw-sampler leakage is active mass placed on empty resident signatures, or an exact path sampler whose per-draw cost is too large.
- Removed the empty-signature leakage for raw expansion when the resident support is terminal-testable. `cor:resident-support-conditioned-raw-expansion` conditions the native aggregate sampler on \(\Sigma^+=\{\alpha:|C_\alpha|>0\}\) using the binary terminal boundary \(H(t)=1\{c_{\alpha(t)}>0\}\). Then \(U_B^+\le L_B\) automatically and both top-down samples and expansion scans are charged by the original point-mass load, with no resident-size boundary and no balance theorem. This is the cleanest current access primitive for code/lattice quotients: expose the resident image/support in the trellis state, sample only terminals that can report a point, and leave the remaining burden to the original tilted miss/load plus cheap support-conditioned backward recursion.
- Specialized that support predicate to linear code/lattice residents. `cor:linear-resident-image-support-conditioning` shows that for a complete affine resident source \(z_0+W\) and linear observer \(A\), the nonempty signatures are exactly the affine image \(Az_0+A(W)\), with equal fiber size on that image and zero outside. Thus support conditioning is just the quotient terminal syndrome \(\pi(Cz)=\pi(Az_0-\gamma)\) for a terminal signature \(\gamma+Cz\). This turns the low-rank balance barrier into a concrete decoder condition: low resident rank prevents global balance over \(G\), but raw expansion still works if the trellis can impose the image-membership syndrome cheaply.
- Added the affine-chart implementation of that decoder condition. `cor:support-conditioned-linear-chart-sampler` augments each chart \(B_az=b_a\) by the quotient syndrome \(\pi C_az=\pi(Az_0-\gamma_a)\), computes its mass \(\omega_a q^{N_a-\operatorname{rank}\widetilde B_a}\), and samples uniformly from the augmented affine solution space. The only algebraic toll is \(\bar R_a=\operatorname{rank}(B_a;\pi C_a)-\operatorname{rank}B_a\), not the full active residue rank \(\operatorname{rank}(B_a;C_a)-\operatorname{rank}B_a\). This is a real improvement over active-residue materialization: broad residue images are acceptable if the quotient support syndrome is cheap to maintain.
- Added the weighted trellis version. `cor:quotient-syndrome-trellis-support-sampler` augments a BCJR trellis by the accumulated quotient syndrome in \(G/A(W)\), uses terminal boundary \(\pi(\gamma+\sum c(e))=\pi(Az_0)\), and samples exactly from the support-conditioned path law. The backward cost is \(O(Q_B|E_B|)\), where \(Q_B\) is the maximum reachable quotient-syndrome width, not the number of full resident residues. This is the concrete audit for Tanner/polar/convolutional/Construction-A samplers with nonconstant product weights: prove \(E[Q_B|E_B|]\le m^\rho\polylog m\) and the point-mass miss/load certificate, or the branch is still open.
- Added the matching random-increment barrier for that trellis audit. `cor:random-quotient-trellis-width-barrier` shows that if quotient increments are pairwise-independent and uniform inside a compact merged trellis, then a native state representing \(N_v\) prefixes expands to expected quotient width at least \(M N_v/(M+N_v)\), where \(M=|G/A(W)|\). Randomness is therefore the wrong property for cheap support conditioning: it thins fixed terminal sets, but it refills merged states. A useful code/lattice/multiply-style quotient must be aligned with the native state, leave only \(O(\log\log m)\) residual quotient coordinates, or use a reporter not charged by quotient-state occupancy.
- Added the positive checkpoint version of the syndrome idea. `cor:checkpoint-syndrome-local-state` splits a long product path into chunks and refreshes an independent small syndrome in each chunk. A fixed terminal set is thinned by \(\prod_sK_s^{-1}\), but recall follows from conditional continuation entropy: if every surviving good prefix has at least \(\eta_sK_s\) good continuations in chunk \(s\), the retained hit probability is at least \(\prod_s\eta_s/(1+\eta_s)\). The decoder carries only the current chunk syndrome, so state width is \(\max_sK_s\) or \(\sum_sK_s|I_s|\), not \(\prod_sK_s\). This is a concrete constructive route around the random-quotient refill barrier: use fresh local randomness after a checkpoint is discharged, and numerically/provably audit the conditional continuation counts.
- Added the missing depth boundary for that checkpoint route. `cor:checkpoint-syndrome-lossy-depth` freezes the checkpoint formulation and computes its route toll \(\mathcal T_{\rm chk}=\sum_s\log(1+\eta_s^{-1})\). The checkpoint certificate is inverse-polylogarithmic exactly when \(\mathcal T_{\rm chk}=O(\log\log m)\); constant-quality lossy checkpoints are therefore allowed only \(O(\log\log m)\) times. If a long cumulative-score route has \(\Theta(\log m)\) checkpoints, then almost all checkpoints must have near-deterministic continuation, e.g. homogeneous \(\eta_s\) must be \(\Omega(\log m/\log\log m)\). This keeps the branch honest: checkpoint syndromes can avoid product state, but they do not by themselves remove subpolynomial overhead for many independent lossy levels. A concrete code/lattice route now has to prove few lossy checkpoints, large continuation entropy at almost every checkpoint, structural quotient alignment, or switch to a route-average leader/responsibility reporter.
- Added the positive cut-rank certificate matching the random quotient refill barrier. `cor:prefix-cut-rank-quotient-width` says that for an affine-chart description of the prefixes reaching a trellis state \(v\), the quotient width is bounded by \(\sum_a q^{\operatorname{rank}(B_{v,a};D_{v,a})-\operatorname{rank}B_{v,a}}\). Thus the exact support-conditioned BCJR sampler is cheap when the accumulated resident-support quotient syndrome is determined by the native prefix state up to \(O(\log\log m)\) residual linear coordinates. Equivalently, a row-span alignment \(D=LB+E\) with \(\operatorname{rank}E=O(\log\log m)\) is the concrete positive target for convolutional, polar, recursive lattice, or low-state trellis constructions. This is the constructive counterpart to the random-increment barrier: randomness refills merged states, while low prefix cut-rank preserves the merging.
- Added the design-facing version of low cut-rank. `cor:coboundary-quotient-trellis-width` proves that if the quotient edge increment is a native-state coboundary modulo a small subspace \(L\), i.e. \(\bar c(e)+L=\Phi(v)-\Phi(u)\) for every trellis edge, then all prefixes reaching a state \(v\) have quotient syndrome in one coset of \(L\), so \(Q_B\le |L|\). Exact coboundaries add no quotient state; \(\dim L=O(\log\log m)\) gives polylog support-conditioning overhead. This is the concrete algebraic design rule for a low-state convolutional/polar/recursive-lattice quotient: make the resident-support syndrome telescope through the native state, with only a small carry space left to track.
- Added the exact finite verifier for that design rule. `cor:merged-prefix-carry-space-verifier` defines \(L_{\rm merge}\) as the span of quotient-syndrome differences between prefixes that merge into the same native trellis state. It proves both directions: \(Q_B\le |L_{\rm merge}|\), and any coboundary-mod-\(L\) explanation must contain \(L_{\rm merge}\). Thus \(\dim L_{\rm merge}=O(\log\log m)\) is not just sufficient but the exact finite-state obstruction for the support-conditioned trellis. A concrete code/lattice experiment or proof should now measure this merge-difference span; if it grows like the full quotient, the construction is in the random-refill regime.
- Added the finite-memory forgetting barrier without turning it into another certificate form. `cor:finite-memory-forgetting-quotient-barrier` applies the merged-prefix verifier to sliding-window trellises: the span \(L_{\rm forget}\) of quotient differences attached to forgotten coordinates is contained in \(L_{\rm merge}\), so any coboundary-mod-\(L\) explanation must carry it. The actual width lower bound is the reachable forgotten sumset \(A_{\rm forget}\), not merely the linear span; if that sumset contains a \(d\)-dimensional affine subspace then \(Q_B\ge q^d\). This keeps the obstruction honest for large fields and says that product/convolutional/shift-register trellises with generic coordinate syndromes across \(\Theta(\log m)\) forgotten positions need telescoping, checkpoint refresh, or only \(O(\log\log m)\) forgotten quotient directions.
- Split the additive pseudo-random subset idea into the bad and good access modes. `cor:state-exposed-additive-filter-width` says that if an additive hash/score-band filter is decoded by carrying its exact auxiliary state through a merged trellis, pairwise-independent uniform prefix scores refill \(\Omega(M)\) states; a \(\delta\)-density nonempty filter therefore pays at least reciprocal width on saturated states. `cor:tilted-additive-tail-sampler` gives the constructive escape for filters of the form \(m(i_1)+\cdots+m(i_k)\ge s\): sample from the exponentially tilted product law and accept a tail sample with likelihood-ratio probability \(e^{-\lambda(M-s)}\). The output is exactly the original product law conditioned on the tail, with expected proposals controlled by tilted local mass near the threshold, not by the rare-event probability. This suggests a concrete pseudo-random route: prove local CLT/anti-concentration under the tilted tuple law, then use aggregate sampling/raw expansion rather than support-state enumeration.
- Added the first checkable local-limit criterion for that route. `cor:tilted-local-gaussian-window` says that if the tilted additive score \(M\) has mean within \(U\) standard deviations of the threshold and Gaussian Kolmogorov error at most \(\gamma_U/(4W_\lambda)\), \(W_\lambda=\max\{1,\lambda\sigma_\lambda\}\), then the tilted rejection sampler accepts in \(O_U(W_\lambda)\) proposals. Thus a pseudo-random additive tuple subset is usable at the clean endpoint if the native decoder can sample the exponential tilt, \(W_\lambda=\polylog m\), and the tilted score law has Gaussian mass in the one-sided threshold window.
