The remaining math for concrete code/lattice weights is no longer vague "random enough"; it is a saddlepoint/local-CLT estimate under the tilted product law.
- Added a moment-level verifier for the tilted local-limit target. `cor:tilted-berry-esseen-filter` applies Berry--Esseen to the tilted coordinate scores \(X_j=m_j(c_j)\): if \(C_{\rm BE}\sum_j\E|X_j-\mu_j|^3/\sigma^3\le\gamma_U/(4W_\lambda)\), then the additive-tail sampler has \(O_U(W_\lambda)\) proposals. In the bounded-increment case \(|X_j-\mu_j|\le B\), \(\sigma^2\ge vh\), it is enough that \(W_\lambda\lesssim_U\sqrt{vh}/B\). So the additive pseudo-random route needs many genuinely varying tilted coordinates and no dominant/coarse score direction; for \(W_\lambda=\polylog m\), product depth above the square-polylog scale is already sufficient at this moment level.
- Connected the additive-filter sampler to the actual high-score tuple event. `cor:joint-tilted-score-filter-sampler` handles two additive observables \(W=\sum w_j\) and \(M=\sum m_j\): sample from the product tilt \(R\exp(\beta W+\lambda M)\), reject outside \(W\ge t,M\ge s\), and accept with likelihood-ratio probability. The output is exactly the original product law conditioned on both the query score and pseudo-random filter. If the tilted standardized pair has bivariate Gaussian rectangle mass around \((t,s)\), with correlation bounded away from \(\pm1\), proposals cost \(O_{U,\kappa}(W_WW_M)\), where \(W_W=\max\{1,\beta\sigma_W\}\) and \(W_M=\max\{1,\lambda\sigma_M\}\). This is the exact-sampling counterpart to the older scalar-mask Lagrange heap and gives the right concrete target for tuple filters: a bivariate saddlepoint/local-CLT estimate under the native tilted product law.
- Made that joint tilted target moment-checkable instead of leaving it as a black-box Gaussian-window assumption. `cor:bivariate-tilted-berry-esseen-filter` applies the standard two-dimensional Berry--Esseen inequality for convex sets to the whitened increments \(Y_j=(w_j-\mu_{W,j},m_j-\mu_{M,j})\): if \(C_{\rm rect}\sum_j\E\|\Sigma^{-1/2}Y_j\|_2^3\le\gamma_{U,\kappa}/(8W_WW_M)\), then the exact score-and-filter sampler costs \(O_{U,\kappa}(W_WW_M)\) proposals. In the bounded-increment, nondegenerate-covariance case \(\|Y_j\|_2\le B\), \(\lambda_{\min}\Sigma\ge vh\), it is enough that \(W_WW_M\lesssim_{U,\kappa}\sqrt{vh}/B\). This is the current concrete test for pseudo-random additive tuple subsets: after the joint saddlepoint tilt, many coordinates must still vary in two independent directions, and the auxiliary filter must not be almost collinear with the query score.
- Answered the "how random does the subset have to be?" question for additive sign filters. `lem:pairwise-sign-mask-effective-support` fixes the query-tilted coordinate law \(P_j\) and shows that unbiased pairwise-independent signs only need large \(\ell_2\) effective support: the signed mass imbalance \(\alpha_j=\sum_aP_j(a)\xi_{j,a}\) has variance \(\nu_j=\sum_aP_j(a)^2\), and the signed score-correlation \(\zeta_j=\sum_aP_j(a)(w_j(a)-\bar w_j)\xi_{j,a}\) has variance \(\sum_aP_j(a)^2(w_j(a)-\bar w_j)^2\). After auxiliary tilting, \(\operatorname{Var}(\xi_j)=(1-\alpha_j^2)/(\cosh\lambda+\alpha_j\sinh\lambda)^2\) and \(\operatorname{Cov}(w_j,\xi_j)=\zeta_j/(\cosh\lambda+\alpha_j\sinh\lambda)^2\). Thus 2-independent affine/multiply-shift-style signs are plausible for a fixed high-effective-support query law, but they do not give a uniform quenched guarantee over all adaptive query laws without stronger limited independence, a deterministic audit, or a query-family net.
- Converted that caveat into a finite-family randomness criterion. `lem:finite-family-limited-independence-sign-filter` says that for a finite family \(\mathcal L\) of decoder-exposed tilted coordinate laws, \(2r\)-wise independent signs satisfy uniformly over all \((\ell,j)\) the bounds \(|\alpha_{\ell,j}|\lesssim\sqrt{r\nu_{\ell,j}}(2h|\mathcal L|/\delta)^{1/(2r)}\) and \(|\zeta_{\ell,j}|\lesssim\sqrt{r\tau_{\ell,j}}(2h|\mathcal L|/\delta)^{1/(2r)}\). Taking \(r\simeq\log(2h|\mathcal L|/\delta)\) gives a quenched seed for the whole finite decoder family. So the randomness answer is now sharp enough to guide constructions: pairwise independence/multiply-shift is enough for annealed or fixed-law tilted sampling, while a reusable data-structure seed needs higher moment order, deterministic effective-support auditing, or a smaller query-family net.
- Added the matching net transfer for continuous query laws. `lem:coefficient-net-sign-mask-transfer` shows that the finite-family sign audit extends from a net to all exposed tilted laws with only additive losses \(\|p-\tilde p\|_1\) and \(\|pg-\tilde p\tilde g\|_1\), because signs are bounded by one. Thus the metric entropy relevant to reusable pseudo-random additive signs is not the ambient query space or tuple universe; it is the covering number of the coordinate tilted laws in total variation together with the centered score-coefficient vector in \(\ell_1\). This gives a concrete route for numerics and for code/lattice proofs: measure effective support on a coefficient net, then choose the limited-independence order from that net size.
- Combined the additive-sign pieces into a usable sampler certificate. `cor:reusable-limited-independent-sign-filter` fixes the \(\beta\)-score-tilted coordinate law \(P_j\), adds an auxiliary sign tilt \(e^{\lambda\xi}\), and gives deterministic conditions under which the joint tilted rejection sampler returns the original product law conditioned on \(W\ge t,M\ge s\) in \(O(W_WW_M)\) proposals. The conditions are now concrete: bounded coordinate score diameter, small signed imbalance \(\alpha_j\), nondegenerate tilted score variance, score/sign correlation bounded away from one, thresholds inside a constant Gaussian window, and \(W_WW_M\lesssim\sqrt h\). The finite-family and coefficient-net lemmas certify the required \(\alpha_j,\zeta_j\) bounds for limited-independent signs. This is the current positive pseudo-random subset route: do not carry the hash state; sample the locally tilted product/code law and reject by likelihood ratio.
- Added the saddlepoint calibration for polylog-density sign thinning. `cor:polylog-density-sign-thinning-calibration` proves that if \(s-A_0=O(\sqrt{h\log K})\) above the base signed mean and the sign saddlepoint \(\lambda_s\) stays in a bounded window, then \(W_M=\max(1,\lambda_s\sigma_M)=O(\sqrt{\log K})\). Consequently a density-\(K^{-1}\) additive sign filter costs local Gaussian width, not reciprocal density or sign-state width. For \(K=\polylog m\), the remaining sampler condition is \(W_W\sqrt{\log K}\lesssim\sqrt h\) plus the finite/net effective-support audit. This is the cleanest current explanation of why pseudo-random additive subsets might remove a polylog branch-and-bound toll without requiring fully random tuple subsets.
- Connected that calibration to cumulative-score depth. `cor:aggregate-sign-tail-avoids-checkpoint-toll` compares \(L\) checkpoint sign filters with one aggregate sign tail over the full route. Enforcing \(M_\ell\ge s_\ell\) separately has generic local-Gaussian proposal toll \(\prod_\ell O(\sqrt{\log K_\ell})\), so it is polylogarithmic only when \(\sum_\ell\log\max\{2,\sqrt{\log K_\ell}\}=O(\log\log m)\). A single aggregate sign score at total density \(K^{-1}\), \(K=\prod_\ell K_\ell\), costs only \(O(\sqrt{\log K})\) and composes with the reusable sign-filter certificate as \(O(W_W\sqrt{\log K})\). Thus the positive long-route normal form is one aggregate additive filter sampled by the final tilted product/code law; checkpoint filters are acceptable only if few, nearly deterministic, or forced by the native decoder.
- Connected the aggregate sign sampler to the saturated route-value target. `cor:aggregate-sign-tail-value-compiler` says: start with an unfiltered route law whose prefixes are dominated by activation masses up to \(p_0^{-1}\); after applying one terminal aggregate filter \(F\), route value is preserved up to the single factor \(B_F=\sup_u P(F\mid u)/P(F)\). Therefore an aggregate sign tail discharges value with no sign-state width if \(p_0^{-1}B_F=\polylog m\), while a prefix or stopping frontier with superpolylogarithmic tail inflation is a real hidden bottleneck. This freezes the next test: prove or measure prefix-tail stability for the final tilted product/code law, rather than adding more checkpoint/certificate forms.
- Replaced the brittle worst-prefix test by a typical filtered-route test. `cor:typical-aggregate-tail-value` shows that the filtered trie value is controlled by the filtered-law quantile of \(H_P(v)+J_F(v)\), where \(H_P\) is the old route-prefix domination height and \(J_F\) is the maximum aggregate-tail inflation along the sampled route. `cor:saddlepoint-prefix-tail-inflation` then bounds \(J_F\) by \(\log(1/a)+\max_{u\preceq v}\log_+L_{\beta,\lambda}(u)\), where \(a\) is the tilted rejection acceptance and \(L_{\beta,\lambda}\) is the prefix likelihood ratio of the joint score/filter tilt. For polylog-density sign tails, \(a^{-1}=O(W_W\sqrt{\log K})\), so the remaining non-reformulation target is sharp: prove or measure that the maximum cumulative tilt likelihood along the filtered route is \(O(\log\log m)\) (or expose a stopping frontier where it is larger).
- Proved the bounded-increment positive case for that likelihood target. `cor:bounded-aggregate-tail-likelihood-toll` treats the native score-conditioned product law \(P\) and an added bounded aggregate tail \(M\ge s\). If the saddlepoint acceptance is \(a\), the variance-growth calibration gives \(s-A_0\le C_s\sqrt{h\log K}\), and \(K,h,a^{-1}\) are polylogarithmic, then under \(P(\cdot\mid M\ge s)\) the maximum prefix tilt likelihood is \(O(\log\log m)\) with probability at least \(1/2\). The proof uses \(P_F\le a^{-1}Q_\lambda\), Bernstein for the prefix log-likelihood under \(Q_\lambda\), and the entropy bound \(D(Q_\lambda\|P)\le C_s^2\log K/v_M\). Thus aggregate sign thinning is no longer just plausible in the product-prefix bounded case: it preserves saturated value up to a polylog factor provided the unfiltered route height was already polylog. The remaining construction burden has moved to certifying that concrete code/lattice/native route laws really reduce to this bounded product-prefix model after the score-side conditioning.
- Composed the bounded aggregate-tail certificate with the actual budget-thinned endpoint. `prop:bounded-aggregate-tail-value-delivery` says that if every unresolved near state supplies a query-decodable product-prefix route law \(P=\prod_jP_j\), unfiltered prefix height \(H_P=O(\log\log n)\), bounded aggregate increments, the saddlepoint variance-growth/acceptance conditions, and polylogarithmic \(K,h,a^{-1}\), then the filtered trie has saturated value \(\log^{-O_c(1)}n\), hence `asm:aggregate-value-delivery` holds and `prop:budget-thinned-polylog` gives \(d n^{\rho_c}\polylog n\) query and \(dn+n^{1+\rho_c}\polylog n\) space. This is now a clean conditional endpoint rather than a chain of equivalent certificates. The remaining pass/fail item is concrete: construct or numerically refute such a product-prefix route law for the actual score/code/lattice decoder.
- Added the missing audit for the unfiltered prefix-height hypothesis. `cor:product-prefix-route-height-bernstein` assumes activation masses factor as \(A(c_{\le r})=\prod_{j\le r}a_j(c_j)\), a product route law \(P=\prod_jP_j\), and a dominated product sampling law \(Q\). It bounds \(H_P=\max_r\log_+P(c_{\le r})/A(c_{\le r})\) by a Bernstein expression in \(D_+=\sum_j\E_Q[\log(P_j/a_j)]_+\), the variance of \(\log(P_j/a_j)\), and its range, concretely \(D_+ + \sqrt{V\log(hL/\eta)}+R\log(hL/\eta)\). For the natural activation-base saddlepoint \(P_j\propto a_j e^{\theta s_j}\), this is exactly a prefix likelihood-ratio audit. Thus the whole aggregate-tail endpoint now has two concrete numerical/theoretical checks: the sign-tail saddlepoint variance check and the route-height log-ratio check.
- Added the matching activation-volume screen. `cor:activation-volume-score-tilt-screen` says that if a product route law \(P\) has \(H_P\le H_0\), then \(D(P\|\bar A)\le\log B_A+H_0\), where \(\bar A\) is the normalized activation-base law and \(B_A=\prod_j\sum_ca_j(c)\) is total leaf activation volume. For an activation-base saddlepoint \(P_j\propto\bar a_j e^{\theta s_j}\), this becomes \(\log B_A\ge\theta\mu_\theta-\psi(\theta)-O(\log\log n)\). Thus random or multiply-shift aggregate tails can thin a route whose domination is already paid, but cannot hide an unpaid polynomial score-tilt entropy; a code/lattice route with too small a raw activation/list volume must change the charged object via quotient weights, Fano-priced prefixes, responsibility, or leader/aggregate sampling.
- Added the affine-hash/carry-rank dichotomy. `cor:affine-hash-carry-rank-dichotomy` makes the multiply-shift intuition precise: an affine residue filter \(y(p)=L(\sum_e g(e))+b\) is cheap as exposed state only when the aggregate feature has small reachable carry sets, equivalently a coboundary modulo polylogarithmic carry. If its images look pairwise-independent and uniform inside merged native states, the reciprocal-width barrier applies; then the same hash should be used only through tilted aggregate sampling, not by carrying hash state through the trellis.
- Added the positive half of the activation-volume screen. `cor:matched-fano-activation-route-height` proves that if the stored activation masses dominate a priced copy of the route law, \(a_j(c)\ge e^{-b_j}P_j(c)\), then \(H_P\le\sum_jb_j\) deterministically; with exact equality \(a_j=e^{-b_j}P_j\), the activation-volume lower bound is tight. This turns the code/lattice design rule into a concrete target: the Fano/stack/trellis price that makes the decoder output-sensitive must be part of the stored activation measure, not an after-the-fact pruning score on raw labels.
- Connected Gibbs near mass to the matched-Fano route height. `cor:gibbs-near-mass-matched-fano` shows that if a product Gibbs sampler has realized block success masses \(h_j\), and the successful route law conditions each block on its success event, then \(H_P=\sum_j\log(1/h_j)\). Thus inverse-polylogarithmic realized near Gibbs mass is exactly the route-height condition, while the averaged \(\mathfrak h_j\) in the sampled-product-panel bookkeeping identify the lower-tail/good-panel condition still needed for randomized code/lattice panels.
- Removed one unnecessary lower-tail burden from that conclusion. `cor:averaged-gibbs-mass-good-fano-hit` observes that for the pairwise sampled-panel ANN success event, the realized product near mass \(H\in[0,1]\) is itself the conditional hit probability. Therefore \(\mathbb E H=\mu\) implies a hit from a panel with \(H\ge\tau\) has probability at least \(\mu-\tau\). With \(\tau=\mu/2\) and \(B=O(1/\mu)\) panels, `(GR3)` already exposes a realized route of Fano height \(\log(2/\mu)=O(\log\log m)\). Separate lower-tail or second-moment work is only needed for stronger uniform/deterministic panel guarantees, not for the ordinary randomized near-hit analysis.
- Composed that observation with the bounded aggregate-tail endpoint. `cor:averaged-gibbs-mass-aggregate-value` instantiates the likelihood-toll lemma on the conditional near route \(P^E=\Pi(\cdot\mid E)\): for a realized panel with product near mass \(H\), if the aggregate filter has acceptance \(a\) and prefix likelihood toll \(B_{\rm like}\le B_*\), then the filtered saturated value satisfies \(U_F(\varnothing)\ge (a/2)e^{-B_*}H\). Averaging gives \(\mathbb E U_F\ge(a/2)e^{-B_*}\mathbb E H\). Thus `(GR3)` survives terminal aggregate thinning up to the explicit local-limit/toll factor; the remaining construction task is the conditional saddlepoint/local-CLT audit under the actual code/lattice/product near route law.
- Added the concrete randomness audit for that remaining sign-tail task. `cor:conditional-effective-support-aggregate-sign-tail` applies the sign-mask formulas after conditioning on the near event \(E\): if the conditional route laws \(P^E_j\) have small sign imbalance \(|\alpha_j|\le a_0<1\), then sign variance grows at rate \((1-a_0^2)e^{-2\Lambda}h\), Berry--Esseen gives \(A_\lambda^{-1}=O(W_\lambda)\), and the aggregate-tail toll \(B_*\) is polylogarithmic when \(K,h,W_\lambda\) are polylogarithmic. Limited-independent signs certify \(|\alpha_j|\) uniformly from the conditional effective supports \(\nu_{\ell,j}=\sum_aP^E_{\ell,j}(a)^2\). Thus pairwise independence is enough for one fixed high-support conditional route, while reusable seeds need moment order set by the finite coefficient net; if conditioning collapses many blocks to singleton laws, the pseudo-random sign tail has no variance and this route fails.
- Turned the conditional effective-support requirement into a block-level pass/fail audit. `cor:block-collision-audit-conditional-sign-tail` defines \(\nu_j^E=\sum_aP_j^E(a)^2=(\sum_{a\in E_j}\Pi_j(a)^2)/h_j^2\) and \(N_{2,j}^E=1/\nu_j^E\). Pairwise signs have \(\mathbb E\alpha_j^2=\nu_j^E\), and limited-independent signs give the same finite-family bound as before with \(\nu_j^E\). Concrete positive screens are \(p_{\infty,j}\le\varepsilon h_j\Rightarrow N_{2,j}^E\ge1/\varepsilon\), uniform weights giving \(N_{2,j}^E=|E_j|\), and \(\Lambda\)-balanced weights giving \(N_{2,j}^E\ge |E_j|/\Lambda\). The matching barrier is \(p_{\infty,j}^E>(1+a_0)/2\): then every deterministic sign assignment has \(|\alpha_j|>a_0\), so multiply-shift/affine/full randomness cannot create aggregate variance. A construction can use only the good coordinate set \(J\); all formulas above apply with \(h\) replaced by \(|J|\).
- Rewrote that audit in the form a code/lattice decoder can compute. `cor:two-temperature-gibbs-block-support` shows that for a Gibbs block \(\Pi_j(a)\propto e^{\beta\phi_j(a)}\) and near event \(E_j\), \(h_j=Z_E(\beta)/Z(\beta)\) while \(\nu_j^E=Z_E(2\beta)/Z_E(\beta)^2\). Equivalently \(\log N_{2,j}^E=2F_E(\beta)-F_E(2\beta)\). Thus the remaining sign-tail test for Tanner/convolutional/polar/product/lattice panels is a two-temperature constrained free-energy gap, using the same tail partition function already needed for near mass. If this gap is too small, the conditioned Gibbs law is too concentrated and pseudo-random aggregate thinning is not the right route.
- Specialized the two-temperature support audit to the simplest Gaussian/product tail block. `cor:gaussian-tail-block-support-audit` assumes the query Gibbs score \(X\) and storage/near score \(Y\) are bivariate Gaussian with correlation \(\eta\), and gives the exact quadrature exponent
  \[
  \log N_2^E=\log N-\beta^2+2\log\overline\Phi(\theta-\eta\beta)-\log\overline\Phi(\theta-2\eta\beta)+O(1).
  \]
  Thus an easy near event leaves only the Gibbs Renyi support \(Ne^{-\beta^2}\), while a hard tail with \(\theta-2\eta\beta\ge1\) has \(\log N_2^E=\log N-(1-\eta^2)\beta^2-\theta^2/2+O(\log(\theta+|\beta|))\). This is a concrete proof/barrier test for code/lattice blocks: if the storage tail is not aligned with the query tilt, the hidden-coordinate loss can consume the conditional support before randomness matters.
- Pushed that Gaussian support audit through the native ANN rates. `cor:critical-gaussian-tail-sign-support-barrier` sets \(T^2=2\tau\log m\), \(\beta=T/a_0\), \(N=m^{\nu+o(1)}\), and shows \(\log N_2^E=(\nu-2\tau/a_0^2)\log m+o(\log m)\). Critical storage and far-load require \(\nu-\tau\le\rho_c\) and \(\tau\ge1-\rho_c\), hence \(\log N_2^E\le-\Delta_c\log m+o(\log m)\) with \(\Delta_c=2(1-\rho_c)/a_0^2-1>0\) (\(43/21\) at \(c=2\)). Since a nonempty finite conditional law has \(N_2^E\ge1\), no subexponential-distortion iid-like Gaussian two-temperature quadrature can satisfy the critical rates and still feed aggregate sign thinning. The surviving code/lattice path must change the charged object (quotient/responsibility/Fano) or build a repulsive low-temperature quadrature with unusually small \(Z_E(2\beta)\).
- Quantified that last survivor condition instead of leaving it qualitative. `cor:repulsive-low-temperature-deficit-necessary` assumes only first-temperature Gaussian-like mass \(Z_E(\beta)\le C_1G_1\), defines the doubled-temperature deficit \(D_2=\log(G_2/Z_E(2\beta))\), and proves that passing the support audit at level \(S=m^{\sigma+o(1)}\) under the critical rates forces \(D_2\ge(\Delta_c+\sigma)\log m-o(\log m)\). At \(c=2\), this is \(m^{43/21+\sigma-o(1)}\) suppression of \(Z_E(2\beta)\) relative to the iid Gaussian/theta prediction. So a positive code/lattice sign-tail route now has a measurable target: either change the charged object via quotient/responsibility/Fano, or prove a genuinely repulsive low-temperature partition function with a polynomial second-temperature deficit while preserving first-temperature near mass and far/storage exponents.
- Added the structural flat-band consequence of that support audit. `cor:support-audit-flat-near-label-band` shows that if \(N_2^E\ge S\) and \(U_E=|E|\), then at least half of the conditional Gibbs mass lies on at least \(S/8\) near labels whose \(\beta\phi\)-scores are contained in an interval of width \(\log(16U_E/S)\). More generally, high conditional atoms have total mass at most \(A^{-1}\) and tiny atoms at most \(\delta\), leaving an explicit middle band. This gives a concrete code/lattice falsifier: a claimed repulsive low-temperature block cannot be powered by one exceptional lattice point or residue; it must exhibit a large comparable near-label band, or else the sign/additive-mask branch is dead and the construction must use quotient/responsibility/Fano reporting.
- Added the matching positive code/lattice target. `cor:affine-flat-near-fiber-sign-audit` says that if the near event is an affine finite-field fiber \(z_0+L\) of size \(M=q^s\) and the Gibbs log-score spread on that fiber is at most \(\Gamma\), then \(N_2^E\ge Me^{-\Gamma}\) and pairwise signs have \(\mathbb E\alpha_j^2\le e^\Gamma/M\). Thus the usable sign branch is now concrete: find query-decodable residual affine fibers or trellis cosets with nearly flat near-conditioned Gibbs weights. If \(M=\polylog m\) they can be enumerated after one affine solve; if \(M\) is larger, a BCJR/theta/partition recursion must aggregate or sample the fiber, because listing all labels would reintroduce the branch-and-bound overhead.
- Connected that flat-fiber target to the row-span/rank decoder cost instead of introducing a new certificate form. `cor:score-observer-rank-affine-support` takes a near chart \(E=z_0+\ker B\) and a residual score observer \(D\). If the Gibbs score is flat inside each joint fiber of \(B\) and \(D\), with \(r_D=\operatorname{rank}\binom BD-\operatorname{rank}B\), then every score state has \(q^{d-r_D}\) hidden labels and \(N_2^E\ge e^{-\Gamma}q^{d-r_D}\). Thus \(r_D\) is the state enumeration/BCJR/theta burden, while \(d-r_D\) is the conditional support feeding pairwise signs. The concrete positive window is \(q^{r_D}=\polylog m\) and \(q^{d-r_D}\ge e^\Gamma S\); generic high-rank score observers or tiny neutral kernels fail for opposite reasons.
- Added the matching random-quotient barrier for that window. `cor:generic-quotient-score-observer-window-barrier` fixes a rank-\(r\) score observer \(D\) and chooses the quotient row space \(B\) randomly with codimension \(d\). If a materialized observer proof can expose only \(Q_{\max}\) score states and needs support \(S\), then it requires \(R_D\le s_\star=\lfloor\min\{\log_qQ_{\max},d-\log_q(e^\Gamma S)\}\rfloor\), but \(\Pr[R_D\le s_\star]\le16q^{-(r-s_\star)(d-s_\star)}\). At the polylog observer window with \(r,d=\Omega(\log_qm)\), this is \(q^{-\Omega((\log_qm)^2)}\). So random low-rate parity checks are not the missing code theorem; the quotient must deliberately align with the score observer while leaving a neutral kernel, or avoid materializing residual score states.
- Added the missing geometric flatness screen for those neutral kernels. `cor:gaussian-flatness-contracts-neutral-fibers` says that if an affine neutral fiber contains an \(M\)-point Euclidean \(\Delta\)-packing of diameter \(R\), then for a Gaussian query score the probability of \(\Gamma\)-flatness is at most \(\exp(- (c\beta\Delta\sqrt{\log M}-\Gamma)_+^2/(2\beta^2R^2))\). Thus algebraic kernel dimension is not enough: at \(\beta=\Theta(\sqrt{\log m})\), the surviving neutral fiber must have small Gaussian width or contracted Euclidean directions in the actual score metric. This recovers the earlier "anchor fibers must be contracted" warning as a concrete pass/fail test for Tanner, Construction-A, and lattice kernels.
- Specialized the geometric screen to weighted one-hot/Hamming code kernels. `cor:hamming-neutral-kernel-flatness-barrier` applies the Sudakov flatness bound to \(E=z_0+L\subseteq\mathbb F_q^b\) with embedding \(x_z=(\omega_i e_{z_i})_i\), giving flatness probability at most \(\exp(-(c\beta\sqrt{d_\omega(L)s\log q}-\Gamma)_+^2/(4\beta^2W_\omega))\). Thus a random-code-like neutral kernel with comparable coordinate weights and linear weighted distance is not the positive flat-fiber instance; the surviving affine branch needs tiny weighted minimum distance, entropy concentrated in low-score coordinates, or a genuine geometric contraction/responsibility change.
- Made the random-code obstruction quantitative. `cor:random-linear-neutral-flatness-barrier` combines the standard q-ary Hamming-ball union bound for a random \(s\)-dimensional subspace with the weighted one-hot flatness screen: below the GV distance threshold, \(\Pr_{L,g}[\mathsf{Sp}_{z_0+L}\le\Gamma]\le2e^{-\varepsilon b\log q}+\exp(-(c\beta\sqrt{\omega_-\delta bs\log q}-\Gamma)_+^2/(4\beta^2b\omega_+))\). At constant rate and comparable weights this is \(e^{-\Omega(b)}\) for ANN-scale \(\beta\) and polylogarithmic \(\Gamma\), so uniformly random low-rate parity checks create distance, not the contracted flat neutral fibers needed by the sign-support branch.
- Added the matching positive screen for the surviving affine branch. `cor:low-score-coordinate-neutral-flatness` lets \(I(L)\) be the coordinates that can vary inside \(L\), \(W_L=\sum_{i\in I(L)}\omega_i^2\), and proves \(\mathsf{Sp}_E\le4\beta\sqrt{W_L(s\log q+\log(2/\eta))}\) with probability \(1-\eta\). Hence \(N_2^E\ge q^s\exp(-\Gamma_\eta)\) and pairwise signs have collision energy at most \(\exp(\Gamma_\eta)q^{-s}\). At ANN scale, polylog flatness forces \(W_L(s\log q+\log(2/\eta))=O_c((\log\log m)^2/\log m)\): the constructive target is neutral entropy sheltered in tiny-score-weight coordinates, or an equivalent geometric contraction.
- Made that positive screen checkable from the coordinate weight spectrum. `cor:sheltered-entropy-coordinate-order-audit` sorts \(a_i=\omega_i^2\) and proves every \(s\)-dimensional sheltered subspace pays \(W_L\ge A_s^\downarrow=\sum_{r\le s}a_{(r)}\), while the coordinate subspace on the \(s\) lightest coordinates attains this value and gives \(N_2\ge\exp(s\log q-4\beta\sqrt{A_s^\downarrow(s\log q+\log(2/\eta))})\). Under equal weights, \(b=\lambda\log m\), and \(s=\kappa\log\log m\), the flatness loss is \(4\kappa\sqrt{B\log q/\lambda}\log\log m+o(\log\log m)\). Thus the plausible positive code/lattice template is not random distance: use a long block to dilute per-coordinate score weight, then leave only \(O(\log\log m)\) light coordinates neutral, giving polylog support with polylog enumeration/sampling.
- Compiled that order statistic into an explicit polylog sign panel. `cor:sheltered-coordinate-polylog-sign-panel` assumes equal weights \(\omega_i^2=1/b\), \(b=\lambda\log m\), \(\beta^2\le B\log m\), and a near chart leaving \(s=\kappa\log\log m\) coordinates free. With failure \((\log m)^{-a}\), it gives \(\log N_2^E\ge[\kappa\log q-4\sqrt{B\kappa(\kappa\log q+a)/\lambda}+o(1)]\log\log m\) and fiber size \(q^s=(\log m)^{\kappa\log q+o(1)}\). Thus \(N_2^E\ge(\log m)^\sigma\) whenever the displayed bracket exceeds \(\sigma\); for constant failure this is possible exactly when \(\lambda>16B/\log q\). The positive construction target is now a long diluted block whose near chart fixes all high-score coordinates and leaves only \(O(\log\log m)\) neutral coordinates to enumerate or sample.
- Added a sharper adaptive binary version of the sheltered chart. `cor:binary-low-gap-frozen-chart` sorts the realized binary coordinate gaps \(\delta_i=|\phi_i(1)-\phi_i(0)|\), freezes all but the \(s\) smallest-gap coordinates to their top symbols, and proves both threshold containment when the deficit is below \(\delta_{(s+1)}\) and deterministic flatness \(\mathsf{Sp}_{E_s}\le\beta\sum_{r\le s}\delta_{(r)}\). For Gaussian equal-weight coordinates, \(\sum_{r\le s}\delta_{(r)}\le C s(s+\log(1/\eta))/b^{3/2}\) with probability \(1-\eta\). Thus for \(b=\lambda\log m\), \(s=\kappa\log\log m\), and \(\beta^2\le B\log m\), the flatness loss is \(O((\log\log m)^2/\log m)=o(1)\) and \(N_2=(\log m)^{\kappa\log2-o(1)}\). This is the cleanest positive low-rate-code pattern so far: one adaptive low-gap chart, polylog enumeration, and no random Hamming-distance kernel.
- Added the matching containment audit for that low-gap chart. `cor:low-gap-chart-deficit-audit` proves \(E_s\) contains every label above \(M-\Delta\) iff \(\Delta<\delta_{(s+1)}\), and with high probability \(\delta_{(s+1)}\le C(s+1+\log(1/\eta))/b^{3/2}\). Thus for \(b=\lambda\log m\) and polylog chart size \(2^s\), complete one-chart threshold reporting only reaches deficits \(O((\log\log m+\log(1/\eta))/(\log m)^{3/2})\). Covering a larger deficit \(\Delta_m\) requires \(s=\Omega(\Delta_m b^{3/2})\) free coordinates and chart size \(2^{\Omega(\Delta_m b^{3/2})}\). This keeps the positive pattern honest: low-gap charts provide flat conditional support near the realized top word, but broad near thresholds need an outer OSD/GMD/list reporter, several charts, or a quotient/responsibility/Fano charge.
- Added the quotient composition that makes that last sentence concrete. `cor:low-gap-shortened-subcode-compiler` takes a linear code, a low-gap coordinate set \(J\), and the shortened subcode \(\mathcal C_J=\{v:v_H=0\}\) of dimension \(d_J\). Any outer reporter on the quotient projection \(D_H\) that lists all quotient labels with upper envelope \(U_H\ge\Theta\), followed by enumeration of the fiber \(F(u)\), is an exact threshold reporter. Interior fibers with lower envelope \(L_H\ge\Theta\) have \(N_2\ge q^{d_J}e^{-\beta R_J}\), while all uncertified fibers lie in the quotient boundary band \(\Theta\le U_H<\Theta+R_J\). For binary low-gap coordinates, \(\beta R_J=O((\log\log m)^2/\log m)\). So the remaining constructive theorem is now specific: build a polylog outer quotient reporter for \(D_H\) and charge/avoid the vanishing-width boundary band `(SLC9)`; the low-gap flatness itself is no longer the obstacle.
- Added the rank-defect audit for that shortened-subcode route. `cor:shortened-low-gap-rank-defect` proves \(d_J=|J|-\operatorname{rank}A_J\) for a parity-check matrix \(A\). MDS/Reed--Solomon-style blocks have \(d_J=(|J|-(b-k))_+\), and a random dense parity-check matrix satisfies \(\Pr[d_J\ge d]\le4q^{-d(h-|J|+d)}\) for a fixed low-gap set, with the all-subset union bound \(4\binom b{|J|}q^{-d(h-|J|+d)}\). At \(b=\Theta(\log m)\), \(|J|=O(\log\log m)\), and \(h=\Omega(\log m)\), random or generic full-rank small-column families have no shortened low-gap entropy. Therefore a positive code/lattice construction must intentionally include local neutral degrees in low-score coordinates, use a quotient/responsibility/Fano charge, or aggregate hidden mass by a theta/trellis recursion; accidental random-code shortening will not supply the polylog fiber.
- Added the explicit planted-neutral construction and its occupancy tradeoff. `cor:parallel-neutral-pocket-occupancy` partitions coordinates into pockets with parallel parity-check columns, so \(d_J=\sum_t(|J\cap P_t|-1)_+=|J|-\#\{\text{occupied pockets}\}\). For a uniform \(r\)-set, \(\mathbb E d_J=r-\sum_t(1-\binom{b-|P_t|}{r}/\binom br)\), and if the largest pocket has size \(L\), then \(\mathbb E d_J\le\binom r2(L-1)/(b-1)\). Thus constant-size duplicate gadgets almost never help when \(r=O(\log\log m)\) and \(b=\Theta(\log m)\). Balanced pockets with \(p=\alpha r\) have \(d_J/r\to1-\alpha(1-e^{-1/\alpha})\) with \(O(\sqrt r)\) concentration, and the low-gap score spread still has \(\beta R_J=o(1)\). So the only viable planted-shortening shape is long pockets of length \(\Theta(\log m/\log\log m)\); this gives the neutral fiber, not the outer quotient reporter or far-load certificate.
- Added the matching answer for using another random additive weighting \(m(i_1)+\cdots+m(i_h)\ge s\). `cor:support-collapse-blocks-bounded-additive-masks` shows that for any bounded coordinate score, after conditioning on near success and applying any bounded saddlepoint tilt, \(\operatorname{Var} M\le R^2e^{\Lambda R}\sum_j(N_{2,j}^E-1)\). For genuinely random mean-zero unit-variance coordinate weights, the untilted expected block variance is exactly \(1-\nu_j^E=1-1/N_{2,j}^E\). Thus the randomness requirement is not full tuple independence: pairwise randomness is enough for a fixed high-support conditional law, but no bounded random weighting, multiply-shift score, tabulation score, or full random score can compensate for singleton-ish conditional support. This ties the scalar/random-subset question back to the frozen two-temperature audit.
